## 2026-06-30 — Ghost overlay spawns in ground for DoubleStep/TripleStep motions

**What changed:** Added `body_pos_w` property override in `GhostMotionCommand` (`mdp/commands.py`) that adds +0.030 m to the Z coordinate of all ghost body positions.

**Why it was wrong:** `pkl_to_npz` stores NPZ `body_pos_w` with the foot capsule contact surface at Z = -0.030 m (`_CONTACT_Z_TARGET = -0.030`). The RSI system compensates at runtime by adding `_FOOT_CONTACT_BELOW_BODY = +0.030` to the root Z in `MotionResetManager._write_rsi_state()`. The ghost overlay (`GhostMotionCommand`) replays positions directly from the NPZ via the inherited `MotionCommand.body_pos_w` property without applying this offset, so the ghost robot spawned 0.030 m into the floor.

**Correct value:** Ghost `body_pos_w` = NPZ positions + env_origins + (0, 0, +0.030). Matches what RSI writes to sim.

**Evidence:** All 4 overlay clips (LeftDoubleStep, RightDoubleStep, LeftTripleStep, RightTripleStep) visually embedded in the floor.

---

## 2026-06-30 — Ghost overlay in ground (v2): baked Z offset into NPZ files

**What changed:**
- Added +0.030 m to `body_pos_w[:, :, 2]` in all 14 NPZ motion files in `src/simple_goalkeeper/motions/data/`
- Removed runtime `_FOOT_CONTACT_BELOW_BODY = 0.030` addition from `_write_rsi_state` in `events.py`
- Changed `_CONTACT_Z_TARGET` in `pkl_to_npz.py` from `-0.030` to `0.0` so future conversions output correct Z directly
- Reverted ghost command override in `commands.py` (previous fix was wrong approach)

**Why it was wrong:** The original fix tried to offset positions at runtime inside `GhostMotionCommand.body_pos_w`. The correct fix is to store the correct floor-relative Z in the NPZ files themselves, so all consumers (ghost overlay, RSI, AMP discriminator) see consistent data without any runtime patches.

**Evidence:** All 4 step motions (LeftDoubleStep, RightDoubleStep, LeftTripleStep, RightTripleStep) spawning in the floor during overlay visualization.

## 2026-07-05 — Ported SimpleGoalKeeper's post-save ball-visibility gate v2

**What changed:** `ball_pos_xy_b` (`mdp/observations.py`) gains `hide_behind_torso`, `hide_after_steps`, and `noise_scale` params. The actor's `ball_pos_b` term (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) now sets `hide_behind_torso=True`, `hide_after_steps=75`, `noise_scale=0.05`, and the manager-level `Unoise` on that term is removed (noise now applied inside the term, before the mask). Play mode explicitly zeroes `noise_scale` since `enable_corruption=False` only disables manager-level noise.

**Why it was wrong (in SimpleGoalKeeper, the source of this port):** The actor's ball observation was fully visible for the entire episode with no post-save release, so the policy never learned to stop reacting once the ball left play — it kept tracking/chasing the ball after a save. An earlier attempt (`hide_when_behind`, a latched-flag gate) destabilized a training run (17% of iterations diverged). This project (interceptV2DualDis) never had a post-save release at all — its own `always_visible=True` came from reverting a *different, stricter* mistake (`8803b6e`: hiding the ball for the whole episode via G1's train-time vanish gate, not just post-save).

**Correct value:** Full visibility during the approach and save (ball visible until `x_body < 0.05` behind the torso, or until episode step 75 — sized for this project's 0.7–1.3 s flight-time range and 3 s episodes, identical to SimpleGoalKeeper's). Neither condition can fire while the ball is still en route.

**Evidence:** Not yet validated against a training run in this project (ported from SimpleGoalKeeper's `ce69f36`, which required and got a fresh run there). Next checkpoint here should be checked in play for post-save tracking/chasing behavior.

## 2026-07-05 — Ported SimpleGoalKeeper's `cleanstop` threshold tightening

**What changed:** `cleanstop`'s `speed_threshold` param (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) lowered from `0.25` to `0.10` m/s.

**Why it was wrong:** After a deflection the ball is typically sliding, and translational friction during the slide-to-roll transition bleeds speed on its own — at 0.25 m/s this decay alone could cross the threshold with no genuine foot-trap, i.e. `cleanstop` could fire from friction rather than a real save.

**Correct value:** `speed_threshold=0.10`. Harder for pure friction decay to clear within the post-save window, still reachable by an actual foot-trap. Mechanism otherwise unchanged (one-shot bonus, weight 25.0, requires `softstop` already fired + correct-foot contact at that moment).

**Evidence:** Ported from SimpleGoalKeeper (`f974942`), not yet independently validated against a training run in this project.

## 2026-07-05 — WandB never actually enabled; missing SGK's full episode-metrics logging

**What changed:** Two separate bugs in `rsl_rl_multi/him_amp_on_policy_runner.py`, fixed together (user-reported: terminal output and the wandb.ai dashboard both looked far sparser than SimpleGoalKeeper's, despite this being "directly copied" from a working SGK setup):

1. `learn()` always constructed a plain `torch.utils.tensorboard.SummaryWriter`, never checking `cfg["use_wandb"]` — unlike the stock `AMPOnPolicyRunner` (`rsl_rl_amp`), which swaps in `WandbSummaryWriter` when the flag is set. The multi-disc config's `"use_wandb": True` was silently ignored; nothing was ever pushed to wandb.ai.
2. The rollout loop never collected `infos["episode"]`/`infos["log"]`, and `learn()` only ever logged 8 raw scalars via one terse `print()` line — vs. the stock runner's ~68-tag breakdown (every `Episode_Reward/*` term, every `Episode_Termination/*` cause, curriculum weights, AMP discriminator stats, policy noise, FPS/perf). This runner is a from-scratch rewrite for the 4-discriminator setup, not an actual copy of the stock runner, so that logging block was simply never written.

Also: `predict_region_routed_amp_reward` (`multi_disc_amp_ppo.py`) discarded the per-sample discriminator logits and raw AMP reward it already computed (`_, _`) — needed to log `Train/mean_amp_reward`/`Train/mean_discri_logits` like the stock runner. Now returns all three.

Separately (same request): `wandb_project` changed from `"SimpleGoalKeeper-MultiDisc"` to `"SimpleGoalKeeper"` (same project as SGK itself, not a separate one), and `experiment_name`/`run_name` gained an `intercept_` prefix (`intercept_simple_goalkeeper_multidisc` / `intercept_phase1`) so intercept's local log folders and W&B run names/groups are clearly distinguishable within that shared project.

**Why it was wrong:** Both gaps trace to the same root cause — this project's multi-discriminator training loop (`HimAMPOnPolicyRunner`) is a bespoke runner written to support 4 region-specific discriminators + HIM estimator heads, and never had either piece (W&B wiring, rich episode logging) ported over from the stock runner it otherwise parallels.

**Correct value:** `learn()`'s writer selection and `log()` method now mirror `AMPOnPolicyRunner`'s exactly (same `Episode/*`/`Loss/*`/`Policy/*`/`Perf/*`/`Train/*` tags and console table format), extended with this runner's own `Loss/est_ball`/`Loss/est_region` auxiliary losses.

**Evidence:** 48/48 existing tests still pass (no test covered this runner directly). Verified with a live smoke run (`--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 3 --agent.use-wandb False`, throwaway experiment folder deleted afterward): full `Episode_Reward/*`/`Episode_Termination/*`/`Curriculum/*` breakdown printed and logged, exit code 0, no crash. A real run (`2026-07-05_12-08-20_intercept_phase1`) separately confirmed the W&B piece lands online (`run-20260705_120826-tw5dzkqb`, not an offline run folder) under the wiring fix alone, before this logging fix was added.

**Evidence:** Ported from SimpleGoalKeeper (`f974942`), not yet independently validated against a training run in this project.

## 2026-07-05 — `region_estimator` regressed to near-chance under `schedule="adaptive"`; two related runner bugs fixed

**What changed:** `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_runner_cfg()`'s `"schedule"` changed from `"adaptive"` to `"fixed"` (`tasks/goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`). Also fixed in `him_amp_on_policy_runner.py`: `learn()` now sets `self.current_learning_iteration = it` right before each periodic checkpoint save (previously only updated once, after the whole `learn()` call returned, so every intermediate `model_{it}.pt`'s embedded `"iter"` field was always `0`); `load()` now restores `self.current_learning_iteration = loaded["iter"]` (previously never touched it at all, so any resume would silently restart iteration numbering at 0 and could overwrite/collide with existing checkpoint filenames in the same run directory).

**Why it was wrong:** User reported the region-conditioned track "again didn't learn to double step" after stopping run `2026-07-05_12-16-46_intercept_phase1` at `model_5750`. A live diagnostic (loaded `model_500.pt` and `model_5250.pt`, drove 512 envs with the trained policy 400 steps, compared `region_estimator`'s live argmax against `env._region_id`) found: `model_500` — 38.5% accuracy (chance=25%), real signal on all 4 classes; `model_5250` — 27.7% accuracy, confusion matrix shows near-total collapse onto `left_near`/`right_near` only, **unable to distinguish true left from true right at all** (~48%/52% split regardless of true side), "far" classes almost never predicted (6% vs true 50%). This is a regression, not "never learned" — and it happened *after* `ball_difficulty` curriculum reached 1.0 (step 2750), when near/far should have gotten easier to tell apart, not harder, ruling out task difficulty as the explanation.

Comparing against `Humanoid-Goalkeeper/rsl_rl/rsl_rl/algorithms/him_ppo.py` (this runner's origin): G1 defaults to `schedule="fixed"` and `g1_29_config.py` never overrides it — the proven reference run used a constant LR throughout. This project's multi-disc config set `"adaptive"` instead, with no divergence-table entry — an undocumented departure from the proven baseline. Root cause: `region_arg` (`torch.argmax(estimate_region)`, identical construction to G1's) is a discrete value feeding the actor's own input; small logit shifts in the still-training `region_estimator` can flip this argmax between minibatch/epoch passes within one PPO update, inflating the *measured* policy KL divergence for reasons unrelated to real policy movement. `MultiDiscAMPPPO` uses **one Adam optimizer / one shared learning rate for the entire `actor_critic`** (actor, critic, history encoder, ball estimator, region estimator together — `multi_disc_amp_ppo.py:75`), so the adaptive-KL scheduler's repeated `lr /= 1.5` in response to that spurious KL froze capacity for every head, not just the policy. Confirmed empirically: `model_5750.pt`'s saved `optimizer_state_dict` shows `lr=7.59e-5` for every param group — 13x below the configured `1.0e-3`, sitting at the scheduler's floor. This also plausibly explains the double-step failure: the actor's own belief about which region it's in was unreliable, and by the time it mattered the whole network's effective learning rate had collapsed, leaving no room to learn a behavior (double-stepping on far balls) that depends on that signal.

The iteration-tracking bugs were fixed alongside because resuming training to test the schedule fix requires them: without the fix, a resume would restart `it` at 0 and silently overwrite `model_0.pt` onward in the same directory.

**Correct value:** `schedule="fixed"`, `learning_rate=1.0e-3` (unchanged base value, now never adaptively touched). Iteration counter now correctly tracked through save/load.

**Evidence:** Live probe confusion matrices (see above) and the checkpoint's own `optimizer_state_dict` lr values are the direct evidence for the collapse; the G1 default comparison is the basis for the fix. **Not yet independently confirmed as the fix** — training was resumed from `model_5750.pt` (fresh optimizer state, `load_optimizer=False`, to avoid inheriting Adam moments tuned for a near-zero LR regime) with `schedule="fixed"` to observe whether `Loss/est_region`/region accuracy recovers; results pending.

---

## 2026-07-05 — `schedule="fixed"` alone isn't resume-safe; region_estimator split into its own optimizer param group; a checkpoint got overwritten along the way

**What changed:** `MultiDiscAMPPPO.__init__` now builds `region_estimator`'s parameters as a separate optimizer param group (name `"region_estimator"`) with its own learning rate, decoupled from the shared `"actor_critic"` group (actor, critic, history_encoder, ball_estimator). New config key `region_estimator_learning_rate` (default `3.0e-3`) in `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_runner_cfg()`. The adaptive-KL block in `update()` now skips the `"region_estimator"` group by name so it can never be silently overwritten if `schedule="adaptive"` is ever re-enabled.

**Why it was wrong:** The previous fix (`schedule="fixed"`, prior entry above) is correct for a *fresh* training run, but three attempts to resume the already-collapsed `2026-07-05_12-16-46_intercept_phase1` run with a single shared LR for the whole `actor_critic` all failed: (1) `load_optimizer=False` at the config's full `1.0e-3` (a 13x jump from the settled `~7.6e-5`) blew the policy up within 5 iterations — reward to -1e8, episode length ~12, every episode ending in `bad_orientation`/`base_height`/`shank_height`, almost certainly Adam's cold-start instability (near-zero `exp_avg_sq` denominator inflates the first few effective steps) compounding an oversized LR on an already-converged policy; (2) `load_optimizer=True` at a gentler `2.0e-4` (~2.5x) avoided the catastrophic blowup but left episode length stuck at ~22 (vs. pre-resume ~130) for 200 iterations with no recovery — even a modest shared-group bump was enough to knock the converged policy out of the narrow basin it had settled into under the slowly-decaying adaptive LR. Splitting `region_estimator` into its own group sidesteps this entirely: the main group can stay at its settled, safe LR (zero intended change to the already-good policy) while `region_estimator` gets real capacity to move, with no path for its gradient to touch any other module's weights.

**Separately, an operational mistake during attempt (1):** it resumed *in place* into the original run directory with `current_learning_iteration` manually set to exactly `5750` — a multiple of `save_interval=250` — so the very first loop iteration's periodic-save check fired immediately and **overwrote `model_5750.pt`** with the already-blown-up post-update state. Caught by checking the corrupted file's own saved `optimizer_state_dict` lr (`0.001`, matching attempt 1's setting, not the expected `~7.6e-5`) and its `"iter"` field (`5750`, only possible because this session's own iteration-tracking fix, from the prior entry, was already in place). Renamed on disk to `model_5750.pt.CORRUPTED-by-resume-attempt-do-not-use` rather than deleted. `model_5500.pt` (saved before that attempt started) and `model_5250.pt` (already pushed to git, commit `666f2f5`) are unaffected and were used for the successful resume instead.

**Correct value:** `region_estimator_learning_rate=3.0e-3` for `region_estimator`'s own param group; main group resumed at the settled `7.593750000000002e-05` read directly from `model_5500.pt`'s own `optimizer_state_dict` (not hardcoded, to avoid a repeat of the exact mismatch that caused attempt 3's blowup when it unknowingly read `1.0e-3` back out of the corrupted `model_5750.pt`). Any future resume must target a run directory distinct from the one being resumed from.

**Evidence:** Resumed from `model_5500.pt` into a separate directory (`..._continued_region_fix`): episode length recovered from ~22 to ~130+ within 7 iterations, reward back to ~29-30 within ~10 iterations, `Loss/est_region` moving from 1.386 to ~1.27 within 30 iterations — no instability observed through the smoke test (60 iterations) or in the subsequent long run. Whether region accuracy actually keeps improving (vs. plateauing again, just at a better point) is not yet confirmed — that requires watching the full run.

---

## 2026-07-06 — `reset_ball_rolling`'s y_end sampling ignored one-sided region ranges: ~50% wrong left/right, "far" regions landed near 75% of the time

**What changed:** `reset_ball_rolling` (`mdp/events.py`) now branches on whether `y_end_range` is one-sided (`y_end_range[0] * y_end_range[1] > 0`, i.e. doesn't straddle zero) vs the original two-sided/symmetric case. For one-sided ranges (all 4 region-conditioned calls from `reset_ball_rolling_by_region`), the sign is now taken directly from `y_end_range` (never randomized), and the sampled magnitude is lerped within that range's own `[lo, hi]` bounds (`lo=min(|bounds|)`, `hi=max(|bounds|)`; `inner=lo`, `outer=lo+(hi-lo)*d` at difficulty `d`) instead of the generic two-sided dead-zone constants. The original two-sided branch (used only by the single-disc task's plain `reset_ball`, `y_end_range=(-0.9,0.9)`) is unchanged.

**Why it was wrong:** `reset_ball_rolling` was written once for the generic, symmetric, either-side target (`(-0.9,0.9)`) and reused verbatim by `reset_ball_rolling_by_region` for the 4 per-region *one-sided* ranges (`left_near=(0.15,0.5)`, `left_far=(0.5,0.9)`, `right_near=(-0.5,-0.15)`, `right_far=(-0.9,-0.5)`) without adapting the sign/magnitude logic. Two independent defects stacked: (1) `side = torch.where(rand()>0.5, 1, -1)` always re-randomizes the sign with a 50/50 coin flip regardless of `y_end_range`'s actual sign — a "left_near" env's ball landed on the *right* side of the goal line roughly half the time, and vice versa for every region; (2) the magnitude bounds (`inner`/`outer`) were derived from the fixed generic constants `_Y_INNER=0.15`/`_Y_OUTER=0.35` and `max_half`, never clamped to the region's own `lo` bound — so a "far" region (`0.5`–`0.9`) sampled magnitudes as low as `0.1`, deep inside "near" territory, at every difficulty level once `d` was past the initial ramp.

This was caught while auditing whether interceptV2DualDis's region/ball-estimator mechanism matched `Humanoid-Goalkeeper`'s (per this project's standing rule to verify every mechanism against the G1 reference before trusting it) — G1's own 6-region split (`legged_robot.py:916-960`, `g1_29_config.py` `ranges_0`..`ranges_5`) uses **disjoint, non-overlapping height/width bounds per region with no re-randomized sign**, so this class of bug can't occur there; the SGK/interceptV2 region-conditioned ball spawn is a new mechanism without a direct G1 analogue, and its sign/magnitude logic was never checked against the region ranges it was actually being fed.

Verified empirically (20k-sample Monte Carlo of the exact pre-fix formula, at `d=1.0`, matching the checkpoint under test at the time — `model_3000.pt`, ball_difficulty curriculum saturated): `left_near` sampled negative (wrong side) 49.4% of the time and landed inside its own intended `[0.15,0.5]` band only 44.4% of the time; `left_far` sampled negative 49.8% of the time and landed inside its own intended `[0.5,0.9]` band only **25.1%** of the time (i.e. 75% of "far"-labeled balls were secretly easier near-magnitude shots). `right_near`/`right_far` showed the mirror pattern. This directly explains two previously-unresolved symptoms: the region_estimator's persistent, unresolved left/right confusion in the live probe (34.9% accuracy at `model_3000`, weak-to-nonexistent left/right separation despite the region_estimator LR-collapse fix already applied and holding) — because the ground-truth label (`env._region_id`) was statistically decorrelated from the ball's actual observable side for ~50% of samples — and the user-reported failure of far-side shots to converge on the double/triple-step AMP reference motions, since 75% of nominally-"far" episodes never actually presented a far shot for the policy to practice on.

**Correct value:** one-sided ranges now sample sign-locked, magnitude-lerped-within-own-bounds `y_end`; see fix description above. Verified via direct Monte Carlo of the post-fix formula: all 4 regions land inside their own intended range 100% of the time, at every difficulty level from `d=0` (deterministic at the region's inner edge) to `d=1` (full region span).

**Evidence:** Reproduction and fix verification scripts run standalone (not committed as tests — see gap noted below). Existing `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_regions.py` did not catch this because it monkeypatches `reset_ball_rolling` entirely and only asserts on the `y_end_range` *parameter* passed to it, never exercising the actual sampling math inside — a real test-coverage gap; a follow-up should add a test that calls `reset_ball_rolling` (or an isolated helper) with a one-sided range and asserts the sign/bounds directly. Not yet confirmed against a full training run — this fix requires a fresh run (the in-flight `2026-07-06_15-13-34_region_fix_g1_match_2026-07-06` run's region_estimator and actor were trained against the corrupted ball-spawn distribution from iteration 0, so resuming it would carry the poisoned region conditioning forward; restarted fresh instead).

---

## 2026-07-07 — `reset_ball_rolling`'s `env._rsi_cross_y` used the wrong crossing-fraction formula (cos(angle) instead of the X-ratio)

**What changed:** the goal-line crossing-Y formula in `reset_ball_rolling` (`mdp/events.py`) changed from `y_start + (y_end - y_start) * (x_start + 0.3) / horiz_dist` to `y_start + (y_end - y_start) * (x_start / (x_start + 0.3))`.

**Why it was wrong:** the ball travels in a straight line from `(x_start, y_start)` to the aim point `(-0.3, y_end)` — 0.3 m *behind* the actual goal line. Parametrizing the line by `f ∈ [0,1]` (0 at spawn, 1 at the aim point), `X(f) = x_start - f*(x_start+0.3)`. The goal line is at `X=0`, not at the aim point, so the correct crossing fraction is `f = x_start / (x_start+0.3)`. The old formula instead computed `f = (x_start+0.3) / horiz_dist` where `horiz_dist = sqrt((x_start+0.3)² + (y_end-y_start)²)` — that's `cos(trajectory angle)`, a completely different quantity that happens to also land in `(0,1]` but has no geometric relationship to "how far along the path is the goal line." The two formulas disagree on every diagonal shot (e.g. `x_start=2.0, dy=0.5`: correct `f=0.870` vs buggy `f=0.977`), making the buggy version place `cross_y` closer to `y_end` than the true crossing point.

Found while investigating a user report that interceptV2DualDis's play viewer showed "near" balls incorrectly getting the blue/green two-stage split — traced the same bug independently to `SimpleGoalKeeper`'s `reset_ball_rolling` (see its `docs/BugFixes.md`, same date), which turned out to have the correct formula computed first and then a *second*, leftover duplicate block silently overwriting it with this exact wrong formula — almost certainly the source this project's single (buggy, never-corrected) copy was ported from. Proved mathematically that this bug is bounded and could not be the actual cause of the reported symptom: since `f ∈ (0,1]` for both formulas, `cross_y` can never leave the interval `[min(y_start,y_end), max(y_start,y_end)]` — a legitimately-narrow target (`|y_end| ≤ 0.5`, with `|y_start| ≤ 0.3`) can never produce a computed `cross_y` exceeding `0.5` regardless of which formula is used. Confirmed via two direct empirical tests (region-conditioned `play=True`/`play=False` configs, and a fresh 95-episode wide-flag consistency check on the sibling SGK project): `env._blue_wide`/the two-stage gate correctly separates near from far in every sampled episode, before and after this fix. The actual explanation for the reported symptom is a design fact, not a bug: "wide" is a purely *lateral* threshold (`|crossing_y - start_y| > 0.5`), independent of forward spawn distance — a physically close-spawning ball can legitimately have a large lateral target and correctly trigger the two-stage split.

**Correct value:** `env._rsi_cross_y[env_ids] = y_start + (y_end - y_start) * (x_start / (x_start + 0.3))`.

**Evidence:** 48/48 existing tests pass unchanged (no test asserted the exact numeric value of `_rsi_cross_y`, only the downstream `wide`/region classification, which this fix cannot affect — see bounded-impact argument above). This is a real accuracy fix for the footreach/foot_proximity/stopball/softstop target geometry on every diagonal shot, not a classification fix — did not warrant restarting the in-flight `blue_ball_gate_2026-07-06` run (its impact is bounded within the existing near/far envelope, unlike the section-12 sign/magnitude bug which flipped classifications outright).

---

## 2026-07-07 — region-far episodes only triggered the two-stage `wide` gate ~80-83% of the time, not 100%: crossing-point/aim-point mismatch

**What changed:** `_get_reach_target_y` (`mdp/rewards.py`) now ORs the threshold-based `wide` flag with the region's own far/near ground truth when `env._region_id` is set (region-conditioned task only): `wide = wide | _REGION_IS_FAR.to(env.device)[region_id]`, where `_REGION_IS_FAR` is a small module-level lookup built from `regions.REGION_NAMES` (`name.endswith("_far")`). No change for the plain single-disc task, which has no `_region_id` and falls through to the unchanged threshold check.

**Why it was wrong:** `_get_reach_target_y`'s `wide` flag compares `_rsi_cross_y` (the ball's Y position **at the goal line**, `x=0`) against the `0.5` m threshold. But `regions.py`'s `_REGION_Y_END_RANGE` (the region near/far boundary, also `0.5`) is defined on `y_end` — the ball's *aim point*, 0.3 m **behind** the goal line, per `reset_ball_rolling`'s own trajectory design. Per the crossing-fraction fix directly above, `_rsi_cross_y = y_start + (y_end-y_start) * f` with `f = x_start/(x_start+0.3) < 1` always — so the crossing point is systematically smaller in magnitude than `y_end` whenever `y_start`/`y_end` share sign (which every region-conditioned range does by construction). This means a region-far episode whose `y_end` sits near the region's own `0.5` inner edge computes a crossing point *below* `0.5` and gets misclassified as narrow — even though the ball was deliberately spawned as a "far" shot by the region system. Found while re-investigating a user report that the play viewer wasn't showing the blue/green split for a `right_far`-pinned single-env (`num_envs=1`) play session at `--difficulty 1.0`; direct empirical measurement (64 envs, forced max difficulty) confirmed `left_far`/`right_far` triggered `wide=True` only 80.4%/83.3% of the time (should be 100% by the region system's own design), while `left_near`/`right_near` correctly never did (0%) — the bug is one-directional, only under-classifying far as near, never the reverse.

This mismatch has been present in training too, not just play — `_get_reach_target_y` backs `footreach`/`foot_proximity`/`stopball`/`softstop`'s landing gate in the currently-running `blue_ball_gate_2026-07-06` run, so ~17-20% of that run's far-region episodes were silently exempt from the two-stage landing requirement the whole experiment is meant to test, collecting full `stopball`/`softstop`/`blue_ball_landed` reward via a direct reach with no genuine waypoint landing required.

**Correct value:** region ground truth (`env._region_id`) is authoritative over the threshold-derived value for the region-conditioned task; see fix above. Verified empirically: `left_far`/`right_far` now trigger `wide=True` 100.0%/100.0% (up from 80.4%/83.3%), `left_near`/`right_near` unchanged at 0.0%/0.0%.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Empirical before/after measurement (64 envs, forced `difficulty=1.0`, 60 steps): before — `left_near` 0/48, `left_far` 37/46 (80.4%), `right_near` 0/48, `right_far` 40/48 (83.3%); after — `left_near` 0/48, `left_far` 43/43 (100%), `right_near` 0/48, `right_far` 48/48 (100%). **Training restarted** (not resumed — this changes which episodes require the landing gate, a large enough behavior change for the exact mechanism this run exists to test that continuing to train under the old, partially-leaky gate would contaminate the results) per the user's standing instruction to restart on finding something that needs fixing; see HANDOFF.md for the new run's launch details.

---

## 2026-07-07 — `blue_ball_landed` fired from a foot sweeping past the blue midpoint mid-stride, not landing there: `landing_radius` tightened 0.05 -> 0.02

**What changed:** `_get_reach_target_y`'s `landing_radius` default (`mdp/rewards.py`) tightened from `0.05` to `0.02`.

**Why it was wrong:** user reported watching `sgk_play` and seeing the assigned foot's actual ground contact land near the GREEN target while `blue_ball_landed` had already fired — the policy was still doing one continuous big step/leap, not a genuine paced double-step, despite the landing gate supposedly requiring a stop at the blue midpoint first. Blue and green share the same X (the goal line) and are always `>= wide_threshold/2` apart in Y for a wide crossing, so a foot planting AT green cannot itself be within `0.05` m of blue — ruled that out directly. The actual mechanism: blue sits directly on the straight-line path from the robot's stance to green (same X, Y strictly between the two), so a single continuous stride/leap toward green necessarily sweeps *through* blue's Y-coordinate at some point mid-flight. If the `feet_contact` sensor registers a momentary/glancing contact during that pass-through (sensor noise, a low ground-clearance shuffle, foot-scuff during swing phase) while the foot's horizontal position happens to be within `landing_radius` of blue, `newly_landed` latches — even though the policy never intended to stop there and continues on to plant at green. This is the same category of exploit SGK found and fixed for the identical reason on 2026-07-05 (`landing_radius` `0.3` -> `0.05`, "a single ordinary stride toward the true (green) target passes close enough to the blue midpoint, mid-stride, to satisfy the old landed check without the robot ever stopping there") — this project inherited SGK's already-tightened `0.05` at port time, but that apparently still wasn't tight enough here.

**Correct value:** `landing_radius=0.02`. Shrinking the radius doesn't eliminate the underlying exploit in principle (a sweep could in theory still graze an arbitrarily small radius by chance), but makes accidental pass-through contact far less likely, requiring something much closer to a genuine, deliberate plant at the exact midpoint coordinate.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass (no test asserted the exact numeric threshold, only qualitative landed/not-landed behavior at a coarser radius, unaffected by the tightening). Not yet independently validated against a training run — behavioral confirmation requires watching a checkpoint from a run trained under this tighter radius. **Known limitation, not fully resolved:** this is a magnitude reduction, not a structural fix — a sufficiently athletic/fast single stride could still theoretically satisfy an arbitrarily small radius by chance. If the one-big-step behavior persists even at `0.02`, the next step should be a velocity-based check (e.g. require the assigned foot's horizontal speed to be near zero at the moment of contact) to distinguish a genuine plant from a foot merely passing through at speed — flagged for a follow-up if this radius alone doesn't resolve it. Training restarted under this fix; see HANDOFF.md.

---

## 2026-07-07 — `sgk_play`'s viewer never applied the region-ground-truth `wide` override, rendering a phantom green marker for episodes the reward side correctly treated as wide

**What changed:** `_patch_viewer_intercept_vis` (`scripts/play.py`) now ORs its local `wide` flag with the region's own far/near label (`region_names[region_id].endswith("_far")`) when `env._region_id` is set, mirroring the same override added to `mdp.rewards._get_reach_target_y` earlier the same day (see the "region-far episodes only triggered the two-stage `wide` gate" entry above).

**Why it was wrong:** after the reward-side region override shipped, the viewer's own separate `wide = abs(rel) > 0.5` computation was never updated to match — it still relied purely on the raw threshold check the region override exists specifically to correct. For a region-far episode whose actual crossing magnitude sits below `0.5` (the exact population the reward-side fix targets), the reward side correctly computes `wide=True` (the robot is required to, and does, walk to and land at the blue midpoint before the target switches to green) — but the viewer computed `wide=False` for that same episode and rendered only a GREEN sphere at the *full* crossing point for the entire episode, never showing blue at all. User directly observed this while watching `sgk_play`: `blue_ball_landed` had fired even though the visible foot never got close to the (only) marker shown — because the robot's foot was correctly walking to and landing at the real (blue) target the whole time, which the viewer simply never rendered. Confirmed via a live diagnostic driving the actual trained policy (`model_6500.pt`) and logging the exact foot position at the moment of each `blue_ball_landed` firing: `dist_to_blue < landing_radius` held in every sampled case (127/127) — the position check itself was never broken; only the viewer's rendering of which marker corresponds to the actual target was.

**Rejected alternative — RSI-assisted free credit:** initially hypothesized (and disproven) that the observed behavior was explained by `env._blue_airborne_at_reset`-flagged (RSI-seeded) free landings rather than a rendering bug. User directly rejected this ("this is not a rsi assisted problem") and was correct — the diagnostic's 100% `airborne_at_reset=True` rate turned out to be a measurement artifact of that flag's narrow 2-step detection window combined with this project's 80% "continue_keep" RSI branch (donor poses are mid-motion by construction, so *some* foot is almost always airborne within the first 2 steps of *any* episode, regardless of whether the actual, much-later landing was genuinely earned) — not evidence that the specific landings sampled were free. The actual, confirmed bug was the viewer/reward `wide`-flag mismatch above.

**Correct value:** viewer's `wide` computation now matches the reward side exactly for the region-conditioned task; unaffected for the plain single-disc task (no `env._region_id`).

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass (play.py's viewer patch has no direct test coverage — verified via code inspection matching the already-tested reward-side override, and via the diagnostic script's confirmation that the position check itself was sound in every sampled case). Play-only rendering fix — does not affect training, no restart needed.

---

## 2026-07-07 — single-env play sessions could never show a near-region episode: `assign_static_regions` degenerates at `num_envs=1`; added play-mode per-episode region randomization

**What changed:** `mdp/regions.py` gains `randomize_region_on_reset` — re-samples `env._region_id` uniformly at random (0-3) on every reset, instead of the permanent per-env split `assign_static_regions` computes once at startup. `goalkeeper_multidisc_env_cfg` (`tasks/goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`) now wires `randomize_region_on_reset` (`mode="reset"`) for `play=True` and keeps `assign_static_regions` (`mode="startup"`) unchanged for training. Also fixed, in the same change: `cfg.events["reset_ball"]`/`"reset_from_motion_data"`/`"tick_catchstep"` are now explicitly popped and re-inserted after `"assign_static_regions"` is added, to force the correct execution order for `mode="reset"` events (see "Why it was wrong" below).

**Why it was wrong:** even after the region-ground-truth `wide` fixes above, the user reported that visually-close-looking shots were still getting the blue/green split in a single-env `sgk_play` session, and asked why "close balls" get split when they shouldn't. Root cause: `assign_static_regions` splits `num_envs` into 4 contiguous blocks (`quarter = n // 4`); at `num_envs=1` (the play default), this gives `counts=[0,0,0,1]` — the single env is **permanently pinned to region index 3 (`right_far`) for the entire session**, every single episode. What looked like "close balls incorrectly getting split" was actually `right_far` episodes whose *sampled magnitude* happened to be small (e.g. 0.55-0.65m, just past the region's own 0.5m inner edge) — correctly still requiring the blue gate by the region's own label, just not looking dramatically "wide" by eye. A single-env session could never show a genuine near-region episode to compare against, since the region assignment is a permanent one-time split, never resampled per episode.

**Real bug found while implementing the fix:** the first version of this change put `randomize_region_on_reset` in `cfg.events["assign_static_regions"]` (a *new* dict key, appended at the end of `cfg.events`) but left the pre-existing `cfg.events["reset_ball"]` reassignment as a plain in-place update. Since `"reset_ball"` already existed in `cfg.events` (inserted earlier, in the base `goalkeeper_env_cfg()` call), a plain reassignment does *not* move its position in the dict — mjlab's `EventManager` executes `mode="reset"` terms in dict/registration order, so `reset_ball_rolling_by_region` (needs `env._region_id`) was still running *before* `randomize_region_on_reset` ever set it, raising `AttributeError: 'ManagerBasedRlEnv' object has no attribute '_region_id'` on the very first reset. Fixed by popping `"reset_ball"`/`"reset_from_motion_data"`/`"tick_catchstep"` before adding `"assign_static_regions"`, then re-inserting them afterward in their original relative order (reset_ball must also stay before reset_from_motion_data — an existing, separate convention from `goalkeeper_env_cfg`, its tier-routing depends on reset_ball's freshly-computed trajectory).

**Correct value:** training keeps `assign_static_regions`/`mode="startup"` unchanged (the region_estimator is trained assuming a stable, balanced ground-truth distribution across the parallel batch — this permanent per-env split, matching G1's `end_regions` mechanism, is intentional and untouched). Play uses `randomize_region_on_reset`/`mode="reset"` instead.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Empirical verification (`num_envs=1`, 2500 steps, 61 episodes observed): region sequence varied freely across all 4 regions episode-to-episode (`left_far` 20, `left_near` 15, `right_near` 13, `right_far` 13) — confirms a single agent now shows a representative mix over time instead of being stuck in one region. Play-only change — training's event dict ends up in the same effective order as before (the pop/re-insert is a no-op for the `mode="startup"` branch's actual runtime behavior), no restart needed for this specific change.

---

## 2026-07-07 — `stopball`/`softstop`'s wide-crossing landing gate accepted RSI-assisted "free" landings, making the two-stage requirement toothless in practice

**What changed:** `stopball` and `softstop` (`mdp/rewards.py`) now compute `landing_ok = ~env._blue_wide | (env._blue_landed & ~env._blue_airborne_at_reset)`, instead of `~env._blue_wide | env._blue_landed`. The gate now requires a *genuine* blue-midpoint landing (same definition as `metrics.blue_landed_genuine`) to unlock the save rewards on wide crossings, not merely any `_blue_landed`.

**Why it was wrong:** the landing gate added 2026-07-07 (`cde4737`) was designed so that on wide crossings, `stopball`/`softstop` (the primary save-reward signal) could only fire after the assigned foot genuinely landed at the blue midpoint — forcing the policy to learn the two-stage step instead of doing one continuous reach straight to green. In practice this gate was almost never binding: a live diagnostic driving the actual trained policy (`model_4500.pt`, current run `blue_ball_fixes_2026-07-07`, 256 envs × 4000 steps, 3210 completed episodes) measured that of 1637 wide episodes, 30.8% satisfied `env._blue_landed` at some point — but only 1.4% (23/1637) were genuine (policy-caused); the other 29.4% (481/1637) were RSI-assisted "free" landings, where the reset donor pose already had the assigned foot airborne near the blue midpoint within the first 2 steps of the episode (`env._blue_airborne_at_reset`), satisfying the gate with zero policy effort. Since the old gate accepted either kind equally, the policy could unlock full save reward on nearly a third of wide episodes without ever learning to deliberately land at blue — the two-stage shaping this run exists to test was live in name only for the large majority of its intended effect.

**Correct value:** `landing_ok` now excludes RSI-assisted landings, reusing the exact same genuine/RSI-assisted distinction `metrics.py` already computes for diagnostics (`blue_landed_genuine` vs `blue_landed_rsi_assisted`) — the two now agree by construction. This is a deliberately aggressive tightening: at the previously-measured 1.4% genuine rate, `stopball`/`softstop` will initially almost never fire on wide crossings, which is the intended forcing function (per explicit user direction: "u cant get stopball and softstop, if u didnt land first on blue ball marker point"), not a bug — the risk is reward sparsity stalling learning on wide shots specifically, to be monitored via the wide-episode genuine-landing rate on future checkpoints (rerun the diagnostic script, not yet promoted into a permanent metric).

**Known caveat, carried over from the 2026-07-07 viewer-fix entry above:** `env._blue_airborne_at_reset` is a per-episode latch that can be true even when the *specific* later landing event wasn't actually free — this project's 80% `continue_keep` RSI branch means donor poses are mid-motion by construction, so some foot is almost always airborne within an episode's first 2 steps regardless of whether the eventual blue landing was earned. This means the tightened gate may over-reject some genuine mid-episode landings whose episode merely happened to start with an early airborne transition unrelated to the actual landing. Accepted as the best available proxy — it's the same definition already used for the `blue_landed_genuine`/`blue_landed_rsi_assisted` metrics, so gate and diagnostics stay consistent; revisit if training shows the gate essentially never opens even as the policy visibly improves.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass (no test asserted the old permissive gate value). Not yet validated against a training run under the new gate — this is exactly what the fresh restart (see HANDOFF.md) is for. Latest checkpoint of the pre-fix run (`model_4500.pt`, `blue_ball_fixes_2026-07-07`) pushed to git before stopping that run, per the diagnostic numbers above. Training restarted (not resumed) — this changes which episodes can earn any save reward at all on wide crossings, too large a reward-structure change to continue training under the old gate.

---

## 2026-07-07 — blue-midpoint landing check was position-only, so nothing distinguished a genuine plant from a fast sweep-through; also nothing disincentivized sweeping: `landing_speed_threshold` added, `landing_radius` loosened 0.02 -> 0.08

**What changed:** `_get_reach_target_y` (`mdp/rewards.py`) now also requires the assigned foot's horizontal speed (`torch.norm(assigned_foot_vel[:, :2], dim=-1)`, from `robot.data.body_link_lin_vel_w`) to be below a new `landing_speed_threshold=0.5` m/s at the moment of contact, in addition to the existing position check. `landing_radius` loosened back from `0.02` to `0.08` now that the speed gate — not radius — is responsible for ruling out grazes.

**Why it was wrong:** after the previous entry's fix made the `stopball`/`softstop` gate airtight (no more RSI-assisted free credit), the user reported the play script still showing the robot sweeping straight past blue to the intercept point with a fresh policy, and asked directly why it still wasn't learning the double-step — correctly noting `footreach`/`stopball`/`softstop` were now all genuinely zero for this behavior (confirming the gate itself was no longer leaking reward). The remaining problem was structural, not a leak: `_get_reach_target_y`'s landing check was pure position (`dist_to_blue < landing_radius`) with no requirement to actually stop, while `footreach`'s own `vel_sigma` term (up to 10x, see `footreach()`) rewards *speed* toward whichever point is currently the reach target — including blue, during phase 1. The two combine into a reward structure that actively favors blasting through blue at speed over decelerating and planting there, and at `landing_radius=0.02` (2 cm, a single 0.02 s physics step) a *deliberate* plant was nearly as hard to hit exactly as an accidental graze — shrinking the radius in the prior fix suppressed the accidental-graze symptom without addressing the underlying incentive to never stop.

**Correct value:** `landing_speed_threshold=0.5` m/s, `landing_radius=0.08` m. Precision now comes from requiring the foot to be moving slowly (ruling out sweep-throughs regardless of radius), not from requiring pixel-perfect position (which made genuine plants nearly unreachable). Cascades automatically to `stopball`/`softstop`'s `landing_ok` and `footreach`/`foot_proximity`'s `ball_close` switch, since all four read `env._blue_landed`/`env._blue_wide` set by this same function — no separate changes needed in those reward functions.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Live smoke test (4 envs, zero-action policy, 30 steps) confirmed the new velocity computation runs without shape/attribute errors (`body_link_lin_vel_w` indexing mirrors the existing pattern already exercised by `feet_slippage`). Not yet validated for actual learning outcome — that requires watching a checkpoint trained under this fix; flagged as the next thing to check once iterations accumulate. Training restarted (not resumed) — this is a large change to the achievability of the core landing event this run exists to test.

---

## 2026-07-08 — added `blue_overshoot_penalty`: no cost to ignoring the blue waypoint entirely meant PPO had no gradient pushing it away from that local optimum

**What changed:** new reward term `blue_overshoot_penalty` (`mdp/rewards.py`), weight `-30.0` (`tasks/goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`). Penalizes the assigned foot for advancing past the blue midpoint (toward green) on a wide crossing before genuinely landing there (`env._blue_wide & ~env._blue_landed`), scaled by how far past (`clamp(signed_progress - landing_radius, 0, 0.5)`, deadband matches the existing `landing_radius=0.08` arrival radius). Zero on narrow crossings and once genuinely landed.

**Why it was wrong (or rather: incomplete) before:** the 2026-07-07 landing-rate diagnostic (see the two entries above) established that even under the tightened, airtight gate, the policy still wasn't discovering the genuine two-stage plant — it was sweeping straight past blue with zero reward for doing so. On investigation (prompted by the user asking for a full audit and floating "a negative reward for going past blue without landing" as one option): a wide episode where the policy ignores the blue waypoint entirely earns exactly the same reward (zero, from `stopball`/`softstop`/`blue_ball_landed`, all landing-gated) as one where it genuinely tries and fails. With no cost differential between "didn't try" and "tried and missed," PPO has no gradient distinguishing them — and since narrow crossings (~half the region-sampled distribution) already pay out fully with minimal movement (a near-default stance blocks many center-ish shots), "specialize in narrow, ignore wide entirely" is a stable local optimum with no force pulling the policy out of it. This is a distinct root cause from the two entries above (which fixed how *achievable* a genuine landing is) — this fixes the complete *absence of exploration pressure* toward attempting one at all.

**Why `footreach`'s `vel_sigma` was NOT touched (user caught an overcorrection in the original plan):** the initial idea (this session's prior message) proposed neutralizing `vel_sigma` during the blue-approach phase, reasoning that it rewards speed toward the target and so works against stopping. Rereading `footreach()` (`rewards.py`) shows this diagnosis was imprecise: `vel_sigma`'s direction is computed from `lateral_error = reach_target_y - robot_y_w`, and `reach_target_y` (from `_get_reach_target_y`) already flips from blue to green exactly on `env._blue_landed` — so `vel_sigma` already only rewards velocity toward blue pre-landing, never toward green early. The actual gap wasn't *what* it targets, it's that nothing penalizes carrying that speed past the target without stopping. Left `vel_sigma` untouched; `blue_overshoot_penalty` supplies the missing piece instead. The combination (fast approach still rewarded + overshoot penalized) should shape an accelerate-then-decelerate-to-a-plant profile matching what's actually in the `LeftDoubleStep`/`RightDoubleStep` AMP demo clips (see the "do i need a better dataset" investigation, same day: the demo's own foot trajectory shows exactly this shape, decelerating to near-zero velocity at a dwell point almost exactly at the geometric midpoint of its total displacement) — rather than needing a separate hand-authored deceleration reward.

**Weight `-30.0`:** not empirically tuned, chosen to be comparable in magnitude to the largest per-step positive rewards (`footreach` max ~10, `foot_proximity` max ~5) so it's a meaningful counter-pressure without being an extreme hard-constraint-scale penalty (cf. `penalize_kneeheight`/`penalize_sharpcontact` at -100). Flagged as the first thing to revisit if training shows either no effect (too weak) or destabilization (too strong).

**Known interaction, not yet resolved:** the velocity-gated landing check added in the entry above still checks foot speed at the instant of the contact-transition, which the same-day diagnostic showed rejects genuine plants (0% genuine, up from 1.4% pre-velocity-gate) because a real footstrike still carries residual velocity at first contact. This penalty doesn't fix that timing bug — it only adds pressure to *attempt* stopping at blue, which should produce more attempts at genuine plants, but whether those attempts can actually register as `_blue_landed` still depends on the separately-flagged, unfixed velocity-check-timing issue. Left as-is per user judgment ("i dont think this is really a big problem") when this was surfaced; worth revisiting together if this fix alone doesn't move the genuine-landing rate.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Live smoke test (8 envs, zero-action policy, 60 steps) confirmed the term registers and runs without error. Not yet validated for actual learning outcome. Training restarted (not resumed) — new reward term changes the return landscape enough that continuing under the old one would conflate results.

---

## 2026-07-08 — AMP setup brought to literal parity with G1: gradient penalty, discriminator width, and replay buffer

**What changed (`src/simple_goalkeeper/rsl_rl_multi/multi_disc_amp_ppo.py`, `tasks/goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`, `beyondAMP/source/rsl_rl_amp/rsl_rl_amp/storage/replay_buffer.py`):**

1. **Gradient penalty:** `discr.compute_grad_pen(..., lambda_=10)` (no further scaling) → `discr.compute_grad_pen(..., lambda_=5) * 0.1`. Effective coefficient 10 → 0.5.
2. **AMP discriminator loss weighting:** `amp_loss = amp_loss + 0.5*expert_loss + 0.5*policy_loss` → `amp_loss = amp_loss + expert_loss + policy_loss` (full sum, not average).
3. **Discriminator trunk width:** `amp_discr_hidden_dims: [256, 256]` → `[512, 256]`.
4. **AMP "policy" sample source:** `amp_storages` (per-region `ReplayBuffer`, previously a persistent 250k-transition FIFO queue sampled across many updates) now cleared (`rb.clear()`, new method added to `ReplayBuffer`) at the end of every `update()`, alongside the existing `self.storage.clear()`.

**Why:** user explicitly requested literal parity with G1's AMP mechanism ("i want the same amp setup as g1 not the same outcome... same code... no unnecessary divergence"), not just equivalent functionality. Dispatched a research agent to do a line-by-line comparison against `Humanoid-Goalkeeper/rsl_rl/rsl_rl/algorithms/him_ppo.py` and `rsl_rl/rsl_rl/modules/amp.py`. Findings, each independently confirmed by direct code reading:

1. G1's `compute_grad_pen` (`amp.py:124-152`) defaults to `lambda_=5`; `compute_loss` (`amp.py:169`) applies an additional `* 0.1` on top: `grad_pen = self.compute_grad_pen(expert_obs, agent_obs) * 0.1`. Net effective coefficient `0.5`. This port used `lambda_=10` with no further scaling — effective `10`, ~20x stronger discriminator regularization than G1, which over-flattens the discriminator's logit landscape (a discriminator regularized this hard toward a near-constant output is a blunter, less spatially-informative reward signal).
2. G1's `gail_loss = expert_loss + policy_loss` (`amp.py:168`, unweighted sum). This port averaged instead (`0.5 * expert_loss + 0.5 * policy_loss`), halving `amp_loss`'s relative magnitude against the rest of the total loss for no documented reason.
3. G1 never overrides `AMP.__init__`'s `hidden_dims` kwarg (`him_on_policy_runner.py:85` constructs `AMP(num_obs, amp_coef, device=...)` with no `hidden_dims` arg), so it uses the class default `[512, 256]` (`amp.py:87`). This port used `[256, 256]` — half the trunk capacity, an undocumented divergence, not a deliberate one.
4. G1's discriminator has **no persistent replay buffer at all**. Its "policy" sample for the discriminator loss (`amp_obs_batch` in `him_ppo.py:181-182`) is unpacked directly from the same `HIMRolloutStorage` minibatch generator as every other PPO quantity — i.e. strictly the current on-policy rollout, and `self.storage.clear()` wipes it every `update()` (`him_ppo.py:324`). This port's `amp_storages` were a 250,000-transition FIFO `ReplayBuffer` per region, populated every env step and never cleared, so the discriminator was trained against a stale mix of past-policy behavior spanning many prior updates instead of strictly what the current policy is doing. Since `ReplayBuffer` had no `clear()` method at all, one was added.

**What was checked and found to NOT be a divergence** (so left unchanged): the reward blend formula itself. G1: `rewards = amp_reward*amp_coef + raw_rewards*(1-amp_coef)`, `amp_coef=0.4`, `amp_reward = predict_reward(...)*0.5` (`him_on_policy_runner.py:175-185`, `amp.py:185-206`). This port: `reward = (1-lerp)*amp_reward + lerp*task_reward`, `lerp=0.6`, `amp_reward = amp_reward_coef * clamp(1-0.25*(d-1)^2, 0)`, `amp_reward_coef=0.5` (`amp_discriminator.py:64-72`). These are the same formula under different parameter names (`lerp=0.6` on task ≡ `amp_coef=0.4` on AMP — complementary), confirmed mathematically equivalent, not a rewrite. Also checked: `amp_min_normalized_std` (despite the misleading name, this is the actor's action-noise std floor, not an AMP/discriminator parameter at all — `him_amp_on_policy_runner.py`/`multi_disc_amp_ppo.py`) has no G1 equivalent because it isn't part of G1's AMP mechanism; left unchanged as an intentional, unrelated, already-existing addition. And: neither discriminator's observation space includes anything beyond `joint_pos`/`joint_vel` — both are strictly joint-space, so "AMP reward is blind to world/ball position" is true in both implementations, not a port-specific gap.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Live end-to-end smoke test — 3 full training iterations (rollout collection + `update()`, 64 envs) via the actual runner — completed without error; confirmed `amp_storages[name].num_samples == 0` and `.step == 0` for all 4 regions immediately after the final `update()`, verifying the clear-every-update behavior actually takes effect at runtime, not just in isolation. Not yet validated for training outcome (whether a less-over-regularized, wider, strictly-on-policy discriminator changes AMP reward quality enough to matter for the still-open two-stage-stepping problem) — that requires watching this run's checkpoints.

---

## 2026-07-08 — blue-midpoint landing check was an instantaneous velocity check at the wrong moment: settle-window added

**What changed:** `_get_reach_target_y` (`mdp/rewards.py`) replaces the instantaneous `foot_in_contact & (dist_to_blue < landing_radius) & (foot_speed < landing_speed_threshold)` check with a settle window: a per-env counter (`env._blue_settle_count`) increments every step the foot stays in contact AND within `landing_radius`, resets to 0 the moment either condition breaks; once the counter reaches `_BLUE_SETTLE_STEPS=3` consecutive steps, `foot_speed < landing_speed_threshold` is checked *at that point* (not at the first instant of contact) to confirm the landing.

**Why it was wrong:** the 2026-07-07 velocity-gate entry (two entries above) checked speed on the exact same step as first ground contact — but a real footstrike still carries residual swing velocity at the instant of impact; it only decays toward zero over the *following* few steps as weight transfers and friction/contact damping take hold, not instantaneously. The same-day live diagnostic (`scratchpad/blue_landing_rate.py` against `model_2000.pt`) measured this drove genuine landings to exactly 0% (down from 1.4% pre-velocity-gate) while RSI-assisted "free" landings rose to 55.8% — the check was rejecting every real plant attempt while a stationary RSI donor pose (already at rest before the episode even starts) trivially passed both conditions simultaneously from step one. Flagged as a known, unfixed issue in that entry and the `blue_overshoot_penalty` entry above; fixing it now alongside the AMP parity work since both are needed before the overshoot penalty's effect (more genuine attempts) can actually be measured (attempts need a landing check that can register them).

**Why 3 steps:** short enough (~0.06s at dt=0.02) to not meaningfully loosen the position/contact requirement (a fast sweep-through still can't hold both contact and the 8cm radius for 3 consecutive steps — that requires the foot's velocity to already be low, which is what we want to detect), long enough to give a genuine footstrike's residual impact velocity time to decay before the speed check fires. Not empirically tuned against real footstrike dynamics; flagged for revisit if landings are still failing to register with the settle window in place.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Live end-to-end smoke test (3 full training iterations, 64 envs) via the actual runner completed without error — `env._blue_settle_count` participates correctly in the reset/latch bookkeeping alongside the other per-episode blue-gate state. Not yet validated for actual learning outcome (whether genuine landings now register above 0%) — requires a live diagnostic against a checkpoint from the restarted run. Training restarted (not resumed) — this is a large change to whether the core landing event this run's reward structure depends on is achievable at all.

---

## 2026-07-08 — AMP observation content: `joint_vel` included alongside `joint_pos`, a real divergence from G1 the first parity pass missed

**What changed:** `goalkeeper_multidisc_env_cfg` (`tasks/goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`) now overrides `cfg.observations["amp"]` to a single `joint_pos` term (dropping `joint_vel`), instead of inheriting the base `goalkeeper_env_cfg()`'s shared `"amp"` group (`joint_pos` + `joint_vel`). `MotionDatasetCfg`'s `amp_obs_terms` for this task's 4 region datasets switched from the shared `AMPObsBaiscTerms` constant (`["joint_pos", "joint_vel"]`) to a new task-local `_MULTIDISC_AMP_OBS_TERMS = ["joint_pos"]`, keeping the expert (demo) and policy (live) discriminator inputs in sync. The shared `AMPObsBaiscTerms` constant itself, and the base `goalkeeper_env_cfg()`'s `"amp"` group it feeds, are untouched — SimpleGoalKeeper's single-disc AMP track and several other `beyondAMP` example tasks still use the unmodified 2-term version.

**Why it was wrong:** the AMP-parity work earlier this same day (two entries above) included checking whether the discriminator's observation *content* diverged from G1's — the first research pass (an Explore agent) reported this as "confirmed: no divergence... both discriminators are strictly joint-space, neither can see world-frame position or ball-relative state." That framing was true but incomplete, and got carried forward into this project's own docs uncorrected. Per the user's explicit request for an independent, skeptical re-verification (worried about exactly this kind of AI-generated imprecision), a second, unbiased agent re-derived every claim from scratch and caught it: G1's `get_amp_observations()` (`Humanoid-Goalkeeper/legged_gym/legged_gym/envs/base/legged_robot.py:157-161`) returns `self.dof_pos.clone()` **only** — no joint velocities at all, `num_obs=29*2` (`g1_29_config.py:363`) is two consecutive frames of *positions*, not a position+velocity pair. This port's AMP obs group (`AMPObsBaiscTerms = ["joint_pos", "joint_vel"]`) is a genuine superset of G1's — velocity information G1's discriminator never sees. "Neither has world-frame/ball data" (true) is a different, narrower claim than "AMP obs content matches G1" (false) — the first pass conflated the two.

**Correct value:** this task's `"amp"` obs group and its `MotionDatasetCfg.amp_obs_terms` both now use `joint_pos` only, matching G1's dof_pos-only structure exactly (structurally, not numerically — T1 is 21 DOF vs G1's 29, same mechanism).

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Live end-to-end smoke test (3 full training iterations, 64 envs): confirmed `env.observation_manager.group_obs_dim["amp"] == (21,)` (was `(42,)`), each region discriminator's `input_dim == 42` (21 × 2 concatenated frames, consistent end-to-end — expert and policy sides match), and training completes without error. Training restarted (not resumed) alongside the other same-day AMP-parity fixes — the discriminator's input dimensionality itself changed, so any in-flight checkpoint's discriminator weights are incompatible regardless.

**Process note:** this is a concrete instance of the exact failure mode the user asked to guard against — a single research pass produced a technically-true-but-incomplete claim that read as a clean "no divergence" finding and was reported as such. A second, independently-dispatched agent given the same question from a skeptical, verify-don't-trust framing caught the gap. Worth continuing to default to an independent re-check for any claim of this shape ("X matches G1 exactly") before treating it as settled, rather than accepting the first pass's framing.
