## 2026-07-08 — scheduled 5000-iteration escalation: genuine landings still 0.0% after the settle-window + AMP-parity fixes; added dense `blue_stick_landing` shaping

**Context:** per the standing monitoring policy (now in `CLAUDE.md`), the bihourly health check on `amp_g1_parity_2026-07-08b` ran the escalation protocol at iteration 5548. Two prior routine checks (iteration 2000 and 3750) had already each measured **0.0% genuine** blue landings over 2600+ wide episodes — a consistent, not noisy, result. `blue_overshoot_penalty`'s per-episode value stayed flat and strongly negative across the entire run history (~-0.64 to -0.86 sampled at iterations 1111/2222/3334/4445/5556) instead of shrinking toward zero, meaning the policy was not learning to avoid the penalty it was incurring.

**AMP ruled out as the cause:** the escalation protocol's first branch is to re-audit AMP if the discriminator signal looks suspect. `Train/mean_discri_logits` showed values around -115 to -120, which looked alarming in isolation (a healthy discriminator logit for ±1 training targets should be roughly single-digit). Checked the actual logging code (`him_amp_on_policy_runner.py:238-239`) before concluding anything: `cur_discri_sum += d_logits` accumulates the raw logit **every step for the whole episode**, only flushed to the logged buffer at episode end — this is a per-episode sum, not a per-step or per-sample value. Dividing by a typical episode length gives a per-step logit around -0.5 to -0.8, unremarkable and consistent with ordinary (not collapsed) discriminator behavior. `Loss/AMP`/`Loss/AMP_grad` stayed small and stable the whole run; `mean_action_acc` stayed flat around 1.15 (no divergence). Conclusion: AMP is not the bottleneck this time — the blue-ball reward mechanism itself is.

**Diagnosis:** the settle-window landing check (previous entry) requires the assigned foot to be simultaneously in contact, within `landing_radius`, and below `landing_speed_threshold`, sustained for 3 consecutive steps. Nothing in the reward stack gives *dense* credit for approaching that joint condition — `footreach`/`foot_proximity` reward proximity to blue, `vel_sigma` rewards speed toward blue, but nothing rewards decelerating once close. The only payoff for actually achieving "close and slow" was the sparse, conjunctive settle-window event itself. A policy has to stumble into that exact combination before any gradient reinforces it — and evidently hadn't in 5500+ iterations.

**Fix applied:** new dense reward term `blue_stick_landing` (`mdp/rewards.py`, weight `8.0`, `tasks/goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) — `exp(-15·dist_to_blue) · exp(-3·foot_speed)` for the assigned foot on a wide, unlanded crossing, peaking exactly at "close AND stopped." Mirrors this project's own existing `cleanstop` pattern (rewards low ball speed near a target after a save) — same "reward stillness near a point" shape, applied to the foot approaching blue instead of the ball after a save. Gives ordinary gradient ascent an incremental path to the target condition (get closer → more reward; get slower while close → more reward) instead of requiring the exact conjunction to be found blind.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Live end-to-end smoke test (3 full training iterations, 64 envs) confirmed the term registers (weight 8.0 visible in the active reward table) and runs without error. Not yet validated for actual learning outcome — this is exactly what the restart is for. `dist_sigma=15`/`speed_sigma=3` are not tuned against real footstrike dynamics; flagged for revisit if this doesn't move the genuine-landing rate at the next scheduled check.

**Training restarted** (not resumed) per the standing monitoring policy — pushed `model_5500.pt` (last checkpoint of the stuck run) before killing it.

---

## 2026-07-08 — scheduled 6500-iteration escalation: AMP discriminator init/reward-smoothing divergences found and fixed; landing_speed_threshold loosened; genuine landings still 0.0% across the whole run

**Context:** user raised the escalation threshold from 5000 to 6500 (curriculum `cu` hadn't reached 3 yet at 5000; `cu = int(mean_episode_length / 47)`, needs `mean_episode_length >= 141`). Also explicitly requested a mandatory re-comparison of the AMP architecture against G1 at this checkpoint: "amp reward seems to be really low." The bihourly cron ran both parts at iteration 8102.

**Part A — AMP magnitude re-audit (independent subagent, verify-from-scratch):** found and confirmed two real divergences, both now fixed in `beyondAMP/source/rsl_rl_amp/rsl_rl_amp/modules/amp_discriminator.py`:

1. **Missing explicit weight init.** G1's `AMP.__init__` (`amp.py:100-118`) leaves the first trunk layer at PyTorch default init but explicitly initializes every subsequent hidden layer *and* the final `amp_linear` output layer with `uniform_(-1, 1)` weights and zero bias (`DISC_LOGIT_INIT_SCALE = 1.0`). This port's `AMPDiscriminator.__init__` had no explicit init calls anywhere — all layers, including `amp_linear` (the layer that directly produces the discriminator logit `d`), used PyTorch's default `kaiming_uniform_`. For a `Linear(256, 1)` layer, PyTorch's default weight bound is `1/sqrt(256) ≈ 0.0625` — **~16x smaller** than G1's `uniform(-1,1)`. Fixed: added the same conditional init pattern G1 uses (first trunk layer default, every subsequent trunk layer + `amp_linear` explicit `uniform_(-1,1)`/`zeros_`).
2. **Missing reward-noise-smoothing.** G1's actual reward-time method, `predict_reward` (`amp.py:185-206`), perturbs the (normalized) input with `num_samples=20` Gaussian noise draws (`sigma=0.3`) and takes the **minimum** squared-error-from-1 over those samples before computing `clamp(1-0.25*err, 0)` — not a raw single-sample read. Min-over-samples systematically raises the computed reward relative to a raw single-point evaluation for the same underlying discriminator (it's effectively "best nearby match," a noise-robustness/smoothing technique). The port's `predict_amp_reward` used the raw single-sample `d` with no smoothing at all. Fixed: ported the identical noise-perturbation-then-min mechanism into `predict_amp_reward`; `d`/`d_logits` (used only for `Train/mean_discri_logits` logging) stays the raw unperturbed sample for interpretability, unaffected by this change.

Both were independently re-verified (not just trusted from the first pass) — the agent read both files fresh, computed the exact magnitude gap, and confirmed the port's discriminator structure is otherwise identical to G1's (hidden dims `[512,256]` match, `amp_normalizer` is a byte-identical `RunningMeanStd` port, same ReLU activation, no batch/layer norm in either). Verdict: "PARTIALLY CONFIRMED" as the dominant cause of low AMP reward specifically (real, verified divergences, but magnitude couldn't be attributed as *the* single cause without a live measurement) — both are now fixed regardless, consistent with the standing no-unnecessary-divergence policy.

**Part B — stuck/progressing judgment:** genuine blue landings measured 0.0% at iteration 8000 (0/1181 wide episodes) — the fifth measurement this run, with only one single 0.1% fluke (iteration 2500) among five checks. `blue_stick_landing`'s dense reward has stayed consistently nonzero (~0.06-0.12/episode) the whole run, meaning the policy demonstrably gets closer-and-slower over time without ever crossing the discrete settle-window threshold to register as a landing. Separately: curriculum crossed into `cu=3` right at this checkpoint (`softstop_curriculum` weight jumped 210→262.5, activating `correct_foot_save_curriculum` bonuses for the first time), and `mean_episode_length` regressed sharply at the same point (119.4→92.5) with `shank_height` terminations spiking (10.6→30.0) — a curriculum-transition destabilization, not necessarily a separate root cause, but a confound worth tracking.

**Judgment: stuck.** Five consecutive measurements at ~0% genuine, despite the settle-window fix, full AMP parity (round 1), and dense `blue_stick_landing` shaping all already being in effect, is a strong enough signal on its own.

**Fix applied (blue-ball side):** `landing_speed_threshold` loosened `0.5 -> 0.8` m/s (`mdp/rewards.py`, `_get_reach_target_y`). Rationale: `blue_stick_landing`'s consistent nonzero reward shows the dense gradient IS pulling the policy toward "close and slow" — but the discrete landing check never fires, suggesting the threshold itself may simply be stricter than a real, deliberate footstrike's residual velocity even while decelerating. Not yet independently confirmed against real footstrike dynamics; flagged for revisit.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Live end-to-end smoke test (3 full training iterations, 64 envs): confirmed `amp_linear` weights now span up to ±1.0 with std ≈0.55-0.58 (matching a uniform(-1,1) distribution's theoretical std of 1/√3≈0.577 — the init fix is working as intended), training completes without error, and the 20x noise-sampling in `predict_amp_reward` adds no meaningful runtime cost (3 iterations in ~9.6s, comparable to pre-change).

**Training restarted** (not resumed) — pushed `model_8000.pt` (last checkpoint of the stuck run) before killing it. Three stacked changes in this restart (AMP init, AMP reward smoothing, landing_speed_threshold) — if the next check shows movement, a future investigation may need to isolate which one mattered, but bundling them was judged reasonable here since all three were independently well-evidenced fixes rather than speculative tweaks.

**Observed transient at restart (not a bug, documented for future monitoring):** the new run's `Loss/AMP`/`Loss/AMP_grad` spiked to 1299/1998 on iteration 0 (vs. the ~0.1-0.3 typical of the pre-fix runs), then decayed monotonically — 465→178→82→62→...→17 by iteration 19 for `Loss/AMP`, similarly for `Loss/AMP_grad`. `mean_action_acc` stayed in [1.56, 1.88] throughout, trending down, never approaching divergence. This is expected: the larger G1-matching initial weights produce larger initial discriminator gradients, and `grad_pen_loss` (which trains the discriminator to shrink that gradient norm toward 0) needs a number of steps to reel it back in — this is very likely how G1 behaves on every fresh run too, just never observed in this port before since its previous under-scaled default init never produced a gradient large enough to trigger a visible transient. Not treated as a divergence; flagged here so a future health check doesn't misread the first ~20 iterations' elevated AMP loss as a new problem.

---

## 2026-07-08 — ROOT CAUSE FOUND: `env._blue_airborne_at_reset` is a sticky per-episode latch that flagged 100% of landings as "free" regardless of when they actually happened, silently blocking `stopball`/`softstop` from ever firing on any wide episode all day

**Context:** user watched `sgk_play` on `model_2250.pt` and reported the robot still appeared to ignore blue and go straight for the ball. Asked for (1) a footreach correctness check, (2) a codebase-wide audit if footreach wasn't the problem, and (3) another independent AMP-vs-G1 architecture comparison, since "the amp has worsen i think looking at reward."

**Footreach: confirmed correct, both by code review and empirically.** Re-read `footreach`/`foot_proximity`'s target-switching logic — `reach_target_y` stays locked to blue's `half_y` for the entire approach on wide-unlanded episodes (`ball_close` structurally cannot become true until landed, since `~env._blue_wide` is false and `env._blue_landed` is false throughout). A live diagnostic driving the actual policy confirmed this empirically, not just by inspection: across 468-499 wide episodes, the assigned foot got within `landing_radius` **while in ground contact** in 97.6% of episodes. Footreach was never the bug.

**AMP: third independent audit came back clean.** A fresh, unbiased subagent re-compared `amp_discriminator.py` against G1's `amp.py` in full, plus `multi_disc_amp_ppo.py`'s `update()` against `him_ppo.py`'s, plus the normalizer and region-routing data volume. Confirmed-no-divergence on every major point already fixed in the two prior rounds; found one minor, likely-low-significance normalizer-width nuance (G1 tracks per-temporal-slot stats on the pre-concatenated state+next_state pair, the port shares single-frame stats across both slots) — not fixed, flagged as low-priority. This ruled AMP out as the remaining problem.

**The actual bug — found via a chain of live diagnostics, including catching a bug in my own diagnostic script along the way:**

1. Measured foot speed at the moment of closest in-contact approach to blue: median 0.815 m/s, just above the then-current `landing_speed_threshold=0.8`. Loosened to `1.0` (98.6% of near-contact moments were below 1.0). Re-checked against the *same* checkpoint — still 0.0% genuine. The threshold wasn't the blocker.
2. Hypothesized the settle-window's 3-consecutive-step requirement was never being satisfied. Wrote a diagnostic tracking `env._blue_settle_count`'s max value per episode — it showed 0% of episodes ever reaching count ≥ 2. Set `_BLUE_SETTLE_STEPS=1` to match. Re-checked — still 0.0% genuine, and the overall "landed at all" rate was *identical* to before the change (94.9-95.0% both times), which shouldn't happen if the settle requirement were actually the bottleneck.
3. That identical-before-and-after result was the tell: re-read my own settle-count diagnostic and found it filtered on `active = wide & ~landed`, which excludes the exact step a landing fires on (since `landed` flips true that same step) — so it could never observe the settle-count value that actually triggered a landing. The "never reaches 2" finding was a measurement artifact of my own script, not reality. **Reverted `_BLUE_SETTLE_STEPS` back to 3.**
4. With the real mechanism cleared of suspicion, investigated why "landed at all" was ~95% but "genuine" was always exactly 0%, by measuring **when** landings actually fire: `episode_length_buf` at the moment `newly_landed` transitions true, over 522 sampled landings. **Landings never fired before episode step 28 (median 35, max 86)** — clearly the product of 0.5+ seconds of real approach behavior, not free RSI credit. Yet **100% of these were still flagged `env._blue_airborne_at_reset=True`**.

**Root cause:** `env._blue_airborne_at_reset` (`rewards.py`, set inside `_get_reach_target_y`) is `env._blue_airborne_at_reset |= first_time_airborne & near_reset` — a sticky **latch** that goes true the first time the assigned foot is airborne within the first 2 steps of an episode, and **never resets** for the rest of a potentially 150-step episode. Since RSI donor poses are mid-motion by construction (documented repeatedly this project), some foot is airborne within the first 2 steps of nearly every episode, regardless of what happens afterward. The flag was never designed to answer "was *this* landing free" — only "was some foot briefly airborne near reset," which is true almost unconditionally and provides zero information about a landing occurring 30-85 steps later. Since `stopball`/`softstop`'s `landing_ok` gated on `env._blue_landed & ~env._blue_airborne_at_reset`, and the second term was essentially always false, **this silently made `stopball`/`softstop` unreachable on every wide-crossing episode for the entire duration of this investigation** — regardless of how well the policy actually performed. Every "genuine landing rate" measurement taken today (and likely going back to whenever this gate was first added, 2026-07-07) was reading this broken signal, not actual policy quality.

**Fix applied:** replaced the sticky latch with `env._blue_landed_was_free`, computed once per landing *event* rather than once per episode: `torch.where(newly_landed, env.episode_length_buf < _BLUE_LANDING_FREE_STEP_THRESHOLD, env._blue_landed_was_free)`, with `_BLUE_LANDING_FREE_STEP_THRESHOLD=10`. This asks the right question — was *this specific* landing achieved suspiciously early — instead of a stale historical flag. `env._blue_airborne_at_reset` is left in place (still computed, unused for gating) in case another diagnostic wants it. Updated `stopball`, `softstop` (`rewards.py`), and `metrics.blue_landed_genuine`/`blue_landed_rsi_assisted` (`metrics.py`) to use the new signal. Updated the scratchpad diagnostic script (`blue_landing_rate.py`) to match.

**Validation — re-ran the exact same diagnostic against the exact same checkpoint (`model_2250.pt`), changing nothing but the classification fix:**

| | Old (broken) classification | New (fixed) classification |
|---|---|---|
| Landed on blue (any) | 94.7% | 94.7% (unchanged, as expected — the landing mechanism itself was never broken) |
| Genuine | **0.0%** | **94.7%** |
| "RSI-assisted" / free | 94.7% | 0.0% |

The landing mechanism (footreach targeting, settle-window, speed threshold, position radius) has been working correctly this entire time. The training run has genuinely been learning to land on blue. The problem was a metrics/gating bug that made this invisible and, critically, prevented the policy from ever being rewarded for it via `stopball`/`softstop`.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Live diagnostic re-validation as shown above (3000 episodes, 1519 wide, same checkpoint before/after). Training restarted (not resumed) — `stopball`/`softstop` can now actually fire on wide-crossing episodes for the first time, which is a large enough change to the achievable reward landscape that continuing the old run would conflate results.

**Process note:** this took four sequential diagnostic scripts to isolate, including catching and correcting a bug in the diagnostic tooling itself mid-investigation (the `active = wide & ~landed` filtering bug in the settle-count check). Worth remembering for future debugging in this codebase: when a "stuck" metric doesn't move despite a fix that empirical evidence says *should* move it, question the measurement before questioning the fix.

---

## 2026-07-09 — the ACTUAL root cause of "genuine landing rate" being meaningless all day: `_get_reach_target_y`'s settle-count mutates on every call, and it's called 7x per real physics step

**Context:** even after the `_blue_landed_was_free` classification fix above (which was real and necessary), the user kept reporting via direct play observation that the robot still visibly skipped blue and went straight for the ball. Every diagnostic this session continued to show 90-95% "genuine" landing rates, flatly contradicting that observation. User pushed back repeatedly rather than accepting the diagnostic numbers, and asked for (1) a footreach correctness check, (2) a codebase-wide audit, (3) a fresh G1 comparison. Footreach was confirmed correct (97.6% in-contact-near-blue empirically). A live foot-velocity trace even showed a seemingly genuine ~15-step near-zero-speed dwell in one sampled episode — real evidence the *mechanism* could produce genuine stops, deepening the mystery of why the aggregate rate didn't match observation.

**The training curve was the tell, in hindsight.** `Episode_Reward/blue_ball_landed`'s logged value, converted through the standard one-shot-reward formula (`event_rate = logged_value × 150 / weight`), implied only ~8% of ALL episodes (~17% of wide episodes) ever fired the landing bonus during actual training rollouts — a 5x+ gap from the ~90% every diagnostic reported on the same checkpoints. Multiple hypotheses were tested and ruled out one at a time: RSI on/off (no difference), stochastic vs deterministic action sampling (no difference, 92.8% either way), ball difficulty matched to training's current curriculum value (no difference). The one variable that finally moved the needle: **switching from play mode's per-episode `randomize_region_on_reset` to training's actual `assign_static_regions`** (permanent per-env region assignment) dropped genuine landing to 28.4% in a fresh test — a real, large, independent factor (region-specific policy specialization behaving differently under per-episode-random vs permanent-per-env conditions), but still short of explaining a further order-of-magnitude gap down toward the training curve's implied ~17%.

**Root cause, found via a fresh independent code audit (dispatched per explicit user request for another full review) plus a fresh full-codebase G1 comparison run in parallel:** `_get_reach_target_y` (`mdp/rewards.py`) is a shared helper, called once each from **7 separate reward term functions** registered in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` — `stopball`, `softstop`, `footreach`, `foot_proximity`, `blue_ball_landed`, `blue_overshoot_penalty`, `blue_stick_landing` — all evaluated by mjlab's `RewardManager.compute()` against the same unchanged post-physics snapshot within a single `env.step()`. The function mutates `env._blue_settle_count` (`env._blue_settle_count = torch.where(candidate, env._blue_settle_count + 1, 0)`) with **no per-step memoization guard**. Since `candidate` depends only on data fixed for the whole tick, every one of the 7 calls that tick sees the same `candidate` value and each increments the counter — so `_BLUE_SETTLE_STEPS=3` (intended to require 3 consecutive *real* 0.02s physics steps, explicitly to rule out a single-tick graze per the 2026-07-08 settle-window fix's own stated purpose) could be satisfied within a **single real tick**, the instant 3+ of those 7 calls landed on a step where the foot happened to be in contact and within radius — even for a foot merely sweeping through mid-stride. This directly defeated the settle-window's entire reason for existing.

This also resolves the earlier live-trace mystery: that trace's apparent "genuine ~15-step dwell" was real *as a physical event*, but the *registration* of `_blue_landed=True` at step 37 likely happened far earlier than 3 true consecutive ticks of contact would have required under a correctly-implemented guard — i.e., the mechanism was still registering low-quality events as landings even when a genuinely good one happened to follow, making individual traces an unreliable way to judge the aggregate criterion's strictness.

**Fix:** added `env._blue_last_settle_step` (per-env, tracks the `episode_length_buf` value at which the settle-count was last incremented). The increment (`+1`) branch now only applies when `episode_length_buf != env._blue_last_settle_step` (first call this real tick); the reset-to-zero branch (when `candidate` is false) is left unguarded since it's naturally idempotent regardless of call count.

**Validation — same checkpoint (`model_3250.pt`), same diagnostic script, only the fix applied:**

| | Before fix | After fix |
|---|---|---|
| Genuine landing rate (wide episodes) | 90-95% (every diagnostic all day) | **1.4%** |

This is the real number. The policy has not actually learned genuine multi-step stopping — the user's play observations were correct the entire time; every prior diagnostic this session was measuring an artifact of the multi-call bug, not policy quality. The `_blue_landed_was_free` classification fix from the previous entry was still a real, necessary, independent bug fix (it was correctly gating `stopball`/`softstop` on a broken signal) — but it could never have produced a meaningful genuine-rate reading while this deeper measurement bug remained.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Live diagnostic re-validation as shown above. Also confirmed via independent code audit (fresh subagent, no access to this investigation's conclusions, arrived at the same root cause independently by static analysis of the call graph). Training restarted (not resumed) — this changes the actual achievability of the landing event during real training too (not just diagnostics), since the same multi-call pattern applies during real rollout collection.

**Open question, not yet resolved:** the static-region test's 28.4% (measured on the OLD, still-buggy code) vs. this fix's 1.4% (measured under play-mode randomized regions) are not directly comparable — they differ in two variables at once (region-assignment mode AND the settle-count bug). The true expected genuine rate under both fixes combined (static regions + correct settle-count) has not yet been measured and is likely to be very low. This is the number the 4000-iteration re-check should establish freshly, not either of these two partial numbers.

---

## 2026-07-09 — `ball_difficulty`/reward-weight curricula were a one-way ratchet with no path back from a bad patch, unlike G1

**Context:** the `settle_count_fix_2026-07-09` run was launched to see whether the settle-count fix (previous entry) alone was enough for the policy to learn genuine blue-ball landing under a now-trustworthy criterion. It reached 8.2% genuine (up from the confirmed 1.4% true baseline) by iteration ~7250 — real progress. User initially asked to boost `blue_overshoot_penalty` 10x to accelerate this; a live trend pull first showed the penalty was actually self-correcting (worsened -0.29→-1.14 through iteration ~4450, then recovering to -0.53 by 7250), so a drastic weight change was deferred pending more data. Two firings later, the user reported the run as "dead and really bad." A fresh pull through the run's actual end (iteration 9139) confirmed the user was right and the earlier "still adapting, give it time" read was wrong: `mean_episode_length` (79→~74, still ~45% below the pre-difficulty-jump peak of 128), `shank_height` terminations (~44, still ~5x the pre-jump level of ~8), and `blue_ball_landed` (flat ~0.003) had **all been flat for 2500+ iterations with zero recovery** — a genuine stuck plateau, not a transient.

**Root cause:** `Episode/Curriculum/ball_difficulty/ball_difficulty` jumped 0.667→1.0 exactly at iteration ~6496, and `shank_height` terminations spiked from ~8 to ~32-38 in the same window — the robot hit full ball speed and started falling much more. Checked `ball_difficulty_curriculum` (`mdp/events.py`, was line 662) and found `env._ball_difficulty = max(env._ball_difficulty, new_difficulty)` — a **monotonic ratchet, explicitly documented as "never goes backward even if ep_len temporarily drops."** The same pattern existed in `reward_curriculum_ep_len` (`mdp/events.py`, was line 560, `env._curriculumupdate = max(env._curriculumupdate, cu)`), which drives `footreach`/`blue_ball_landed`/`stopball`/`softstop` weights and (via the shared `_curriculumupdate`) the `cu>=3` quality bonuses (`single_foot_save`, `inner_face_orientation_save`, `cleanstop`, `airborne_at_save`).

**Confirmed this diverges from G1:** `Humanoid-Goalkeeper/legged_gym/legged_gym/envs/base/legged_robot.py:329`: `self.curriculumupdate = int(torch.mean(self.episode_length_buf[env_ids].float()) / 50.)` — a **fresh reassignment every update, no `max()`, no floor.** G1's own `command_ranges` (lines 333-336) are similarly recomputed from the current `curriculumupdate` each time, not accumulated. If G1's episode length regresses, its difficulty and reward scales ease back down automatically. Our port's ratchet was a real, undocumented-as-such (the "monotonic" behavior was commented as intentional but never justified against G1's actual behavior, and no prior audit pass today had checked this specific mechanism) divergence from the proven baseline.

**Why this produces exactly the observed failure:** once `ball_difficulty` hit 1.0 and the robot's fall rate increased, there was no mechanism to hand back easier practice. Every subsequent episode kept facing max-difficulty balls the current policy couldn't yet reliably handle, with less time per episode (shorter episodes) to practice anything, including the blue-ball landing task this whole investigation has been about. A transient bad patch became a permanent plateau by construction, independent of whether the settle-count fix or any reward-shaping term was otherwise working correctly.

**External validation (dispatched research pass, general-purpose subagent, WebSearch):** confirmed a pure one-way ratchet is not a recognized pattern in curriculum-RL literature. OpenAI's ADR (Rubik's Cube paper, arXiv 1910.07113 §5.2) explicitly contracts a randomization boundary when performance drops below a threshold (`p̄ ≤ t_L → φ -= Δ`), not just expands. IsaacLab's own terrain curriculum (`isaaclab_tasks/.../locomotion/velocity/mdp/curriculums.py`, `terrain_levels_vel`) explicitly promotes OR demotes each robot's terrain difficulty every episode based on distance traveled vs. commanded velocity, with no ratchet. Recommendation: match G1 exactly (fresh recompute, no floor) rather than a more complex per-env bidirectional scheme, since our difficulty is a single global scalar, not per-env terrain bins.

**Fix:** removed both `max()` calls. `env._curriculumupdate = cu` and `env._ball_difficulty = new_difficulty`, both fresh reassignments every `update_interval`, exactly matching G1's mechanism. Also fixed `ball_difficulty_curriculum`'s docstring, which described an accumulating `difficulty += step_size × curriculumupdate` formula that didn't match the actual (and unchanged) `min(1.0, curriculumupdate / 3.0)` implementation — a pre-existing doc/code mismatch, not a behavior change.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Root cause confirmed via direct tensorboard trend inspection (`ball_difficulty` step function exactly co-occurring with the `shank_height` spike) and direct code comparison against G1's `legged_robot.py:329-336`. Fix validated only mechanically (matches G1's formula exactly) — not yet validated on a live run, since this requires a fresh training run to observe whether the plateau actually resolves.

**Not yet resolved:** whether this fix alone resolves the plateau, or whether the underlying policy capability gap at full difficulty needs separate attention (e.g. the difficulty curriculum ramping too fast relative to how hard "genuine multi-step stop-then-save" actually is to learn). The next run should be watched specifically for whether `ball_difficulty` ever dips back down after a regression, and whether episode length/shank_height recover this time instead of plateauing.

---

## 2026-07-09 — Curriculum ratchet fix confirmed the plateau was fixed, but exposed a new, sharper failure: genuine landing rate collapses 43%→2% mid-run while stability improves

**Context:** `curriculum_ratchet_fix_2026-07-09` (previous entry) fixed the stuck-plateau symptom cleanly — live-validated `ball_difficulty` dipping 0.667→0.333 then recovering to 0.667 on its own (bidirectional curriculum working as designed), `shank_height` back to healthy baseline (~8-12 vs. the ~44 plateau), genuine landing rate measured at **43.4%** (iteration 1750) and **35.1%** (iteration 3250) — both comfortably in the "healthy" range. But at iteration 5000, a routine re-check measured **1.9%** genuine. The training-time `blue_ball_landed` metric confirmed this wasn't a step-function event: it declined smoothly from 0.019 (step 3200) to ~0.002-0.004 by step 3850 and stayed there. Over this exact window, general health metrics stayed flat or improved: `mean_episode_length` 105→~120, `mean_action_acc` 1.11→1.20 (both stable, no divergence), `Policy/mean_noise_std` 0.56→0.60 (stable), `ball_difficulty` flat at 0.667 (ruling out a difficulty-jump explanation this time). The only visibly-moving reward signal was `blue_overshoot_penalty` getting steadily worse (-0.42→-0.84+) without ever self-correcting.

**This ruled out a general policy collapse** (stability metrics improving, not degrading) and is a different, more specific failure than the curriculum-ratchet bug: the robot got *better* overall while specifically abandoning the harder "genuinely stop at the intermediate waypoint" subtask.

**User granted full autonomy to research + experiment.** Dispatched two independent research passes (general-purpose subagents, WebSearch):

1. **Mechanism research** converged on a **self-reinforcing "zero collapse" + rare-event credit-assignment failure**, not classic reward hacking or entropy collapse. `blue_stick_landing`'s reward (`exp(-15*dist)*exp(-3*speed)`) is a sharp exponential cliff — once stochastic exploration pushes the policy just past this narrow basin, gradient there vanishes (arXiv:2605.30896, "zero collapse" in discontinuous-reward landscapes; actor-critic methods flagged as particularly susceptible since a biased value estimate accelerates drift once the critic starts scoring "near blue" states as low-value). Separately, rare-event scarcity (arXiv:2103.06846) means gradient-based policy search specifically degrades as a reward-bearing event's frequency falls — directly matching the 43%→35%→2% self-feeding trend. The flat `Policy/mean_noise_std` does NOT rule this out: it's a marginal per-step statistic that says nothing about whether the policy's *trajectory-level mean* still visits the narrow conjunctive joint-space basin (position + velocity + duration all satisfied together) — a policy can keep per-step noise constant everywhere while its mean approach trajectory drifts outside the capture basin entirely (a coverage-collapse, not a determinism-collapse). Mechanistically: the -30 overshoot penalty (capped at -0.5/step) is cheap, immediate, low-variance, and present in nearly every wide rollout, so it dominates the policy-gradient batch by sheer sample count even though the foregone landing bonus has higher expected value when it does land — essentially class imbalance in the PPO batch for a threshold-gated conjunctive reward.

2. **Fix research** (ALLSTEPS arXiv:2005.04323 read directly; also arXiv:2603.05113, 2104.06411, 2204.02372, 1707.05300) found ALLSTEPS itself curricula over *task difficulty distribution*, not reward-term gating — not directly applicable here. The closer match ("Decoupling Task and Behavior," arXiv:2603.05113) confirms disabling/delaying downstream reward until an upstream subtask is mastered is a real, working pattern, but both dispatched passes independently ranked the SAME lower-effort fix first: extend the existing bidirectional curriculum (already fixed in the previous entry) to also scale the stage-1 rewards (`blue_overshoot_penalty`, `blue_stick_landing`), which currently have fixed weights while `stopball`/`softstop`/`footreach`/`blue_ball_landed` all grow via the same curriculum -- letting the "must land first" incentive shrink relative to the growing gated payoff as training matures. A heavier subtask-curriculum restructure (delaying stage-2 reward, or an RSI-style episode-start curriculum spawning near blue early per JSRL/reverse-curriculum-generation) was ranked as a follow-up if this doesn't resolve it, not a first move.

**Fix applied (`curriculum_landing_gradient_fix_2026-07-09`):**
1. Added `blue_overshoot_penalty_curriculum` and `blue_stick_landing_curriculum` (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`), tying both to the same `reward_curriculum_ep_len` mechanism as `stopball`/`softstop`/`footreach`/`blue_ball_landed`. `blue_overshoot_penalty`: -30→-45→-60→-75 across cu=0..3. `blue_stick_landing`: 8→12→16→20.
2. Widened `blue_stick_landing`'s reward basin directly (`mdp/rewards.py`): `dist_sigma` 15→8 (0.5-decay point 5cm→9cm, 0.1-decay point 15cm→29cm), `speed_sigma` 3→1.5 (0.5-decay point 0.23→0.46 m/s, 0.1-decay point 0.77→1.5 m/s) — directly attacks the "zero collapse" cliff mechanism so a near-miss still carries usable gradient back toward genuine landing instead of falling into a flat near-zero region.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Config loads correctly (both new curriculum terms confirmed present in a fresh `load_env_cfg` call). Not yet validated on a live run — this is the first of a planned series of experiments, each to be run to 5k iterations and judged on the genuine-landing-rate trend (not just whether it avoids the specific 43%→2% collapse shape) before deciding whether to keep iterating on this direction or move to the RSI-episode-start-curriculum fallback.

**Not yet resolved:** whether this combination (curriculum-scaled penalty/bonus + widened basin) is sufficient, or whether the rare-event-scarcity mechanism specifically requires attacking exposure/practice frequency directly (the RSI-style episode-start curriculum idea) rather than just incentive magnitude and gradient shape. This is exactly the open question the next 5k-iteration checkpoint is meant to answer.

---

## 2026-07-10 — Branch split: `blue-ball-waypoint` (preserved) + `green-ball-baseline` (two-stage mechanism removed)

**Context:** while `curriculum_landing_gradient_fix_2026-07-09` continued training (landing rate climbing 7.9%→14.2%→19.9% across iterations 1500-4000, no collapse), user requested isolating the core AMP+PPO+save-reward mechanism from the two-stage blue-waypoint mechanism entirely, to verify the underlying system works correctly on the simpler "always target the true crossing point directly" task (closer to G1's own approach, which has no waypoint concept at all).

**Branches created from master (commit `68f5d42`):**
- `blue-ball-waypoint`: exact preservation of everything up to and including the curriculum-scaling/basin-widening fix. The live training experiment continues on `master`/this lineage.
- `green-ball-baseline`: new branch with the entire two-stage waypoint mechanism removed.

**Removed on `green-ball-baseline`:**
- `mdp/rewards.py`: deleted `_get_reach_target_y` (the two-stage midpoint-then-full target scheduler, its settle-window landing detection, and all `env._blue_*` state), `blue_ball_landed`, `blue_overshoot_penalty`, `blue_stick_landing`. `footreach`/`foot_proximity` now target `_get_ball_crossing_y` (the true crossing point) directly and unconditionally, matching G1's own eereach (no waypoint concept upstream). `stopball`/`softstop` fire unconditionally on a qualifying deflection, no longer gated behind a prior blue-midpoint landing. Removed the now-unused `_REGION_IS_FAR` constant and its `regions` import.
- `mdp/metrics.py`: deleted entirely (`blue_landed_genuine`/`blue_landed_rsi_assisted` were its only content).
- `mdp/__init__.py`: removed blue-ball/metrics imports and exports.
- `tasks/goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`: removed the three blue-ball `RewardTermCfg` entries, their three `CurriculumTermCfg` entries (`blue_ball_landed_curriculum`, `blue_overshoot_penalty_curriculum`, `blue_stick_landing_curriculum`), and the metrics-manager block (along with the now-unused `MetricsTermCfg` import).
- `scripts/play.py`: removed the blue/green two-stage viewer overlay logic -- now always draws the green sphere at the direct crossing point.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass (no blue-ball-specific tests exist in this project's own suite -- those live in the separate `SimpleGoalKeeper/` project, untouched). Live smoke test: fresh `ManagerBasedRlEnv` construction, reset, and 50 steps with `num_envs=16` all succeed cleanly; `reward_manager`'s active-terms list confirmed no `blue_*` terms remain; `metrics_manager` shows only the base `mean_action_acc` term. Full sweep of the codebase for remaining "blue" references confirmed only historical/explanatory comments remain (documenting the removal itself), no active mechanism code.

**Not yet resolved:** no training run launched yet on this branch at the time this entry was written -- documents the removal only. A run (`green_ball_baseline_2026-07-10`) was subsequently launched and ran cleanly to 5750+ iterations before being stopped to apply the curriculum-oscillation fix below (this same fix, ported from the sibling `blue-ball-waypoint` branch since the underlying bug is in shared curriculum code, not the removed blue-ball mechanism).

## 2026-07-10 — Curriculum oscillation fix, ported from `blue-ball-waypoint`

**Context:** this branch shares `mdp/events.py`'s `reward_curriculum_ep_len`/`ball_difficulty_curriculum` classes with `blue-ball-waypoint` (the blue-ball removal only touched `rewards.py`/`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`/`metrics.py`, not these curriculum classes). The same oscillation bug found and fixed on that branch applies here identically -- `green_ball_baseline_2026-07-10` was stopped and this fix cherry-picked over before relaunching, rather than letting a known-bad curriculum mechanism keep running on this branch too.

**Root cause (full detail in the `blue-ball-waypoint` branch history, commits `70421f8` and `cb2cb18`):** G1 uses two structurally different curriculum patterns -- reward weights are freshly overwritten every step (genuinely bidirectional), while task difficulty (`command_ranges`) is an accumulator nudged relative to its current value every ~500-step window, which is structurally incapable of decreasing since the driving signal (`curriculumupdate`) is never negative. The 2026-07-09 fix on this project correctly ported the reward-weight pattern into `reward_curriculum_ep_len`, but wrongly applied that same fresh/bidirectional pattern to `ball_difficulty_curriculum` too -- a live run on `blue-ball-waypoint` showed this causing `ball_difficulty` to oscillate 0.667<->0.333 repeatedly, never settling, with episode length bouncing noisily as a direct consequence.

**Fix:** (1) `_update_smoothed_ep_len` -- EMA (alpha=0.3) applied to `mean_episode_length` before either curriculum term computes `cu`, so single noisy windows can't flip it. (2) `ball_difficulty_curriculum` reverted to a step-size accumulator matching G1's `command_ranges` exactly (`env._ball_difficulty = min(1.0, current + step_size*cu)`), monotonic-non-decreasing by construction rather than a fresh bidirectional recompute. Independently verified against G1's actual source by 3 separately-framed subagents before being applied on the originating branch.

**External validation:** OpenAI's ADR (arXiv 1910.07113) avoids exactly this failure mode the correct way -- difficulty boundaries move by small bounded steps based on a rolling performance buffer, never an instant snap to the latest single reading.

**Independent verification:** the accumulator-vs-fresh-recompute distinction above was independently confirmed by 3 separately-framed Explore subagents (no shared context) tracing every use of `curriculumupdate` in G1's `legged_robot.py` from scratch, on the originating `blue-ball-waypoint` branch, before this fix was cherry-picked here. All three converged on the identical finding with cited line numbers.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Standalone simulation (same as on `blue-ball-waypoint`) confirms monotonic-non-decreasing behavior under the exact noisy sequence that broke the previous version, including a simulated late-run regression. Not yet validated on a live run on this branch specifically -- `green_ball_baseline_2026-07-10` is being relaunched fresh with this fix in place per user request.

---

## 2026-07-10 — Left/right foot-orientation sign bug: `.abs()` let the policy skip learning the mirror-image direction for one side

**Context:** user reported "weird behavior on the arm and the orientation of the foot" specifically on the +Y (left) side while watching play, and asked for a full audit, green-ball-baseline first.

**Root cause:** `inner_face_orientation_save` and `foot_inner_face_continuous` (`mdp/rewards.py`) both rewarded the assigned foot's long axis being aligned with world/robot-local Y using `.abs()` on the dot product -- explicitly justified in the old comment as "either +Y or -Y direction is a valid sideways save." That reasoning is wrong: a left-side save needs the foot rotated toward +Y, a right-side save needs -Y -- mirror images, not interchangeable. `.abs()` rewarded either direction equally, so the policy had zero pressure to learn the correct, side-matched rotation -- it could satisfy the reward on every save by consistently rotating one way, even when that's backwards for half the cases.

**Empirical confirmation before fixing anything:** a zero-action rollout (pure physics/default pose, no learned behavior, 512 envs) showed NO left/right asymmetry in arm joint positions or foot orientation -- ruling out a robot-model/XML/default-pose bug. A rollout with the actual trained checkpoint (`green_ball_curriculum_fix_2026-07-10`, `model_7000.pt`) then showed a clear, real asymmetry: the assigned foot's quaternion z-component averaged -0.317 on right-ball envs (a genuine ~38 degree rotation toward -Y, correctly learned) vs only -0.024 on left-ball envs (~3 degrees, essentially never rotated) -- the policy learned the correct right-side rotation but never discovered the mirror-image left-side rotation, exactly matching the user's report.

**Fix:** both functions now require the SIGNED alignment to match an `expected_sign` (+1 when the left foot is closest/assigned, -1 for the right foot), derived from the empirically-confirmed-correct right-foot behavior mirrored to the left, rather than accepting either sign via `.abs()`. `foot_inner_face_continuous` (the dense, per-step version -- far more total training signal than the one-shot bonus, so the more impactful fix of the two) is left deliberately unclamped so a wrong-direction rotation gets a mild penalty, not just zero reward -- clearer gradient toward the correct mirror-image direction than merely withholding reward, which would look identical to "never rotated at all."

**Re-audit (user explicitly asked for a second, more skeptical pass rather than accepting the first fix at face value):** checked every other `.abs()` in `rewards.py` (all genuinely symmetric-by-design: distance-from-target, velocity/torque magnitude, timing windows -- none are the same bug class), re-verified `_get_correct_foot_idx`'s left=0/right=1 mapping, checked `feetorientation`/`foot_ang_vel_xy`/`single_foot_save` (all correctly symmetric by design), read the T1 XML directly (`Shoulder_Roll`/`Elbow_Yaw` correctly sign-mirrored between sides, `Shoulder_Pitch`/`Elbow_Pitch`/`Ankle_Pitch` correctly same-sign, foot body Y-offsets correctly mirrored), and compared `LeftDoubleStep`/`RightDoubleStep` AMP reference clip arm-joint ranges directly (reasonable and bounded on both sides -- these are independent real motion captures, not a synthetic mirror pair, so exact symmetry isn't expected or required). Found nothing beyond the two already-fixed functions.

**Separately investigated (same conversation, not a bug):** user asked whether G1 includes arms in its AMP observation the same way this project does, hypothesizing that AMP-driven arm motion with no grounding task reward could explain "extreme" arm movement. Confirmed: G1's `get_amp_observations()` (`legged_robot.py:157-162`) returns `self.dof_pos.clone()` -- all joints, arms included, same as this project's `joint_pos_abs`/`joint_vel_abs` (`SceneEntityCfg("robot")` default, unfiltered). No divergence in AMP observation composition. The real difference: G1 has `_reward_eereach`, a genuine ongoing task reward pulling the hand toward the ball throughout the approach, directly constraining arm motion -- this project's feet-only design has no equivalent (`postupperdofpos` only activates post-save, pulling toward a static default, not toward anything during the approach). Not fixed here -- flagged as a real architectural difference worth a deliberate design decision, not an unintentional bug, and out of this fix's scope.

**Evidence:** 48/48 tests pass. Standalone unit test verifying the sign logic against all four left/right x correct/wrong-direction combinations. Live smoke test: fresh env, reset, 60 steps, no errors.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated on a live training run -- both trainings are being restarted fresh with this fix per user request.

## 2026-07-12 -- replaced the full AMP motion dataset with only the 2.5x-retimed DoubleStep clips

**Context:** ported from `blue-ball-waypoint`'s AMP-dilution fix (that branch's `docs/BugFixes.md`, `blue_amp2xonly_decelfix_2026-07-12`): a single AMP discriminator trained on many motion clips of very different pace/style mixes incompatible motion statistics (arXiv:2605.18611, arXiv:2606.08922). This branch's `goalkeeper_amp_cfg.py::_motion_files()` previously globbed ALL 14 NPZ files in `motions/data/` (single-step, double-step, triple-step, and various "safe" pose clips) into one AMP discriminator -- the same failure class, applied per user request even though this branch has no near/far region split (it uses one AMP discriminator for every crossing, unlike the multidisc blue-ball task).

**Fix:** `_motion_files()` now returns exactly two files: `LeftDoubleStep_own_booster_t1_2p5x.npz` and `RightDoubleStep_own_booster_t1_2p5x.npz` (2.5x-retimed via `scripts/retime_motion.py`, copied over from `blue-ball-waypoint` where the retiming tool lives -- 73 -> 29 frames, 1.44s -> 0.576s). These two files are used for every crossing on this branch (no separate near/far dataset exists here to split between). Started at 2x per the initial port, then upgraded to 2.5x same day after the user watched blue-ball-waypoint's play with the 2x pace and reported it still looked too slow relative to the fastest observed wide-crossing window (0.58s at full difficulty) -- 2.5x's 0.576s matches that almost exactly, vs. 2x's 0.72s.

**Verification:** 48/48 tests pass unchanged (`test_amp_motion_weights.py`'s dynamic tests adapt automatically since they call `_motion_files()` directly rather than hardcoding the file list). Live smoke test (`Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1`, `--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`) clean, no errors.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a real training run on this branch (green-ball's own training was already stopped/plateaued this session, per `master`'s `model_19000` push -- this change would need a fresh run to evaluate).

## 2026-07-12 -- widened green-ball's hard-difficulty `t_flight_range` upper bound from 1.1s to 1.5s

**Context:** user request, alongside restarting this branch's training with the 2.5x AMP dataset fix above. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s `reset_ball` event (both training and play blocks) passed `t_flight_range=(0.7, 1.1)` as the full-difficulty (`ball_difficulty=1.0`) reaction-time range to `events.py::reset_ball_rolling` -- at max curriculum difficulty, ball flight time ranged from 0.7s (fastest) to only 1.1s (slowest), giving the robot at most 1.1s reaction time even on the "easiest" hard-difficulty ball.

**Fix:** widened the upper bound to 1.5s: `t_flight_range=(0.7, 1.5)` in both the training and play `reset_ball` blocks (train/play parity rule, `CLAUDE.md`), plus `reset_ball_rolling`'s own default parameter value and inline comment for consistency (the default itself is unused in practice -- both call sites always pass explicit params -- but was left stale otherwise). Only the slow end widened; the fast end (0.7s, the hardest single-side of the range) is untouched, so peak difficulty is unchanged -- this only adds more slow-ball variety at full difficulty rather than making anything harder.

**Verification:** 48/48 tests pass (none hardcoded the old `1.1` value). Live smoke test (`--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`) clean, no errors.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a real training run -- restarting this branch's training with both this change and the 2.5x AMP dataset fix together, so any effect on training is confounded between the two (both are small, well-reasoned, non-interacting changes -- one to the AMP reference data, one to the task's own ball-speed curriculum -- so bundling them for one restart is a reasonable risk).

User confirmed the parameter is `y_end_range` (the hard-difficulty goal-target lateral spawn range in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s `reset_ball` event, both training and play blocks) and, after confirming the actual current value was `(-0.9, 0.9)` (not `1.1` as the user initially recalled), chose to widen the single-sided max reach from `0.9` to `1.3` meters each side.

## 2026-07-13 -- reverted green-ball's AMP dataset back to the full 14-file glob, undoing the 2026-07-12 2-file experiment

**Root cause:** the 2026-07-12 "only 2.5x DoubleStep clips" change was ported from blue-ball-waypoint's AMP-dilution fix, but that fix targeted a *region-conditioned* discriminator that already only serves one specific crossing type (far, one side) -- cutting it to one pace of one clip removed a redundant blend, not the only source of motion data. This branch's single discriminator serves EVERY crossing (single-step, double-step, triple-step, recovery poses); cutting it to 2 files removed the only AMP grounding for everything except double-stepping. The very next run (`green_2p5x_tflight_widen_2026-07-12`) saw `action_acc_l2`/`action_rate_l2` explode by 3-4 orders of magnitude around iteration ~14665 and never recover (see the `mean_action_acc` investigation, same conversation) -- a discriminator no longer constraining most of the task's motion is a plausible driver of that unconstrained actor behavior.

**Fix:** `_motion_files()` reverted to `sorted(_MOTIONS_DIR.glob("*.npz"))` (the original implementation). The two `_2p5x.npz` files added on 2026-07-12 were deleted from `motions/data/` so the glob picks up exactly the original 14 files, not 16.

**Verification:** 48/48 tests pass (`test_amp_motion_weights.py`'s dynamic tests adapt automatically). Confirmed directly: `_motion_files()` now returns exactly 14 files, weights correctly boost the 3 DoubleStep/TripleStep files (4.0) over the other 11 (1.0), matching the pre-2026-07-12 behavior exactly. Live smoke test clean, no errors.

**Kept from 2026-07-12/07-13 (not reverted):** `t_flight_range`/`ep_len_divisor` were already reverted in the prior entry; `y_end_range` widening to `(-1.3, 1.3)` stays, per user request -- unrelated to the AMP dataset and not implicated in the collapse.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a real training run -- relaunching with this fix plus the kept `y_end_range` widening.

## 2026-07-13 -- interchanged `DoubleStep` for the 2.5x-retimed pace within the full 14-file dataset, weighted higher than `TripleStep`

**Context:** per user request, after reverting to the full 14-file AMP dataset above. Separately, `_motion_weights()` reworked so `DoubleStep` and `TripleStep` no longer share one boost constant: `_DOUBLE_STEP_WEIGHT = 5.0` (up from the shared 4.0, since DoubleStep is now the timing-matched 2.5x pace and per user request should be weighted higher), `_TRIPLE_STEP_WEIGHT = 4.0` (unchanged, still 1.0x pace).

Confirmed directly: 14 files total, `DoubleStep` weight 5.0, `TripleStep` weight 4.0, everything else 1.0. Live smoke test (`--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`) clean, no errors.

## 2026-07-14 -- killed wrong-architecture green run, restored multi-disc as this branch's actual target, ported 3 fixes from blue-ball-waypoint

**Context:** `green_doubletriple25x_2026-07-13` (single-disc task, `Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1`) had been running for ~17 hours when a checkpoint `state_dict`/`discriminator_state_dict` key comparison against a known-multi-disc checkpoint confirmed it was on the wrong architecture. Root cause: `CLAUDE.md`'s "Training Commands" section documented only the plain single-disc task string and was never updated when the region-conditioned multi-discriminator task (`Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc` -- history encoder, ball/region estimators, 4 region-conditioned AMP discriminators) became this project's actual target; `HANDOFF.md` (blue-ball-waypoint's run log) had always used `-MultiDisc` correctly. The single-disc AMP-dataset tuning entries immediately above this one (2026-07-12/13, `goalkeeper_amp_cfg.py`'s 14-file weighted glob) are a separate, independent mechanism from the multi-disc task's `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py::REGION_MOTION_FILES` and don't carry over -- they were real, deliberate tuning of the wrong track, not accidentally-discarded work relevant to multi-disc.

**Fix:** killed the wrong-architecture process. Ported 3 fixes from blue-ball-waypoint's post-2026-07-10 multi-disc work that are general (not blue-waypoint-specific) and were still missing here:
1. `inner_face_orientation_save` (`mdp/rewards.py`): foot selection was the geometrically-closest foot to the ball's live position (`dist[:,0] <= dist[:,1]`), not the fixed task-assigned foot (`_get_correct_foot_idx`) that `correct_foot`'s own gate already requires -- an assigned-foot overshoot could let the stationary lagging foot's orientation get silently checked instead. Now uses `_get_correct_foot_idx` directly (blue's 3b638d9, Fix 1 only -- the same commit's `track_blue_landing_success`/wide-crossing payoff scaling is blue-waypoint-specific and intentionally not ported).
2. `beyondAMP/source/rsl_rl_amp/rsl_rl_amp/utils/wandb_utils.py`: `WandbSummaryWriter._flush_wandb` reused whatever `global_step` triggered the flush for `wandb.log(..., step=...)`, which could be a float (the `*/time` series log wall-clock seconds through the same path as integer iteration-keyed scalars) and crash `wandb.log` (requires an int step). Now tracks the last int step seen separately (blue's 6f1563d, verbatim).
3. `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py::REGION_MOTION_FILES`: was untouched since 2026-07-10 (`40e6936`) -- still a `dict[str, str]` pointing `left_far`/`right_far` at the original 1.0x-pace `DoubleStep` clips. Restructured to `dict[str, list[str]]`; `left_far`/`right_far` now use the `_2p5x.npz`-retimed clips (matching blue's converged choice after its own 1.5x/2x/2.5x escalation history), `left_near`/`right_near` stay single-item lists at the unchanged 1.0x pace. Ghost-overlay flattening and `amp_runner_cfg`'s per-region loop updated to match the list type. Verified byte-identical to blue-ball-waypoint's current code apart from the intentionally-excluded `track_blue_landing_success` wiring.

**Verification:** all three ported blocks diffed line-for-line against blue-ball-waypoint's current tip after applying (see git history) -- confirmed no transcription drift. `py_compile` clean. Smoke test pending (next entry / HANDOFF.md).

**Not yet resolved:** fresh multi-disc training run not yet launched at the time of this entry -- see HANDOFF.md for the run this restarts and its plan (resume decision: fresh start, not resuming `model_19000.pt`, per explicit user choice weighing avoidance of stale-checkpoint orientation/pace habits against the ~19k-iteration wall-clock cost).

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a real training run -- relaunching with both interchanges together.

## 2026-07-14 -- `stopball`/`softstop` fired regardless of which foot deflected the ball; both now require the task-assigned foot

**Context:** user reported watching play and noticing a wrong-foot exploit: if one leg touches the ball and it deflects toward the other leg's side, the save is still rewarded. Traced directly.

**Root cause:** `softstop` already computed `correct_foot_contact` (via `_get_correct_foot_idx` + the `feet_contact` sensor), but only to *record* `env._softstop_correct_foot` for downstream quality bonuses (`single_foot_save`, `inner_face_orientation_save`) -- it never gated `softstop`'s own `fired` condition, which only checked ball physics (`ball_x_vel > threshold`, in front). `stopball` had the same gap but worse -- no foot-contact check at all, not even a post-hoc one. Between them these are the two primary save-detection rewards (`stopball` up to weight 37.5, `softstop` up to 262.5, the single largest term in the whole reward table), so any foot contact that deflected the ball earned full credit regardless of which foot did it.

Checked G1 upstream (`_reward_stopball`, `legged_robot.py:1407`) per this project's verify-against-G1-first rule: G1 also has no foot-side gating, but that's not a divergence to fix -- G1 catches with either hand independently, no fixed per-side assignment, so there's no "wrong hand" concept there. This project already has a stricter per-crossing-side assigned-foot concept (`_get_correct_foot_idx`) that other reward terms already respect; it was just never applied to the two primary detection rewards themselves.

**Fix:** both functions now compute `correct_foot_contact` unconditionally (same `_get_correct_foot_idx` + `feet_contact` sensor pattern already used) and require it in `fired`:
- `stopball`: `fired = (delta_vx > delta_vel_threshold) & in_front & correct_foot_contact & ~env._sb_flag`.
- `softstop`: `fired = (ball_x_vel > velocity_threshold) & in_front & correct_foot_contact & ~env._softstop_flag`. `env._softstop_correct_foot` is now always `True` whenever `_softstop_flag` is `True` (guaranteed rather than merely tracked) -- downstream gates on it become no-ops, which is harmless.

**Verification:** 48/48 tests pass -- 2 pre-existing failures also fixed while in this file (`test_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`'s `test_region_motion_files_assignment`/`test_runner_cfg_amp_data_has_one_file_per_region_and_no_triple_step` were still asserting the old single-string `REGION_MOTION_FILES` format from before the 2026-07-14 list-restructure earlier this session, and `test_no_triple_step_anywhere_in_region_motion_files` had silently degraded to a vacuous check -- `"TripleStep" not in path` on a list checks list *membership*, not substring, so it always passed regardless of content once `path` became a list). All three updated to match the current list-based format and properly iterate paths. Live smoke test (`--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`) clean, no errors; `stopball`/`softstop` still fire occasionally under the new gate (random early policy still gets correct-foot contact by chance sometimes).

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a real training run for whether removing the wrong-foot exploit changes learned behavior in the expected direction -- relaunching fresh (green-ball only; blue-ball-waypoint stays stopped per separate user direction).

## 2026-07-15 -- far-region y_end_range still 0.9 (stale vs. single-disc's 1.3), and footreach never gave far regions any extra incentive for the additional travel a double-step needs

**Context:** user reported green also fails to reliably double-step on far crossings (same symptom blue has struggled with for weeks), and separately caught that `regions.py`'s far-region bound was still `0.9` despite the single-disc track's own `y_end_range` having been widened to `1.3` on 2026-07-13.

**Root cause 1 (stale constant):** the 2026-07-13 widening only touched `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s base `reset_ball` event, used directly by the single-disc task. The multi-disc task's region-conditioned reset (`reset_ball_rolling_by_region`) uses `regions.py::_REGION_Y_END_RANGE`'s own fixed per-region bounds instead and was never updated, so far-region crossings topped out at 0.9m while the single-disc track's equivalent reach out to 1.3m.

**Root cause 2 (no region-specific incentive for the required style of motion):** checked G1 upstream per this project's verify-first rule. G1's `_reward_eereach` uses a REGION-CONDITIONAL velocity multiplier: its two most extreme regions (needing a dive) get an escalating `jump_scale = 3.0 + 3.0*curriculumupdate`, all other regions get a flat `3.0` (`legged_robot.py:1379-1388`). This project's `footreach` used the flat `3.0` multiplier for every region regardless of near/far -- no extra incentive was ever given for the additional travel/step-count a far (double/triple-step) crossing needs in the same time budget as a near, single-step one. This is a real, previously-unnoticed divergence from the reference design, not merely a tuning gap.

**Fix:**
1. `regions.py::_REGION_Y_END_RANGE`: far bound widened `0.9 -> 1.3` for both `left_far`/`right_far`, matching the single-disc track.
2. `footreach`'s `vel_sigma`: now region-conditional when `env._region_id` exists -- far regions (`region_id` 1 or 3) get `far_scale = 3.0 + 3.0*env._curriculumupdate` (identical formula and curriculum variable as G1's `jump_scale`), near regions keep the flat `3.0`. At `cu=0` both are identical (matching G1, where the dive regions' jump_scale also starts at 3.0); the differentiation only grows as curriculum advances. Adapted to lateral velocity rather than vertical, since this is feet-only stepping, not a hand-dive. Falls back to flat `3.0` when `_region_id` doesn't exist.

**Verification:** 48/48 tests pass. `py_compile` clean.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a real training run. A per-region save-rate baseline (`region_save_rate.py`, scratchpad) was captured on the current checkpoint before this fix for later comparison -- see next entry once training resumes.

## 2026-07-15 -- three more fixes: y_end_range reverted to isolate the vel_sigma boost, overshoot-and-fall root cause found (no G1 fix exists), arms removed from AMP, and stopball/softstop's correct-foot check was checking the wrong sensor

**Context:** user wanted to test the footreach `vel_sigma` boost alone (reverted `y_end_range` back to `0.9`, restarted as `green_velboost_only_2026-07-15`), then reported two things watching that run: (a) episodes going short because the robot overshoots and falls, (b) arms moving weirdly again. Separately reconfirmed a live bug in the wrong-foot fix from two entries ago: softstop still rewarded a right-foot deflection that rolled to a stop near the left foot.

**1. `y_end_range` reverted to 0.9** (`regions.py`) -- isolates the vel_sigma boost from the range-widening change for a cleaner test, per user request.

**2. Overshoot-and-fall root cause:** re-read G1's full reward section, not just `_reward_eereach`. G1's `jump_scale` multiplier is never used standalone -- it's paired with two jump-region-specific safety rewards, `_reward_airfeetorientation` and `_reward_successland` (`legged_robot.py:1427-1466`), both gated on the same `jump_ids` mask, managing foot orientation while airborne and landing quality. Those exist because G1's far regions are a literal DIVE (`root_states[:,2] > 1.0` -- torso leaves the ground). This project's far regions are a grounded double/triple-STEP -- continuously balance-critical, with no equivalent safety net. Porting G1's full escalation (up to 1+12*3=37x by cu=3) onto a task with no landing-safety mechanism plausibly caused the overshoot. **No G1-equivalent fix exists for a grounded task** (checked directly) -- `footreach`'s far-region slope halved (`3.0 + 1.5*cu` instead of `3.0 + 3.0*cu`, max 1+7.5*3=23.5x instead of 37x at cu=3). Not a G1-derived number, a judgment call pending validation.

**3. Arms removed from AMP entirely.** The `"amp"` observation group (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) used `joint_pos_abs`/`joint_vel_abs` with no joint filtering -- default `SceneEntityCfg("robot")`, all 21 DOF including all 8 arm joints, despite this being a known architectural gap flagged back on 2026-07-10 (arms have no task-grounding reward, only AMP-driven imitation -- a plausible "fake gradient" source for ungrounded arm swinging). Root cause of why this wasn't already filterable: `joint_pos_abs`/`joint_vel_abs` ignored `asset_cfg.joint_ids` entirely, always returning the full DOF vector regardless of what `asset_cfg` was passed. Fixed:
   - `observations.py`: `joint_pos_abs`/`joint_vel_abs` now slice by `asset_cfg.joint_ids`.
   - `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`: new `NON_ARM_JOINT_NAMES` (13 of 21 joints: Waist + 6 hip + 2 knee + 4 ankle), multi-disc's `"amp"` group override now passes `asset_cfg=SceneEntityCfg("robot", joint_names=NON_ARM_JOINT_NAMES)`.
   - `beyondAMP/.../motion_dataset.py`: added `joint_names` field to `MotionDatasetCfg`; `MotionDataset` resolves indices via `robot.find_joints(joint_names, preserve_order=True)` and exposes `joint_pos`/`joint_vel` as properties sliced by those indices (mirroring the existing `body_pos_w` pattern), keeping expert (reference clip) and policy sides in the same joint subset. `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_runner_cfg()` now passes `joint_names=NON_ARM_JOINT_NAMES` to match.
   - Scoped to the multi-disc task only (where the user observed the arm issue); single-disc's own `"amp"` group is untouched.

**4. `stopball`/`softstop`'s correct-foot check was checking the wrong sensor.** `feet_contact` (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) has `secondary=None` -- it fires whenever a foot touches ANYTHING (in practice, mostly the ground), not specifically the ball. The 2026-07-14 wrong-foot fix required "assigned foot in contact" via this sensor, but a foot standing normally on the ground already satisfies that, regardless of which foot actually deflected the ball -- confirmed by the user's report: right foot genuinely deflects the ball, ball rolls to a stop near the left foot, softstop still fires. Fixed: added a new `ContactSensorCfg` named `"ball_contact"` (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, `primary=` foot geoms, `secondary=` `ball_geom`/`ball` entity -- genuine foot-vs-ball contact), and rewired `stopball`/`softstop` to use it instead of `feet_contact`. `penalize_sharpcontact`, `feet_slippage`, and `airborne_at_save` correctly stay on the generic `feet_contact` sensor (they intentionally want ground-contact/force semantics, not ball-specific).

**Verification:** 48/48 tests pass. Live smoke test (`--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`) clean, no errors, no dimension mismatches from the AMP joint-count change (21 -> 13). `stopball`/`softstop` still fire occasionally under the new, stricter ball-contact gate.

**Not yet resolved:** none of these four fixes validated against a real training run yet -- all four bundled into one restart (`green_arms_ballcontact_fix_2026-07-15` or similar), so if things improve, they're confounded and would need an ablation to attribute credit individually. Given the overshoot fix in particular is an unvalidated judgment call (no G1 reference to check it against), this run should be watched specifically for whether short-episode/falling behavior improves.

## 2026-07-16 -- both `ball_contact` and blanket arm removal made things worse in practice; reverted/refined both

**Context:** user watched the `green_overshoot_arms_contact_fix_2026-07-15` / `green_widerange_2026-07-16` runs and reported two regressions from the prior entry's fixes: (a) `stopball`/`softstop` basically stopped rewarding at all, (b) arms swinging a lot again, worse than before.

**1. `ball_contact` sensor reverted, replaced with a same-step correlation gate instead of foot-vs-ball geometry.** The dedicated ball-contact `ContactSensorCfg` added in the previous entry turned out unreliable in practice -- MuJoCo contact detection between a small rolling ball and foot geoms is apparently too narrow a window to reliably register `found` at the exact step `stopball`/`softstop`'s velocity thresholds trip, so the correct-foot gate almost never passed. Reverted both rewards' sensor back to `feet_contact` (the original, ground-contact-based one). Added a different correctness signal instead of contact geometry: `stopball` now exposes its raw deflection condition (before the one-shot `~env._sb_flag` latch) as `env._sb_deflection_now`, computed every step; `softstop` additionally requires this to be `True` in the SAME step before firing. `stopball` is registered before `softstop` in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s rewards dict, so this is fresh (not stale) by the time `softstop` reads it. Rationale: a genuine single-contact deflection event should trip both stopball's delta-vx check and softstop's absolute-reversal check at essentially the same physical instant; a wrong-foot-standing-nearby coincidence shouldn't line up with both conditions simultaneously.

**2. Arm removal from AMP changed from a blanket 21→13 joint SLICE (all 4 regions) to a region-CONDITIONAL MASK (near regions keep arms, far regions don't).** Removing arms from every discriminator made arm swinging worse, not better -- with zero AMP constraint of any kind on arm motion, the policy had even less incentive to move them sensibly (the underlying "arms have no task-grounding reward" gap was unaddressed, just removing the one soft constraint that existed). User's fix: keep arms in the near-region discriminators' AMP input (real, live arm motion, presumably simpler/less prone to bad artifacts), only exclude them from the far/double-step discriminators specifically (where the original swinging complaints were about).

Implementation, since the multi-disc architecture routes ONE shared `amp_obs` tensor to whichever per-region discriminator matches each env (all regions must therefore stay the same tensor shape -- can't slice some regions to 13 dims and leave others at 21):
- `observations.py`: new `joint_pos_abs_arms_masked_by_region` -- returns the full 21-DOF vector always, but for envs whose `env._region_id` is a far region (1 or 3), the 8 arm-joint columns are overwritten with the default pose (uninformative constant) instead of their live values. Near-region envs' arm columns are untouched (real motion). Replaces the previous blanket-sliced `joint_pos_abs` usage in the `"amp"` observation group.
- `motion_dataset.py` (beyondAMP): new `MotionDatasetCfg.freeze_joint_names` field (a MASK, distinct from the existing `joint_names` SLICE field added 2026-07-15) -- freezes the given joints to the robot's default pose/zero velocity across the whole dataset at load time, keeping the tensor's joint-count shape unchanged.
- `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_runner_cfg()`: only `left_far`/`right_far`'s `MotionDatasetCfg` get `freeze_joint_names=ARM_JOINT_NAMES`; `left_near`/`right_near` get `None` (their reference clips' real arm motion capture data is used unmodified).

**Verification:** 48/48 tests pass. Live smoke test (`--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`) clean, no errors, no dimension mismatches. `stopball`/`softstop` fire under the new same-step gate.

**Not yet resolved:** neither fix validated against a real training run yet -- restarting fresh with both. The same-step gate is a judgment call (user's own suggested direction, "or something") -- if it proves too strict (real saves where the two conditions trip 1-2 steps apart get rejected), a small window (matching `single_foot_save`'s existing step-tracking pattern) would be the natural next refinement rather than an exact-same-step requirement.

## 2026-07-18 -- far-region AMP discriminators switched from the synthetic 2.5x-retimed DoubleStep clip to three real-mocap short/base/wide clips

**Context:** commit `36fc902` (pulled from origin) added six new AMP reference clips derived from raw mocap (`motions/raw/new_doublestep{left,right}{,_short,_wide}_booster_t1.pkl`), cropped to the active double-step window, leveled (root orientation, ankle flatness, forward-lead gait fix), and hip-straightened. Three distances per side: short (~0.7s, ~0.7m), base (~1.0s, ~0.9-1.1m), wide (~1.2s, ~1.3m) -- these line up closely with the far region's own `y_end` range (0.5-1.3m, `regions.py`). This directly addresses finding #2 of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-18-doublestep-research-and-plan.md`: the prior `LeftDoubleStep_own_booster_t1_2p5x.npz`/`RightDoubleStep_own_booster_t1_2p5x.npz` clips had no raw mocap source at all (synthetic, "own"-suffixed) and their 2.5x pace was reverse-engineered from the ball-flight-time budget via a flat playback-speed multiplier, not validated as a naturally-paced capture.

**What changed:** `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py::REGION_MOTION_FILES["left_far"]`/`["right_far"]` now each list all three new same-side clips (short, base, wide) instead of the single old 2.5x file. `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_runner_cfg()` passes `motion_weights=[1.0, 1.0, 1.0]` for far regions' `MotionDatasetCfg` -- the three clips differ in frame count (36/51/61 frames at 50fps), so without an explicit weight, per-transition-by-frame-count sampling would over-represent "wide" and under-represent "short" purely from clip length, not because either distance matters more; equal per-file weighting treats all three distances as equally valid style targets. Near regions (`LeftStep_own`/`Rightstep_own`) are unchanged -- the user's request was scoped to the far/"wide" discriminators only.

**Not changed (flagged, not applied):** (1) the RSI motion pool (`events.py::_STEM_TO_POOL`) has no entry for the new stems (`new_doublestepleft_booster_t1`, etc.) -- they currently fall into the pool loader's generic "combined pool" catch-all rather than being routed into the side/distance-specific `wide` RSI pool the old `leftdoublestep_own_booster_t1` stem was mapped to. RSI seeding and AMP grounding are now inconsistent for far crossings until this is updated. (2) the single-disc "green" track's `goalkeeper_amp_cfg.py::_motion_files()` globs the same `motions/data/` directory, so it silently picked up all 6 new files too, at the default weight 1.0 (`_motion_weights()`'s substring match is case-sensitive and doesn't match lowercase `doublestep`) -- harmless in practice since that single-disc task isn't the one currently being trained (multi-disc is, per the 2026-07-14 entry above), but worth knowing if that track is ever revived.

**Verification:** 49/49 tests pass (`test_multidisc_amp_cfg.py` updated: `test_region_motion_files_assignment` now asserts the full 3-file list per far region; new `test_far_region_motion_files_equally_weighted`). Live smoke test (`--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`, throwaway run dir deleted after) clean, no errors, no dimension mismatches -- region/ball estimator losses and AMP stats all populated normally.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a real training run -- the currently-running `green_samestepgate_armregion_2026-07-16` process (PID 3275406, 45000+ iterations) already has the old config loaded in memory and is unaffected by this source change; it needs to be stopped (checkpoint pushed first) and a fresh run launched for the new discriminator data to actually take effect. Left to the user's explicit call given the current run is otherwise healthy and 45k iterations deep.

## 2026-07-18 -- decoupled far-region required-travel-distance from ball_difficulty: new far_travel_curriculum (research-doc recommendation B)

**Context:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-18-doublestep-research-and-plan.md` recommendation B. `reset_ball_rolling`'s one-sided-range branch (the only branch region-conditioned calls use) lerped its outer `y_end` bound directly on `env._ball_difficulty` -- `outer = lo + (hi - lo) * d`, same `d` that also governs spawn distance, ball speed, and reaction time. Since `ball_difficulty` saturates to 1.0 within the first few thousand iterations (observed: ~iter 11,000 on the current run, faster in several historical runs), the far region's full 1.3m lateral travel requirement went live essentially from the start of training, stacked on top of max ball speed and minimum reaction time. Worse: the far region's own inner edge (0.5m) already exceeds the reference clips' measured single-step reach (~0.3m, per the 2026-07-03 waypoint-design investigation), so there was never an "easy end" of the far range reachable in one step -- every far episode has always demanded genuine multi-step travel, with no ramp. ALLSTEPS (arXiv:2005.04323) found a no-curriculum baseline fails outright on exactly this class of problem (biped committing to discrete, non-trivial foot placement); this project had a curriculum for ball speed/spawn-distance/reaction-time and for reward weights, but never for the one dimension that actually requires the hard skill.

**What changed:**
1. `mdp/events.py`: new `far_travel_curriculum` class, mirroring `ball_difficulty_curriculum`'s exact accumulator pattern (same shared EMA-smoothed episode-length signal via `_update_smoothed_ep_len`, same `env._frac = min(1.0, frac + step_size*cu)` monotonic formula, matching G1's `command_ranges` mechanism rather than its reward-weight mechanism -- see that class's own docstring for why this pattern is correct for a task-difficulty range and not the fresh-recompute one).
