Maintains a NEW, independent state variable `env._far_travel_frac`, default `step_size=0.004` (vs. `ball_difficulty`'s `0.01`) so it structurally lags rather than racing the existing curriculum -- not a derived value, a starting point to tune.
2. `mdp/events.py::reset_ball_rolling`: new `use_far_travel_curriculum: bool = False` parameter. When `True`, the one-sided branch's outer-bound lerp uses `env._far_travel_frac` (via a new `d_y_end` local, defaulting to `1.0` via `getattr` when uninitialized -- matching how `env._ball_difficulty` already defaults for play/eval) instead of `env._ball_difficulty`. Every other dimension (spawn distance, ball speed, reaction time, the two-sided branch, near-region `y_end`) is untouched and still uses `env._ball_difficulty` exactly as before.
3. `mdp/regions.py::reset_ball_rolling_by_region`: passes `use_far_travel_curriculum=True` only for `r in (1, 3)` (`left_far`/`right_far`); near regions unaffected.
4. `tasks/goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py::goalkeeper_multidisc_env_cfg`: registers `cfg.curriculum["far_travel"] = CurriculumTermCfg(func=gk_mdp.far_travel_curriculum, ...)`, gated `if not play:` (matching the base config's own curriculum gating -- `CurriculumManager` is inactive in play mode regardless). Registered here rather than in the shared `goalkeeper_env_cfg()` since `use_far_travel_curriculum` is only ever set by this task's region-conditioned reset.

At `far_travel_frac=0`, the far region's outer bound collapses to its own inner edge (0.5m) -- close to (though still slightly above) genuine single-step reach, a much gentler start than the previous immediate 1.3m. As training progresses, the outer bound widens toward 1.3m independently of how fast `ball_difficulty` itself has already saturated.

**Verification:** 55/55 tests pass (new `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_far_travel_curriculum.py`: curriculum starts at 0, only updates once per window, ramps slower than `ball_difficulty`'s default step_size under identical `cu` history, clips at 1.0, and `reset_ball_rolling` genuinely uses `_far_travel_frac` instead of `_ball_difficulty` for the flagged branch; `test_regions.py` gained a test asserting `use_far_travel_curriculum` is `True` only for `left_far`/`right_far`). Live smoke test (`--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`, throwaway run dir deleted after): `far_travel` appears in the active curriculum term table and logs `Curriculum/far_travel/far_travel_frac`, no errors.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a real training run -- launching fresh, bundled with the far-region AMP dataset fix above (same restart, since both were pending on the same currently-running process). `step_size=0.004` is an unvalidated starting guess; if the far region ramps too slowly (policy never sees the harder end of the range within a reasonable iteration budget) or too fast (races `ball_difficulty` again in practice despite the smaller constant), this is the first parameter to revisit.

## 2026-07-18 (same day, caught ~10 minutes into the run above) -- far_travel_frac's 0.0 default was a degenerate single point, and it exactly coincided with near-region's own maximum -- a direct region_estimator label collision, not just a curriculum-cliff concern

**Context:** user pushed back directly on the just-launched run's design ("far regions start at 0.5 or 0?" then "isn't that bad? look how g1 did it") and separately flagged a second, sharper concern once shown the mechanics ("doesn't this completely destroy the region estimator, do a unbias check").

**Finding 1 -- degenerate start, no G1 precedent:** checked G1's actual jump-region `command_ranges` (`g1_29_config.py` `ranges_2`/`ranges_3`, the only regions requiring extra reach) directly rather than assuming the lerp-from-zero shape was reasonable. Both width and height start at **exactly 66.7%** of their eventual max span (width: `[0,1.0]` init vs `[0,1.5]` max; height: `[1.2,1.6]` init vs `[1.2,1.8]` max) -- G1's curriculum only ever stretches the last third of the range, never starts from nothing. `far_travel_curriculum`'s `env._far_travel_frac` default of `0.0` collapsed `reset_ball_rolling`'s one-sided branch to `inner == outer == lo` -- every far-region episode spawned at the *exact same point* (0.5m), zero variance, a much harsher degenerate start than G1 ever used anywhere.

**Finding 2 -- that degenerate point exactly coincides with near-region's own maximum, confirmed via direct Monte Carlo (20,000 samples) of the real `reset_ball_rolling` code:**

| region | cross_y range (20k samples) | std |
|---|---|---|
| near (`left_near`, ball_difficulty=1) | `[0.130, 0.435]` | 0.088 |
| far, OLD default (`far_travel_frac=0.0`) | `0.435` exactly, every sample | **0.0000** |
| far, NEW default (`far_travel_frac=0.65`) | `[0.435, 0.887]` | 0.130 |

Under the running (unfixed) code, 100% of far-labeled training examples produced a ball trajectory ending at the *exact same value* as near-region's rarest, most extreme edge case (`~3.4%` of near's own density sits within 0.01 of that point) -- not merely a hard classification boundary but a literal label collision: the `region_estimator` (trained on `env._region_id` ground truth, inferring purely from observed ball trajectory history) had zero geometric signal anywhere in its input to separate "near episode landing at the far edge of its own range" from "far episode" during this entire curriculum phase. Same failure class as the 2026-07-06 region_id/y_end decorrelation bug (`docs/BugFixes.md`), caught this time before any real training happened rather than after thousands of iterations of confused `Loss/est_region`.

**Fix:** already applied as part of the same-day `_DEFAULT_INITIAL_FRAC=0.65` correction above -- verified by the same Monte Carlo check: far now spans `[0.435, 0.887]`, cleanly separated from near's `[0.130, 0.435]` with only the single unavoidable touching boundary point (same adjacency every partition of a continuous range has, not a bias source) instead of far's entire mass sitting on it.

**Action:** killed the run that had been training under the biased default (10 minutes in, `model_0.pt` only, no real progress -- no checkpoint push needed per the standing `<250 iterations` exception) and deleted its log directory. Relaunching fresh under the corrected code.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a real training run -- this is the second restart of the same launch attempt, now with three bundled changes (far-region real-mocap AMP clips, far_travel_curriculum decoupling, and the 0.65 initial-frac correction). `Loss/est_region` should be watched specifically on the next check as direct confirmation the fix holds under real training, not just the offline Monte Carlo check.

## 2026-07-18 (same day, ~15 minutes into the run above) -- far_travel_curriculum and footreach's far_scale were both modeled on the wrong G1 region (jump, not step); redesigned to move both range edges

**Context:** user pushed back a third time on the same-day far-region redesign, pointing out G1 has a region literally named "step" (`ranges_4`/`ranges_5`) -- a far more obvious analog for a foot-only project than "jump" (`ranges_2`/`ranges_3`), which both `far_travel_curriculum`'s frozen-inner-bound design and `footreach`'s escalating `far_scale` had been justified against. Dispatched two independent subagents (no shared context, identical framing) to read G1's actual reward and curriculum code from scratch and determine the correct analog. Both converged unanimously.

**Finding -- "step" is the correct analog, "jump" is not:**

| | hand (`ranges_0/1`) | jump (`ranges_2/3`) | **step (`ranges_4/5`)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| `_reward_eereach` axis | lateral (`rigid_body_states[...,8]`) | **vertical** (`[...,9]`) | lateral (`[...,8]`) |
| Scale | flat `1+3.0·v` | **escalating**, `jump_scale=3.0+3.0·cu` | flat `1+3.0·v` |
| `_reward_airfeetorientation`/`_reward_successland` | not gated in | **gated in** (`root_states[:,2]>1.0` -- literal dive) | not gated in |
| height curriculum | grows | grows | **frozen** `[0.1,0.3]` init=max (no height axis) |
| width curriculum | both edges move: `[0.2,1.2]→[0,1.8]` | inner already at its own floor (coincidence) -- only outer moves | both edges move: `[0.2,1.2]→[0,1.8]` (identical to hand) |

Step is grounded, lateral-axis, flat-scale, no airborne/landing mechanic, and its height axis is permanently frozen at ground level -- a mechanical match to this project's foot-only, always-grounded problem on every axis. Jump's escalating scale exists specifically *because* it's paired with a literal dive; it doesn't transfer to a task that can never leave the ground. Jump's frozen inner bound (which the same-day `_DEFAULT_INITIAL_FRAC` fix above was modeled on) turned out to be a coincidence specific to that region -- its width floor (0) happens to already equal its init value (0) -- not a general "freeze the inner edge" principle. Step's actual curriculum moves both edges.

**What changed:**
1. `mdp/events.py::far_travel_curriculum`: redesigned to move BOTH `env._far_inner` and `env._far_outer` (previously only `env._far_travel_frac` drove a single outer-bound lerp with `inner` permanently fixed at `lo`). Seeded using G1 step's own init-fraction-of-span (`inner_frac=0.2/1.8=0.111`, `outer_frac=1.2/1.8=0.667`) applied to this task's own floor/ceiling (`lo=0.5`, the near/far boundary -- a structural constraint G1 doesn't have, since G1 separates regions by height rather than width, so its width curricula are free to shrink toward a true geometric floor of 0; this project's far region can't go below 0.5 without re-colliding with near). Both edges update via the same shared `cu`/EMA signal and step_size each check, mirroring G1's identical `±0.3*cu` for both edges (`legged_robot.py:333-334`). New seeds: `far_inner` starts at `~0.589` (shrinks to `0.5`), `far_outer` starts at `~1.033` (grows to `1.3`) -- coincidentally an even *larger* initial near/far separation than the previous `[0.5,1.02]` single-edge design, since inner no longer starts pinned at the boundary.
2. `mdp/events.py::reset_ball_rolling`: `use_far_travel_curriculum=True` now reads `env._far_inner`/`env._far_outer` directly for both bounds, replacing the single `d_y_end`-based outer-only lerp.
3. `mdp/rewards.py::footreach`: removed the region-conditional `far_scale = 3.0 + 1.5*cu` escalation entirely (added 2026-07-15, itself already a halved version of an original `3.0+3.0*cu` literal jump-scale port). Far regions now use the same flat `3.0` multiplier as every other region, matching step's actual formula. The "far crossings need more incentive than near" premise this multiplier was built on has no support in the correct G1 analog -- step's own difficulty ramp comes entirely from its width curriculum, not an escalating reward multiplier.

**Verification:** 57/57 tests pass (`test_far_travel_curriculum.py` rewritten for the two-edge interface: seeds match G1 step's proportions, both edges move together and clip correctly at `lo`/`hi`, `reset_ball_rolling` reads `_far_inner`/`_far_outer` directly and defaults to the full span when the curriculum hasn't run -- matching `_ball_difficulty`'s own play/eval default). Live smoke test (`--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`, throwaway run dir deleted after): logs `Curriculum/far_travel/far_inner: 0.5889` and `Curriculum/far_travel/far_outer: 1.0333`, exact match to the hand-computed values, no errors.

**Action:** killed the run launched under the jump-modeled design (~15 minutes in, negligible progress, no checkpoint push needed) and relaunched fresh under this redesign.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a real training run -- third restart of the same launch attempt today. This is the first version of the far-region mechanism actually derived from the correct G1 region; prior restarts today were course corrections on an initially-wrong analog. `Loss/est_region` and per-region save rate are the key things to watch on the next check.

## 2026-07-18 (same day, ~15 minutes into the run above) -- near regions had the SAME degenerate-point bug as far, just never caught because it wasn't the day's focus

**Context:** user asked directly "shouldn't we fix left_near 0.15 since it is still a bug" after the step-region comparison table made the pattern visible: `left_near`'s `y_end_range=(0.15,0.5)` uses the generic one-sided branch's `else` case (`inner=lo; outer=lo+(hi-lo)*ball_difficulty`) -- the exact same formula shape `far_travel_curriculum` was built to replace for far. At `ball_difficulty=0` (the very start of every training run, before the curriculum has advanced at all), near's `outer` also collapses to exactly `lo`, producing a single degenerate point with zero variance -- same bug class, same "no G1 region ever starts at a degenerate point" finding, just not yet applied here.

**Key difference from far, checked before fixing:** `0.15` traces back (confirmed via `docs/BugFixes.md`, 2026-07-06 entry) to the two-sided branch's own `_Y_INNER` dead-zone constant -- "mirrors G1's ±0.2m minimum offset... never through the robot's legs" -- reused as near's literal `lo` when the region-conditioned system was built. Unlike far's inner bound (0.5, purely a partition artifact with no independent physical justification, which is why it was correctly given room to start higher and shrink toward 0.5), near's inner is a deliberate leg-clearance minimum and should never move. Only `outer`'s degenerate start needed fixing.

**Fix:** `mdp/events.py::reset_ball_rolling`'s generic one-sided `else` branch (the only remaining caller of which is near regions -- far uses `use_far_travel_curriculum`, the single-disc task always uses the two-sided branch) now seeds `outer` at G1 step's own outer-fraction-of-span (`_G1_STEP_OUTER_FRAC = 1.2/1.8`, promoted to a shared module-level constant alongside `_G1_STEP_INNER_FRAC`, both now referenced by `far_travel_curriculum` too) applied to near's own span, instead of starting at `lo`: `outer_seed = lo + _G1_STEP_OUTER_FRAC*(hi-lo)`, then `outer = outer_seed + (hi-outer_seed)*d`. For `left_near` (`lo=0.15, hi=0.5`): outer now starts at `~0.383` (was `0.15`) at `ball_difficulty=0`, still reaching `0.5` at `ball_difficulty=1`. `inner` stays `lo` unconditionally, unchanged.

**Verification:** Monte Carlo re-check (20k samples, mirroring the far-region check earlier today): near at `ball_difficulty=0` now has `std=0.058` (was `0.0000` under the old formula) with real spread `[0.118,0.337]` (crossing-Y scaled) instead of a single point. 60/60 tests pass (3 new tests: outer is no longer degenerate at `d=0`, inner stays fixed across `d`, outer reaches `hi` at `d=1`). Live smoke test clean, no errors.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a real training run -- bundled into the same restart as the step-region redesign above, since that run was still in its first ~15-20 iterations when this was caught.

## 2026-07-18 (same day, ~300 iterations into the run above) -- far_travel_curriculum's step_size made it finish BEFORE ball_difficulty, not after

**Context:** user asked directly whether far_travel was reaching full extent around the same time as ball_difficulty. It was worse than that -- checked live: at iteration ~296, `far_inner` had covered 47% of its journey (0.589→0.547) while `ball_difficulty` had covered only 12% (0→0.12).

**Root cause:** `far_inner`/`far_outer` share the exact same `cu` signal as `ball_difficulty_curriculum` (same `_update_smoothed_ep_len`, `ep_len_divisor=50`, `update_interval=500`), so their relative speed is set by `(distance to cover) / (step per cu-unit)`, not `step_size` in isolation. The `0.004` default was carried over unchanged from the pre-step-region single-edge design, where `far_travel_frac` had to cover the full `[0,1]` range. After today's step-region redesign seeded `far_inner`/`far_outer` 65-67% of the way to their bounds already, the *remaining* distance is much smaller (0.267m for outer, 0.089m for inner vs. `ball_difficulty`'s full 1.0), so the same step emptied it far faster: outer needed only 83 cu-units to saturate (vs. `ball_difficulty`'s 100), inner only 28 -- finishing first, the opposite of the intended lag.

**Fix:** `step_size` default (both the class default and the explicit registration in `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`) changed `0.004 -> 0.0013`, solved so `far_outer` (the binding constraint, larger gap) needs ~250 cu-units (~2.5x `ball_difficulty`'s 100, matching this class's original "roughly 2.5x slower" intent); `far_inner` now needs ~83 cu-units under the same step (close to `ball_difficulty`'s own pace, not wildly ahead of it).

**Verification:** 61/61 tests pass -- new `test_far_travel_default_step_size_genuinely_lags_ball_difficulty_default` directly asserts `far_outer`'s default-config cu-units exceed `ball_difficulty`'s default-config cu-units, a regression guard against this exact class of bug recurring silently on a future edit. Live smoke test clean.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a real training run -- fifth restart of the same launch attempt today, ~300 iterations of negligible progress lost. `0.0013` targets a 2.5x ratio by direct calculation, not empirical tuning -- flagged as the first parameter to revisit if far-region difficulty still ramps too fast or too slow in practice.

## 2026-07-18 (same day, ~2 minutes into the run above) -- widened the pre-termination warning zone for penalize_kneeheight

**Context:** user asked whether `penalize_kneeheight`'s threshold (not weight) should be raised, reasoning the policy doesn't seem to be learning to avoid the `shank_height` hard termination.

**Finding:** `penalize_kneeheight`'s `min_height` (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py:424`) was `0.29` -- only 1.5cm above `shank_height_termination`'s hard-termination threshold (`0.275`). The penalty itself is graded (`clamp(min_height - shank_z, 0)`, proportional to violation depth, not a step function), but with only 1.5cm of shank travel between "penalty starts" and "episode ends" -- a distance a foot covers almost instantly during a dynamic step or lunge -- the policy had very little runway to feel the ramping penalty and pull back before the episode just terminated.

**Fix:** `penalize_kneeheight`'s `min_height`: `0.29 -> 0.32`, widening the warning zone from 1.5cm to 4.5cm (3x). `shank_height_termination`'s own threshold (`0.275`) is unchanged -- this widens how much advance warning the policy gets, not how deep a crouch is ultimately allowed before the episode ends.

**Verification:** 61/61 tests pass (none hardcoded the old value). Live smoke test (`--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`) clean, term registers and fires, no errors.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a training run -- `0.32` is a starting point (this project has no G1 equivalent for this exact grounded-crouch failure mode, same category as the far-region `vel_sigma`/reward-shape judgment calls elsewhere in this log), not an empirically-derived value. Bundled into the same restart as the `far_travel_curriculum` step_size fix above (that run was only ~2 minutes old).

## 2026-07-19 -- revised the kneeheight thresholds down: 0.32 pulled the warning zone in too far for the deep crouches the far-region task actually needs

**Context:** user judgment call (not a training-run result) -- `0.32` was assessed as too aggressive for the athletic depth genuinely required by the far-region multi-step task (the same day's G1-step-region redesign). Explicit instruction: adjust the thresholds, do not restart the currently-running `green_kneeheightfix_2026-07-18` (still healthy at full curriculum maturity per the same-day health check) -- this is a source change for the *next* run, not applied retroactively to the live one.

**What changed:** `penalize_kneeheight`'s `min_height`: `0.32 -> 0.295`. `shank_height_termination`'s own threshold (previously untouched): `0.275 -> 0.27`. Net effect: both thresholds sit lower overall (more legitimate crouch depth allowed before either fires), while the graded warning gap between them widens slightly from the original 1.5cm to 2.5cm (0.295 vs 0.27) -- smaller than the previous 4.5cm gap (0.32 vs 0.275), but still a real improvement over the original near-zero runway.

**Verification:** 61/61 tests pass. Live smoke test (`--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`) clean, both terms register and fire, no errors.

**Not yet resolved:** not applied to the live run per explicit instruction -- takes effect on the next restart. Neither `0.295`/`0.27` nor the earlier `0.32` have been validated against an actual training run; this remains a judgment call pending evidence either way.

## 2026-07-20 -- reward-weight audit: task reward's raw magnitude had grown to ~3x AMP's, diluting AMP's real influence on the blended objective despite an unchanged nominal blend ratio

**Context:** user asked for a full audit of total reward weights, ours vs G1's, on the theory that the many task rewards added over the project's history (individually necessary, but never re-audited in aggregate) might now be outweighing AMP in practice even though the AMP blend coefficients themselves were never touched.

**Finding, empirically measured (not just weight-counted):** the AMP blend is `reward = (1-lerp)*amp_reward + lerp*task_reward`, `lerp=0.6` (matches G1's `amp_coef=0.4`, confirmed unchanged). Pulled the live run's actual `Train/mean_reward` (~25.5/episode, blended total) and `Train/mean_amp_reward` (~11.4/episode, raw pre-blend AMP signal) and cross-validated against summing every `Episode_Reward/*` term's logged average (already per-second-normalized per this project's own documented TensorBoard convention) -- both paths independently converge on raw task-reward magnitude ≈ 35/episode. So: raw task reward (~35) is ~3x raw AMP reward (~11.4), meaning AMP's REAL share of the blended objective PPO actually optimizes is `0.4×11.4 / (0.4×11.4 + 0.6×35) ≈ 18%`, not the nominal 40% the blend ratio implies. The blend ratio itself was never wrong -- one of its two inputs had grown in scale.

**What changed** (task-reward side only -- AMP coefficients/weights explicitly left untouched per user instruction):
1. `stopball_curriculum`/`softstop_curriculum` base weights halved: `15.0→7.5` / `105.0→52.5` (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`). Combined, these two terms (a historical split of G1's single `stopball=100`, made to give softstop "the clear end goal" -- 2026-06-24 entry) totaled up to 240 at cu=3, 2.4x G1's original single-term budget for the same "did you stop it" signal -- the single largest driver of the magnitude gap.
2. `action_rate_l2`: `-0.3 -> -0.1`, reverted to G1's literal matched weight (`smoothness=-0.1`). Was the largest negative real-magnitude contributor (-3.79/step), a direct 3x divergence from the G1-matched term.
3. `feet_slippage`: `5.0 -> 3.0`, reverted to G1's literal matched weight.
4. `action_acc_l2`: `-0.1 -> -0.05` (halved). No G1 equivalent; real magnitude (-3.43/step) was the 3rd-largest contributor in the whole table despite the small nominal weight -- halved rather than removed since the jerk penalty likely still does real work.
5. `foot_ang_vel_xy`: `-0.5 -> -0.25` (halved). No G1 equivalent; real magnitude (-1.80/step) was meaningful.

**Explicitly NOT changed, with reasoning:** `footreach`/`postorientation`/`feetorientation`/`postangvel` all show elevated real magnitude despite matching G1's nominal weight exactly -- flagged as a likely formula/scale difference under MuJoCo vs PhysX dynamics, not a weight problem; touching their weights would break the literal-G1-match these terms were built to preserve, so left alone pending a separate investigation. The "quality bonus" terms added on top of G1 (`single_foot_save`/`cleanstop`/`inner_face_orientation_save`/`foot_inner_face_continuous`/`foot_proximity`/`foot_clearance`) have large nominal weights but tiny real per-step contribution (~+1.94/step combined, rare one-shot bonuses) -- not touched, already correctly small in practice. `penalize_self_collision` (-50, no G1 equivalent) similarly negligible in practice (-0.02/step) -- left alone.

**Verification:** 63/63 tests pass. Live smoke test (`--num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`) clean -- confirmed all six new values active (`stopball_curriculum`/`softstop_curriculum` weights 7.5/52.5, `foot_ang_vel_xy=-0.25`, `feet_slippage=3.0`, `action_rate_l2=-0.1`, `action_acc_l2=-0.05`), no errors.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a training run -- this reduces raw task-reward magnitude by roughly a third; whether it meaningfully changes the `Loss/est_region` plateau, the far-region footwork behavior, or anything else observable requires a fresh run to see.

## 2026-07-20 -- discriminator-probe finding: AMP style reward collapsed to its floor within the first ~2000 iterations, in every region, and never recovered

**Context:** user reported that even after the reward-weight audit and G1-step-region redesign above, `--force-region` visual inspection still showed one continuous lunge on far balls (and an oddly-ordered "save first, double-step after" on near balls). Rather than continue adjusting reward weights, ran a direct probe of the trained discriminators themselves (not the aggregate TensorBoard loss, the actual per-sample `d`/`amp_reward` outputs) to check whether AMP was trying to penalize the exaggerated motion and losing to task reward, or wasn't detecting it as anomalous at all.

**Finding, measured directly from checkpoints across this run's history (`green_rewardaudit_2026-07-20`, `model_500` through `model_12000`):** at `model_500`, real mocap and live rollout samples (both a near-default early-episode frame and an exaggerated peak-hip-roll frame) all scored close to the discriminator's reward ceiling (~0.43-0.50 out of max ~0.50) -- the discriminator was still too lenient to differentiate anything. By `model_2000` (roughly the first 15-17% of a 12000-iteration run), every live rollout sample -- regardless of whether it looked like a benign standing pose or a wildly exaggerated ~1.2-1.3 rad hip-roll lean (vs ~0.40-0.42 rad in the real reference clips) -- had collapsed to the discriminator's reward floor (~0.01-0.12), and stayed there through `model_12000`. The transitional window where the discriminator could have given genuine graded feedback ("this approach looks more/less natural than that one") closed almost immediately and never reopened.

**Root-cause assessment:** not a coding bug -- weight_decay (`10e-4` trunk / `10e-2` head), grad-penalty coefficient (`lambda_=5 * 0.1` = effective 0.5), `num_learning_epochs=5`, `num_mini_batches=4` all independently verified to match `Humanoid-Goalkeeper/rsl_rl/rsl_rl/algorithms/him_ppo.py` exactly. This is a real AMP/GAN dynamic (discriminator races ahead of the generator) that G1 can tolerate because its regions are single atomic actions needing only coarse style discrimination; this project's compositional double-step target needs a much longer-lived, finer-grained discriminator signal than the same recipe naturally provides. Likely aggravating factor: each of the 4 region discriminators only ever trained on `num_envs/4` = 1024 samples per batch at the previous `--num-envs 4096`, a smaller/less-diverse batch that sharpens (and saturates) faster than a larger one would -- already flagged as an open risk in the original multi-disc design spec.

**What changed:** `--num-envs 4096 -> 6144`, matching G1's actual value (`legged_gym/legged_gym/envs/base/legged_robot_config.py:35` and `g1_29_config.py:5`, both `6144`) -- raises each region discriminator's per-batch sample count from 1024 to 1536 (G1's own 6-region split gives it 1024/discriminator, so this is deliberately *higher* than G1's per-discriminator batch, not just matched, since our 4-region split needs more per-region samples than G1's 6-region split to reach the same total). Pure launch-flag change, no source edit. VRAM headroom confirmed (previous run: ~5.4GB/23GB at 4096 envs; new run: ~7.7GB/23GB at 6144 envs after first iteration).

**Verification:** live smoke test via the actual restart -- first iteration completed cleanly (6.35s, 147456 timesteps, no NaN/Inf, all reward/termination/curriculum terms logging as expected).

**Not yet resolved:** this is a live experiment (`green_numenvs6144_2026-07-20`), not a confirmed fix -- needs the same discriminator-probe methodology re-run against a future checkpoint from this run to see whether the AMP-reward floor collapse is delayed/avoided with the larger per-discriminator batch, and ultimately needs `--force-region` visual inspection to see whether genuine double-stepping emerges. If this doesn't help, the next candidates are a discriminator-specific (lower) learning rate to slow the collapse directly, or abandoning AMP-driven double-step shaping in favor of an explicit task-reward term for a second distinct foot-contact event.

## 2026-07-20 -- bad_orientation termination used mjlab's built-in arccos formula, a different physical quantity than G1's XY-norm formula, not just a different threshold

**Context:** item 19 of a prior 5-way audit against `Humanoid-Goalkeeper`. Re-verified by reading both sides directly before implementing.

**Finding, confirmed by direct code read:** G1's `check_termination` (`Humanoid-Goalkeeper/legged_gym/legged_gym/envs/base/legged_robot.py:257`) computes `self.gravity_termination_buf = torch.any(torch.norm(self.projected_gravity[:, 0:2], dim=-1, keepdim=True) > 0.8, dim=1)` -- the XY-plane norm of `projected_gravity`, a sine-of-tilt-angle proxy, fixed threshold `0.8` (hardcoded in `legged_robot.py`, not a config value -- the audit's pointer to `g1_29_config.py:180` was actually the `upper_body_link = "pelvis"` setting, not the threshold itself). `projected_gravity` itself (`legged_robot.py:190,904`) is `quat_rotate_inverse(self.rigid_body_states[:, self.upper_body_index, 3:7], self.gravity_vec)`, where `upper_body_index` resolves to the `"pelvis"` link (`g1_29_config.py:180`) -- G1's floating-base/root link.

SGK wired `bad_orientation` (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py:634-637`, prior to this fix) to mjlab's built-in `mjlab.envs.mdp.terminations.bad_orientation` (`.venv/.../mjlab/envs/mdp/terminations.py:24-32`): `torch.acos(-projected_gravity[:, 2]).abs() > limit_angle` with `limit_angle=1.0`. This is the true tilt angle via arccos of the Z component, a genuinely different formula from G1's XY-norm -- not just a different threshold, even though the effective angles are numerically close (0.8 XY-norm -> ~53.1 deg vs. 1.0 rad -> ~57.3 deg).

`projected_gravity_b` for T1 (mjlab `EntityData.projected_gravity_b`, `entity/data.py:584-586`) is `quat_apply_inverse(self.root_link_quat_w, self.gravity_vec_w)` -- sourced from the free-joint root body's orientation, the T1 equivalent of G1's `"pelvis"` (also G1's floating-base/root link). This is already the same frame this project uses for `projected_gravity_b` elsewhere (`mdp/rewards.py`: `feetorientation`, `postorientation`), so no body/frame remapping was needed -- only the formula.

**Fix:** added `bad_orientation_xy_norm(env, limit=0.8, asset_cfg)` to `src/simple_goalkeeper/mdp/events.py` (next to the project's other custom terminations, `sharpforce_termination`/`shank_height_termination`), computing `torch.norm(robot.data.projected_gravity_b[:, :2], dim=-1) > limit` -- G1's exact formula and threshold. Exported via `mdp/__init__.py`. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py:634-639`'s `bad_orientation` termination now points at `gk_mdp.bad_orientation_xy_norm` with `params={"limit": 0.8, "asset_cfg": _ROBOT_CFG}`, replacing `mjlab_mdp.bad_orientation`/`limit_angle=1.0`. Applies to both training and play configs (single `goalkeeper_env_cfg(play: bool)` function builds both).

**Verification:** no existing test file matches `-k "termination or orientation"` (0 collected). Standalone check: imported `bad_orientation_xy_norm` via both its defining module and the `gk_mdp` re-export, confirmed identity; ran it against synthetic `projected_gravity_b` tensors -- upright (`[0,0,-1]`) -> `False`; exactly at the threshold boundary (`norm=0.8`, strict `>`) -> `False`; just past threshold (`norm=0.9`) -> `True`; 2-env batch (`[upright, past-threshold]`) -> `[False, True]` -- all as expected. Also built the full env cfg via `simple_goalkeeper.tasks.goalkeeper_env_cfg.goalkeeper_env_cfg(play=False)` and `(play=True)`: `terminations["bad_orientation"].func` resolves to `bad_orientation_xy_norm`, `params={"limit": 0.8, "asset_cfg": SceneEntityCfg(name="robot", ...)}` in both.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a training run -- the two formulas' effective thresholds are numerically close (~53 deg vs ~57 deg) so no large behavioral shift is expected, but this has not been confirmed empirically.

## 2026-07-20 -- AMP subsystem audit (items 1-3): reward-smoothing item was already fixed under a different (unused-duplicate) file; motion-transition speed jitter and normalizer half-update were real and fixed

**Context:** items 1-3 of a prior 5-way audit against `Humanoid-Goalkeeper`, assigned as an AMP-subsystem batch. Re-verified each by reading the actual current G1 and SGK code directly rather than trusting the prior audit's summary.

**Item 1 (AMP style-reward smoothing) -- NOT CONFIRMED, already fixed in the code path that's actually wired in.** The audit's pointer, `beyondAMP/source/beyondAMP/beyondAMP/modules/amp_discriminator.py:52-65`, does genuinely still have a raw single-sample `predict_amp_reward` with no noise smoothing. But `multi_disc_amp_ppo.py:16` and `him_amp_on_policy_runner.py:31` both import `AMPDiscriminator` from `rsl_rl_amp.modules.amp_discriminator` (a different package under `beyondAMP/source/rsl_rl_amp/`), not from the `beyondAMP.modules.amp_discriminator` the audit pointed at. That actually-used file's `predict_amp_reward` (`rsl_rl_amp/rsl_rl_amp/modules/amp_discriminator.py:75-111`) already ports G1's exact mechanism -- `num_samples=20` Gaussian noise draws, `sigma=0.3`, min-squared-error-from-1 selection, `clamp(1-0.25*err, min=0)` -- dated 2026-07-08 (see that day's "AMP discriminator init/reward-smoothing divergences found and fixed" entry above, ~line 295). The `beyondAMP.modules.amp_discriminator` copy is dead code as far as this project's multi-disc training path is concerned; left untouched (out of scope -- not imported by anything under `src/simple_goalkeeper/`, and per this batch's instructions, only implement confirmed items). No change made.

**Item 2 (zero playback-speed diversity in AMP transitions) -- CONFIRMED, fixed.** G1's `get_expert_obs` (`Humanoid-Goalkeeper/legged_gym/legged_gym/envs/g1/g1_utils.py:158-190`) samples a base integer frame, then for each subsequent frame computes `next_pos = motion_ids + i*ratio` with `ratio = (self.fps/self.env_fps) * U(0.25,1.25)` (line 174-175), floor/ceil-interpolating between `self.motion_dof_pos[floor]` and `[ceil]` (lines 179-187) -- so the "next" frame used in a transition is a randomized, sub-frame-interpolated position, not the literal adjacent NPZ frame. SGK's `MotionDataset._build_transition_indices` (`beyondAMP/source/beyondAMP/beyondAMP/motion/motion_dataset.py`, pre-fix lines 304-323) built a fixed table of `(t, t+1)` integer pairs, and `build_transition` (pre-fix lines 350-356) read `getattr(self, term)[tp1]` literally -- deterministic, zero speed jitter, every transition always exactly 1/50s of motion regardless of NPZ fps.

**Fix:** added per-frame trajectory metadata (`self._frame_fps`, `self._frame_traj_max_idx`) built in `load_motions()`, and an `env_fps` property (`1.0/self.env.step_dt` when available, else G1's literal hardcoded `50.0` fallback for envs/test doubles without `step_dt`). Rewrote `build_transition(t, tp1)`: `t` (the base frame, chosen by whichever `sample_batch` is active -- uniform in the base class, per-motion-weighted in `WeightedMotionDataset`, unchanged either way) stays an exact integer lookup exactly like G1's un-interpolated first frame; the second half is now `next_pos = t + (fps/env_fps)*U(0.25,1.25)`, floor/ceil-interpolated. Deliberate small adaptation vs. G1: G1's floor/ceil clamp is to the whole concatenated dataset's global bound (`g1_utils.py:182-184`), which can blend two unrelated motion files' frames near a trajectory tail; SGK clamps to the sampled frame's own trajectory bound (`self._frame_traj_max_idx[t]`) instead, since that information was cheap to precompute here and avoids cross-motion contamination without changing the sampled distribution anywhere in a trajectory's interior. This composes with `WeightedMotionDataset` unchanged: that subclass only overrides `sample_batch` (which transition to draw, still respecting per-motion weights) and inherits `build_transition` as-is, so the weighted-motion feature and the new speed jitter are fully independent and don't interact. `beyondAMP/source/beyondAMP/beyondAMP/motion/motion_dataset.py`.

**Item 3 (AMP normalizer only updates on half the transition) -- CONFIRMED, fixed.** G1's normalizer update (`Humanoid-Goalkeeper/rsl_rl/rsl_rl/algorithms/him_ppo.py:304-305`) feeds the FULL concatenated `[state, next_state]` vector (`amp_obs_batch`/`amp_expert_obs_batch`, already double-width since G1's `get_expert_obs`/rollout `amp_obs` are single per-step vectors representing both frames) into `.update()` every step. SGK's `MultiDiscAMPPPO.update` (`src/simple_goalkeeper/rsl_rl_multi/multi_disc_amp_ppo.py`, pre-fix lines 361-364) called `self.amp_normalizer.update(policy_state...)` / `.update(expert_state...)` -- only the "t" half of each transition -- despite normalizing and using `policy_next_state`/`expert_next_state` for the actual discriminator forward pass two lines earlier (lines 314-316). SGK's normalizer operates in single-frame-width space (called separately per half, unlike G1's double-width normalizer), so the correct analog of "feed the full transition" here is updating with BOTH halves rather than doubling the vector width.

**Fix:** `normalizer_states.append(...)` now stores `(policy_state, policy_next_state, expert_state, expert_next_state)` (was `(policy_state, expert_state)`), and the update loop calls `.update()` on all four. `multi_disc_amp_ppo.py`.

**Evidence:** 17/17 relevant tests pass (`uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -k "amp or motion" -v`). Manual smoke test (not covered by existing unit tests, since none exercised `build_transition`'s numeric output): built a synthetic 20-frame motion with `joint_pos` incrementing linearly per frame (so "frames advanced" between `t` and the interpolated next state is directly measurable), confirmed `env_fps` derives correctly (`step_dt=0.02` -> `50.0`), and that 2000 sampled transitions' effective frames-advanced spanned `[0.2500, 1.2485]`, matching the `U(0.25,1.25)` target range with no clipping/bias and no cross-trajectory contamination when two motions with different original fps (30 and 50) were used together with `WeightedMotionDataset`. `test_update_refreshes_amp_normalizer_running_stats` (existing test, still passes) confirms the normalizer's `count`/`mean`/`var` move off their init values after `update()` runs with the widened set of calls.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a training run -- both fixes change the AMP discriminator's effective training signal (transition diversity, normalizer statistics), and this project's most recent live finding (`green_rewardaudit_2026-07-20`, entry above) shows the discriminator subsystem is already under active investigation for a separate style-reward-collapse issue; whether these two fixes help, hurt, or are neutral for that needs a fresh checkpoint comparison.

## 2026-07-20 -- curriculum/RSI audit (items 12-16): EMA double-application, hard/soft limit mixup, and an over-broad ball_difficulty coupling, all confirmed and fixed; item 14's specific y_end claim confirmed WRONG (it's a match, not a divergence)

**Context:** items 12-16 of a prior 5-way audit against `Humanoid-Goalkeeper`. Re-verified each by reading the actual current G1 and SGK code directly. Item 14 was explicitly flagged by the user as suspicious ("this should be how g1 does it with ball difficulty changing y end range") and re-derived from scratch rather than trusting the prior audit's framing.

**Item 12 (EMA curriculum smoothing applied multiple times per window) -- CONFIRMED, fixed.** `CurriculumManager.compute()` (`mjlab/managers/curriculum_manager.py:112-117`) calls every registered curriculum term in one loop, same `env_ids`, every `_reset_idx`. All curriculum term classes in `events.py` (`ball_difficulty_curriculum`, 3x `reward_curriculum_ep_len` [softstop/footreach/stopball], plus `far_travel_curriculum` on the multi-disc task) share `update_interval=500` and self-synchronize to become eligible on the exact same `common_step_counter`, so all 4 (single-disc) or 5 (multi-disc) instances independently called `_update_smoothed_ep_len` with the identical `mean_ep_len` reading in the same window. Applying the alpha=0.3 EMA update k times in a row with the same input gives effective weight `1-(1-0.3)^k` on the newest reading instead of 30% -- k=4: 76%, k=5: 83% -- defeating the function's whole purpose (damping single-window noise, the 2026-07-10 fix above). **Fix:** `_update_smoothed_ep_len` (the single function every curriculum term already funnels through) now guards its own update with a shared `env._last_ema_update_step`: only the first call in a given `common_step_counter` actually applies the EMA step; later calls in the same window just return the already-updated cached value. Chosen over gating in each term class since this function was already the single shared choke point. `mdp/events.py:_update_smoothed_ep_len`.

**Item 13 (reset joint-position clip uses hard limits instead of soft limits) -- CONFIRMED, fixed.** G1's `_reset_dofs` (`legged_robot.py:657-682`) clips to `self.dof_pos_limits`, but `_process_dof_props` (`legged_robot.py:545-563`) overwrites `self.dof_pos_limits` in place to the SOFT bound (`mean +/- 0.5*range*cfg.rewards.soft_dof_pos_limit`, `soft_dof_pos_limit=0.9`, `g1_29_config.py:348`) before `_reset_dofs` ever runs -- the untouched hard values live separately in `self.hard_dof_pos_limits`, which `_reset_dofs` never reads. So G1's reset clip is soft (90% of URDF range) despite the variable name. SGK's `MotionResetManager.reset()` else-branch (`mdp/events.py`) clipped to `robot.data.joint_pos_limits`, confirmed via `mjlab/entity/entity.py:695-727` to be the HARD field (`mj_model.jnt_range`, no scaling) -- distinct from `soft_joint_pos_limits` (`joint_pos_mean +/- 0.5*joint_pos_range*soft_joint_pos_limit_factor`). `t1_constants.py:112` already sets `soft_joint_pos_limit_factor=0.9`, the same factor G1 uses, so `soft_joint_pos_limits` is the exact equivalent of G1's actual reset-time bound. `_write_rsi_state` (the tier-pool code, item 15) already used the correct soft field; only the live `reset()` else-branch had the mixup. Both the code comment and CLAUDE.md's divergence table's claim that this matches G1's "hard" limits were wrong. **Fix:** changed the else-branch clip to `robot.data.soft_joint_pos_limits`. `mdp/events.py:MotionResetManager.reset`.

**Item 14 (ball-difficulty curriculum coupling distance/speed to the target window) -- PARTIALLY CONFIRMED. The prior audit's headline claim (G1 never curriculum-scales spawn distance or reaction time) is correct; its specific claim that SGK's `y_end_range` coupling is a divergence is WRONG -- confirmed as a genuine match, exactly as the user suspected.** Full reasoning table, derived from `Humanoid-Goalkeeper/legged_gym/legged_gym/envs/base/legged_robot.py`:

| Parameter | G1 mechanism | G1 curriculum-scaled? | SGK (`reset_ball_rolling`, the only function actually wired into any task cfg) | SGK curriculum-scaled (pre-fix)? | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Spawn distance (X) | `ball_start_local[:,0] = 2.0*rand()+3.0` (`legged_robot.py:779`), hardcoded constant, no config field | NO | `dist_range` lerped `_EASY_DIST_R->dist_range` via `d` | YES | **DIVERGENCE -- fixed** |
| Spawn lateral offset (Y) | `ball_start_local[:,1]` samples `self.init_ranges[0:2]` (`:780`), set ONCE at init from config `maxw` bounds (`:960-963`), never reassigned by the curriculum update (`:325-336` only ever writes `self.command_ranges`) | NO | `y_start_range` lerped `_EASY_Y_ROLL->y_start_range` via `d` | YES | **DIVERGENCE -- fixed** |
| Reaction time / flight duration | `t_flight = 0.4+0.6*rand()` (`:803`; play override `:806` also hardcoded) | NO | `t_flight_range` lerped `_EASY_T_FLIGHT_R->t_flight_range` via `d` | YES | **DIVERGENCE -- fixed** |
| Target Y / crossing window ("y_end") | `ball_end_local[:,1]` samples `self.command_ranges[:,0:2]` (`:786`), which IS the curriculum accumulator (`:333-334`, region-specific `maxw` bound) | **YES** | `y_end_range` curriculum-scaled (two-sided dead-zone shrink, one-sided outer-seed lerp, or `far_travel_curriculum`'s decoupled `_far_inner`/`_far_outer`) | **YES** | **MATCH -- user's suspicion confirmed correct, no change** |
| Target Z / arrival height | `ball_end_local[:,2]` samples `self.command_ranges[:,2:4]` (`:787`), also curriculum-scaled (`:335-336`) | YES (mechanism exists; G1's own foot-analog STEP region ranges_4/5 freezes height `[0.1,0.3]` both init/max, i.e. no growth in practice for the foot-comparable region) | Not a range at all -- `reset_ball_rolling` uses a single fixed `spawn_z` scalar (ground-rolling design, `vz=0`) | NO | Not applicable -- SGK has no Z-target axis by design (foot-only, ground-rolling); consistent with G1's own foot-region height being frozen too. No change. |
| Ball speed | Derived: `ball_vel = delta_pos/t_flight` (`:810-813`) | Indirect only (delta_pos includes the curriculum-scaled Y/Z target; X-distance and t_flight are fixed) | Derived: `dx/t_flight`, same structure | Indirect, same as G1, once distance/t_flight stop being separately curriculum-lerped | Was doubly-indirect (curriculum on top of curriculum) pre-fix; now matches G1's single level of indirection | 

**Fix:** in `reset_ball_rolling` (`mdp/events.py`), removed the `_lerp_range` calls for `dist_r`/`y_start_r`/`t_flight_r` -- these three are now sampled directly from the full configured range on every call, matching G1's "fixed constant from iteration 0" behavior. `d` (`env._ball_difficulty`) is still computed and still drives the `y_end` branches exactly as before (unchanged, confirmed correct). `reset_ball_local_frame`/`reset_ball_global_frame` (dead code -- grepped, zero references outside their own definitions, not wired into any task cfg) still lerp all their spawn params the old way; left untouched, out of scope for a fix to unreferenced code. Training/play parity holds automatically since the fix is inside the shared function body, not per-cfg params (both `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s train and play `reset_ball` registrations, and the multi-disc task's `reset_ball_rolling_by_region`, call the same `reset_ball_rolling`).

**Item 15 (dead NPZ tier-pool RSI code vs. stale CLAUDE.md description) -- CONFIRMED, doc-only fix.** `MotionResetManager.reset()` (the method registered as `reset_from_motion_data`, the actual training-time RSI event) never references `self.pools` or `_write_rsi_state` -- confirmed by reading the method body directly. `scripts/play_rsi_doublestep.py` (registered as the live `sgk_play_rsi` console script in `pyproject.toml:45`, added 2026-07-02, not stale) genuinely calls `mgr._write_rsi_state(...)` and reads `mgr.pools` directly (`play_rsi_doublestep.py:101,115`) to lock playback to a specific motion-tier pool for visual inspection -- so the tier-pool code is dead for training but alive for this diagnostic. Given it's still in active use, deletion was rejected per the task's own conservative-fix guidance. CLAUDE.md's "Divergences from G1 Upstream" table's first row ("Robot reset (RSI)") described the tier-pool/`_write_rsi_state` mechanism (`body_pos_w`/`body_quat_w`/`body_lin_vel_w`/`body_ang_vel_w` loaded from NPZ, "Mirrors BoosterT1mjlab `_write_reset_state` exactly") as if it were the current training behavior, contradicting the later, correct "RSI mechanism (`reset_from_motion_data`)" row in the same table (continue_keep-style donor copy, root untouched). **Fix:** added a clarifying note to the first row in `CLAUDE.md` marking it as the legacy mechanism, kept only for the `sgk_play_rsi` diagnostic, and pointing to the correct row.

**Item 16 (curriculum fires on iteration 1 instead of waiting a full window) -- CONFIRMED, fixed.** G1 requires `common_step_counter > 500` before its first curriculum update (`legged_robot.py:325`, with `last_step_counter=0` at init, `legged_robot.py:881`) -- a full window must elapse before the first fire. SGK's curriculum term classes (`reward_curriculum_ep_len`, `ball_difficulty_curriculum`, `far_travel_curriculum`) all initialized `self._last_update = -(self._update_interval)`, which makes `common_step_counter - self._last_update >= update_interval` true immediately at `common_step_counter=0` -- eligible to fire on the very first `compute()` call, using whatever noisy/near-zero episode-length reading exists that early. **Fix:** changed all three initializations to `self._last_update = 0`, matching G1's `last_step_counter=0` semantics -- first eligible fire is now `common_step_counter >= update_interval`. `mdp/events.py`: `reward_curriculum_ep_len.__init__`, `ball_difficulty_curriculum.__init__`, `far_travel_curriculum.__init__`.

**Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -k "curriculum or rsi or travel or reset" -v` -- 35/35 pass. Two pre-existing tests in `test_live_rsi.py` (`test_reset_rsi_fraction_0_randomizes_around_default_pose_and_clips_to_hard_limits`, `test_reset_default_pose_branch_never_reads_soft_joint_pos_limits`) encoded the pre-fix (incorrect) hard-limit assumption and were updated to assert the corrected soft-limit behavior (renamed accordingly; `_make_env` gained a `soft_bound` parameter, and a new `_make_env_poisoned_hard_limits`/`_PoisonedHardLimitsData` fixture pair mirrors the existing poisoned-soft-limits one, flipped, to confirm the default-pose branch now genuinely never reads the hard field).

**Not yet resolved:** none of items 12/13/14/16 have been validated against a live training run yet -- item 14 in particular changes the effective early-training difficulty curve materially (distance/lateral-offset/reaction-time are now full-range from iteration 0 instead of ramping in), which is a real behavioral change worth watching in the next run's early episode-length trend, not just a no-op bug fix. Item 12/16 should mainly reduce curriculum noise/jitter, not change its long-run destination. Item 13 only affects the default-pose (20%) RSI branch's joint-position spread at reset.

## 2026-07-20 -- task-reward subsystem audit (items 4-11): re-verified against G1, fixed six confirmed divergences, ported one missing G1 term, corrected two stale docs

**Context:** items 4-11 of a prior 5-way audit against `Humanoid-Goalkeeper`, assigned as the task-reward-subsystem batch. Each item re-verified by reading the actual current G1 (`legged_robot.py`/`g1_29_config.py`) and SGK (`rewards.py`/`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) code directly rather than trusting the prior audit's summaries.

**Item 4 (action_rate_l2/action_acc_l2 weight mix-up) -- CONFIRMED, fixed.** Read mjlab's actual implementations (`mjlab/envs/mdp/rewards.py:58-78`): `action_rate_l2` is `action - prev_action` (FIRST order), `action_acc_l2` is `action - 2*prev_action + prev_prev_action` (SECOND order) -- algebraically identical to G1's `_reward_smoothness` (`legged_robot.py:1532-1534`: `actions - last_actions - last_actions + last_last_actions`), weight `-0.1` (`g1_29_config.py:325`). The 2026-07-20 weight-audit had assigned G1's matched `-0.1` to `action_rate_l2` (wrong term) and left `action_acc_l2` at `-0.05`, commented "no G1 equivalent" (also wrong). Swapped: `action_acc_l2` now `-0.1` (G1's literal match), `action_rate_l2` now `-0.05` (no G1 equivalent; halved from the mistaken `-0.1`, following the same day's halving treatment of other no-G1-equivalent terms with elevated real magnitude, e.g. `foot_ang_vel_xy`). `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`.

**Item 5 (ang_vel_xy drifted 5x from G1) -- CONFIRMED, fixed.** `git log -p` on `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` shows `ang_vel_xy` was `-0.1` (matching G1's `-0.1`, `g1_29_config.py:323`) from this project's inception, changed to `-0.5` in commit `ee7eb04` ("stronger body roll/pitch penalty") -- an ad hoc training tweak with no G1-based justification, not a documented divergence. The 2026-07-20 weight-audit touched many other terms but left this one at `-0.5`; CLAUDE.md's own Reward Design table kept `-0.1` the whole time, confirming the doc was never updated to match the accidental drift. Reverted to G1's literal `-0.1`. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`.

**Item 6 (feet_slippage kernel 5x steeper than G1) -- CONFIRMED, fixed.** G1's `_reward_feet_slippage` (`legged_robot.py:1469-1473`) is `exp(-10*contactvel)`. SGK's `feet_slippage` (`rewards.py`) has used `exp(-50.0*contactvel)` since this function's very first commit (`c2b8b69`) -- never changed since, so this was a porting error, not later drift. Notably, the function's own docstring already claimed "Mirrors upstream _reward_feet_slippage: exp(-10 * sum(...))", directly contradicting its own code -- a doc/code mismatch, not a documented divergence. Reverted the coefficient to G1's literal `-10.0`. `rewards.py:feet_slippage`.

**Item 7 (dof_vel_limits flat threshold vs per-joint) -- CONFIRMED, fixed, per real hardware specs.** Checked the actual T1 MJCF first, per the user's explicit instruction: grepped `xmls/t1_headless.xml` and `xmls/t1.xml` for `<actuator>`/`<general>`/velocity attributes -- neither file defines any actuator block or joint velocity limit at all; joints only carry a position `range`. MuJoCo does not require or enforce a joint velocity limit on its own, so there is no XML-sourced value to read. Found the correct alternative already in the codebase: `t1_constants.py` defines five `ElectricActuator` instances (ARM/WAIST_HIP_ROLL_YAW/HIP_PITCH/KNEE/ANKLE) each with a real `velocity_limit` field (rated RPM converted to rad/s via `_rpm()`) sourced from actual T1 motor specs -- already used for `action_scale`/kp/kd computation, but the `velocity_limit` field itself was never forwarded into any `BuiltinPositionActuatorCfg` or consumed anywhere (confirmed via `grep -rn velocity_limit src/`: only its own definition, no other reference). These are real, per-joint-group values, not guesses: ARM ~9.32 rad/s, WAIST/HIP_ROLL/HIP_YAW ~7.33 rad/s, HIP_PITCH ~16.44 rad/s, KNEE ~14.66 rad/s, ANKLE ~12.25 rad/s -- the flat 10 rad/s cap SGK used previously silently never fired for the two slowest groups (arms, waist/hip-roll/hip-yaw), which sit below 10.

**Fix:** added `_T1_VEL_LIMIT_MAP` (`rewards.py`, mirrors the existing `_T1_KP_MAP`/`_T1_EFFORT_MAP` per-joint-dict pattern, importing the `ElectricActuator` objects directly from `t1_constants.py` as the single source of truth rather than re-hardcoding the rad/s numbers). Rewrote `dof_vel_limits` to use per-joint limits x 0.9 (`soft_factor`, matching G1's `soft_dof_vel_limit=0.9`, `g1_29_config.py:349`) instead of the flat `vel_threshold=10.0` parameter (removed). Also switched the excess kernel from squared (`excess.pow(2).sum(-1)`, SGK's prior form) to G1's literal linear form (`legged_robot.py:1550-1553`: `sum(clip(|dof_vel| - limits*0.9, min=0))`) while already rewriting this function to match G1's mechanism exactly -- not separately flagged by the audit item, but directly visible in the G1 code being matched, so fixed alongside the per-joint sourcing rather than left as a second undocumented divergence. `rewards.py:dof_vel_limits`, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` (`dof_vel_limits` params: removed `vel_threshold`).

**Item 8 (reward-surface peak around the save event still exceeds G1's ceiling) -- CONFIRMED, fixed with an exact proportional cap.** Computed the actual combined peak (all six terms at max curriculum, cu=3) from the current post-2026-07-20-halving code:

| Term | curriculum base (pre-item-8) | multiplier @ cu=3 | peak (pre-item-8) |
|---|---|---|---|
| stopball | 7.5 | x2.5 | 18.75 |
| softstop | 52.5 | x2.5 | 131.25 |
| single_foot_save | 50.0 | x2.0 | 100.0 |
| cleanstop | 25.0 | x2.0 | 50.0 |
| inner_face_orientation_save | 25.0 | x2.0 | 50.0 |
| foot_inner_face_continuous | 5.0 | x2.0 | 10.0 |
| **Sum** | | | **360.0** |

360.0 vs G1's ceiling of 250 (`_reward_stopball`, curriculum-scaled `100 -> 250`, `stopball=100` at `g1_29_config.py:301`, same `x2.5` formula at cu=3) -- 1.44x over, confirming the item even after the same-day halving pass. Scaled the whole group down by `250/360 = 25/36 = 0.694444...` (chosen so the new peak lands on exactly 250, not just under it), preserving each term's relative share:

| Term | new curriculum base | new peak (@cu=3) |
|---|---|---|
| stopball | 5.21 | 13.02 |
| softstop | 36.46 | 91.15 |
| single_foot_save | 34.72 | 69.44 |
| cleanstop | 17.36 | 34.72 |
| inner_face_orientation_save | 17.36 | 34.72 |
| foot_inner_face_continuous | 3.47 | 6.94 |
| **Sum** | | **250.0** (13.02+91.15+69.44+34.72+34.72+6.94) |

**Fix:** updated the six `base_weight` params in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s curriculum registrations (`softstop_curriculum`, `stopball_curriculum`, and the `single_foot_save`/`cleanstop`/`inner_face_orientation_save`/`foot_inner_face_continuous` loop). Also synced the static `weight=` values declared in `cfg.rewards` for `stopball`/`softstop` (previously stale pre-halving leftovers, `15.0`/`105.0`, that no longer matched their own curriculum's base_weight at all -- these are the values play mode actually uses, since curriculum is only registered `if not play`) and for the four quality bonuses (previously already matching their curriculum base, kept matching). Training/Play Parity Rule: both training (via curriculum, which overwrites the static weight almost immediately after the first reset) and play (static weight only, no curriculum) now agree at the cu=0 value. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`.

**Item 9 (postorientation gating) -- NOT CONFIRMED as a bug; doc-only fix.** G1's `_reward_postorientation` (`legged_robot.py:1480-1484`) is gated by `behind`. SGK's `postorientation` (`rewards.py`) is unconditional, but its docstring already contains a specific, evidence-based justification: "AMP only sees joint_pos/joint_vel, not root orientation, so it cannot push the root upright. Gating on ball-is-behind means no upright signal during ball approach (80% of episode), causing the policy to drift into backward lean." Per this project's own Design Rule (divergence is legitimate when explicitly justified), left the code unchanged. **Fixed instead:** CLAUDE.md's Reward Design table row for `postorientation`, which incorrectly said "active only when ball is behind" -- updated to describe the actual always-active behavior and the reason.

**Item 10 (penalize_sharpcontact threshold: doc-only) -- confirmed code is correct, docs were out of sync.** Code (`rewards.py`/`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) has used `1700.0` N since this project's first commit (`c2b8b69`); no in-project history of a change from 1000/1200. Traced the value's origin to the sibling `SimpleGoalKeeper/` project's own git history: `1000 -> 1200` (2026-06-20, "1000N too sensitive, penalized normal lateral stepping") `-> 1350 -> 1450 -> 1700` (2026-06-20, commit `b56d75a`: "raises the bar further to avoid penalising aggressive lateral steps during saves"), each step raised because the previous still fired during legitimate save motion. **Fixed:** CLAUDE.md's "Reward Design" table (was `1000 N`) and "Divergences from G1 Upstream" table (was `1200 N`, a stale earlier FIX entry) both updated to `1700 N`, with the progression above folded into the divergence-table rationale. No code change (per explicit instruction: the user confirmed 1700N is correct).

**Item 11 (ported G1's `success` reward) -- CONFIRMED missing, implemented.** G1's `_reward_success` (`legged_robot.py:1402-1403`: `(success_flag + 1.0) * (dist < strict_th)`) is a continuing, doubled-after-first-save, close-to-target signal: `success_flag` is set once inside `_reward_stopball` on the same delta-vx deflection event that fires `stopball` itself (`legged_robot.py:1411-1413`) and persists until reset; `dist` is a continuously-updated hand-to-`end_target` distance recomputed every physics step, NOT gated on ball-behind (`post_physics_step` lines 203-223) -- the hand keeps earning reward for staying near the target after the save, at 2x once `success_flag` is set. `strict_th=0.15` (`g1_29_config.py:342`), tighter than `eereach`'s own `reach_th=0.2`. Weight `5.0 -> 12.5` at cu=3 (`success_init=5.0`, `g1_29_config.py:300`, same `weight=base*(1+0.5*cu)` formula as every other curriculum-scaled term here).

**Fix:** added `success()` to `rewards.py`, ported functionally for feet: `success_flag -> env._sb_flag` (`stopball`'s own persistent flag, set on the identical triggering event); `dist -> ` distance from the task-assigned foot (`_get_correct_foot_idx`) to the same frozen-far/live-close `crossing_point` used by `footreach`/`foot_proximity`, not gated by `_ball_is_behind`, matching G1 exactly. Registered as a new `"success"` reward term (weight `5.0`, right after `softstop` so `env._sb_flag` is fresh) and a `success_curriculum` entry (`base_weight=5.0`, standard `reward_curriculum_ep_len`, max `12.5` at cu=3) in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`. Exported via `mdp/__init__.py`. Deliberately NOT folded into item 8's peak-magnitude cap -- G1 itself keeps `success` outside `_reward_stopball`'s own weight ceiling, as a separate-scale term; this port preserves that same separation.

**Note on the task brief's own numbers:** the assignment text for item 11 asked for "5 -> 18.75" under "the standard curriculum formula weight=base*(1+0.5*cu)" -- these two are mutually inconsistent (base 5 under that formula maxes at `5*2.5=12.5` at cu=3, the max cu this project ever reaches; reaching 18.75 from base 5 would need a x3.75 multiplier, unachievable under the stated formula). Implemented the value that is actually consistent with both the stated formula and G1's own literal scaling (`5 -> 12.5`), and flagged the discrepancy here rather than guessing which number was intended.

**Verification:** 63/63 tests pass (`uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v`, no test hardcoded any of the touched values). Built both training and play env configs directly (`goalkeeper_env_cfg(play=False/True)`): 34 reward terms, 9 curriculum terms, confirmed `success` present in both with weight `5.0`, `dof_vel_limits` params no longer include `vel_threshold`, `action_rate_l2=-0.05`/`action_acc_l2=-0.1`, `ang_vel_xy=-0.1`, `stopball=5.21`/`softstop=36.46` -- all matching the intended values.

**Not yet resolved:** none of items 4-11 have been validated against a live training run -- this batch is a source-level correctness/parity pass (re-verified against G1, tests pass, configs build cleanly), not an empirically-tuned retune. The item-8 rescale in particular reduces the save-event reward group's magnitude by ~31% (360 -> 250 peak); combined with the same-day AMP-side reward-weight audit (stopball/softstop already halved once), this run has now had its task-reward peak revised twice in one day -- worth watching specifically for whether the primary save signal is still strong enough relative to AMP after this second reduction.

## 2026-07-20 -- observations/PPO subsystem audit (items 21-24): re-verified against G1, ported per-term obs scaling and the policy/value smoothness regularizer, removed a G1-less obs-delay mechanism, reverted an unexplained actor/critic hidden-dims deviation

**Context:** items 21-24 of the same prior 5-way audit against `Humanoid-Goalkeeper`, assigned as the observations/PPO-network batch. Scope: `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` (observation term registration), `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py` (runner/network/PPO config), `rsl_rl_multi/multi_disc_amp_ppo.py` (PPO update loop), `rsl_rl_multi/him_actor_critic.py` (network), `rsl_rl_multi/him_amp_on_policy_runner.py`. Each item re-verified by reading the actual current G1 and SGK code directly, not the prior audit's summaries.

**Item 21 (no obs scaling/normalization on SGK's actor/critic input) -- CONFIRMED, fixed, with one correction to the audit's own premise.** G1's `compute_observations()`/`compute_termination_observations()` (`legged_robot.py:405-419,445-459`) bake fixed per-term `obs_scales` into the obs vector before the network ever sees it: `ang_vel=0.25` (applied line 407/447), `dof_pos=1.0` no-op (line 409/449), `dof_vel=0.05` (line 410/450), `lin_vel=2.0` (line 412/452), `ball_vel=0.2` (line 415/455) -- all confirmed genuinely applied by grep + line read, not just declared in `g1_29_config.py:191-201`. Separately confirmed dead: `rsl_rl/rsl_rl/modules/actor_critic.py`'s `RunningMeanStd`/`Normalizer` classes are never instantiated by `HIMPPO`; `HIMOnPolicyRunner.train_mode()`/`eval_mode()` (the only methods that reference `self.empirical_normalization`/`self.obs_normalizer`) are themselves never called from anywhere in `legged_gym`, and `self.empirical_normalization`/`self.obs_normalizer` are never assigned in `__init__` either -- doubly dead. SGK set no `scale=` on any `ObservationTermCfg` in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, and separately set `"empirical_normalization": True` in `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`, which `HimAMPOnPolicyRunner.__init__` (`rsl_rl_multi/him_amp_on_policy_runner.py`) never reads at all (grepped the whole file: zero references) -- this runner doesn't even define `train_mode()`/`eval_mode()`, so the flag was pure noise, not merely unused.

**Correction to the audit brief's premise:** the brief's instruction to apply "`ball_pos=0.3`... as applicable" is itself based on a config value that is dead in G1, same class of bug as the `empirical_normalization` flag. `obs_scales.ball_pos=0.3` is declared (`g1_29_config.py:198`, `legged_robot_config.py:268`) but grep of `legged_robot.py` finds zero uses of `obs_scales.ball_pos` anywhere -- G1's actual ball-position term (`end_target_local`, line 406/446), hand-position terms, and `dist` are all left as raw, unscaled meters. Matching G1's *real* mechanism (not its aspirational config) means `ball_pos_b` should NOT be scaled by 0.3.

**Fix:** in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, added `scale=0.25` to `base_ang_vel`, `scale=1.0` to `joint_pos_rel` (no-op, kept explicit for parity), `scale=0.05` to `joint_vel` (actor+critic, via the `critic_terms` dict-comprehension now propagating `scale=v.scale`), `scale=2.0` to critic's `base_lin_vel`, `scale=0.2` to critic's `ball_vel_b`. Left unscaled (matching G1's real behavior, not its dead config): `ball_pos_b` (both actor XY and critic 3D), `projected_gravity`, `actions`. Left unscaled by judgment (no G1 analog, natural magnitude already O(0.1-0.8) m -- leg length -- consistent with G1's own practice of leaving position-type terms raw): `left_foot_pos_b`, `right_foot_pos_b`. In `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`, set `"empirical_normalization": False` (matches G1's actual fixed-scale-only approach; wiring a real normalizer would need new state threaded through `act()`/`evaluate()`/`save()`/`load()` for both the single-step and 10-step-history obs paths -- a genuine feature addition, not "a small amount of missing plumbing", so left unimplemented per the item's stated preference).

**Item 22 (per-observation delay mechanism has no G1 equivalent) -- CONFIRMED, removed.** G1 only delays actions: `domain_rand.delay=True` (`g1_29_config.py:292`), applied in `step()`'s action-interpolation loop (`legged_robot.py:130-134`, `delay_steps = randint(0, decimation)`, blends `last_actions`/`actions` per-env). Grepped `legged_robot.py`/`legged_robot_config.py`/`g1_29_config.py` for "delay": the only hits are this action-delay mechanism -- no per-observation delay exists anywhere in G1. SGK's actor observation terms (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) each set `delay_min_lag=0, delay_max_lag=2, delay_per_env=True`, a second, compounding source of temporal staleness with no G1 analog.

**Fix:** removed the `for term_cfg in cfg.observations["actor"].terms.values(): term_cfg.delay_min_lag = ...` block from `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`. Verified SGK's action-delay equivalent is a separate, untouched mechanism: `t1_constants.py`'s five `BuiltinPositionActuatorCfg` instances (`T1_ACTUATOR_ARM`/`WAIST`/`HIP_PITCH`/`KNEE`/`ANKLE`) each already carry their own `delay_min_lag=1, delay_max_lag=3` (per-actuator PD-target delay, `DELAY_MIN, DELAY_MAX = 1, 3`) -- this is SGK's real action-delay mechanism and was not touched by this fix.

**Item 23 (hidden dims [512,256,128] vs G1's [512,256,256]) -- CONFIRMED as an unexplained deviation, reverted. Full git-history investigation below, per the item's explicit instruction to report regardless of outcome.** The task brief flagged this as user-flagged ("we changed this already thinking it was correct... double check before reverting") and instructed not to revert blindly without first checking for a real justification.

*Investigation:* `git log -p --follow --all -- src/simple_goalkeeper/tasks/goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py` shows `actor_hidden_dims`/`critic_hidden_dims` appear exactly once in this file's entire history, as a pure addition (`+`) in commit `3ec24b8` (2026-07-02, "feat(multidisc): register 4-region motion config and new task id") -- never modified since. `git log -p --follow --all` on `him_actor_critic.py` and `goalkeeper_amp_cfg.py` (single-disc track) show the same: `[512, 256, 128]` introduced once, never changed. `git log -S "[512, 256, 256]" --all -- src/simple_goalkeeper` (pickaxe search for the literal G1-matching string across all of this project's history) returns **zero commits** -- `[512, 256, 256]` has never appeared in this project's git history at all. No commit message, `docs/BugFixes.md` entry, `CLAUDE.md` row, or `docs/superpowers/plans|specs/*.md` design doc states a reason for 128 over 256 (checked all three explicitly) -- contrast with `amp_discr_hidden_dims` in the same file, which DID get an explicit, dated (2026-07-08), reasoned fix to match G1's discriminator width exactly (`[256,256] -> [512,256]`, "an undocumented divergence, not a deliberate one" -- see that entry above). **Conclusion: no "we changed it, believing 128 was correct" event exists in the record for the actor/critic trunk.** Likely explanation for the user's recollection: the 2026-07-08 discriminator fix is a real "we already fixed a hidden-dims value to match G1" event, but for a *different* network (the AMP discriminator, not the actor/critic policy trunk) -- plausible source of the misremembering the brief itself anticipated.

Also relevant: `[512, 256, 128]` is not an arbitrary number someone typed -- it is simultaneously (a) G1's own `rsl_rl/rsl_rl/modules/actor_critic.py:100-101` `ActorCritic.__init__`'s unused default kwarg value (dead once a config supplies hidden dims explicitly, exactly as G1's own `LeggedRobotCfgPPO.policy` does at `legged_robot_config.py:309-310` with `[512,256,256]`), and (b) beyondAMP's own framework default (`beyondAMP/source/beyondAMP/beyondAMP/mjlab/rsl_rl/configs/rl_cfg.py:28-29`, `PolicyCfg.actor_hidden_dims`/`critic_hidden_dims`). Confirmed G1's *actually exercised* value is `[512, 256, 256]`: `G129CfgPPO(LeggedRobotCfgPPO)` (`g1_29_config.py:367`) inherits `policy` unmodified from `LeggedRobotCfgPPO`, which explicitly sets `[512, 256, 256]` (`legged_robot_config.py:309-310`) -- this is what trained the reference G1 policy, not the library's own dead default.

**Fix:** reverted `actor_hidden_dims`/`critic_hidden_dims` in `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`'s `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_runner_cfg()` from `[512, 256, 128]` to G1's actual value `[512, 256, 256]`. Not touched: `goalkeeper_amp_cfg.py` (single-disc track) and `him_actor_critic.py`'s own function-default kwarg -- both out of this item's stated scope (`goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py` only); the single-disc track has the identical divergence and should get the same fix in a future pass.

**Item 24 (missing PPO policy/value smoothness regularizer) -- CONFIRMED missing, implemented.** Read G1's mechanism completely (`him_ppo.py:55-57,231-242`): `HIMPPO` maintains `value_smoothness_coef=0.1, smoothness_upper_bound=1.0, smoothness_lower_bound=0.1` (class defaults, never overridden by `g1_29_config.py`/`legged_robot_config.py` -- confirmed by grep, so G1's reference run used these verbatim). Each minibatch, G1 computes `epsilon = lower/(upper-lower)`, `policy_smooth_coef = upper*epsilon`, `value_smooth_coef = value_smoothness_coef*policy_smooth_coef` (fixed scalars: 0.1111 and 0.01111 respectively, since none of the three inputs change during training), then mixes each sample's actor/critic obs toward its own next-step (t+1) obs from the SAME rollout via a random per-sample weight in `[-1,1]` gated to `0` on episode-boundary transitions (`cont_batch = not_dones`), and penalizes `||mu - act_inference(mix_obs)||^2` / `||value - evaluate(mix_critic_obs)||^2`. This needs each minibatch sample's **next** (t+1) observation and a not-done mask alongside the standard PPO fields -- G1's `HIMRolloutStorage.mini_batch_generator` (`rsl_rl/storage/him_rollout_storage.py:134-179`) provides this by reserving the buffer's last per-env timestep (batch size uses `T-1`, not `T`) and index-shifting the SAME already-recorded `[T,N,*]` observation buffer (`observations[1:]` vs `observations[:-1]`) -- **no dedicated new storage tensor**, just a second read of data already being stored for the ordinary PPO losses.

Read SGK's storage (`rsl_rl_amp/storage/rollout_storage.py`, shared by other beyondAMP tasks): confirmed it's the "plain" `RolloutStorage` referenced by the deferred comment in `multi_disc_amp_ppo.py` -- it stores the same `[T,N,*]` buffers (`observations`, `privileged_observations`, `dones`, etc., all public attributes) but its `mini_batch_generator` uses the full `T` (not `T-1`) and never yields next-step data. Confirmed this is genuinely computable on-the-fly (per the item's own suggested approach) without modifying the shared `RolloutStorage` class: `MultiDiscAMPPPO.storage.observations`/`.privileged_observations`/`.dones`/etc. are already public, so a second, PPO-local generator can read them directly and apply G1's exact `T-1` + index-shift trick, entirely within `multi_disc_amp_ppo.py`.

**Fix:** added `MultiDiscAMPPPO._mini_batch_generator_with_next()` (`multi_disc_amp_ppo.py`), a drop-in replacement for the `self.storage.mini_batch_generator(...)` call in `update()` that reads the same raw storage buffers and additionally yields `next_obs_batch`/`next_critic_obs_batch`/`cont_batch` via G1's `T-1` + `buffer[1:]`/`buffer[:-1]` shift -- `RolloutStorage` itself is untouched, so other beyondAMP tasks consuming it are unaffected. Added `value_smoothness_coef`/`smoothness_upper_bound`/`smoothness_lower_bound` constructor params (defaults matching G1's class defaults) to `MultiDiscAMPPPO.__init__`. In `update()`, ported the smooth-loss computation verbatim (same coefficient formula, same `mix_weights = cont_batch * (rand-0.5)*2.0` construction), splitting the mixed combined-obs vector back into `obs_current`/`obs_history` via the existing `self._obs_current_dim` before calling `act_inference()` (SGK's `HimActorCritic.act_inference(obs_current, obs_history)` takes two args, unlike G1's single-arg `ActorCritic.act_inference(obs)` -- the mixing itself still happens on the single combined vector first, matching G1's semantics, then splits for the call). **Placement correctness note:** the smooth-loss block must run strictly after `est_loss`/`region_loss` are computed, not before -- `act_inference()` internally recomputes `self.actor_critic.estimate_ball`/`estimate_region` as a side effect (`HimActorCritic._build_actor_input`), which would silently corrupt `est_loss`/`region_loss` if the smooth-loss block ran first; `mu_batch`/`value_batch` are already-materialized tensors by that point so they're unaffected either way. Added explicit `value_smoothness_coef`/`smoothness_upper_bound`/`smoothness_lower_bound` entries to `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`'s `"algorithm"` dict for documentation parity even though they match the new constructor defaults. `multi_disc_amp_ppo.py`, `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`.

**Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v` -- 63/63 pass, including three pre-existing `test_multi_disc_amp_ppo.py` tests that call `alg.update()` end-to-end with synthetic data (`num_envs=4, num_transitions=2`, exercising the new `T-1`-sized generator and the smooth-loss forward passes with no shape errors or crashes). Smoke-constructed both `goalkeeper_multidisc_env_cfg()` and `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_runner_cfg()` directly and asserted the four fixed values (`actor_hidden_dims==[512,256,256]`, `empirical_normalization is False`, `value_smoothness_coef==0.1`, `base_ang_vel.scale==0.25`) — all pass.

**Not yet resolved:** none of items 21-24 have been validated against a live training run. Item 24 in particular adds two extra forward passes per minibatch (policy + value smooth loss) -- a real compute-time increase, and its gradient contribution is new and untested at this project's actual observation/reward scale (G1's `smoothness_upper_bound`/`lower_bound` were tuned against G1's own obs magnitudes, now partially changed by item 21's newly-added scale= factors in the same commit -- watch for interaction effects between the two). Item 23's hidden-dims revert increases actor/critic parameter count; combined with item 24's extra compute, expect a measurable throughput (fps) regression in the next run's `Perf/total_fps` metric versus pre-this-fix runs, which is expected and not itself a bug.

## 2026-07-21 -- item 25 (num_steps_per_env): raised 24 -> 64 (attempted G1's exact 100 first, CUDA-OOM'd), after empirical evidence from a live run showed AMP-discriminator saturation persisting even with every other audit fix already applied

**Context:** item 25 of the same G1-comparison audit series (items 1-24 covered in the entries above), deliberately saved for last per the original instruction ("do num steps per env last") since it's a training-hyperparameter change rather than a source-level correctness fix, and the plan was to gather empirical evidence first rather than change it speculatively.

**Evidence gathered:** run `green_g1auditfixes_2026-07-20` (launched with items 1-24 already applied -- AMP reward smoothing/motion diversity/normalizer fix, all reward-weight/curriculum/termination/observation-scaling fixes) was monitored from launch through iteration 3054:

| Iteration | AMP mean policy pred | AMP mean expert pred | AMP loss | AMP grad pen loss |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 27 | -0.9989 | 0.9996 | 8.2976 | 24.1750 |
| 687 | -0.9983 | 0.9975 | 0.0585 | 0.1189 |
| 3054 | -0.9567 | 0.9585 | 0.1174 | 0.1884 |
