The discriminator saturated at the LSGAN targets (±1) within the first 27 iterations and stayed saturated through iteration 3054 (3054/27 = 113x more iterations, negligible movement in the prediction gap) -- matching the exact shape of the previously-documented "style reward collapsed to its floor and never recovered" finding (`docs/BugFixes.md`, 2026-07-20, "discriminator-probe finding" entry), this time with every other known contributing factor already fixed. `predict_amp_reward`'s `clamp(1-0.25*err, min=0)` formula (ported correctly from G1, confirmed in the 2026-07-20 AMP-subsystem audit) means a policy prediction pinned at -0.96 to -0.999 (vs. the +1 "expert-like" target the reward formula scores against) keeps the computed style reward near its floor for the entire run, regardless of how much real motion-quality variation exists in the policy's actual behavior -- the discriminator's job is too easy, so it stops providing a useful gradient almost immediately.

**Root cause (per-discriminator batch starvation):** SGK's PPO batch (`num_envs=6144, num_steps_per_env=24`) is 6144x24=147,456 transitions/update, split across 4 region discriminators (~36,864/discriminator). G1's proven batch (`num_envs=6144, num_steps_per_env=100`, `Humanoid-Goalkeeper/legged_gym/legged_gym/envs/g1/g1_29_config.py:373`, `G129CfgPPO.runner.num_steps_per_env`, never overridden) is 6144x100=614,400/update, split across 6 discriminators (~102,400/discriminator) -- 2.8x more samples per discriminator than SGK had, even before counting that SGK's overall batch is also 4.2x smaller in absolute terms. A discriminator retrained from a much smaller, more homogeneous batch every single update can memorize/separate a still-poor early policy almost instantly and never lose that confidence, since it never sees enough policy-side diversity per update to be forced into a more calibrated decision boundary.

**Fix, attempt 1 (failed):** raised `"num_steps_per_env"` in `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`'s `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_runner_cfg()` from `24` to G1's exact value, `100`. Checked GPU memory headroom before committing to the full value: `RolloutStorage`'s own tensors (`beyondAMP/source/rsl_rl_amp/rsl_rl_amp/storage/rollout_storage.py`) scale linearly with `num_steps_per_env` -- computed the actual per-`(env,step)` footprint from the tensor shapes `MultiDiscAMPPPO.init_storage` passes in (`combined_obs_shape=781` [`obs_current`(71) + `obs_history`(710)] + `critic_obs=92` + `actions`/`mu`/`sigma`(21 each) + scalar fields, ~3.77KB/(env,step)): at `num_envs=6144` this is ~0.52GiB at 24 steps vs. ~2.15GiB at 100 steps, a ~1.6GiB delta, comfortably inside the ~14.3GiB headroom `green_g1auditfixes_2026-07-20` had shown (8.67GB/23.03GB used at `num_steps_per_env=24`). **This estimate was wrong in practice.** Launched `green_numsteps100_2026-07-21` with `num_steps_per_env=100`: it completed exactly one full iteration (`Total timesteps: 614400` = `6144x100`, `Iteration time: 24.93s`) then crashed at the start of iteration 2's `env.step()` with `RuntimeError: Graph launch error: Warp CUDA error 2: out of memory (in function wp_cuda_graph_launch, warp.cu:4316)`. The RolloutStorage-only estimate missed that the PPO update's minibatch/smoothness-regularizer forward-backward passes (`_mini_batch_generator_with_next`, item 24) and the per-minibatch AMP expert-batch draws (`ds.feed_forward_generator(num_mini_batches, minibatch_size)`) also scale with `num_steps_per_env` -- these run alongside the collected rollout still resident in memory, and real peak usage is set by that combination, not by RolloutStorage's static tensor size alone.

**Fix, attempt 2 (succeeded):** backed off to `64` (2.67x the original 24 -- a meaningful batch-size increase, well short of G1's 100 but empirically safe). Verified: process survived past the exact point attempt 1 crashed (4+ iterations completed, `Total timesteps` climbing steadily through 1,572,864), GPU memory flat at ~16.2GB/23.03GB across all 4 iterations (no creep), zero NaN/traceback/CUDA-error lines in the log.

**Verification:** `uv run python -c "from simple_goalkeeper.tasks.goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg import goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_runner_cfg; print(goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_runner_cfg()['num_steps_per_env'])"` -> `64`. `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v` -- 63/63 pass (both before and after the 100->64 correction), no test hardcoded the old value.

**Stopped-run baseline (for comparison against the new run):** `green_g1auditfixes_2026-07-20` stopped at iteration 3054/model_3000.pt (pushed to git), `ball_difficulty` curriculum at 1.0 (max), mean episode length 125.09 steps, mean reward 24.40, `footreach=7.01`, `success=6.25`, `softstop=0.385` (~54% event rate via the standard conversion), `stopball=0.064`. Task-reward-side learning looked genuinely healthy throughout -- only the AMP-discriminator saturation looked wrong. New run `green_numsteps64_2026-07-21` (final name, after the failed `green_numsteps100_2026-07-21` attempt above) launched to test whether the larger per-discriminator batch prevents/delays the saturation pattern. Per-discriminator batch at the new value: `6144x64/4 = 98,304` -- despite `num_steps_per_env=64` being well short of G1's `100`, SGK's 4-way discriminator split (vs. G1's 6-way) means this lands remarkably close to G1's own `6144x100/6 = 102,400`/discriminator; the memory-driven compromise turned out to nearly reach full G1 parity on the metric that actually matters (per-discriminator batch size), not just a partial step toward it.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated beyond the first 4 iterations at time of writing this entry (confirmed stable through 1,572,864 timesteps, GPU flat at ~16.2GB, no errors -- but AMP loss curves take hundreds of iterations to reveal the saturation pattern, per the previous run's own timeline). The key metric to watch is whether `AMP mean policy pred`/`AMP mean expert pred` stay away from ±1 saturation for longer (or indefinitely) than the 27-iteration collapse seen in the previous run, which would confirm per-discriminator batch size as the root cause; if saturation recurs at a similar early iteration despite the ~2.67x larger per-discriminator batch (36,864 -> 98,304), the root cause lies elsewhere (candidates from the earlier AMP-subsystem audit not yet tried: LSGAN target relaxation/label smoothing, or delaying/ramping the AMP blend weight early in training -- both would be novel departures from G1's mechanism, not G1-parity fixes).

## 2026-07-21 -- AMP discriminator policy-sample oversampling (genuine bug, not a G1-divergence experiment): ~20x with-replacement reuse per update, fixed to match G1's deterministic 5x reuse; num_steps_per_env reverted 64->24

**Context:** after `green_numsteps64_2026-07-21` ran ~2600 iterations without showing a meaningfully different AMP-saturation trajectory from the 24-batch run (per-environment-step comparison, not per-iteration -- see the previous entry's numbers), the user asked to look for and implement a genuine fix from the AMP-subsystem research list, prioritizing the most concrete finding first, and to revert `num_steps_per_env` back to 24 first since the larger batch showed no AMP benefit and only cost wall-clock throughput.

**Root cause found:** `ReplayBuffer.feed_forward_generator` (`beyondAMP/source/rsl_rl_amp/rsl_rl_amp/storage/replay_buffer.py`) drew `num_learning_epochs * num_mini_batches` (20) independent WITH-REPLACEMENT samples (`np.random.choice(self.num_samples, size=mini_batch_size)`, numpy's default `replace=True`) every single `update()` call, where `mini_batch_size` was the CROSS-REGION total (`num_envs * num_transitions_per_env // num_mini_batches`) rather than the calling region's own share. In the current 4-region/4-minibatch config this size coincidentally equals each region's actual pool size (~98k at `num_steps_per_env=64`, ~37k at 24), so each of the 20 draws independently resampled ~all of the region's unique on-policy transitions with replacement -- ~20x average reuse per transition, high per-draw variance (some transitions drawn 0 times, others 30-40+, pure chance).

Compare to G1's actual mechanism (`Humanoid-Goalkeeper/rsl_rl/rsl_rl/algorithms/him_ppo.py`): no separate replay buffer at all -- the discriminator's "policy" batch for a given motion type is a direct mask-filtered slice (`amp_obs_batch[motion_mask]`) of the SAME already-permuted PPO minibatch used for the policy/critic update. `HIMRolloutStorage.mini_batch_generator` (confirmed via direct read, `him_rollout_storage.py:137`) generates its `torch.randperm` ONCE per `update()`, splits into `num_mini_batches` non-overlapping chunks, and replays that SAME partition unchanged across all `num_learning_epochs` epochs -- every real transition is seen by the discriminator exactly `num_learning_epochs` times (5), deterministically, never duplicated within a single epoch. This exact pattern was already independently verified correct for SGK's own actor-critic minibatch generator (`_mini_batch_generator_with_next`, item 24's earlier port) -- only the discriminator's separate `ReplayBuffer` path had drifted from it.

This is a genuine correctness bug (an unintended divergence from the intended reuse scheme), not a deliberate G1-parity experiment like `num_steps_per_env` was -- it also matches the AMP paper's own stated concern (section 6.3) that a discriminator retrained repeatedly against a narrow, independently-resampled slice of the *current* policy's rollout is exactly the setup that causes fast, hard saturation.

**Fix:** rewrote `ReplayBuffer.feed_forward_generator(num_mini_batches, num_learning_epochs)` (signature changed from the old flattened `(draw_count, draw_size)` pair) to permute the region's pool ONCE per call, split into `num_mini_batches` chunks of size `num_samples // num_mini_batches` (the region's own fair share, not the cross-region total), and yield the SAME chunks unchanged across all `num_learning_epochs` passes -- matching G1's cadence exactly. Updated the call site in `multi_disc_amp_ppo.py`'s `update()` to pass `(self.num_mini_batches, self.num_learning_epochs)`. Confirmed safe to leave the expert-side `MotionDataset.feed_forward_generator` untouched (different class, not part of this bug) and that policy/expert draw sizes no longer needing to match is fine: `expert_loss`/`policy_loss` (`multi_disc_amp_ppo.py:455-456`) each reduce independently via `torch.nn.MSELoss` against a same-shaped target tensor, exactly like G1's own variable-sized per-motion masked batches -- there's no cross-batch pairing requirement anywhere in the loss.

**`num_steps_per_env` reverted 64 -> 24:** per the evidence above (no AMP-saturation improvement at the larger batch, per-timestep-normalized comparison), plus the added wall-clock cost. `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`.

**Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v` -- 63/63 pass (including `test_multi_disc_amp_ppo.py`'s tests that call `alg.update()` end-to-end; that file's own mock `ReplayBuffer` stand-in still satisfies the new call site positionally, unaffected since it doesn't validate sampling statistics). `uv run python -c "...goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_runner_cfg()['num_steps_per_env']"` -> `24`.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a live training run at time of writing -- launching `green_disc_oversample_fix_2026-07-21` (24 steps/env + the replay-buffer fix) next. If `AMP mean policy/expert pred` still saturates near ±1 within the first ~50 iterations and stays there, this specific mechanism is not the (or not the only) driver and the next candidates are R1 gradient-penalty strengthening (effective 0.5 vs. the AMP paper's own default 10) or one-sided LSGAN label smoothing (±0.9), both flagged in the earlier AMP-subsystem research as the next-most-evidenced options -- see the AMP-subsystem-research findings summarized in conversation (not yet written to this file as their own entry).

## 2026-07-21 -- R1 gradient penalty strengthened 0.5 -> 10 (deliberate G1 divergence, not a parity fix): discriminator-oversampling fix and num_steps_per_env increase both left AMP saturation unchanged, and a live G1 baseline run today showed non-collapsing behavior at the SAME effective 0.5 value SGK already had

**Context:** two prior fixes today (discriminator policy-sample oversampling -- see the entry above -- and the earlier `num_steps_per_env` 24->64 batch-size experiment) both left `AMP mean policy pred`/`expert pred` saturated near ±1 unchanged, tested across 2600+ live iterations (`green_discsamplefix_resume_2026-07-21`). In parallel, `Humanoid-Goalkeeper` (frozen G1 reference) was gotten running today for the first time via a correct Python 3.8 + modern-PyTorch conda env (see the Isaac Gym setup notes elsewhere in this conversation) and run alone, full scale (6144 envs), for `g1_amp_longrun_2026-07-21`. Over 17 real iterations, G1's `Loss/amp_expert_loss`/`amp_policy_loss` collapsed toward near-zero just as fast as SGK's `amp_loss` does (132.7->0.2 and 302.7->36.1 respectively) -- confirming fast loss collapse is normal/expected AMP behavior on both sides, not itself the bug. But the metric that actually reaches the policy, `Train/mean_amp_reward`, rose from 7.64 to a sustained ~15-17 plateau and kept climbing with noise -- never floored. This is under the exact same effective R1 coefficient (0.5) SGK already uses (both independently verified to match `Humanoid-Goalkeeper/rsl_rl/rsl_rl/modules/amp.py:124-173`'s `lambda_=5 * 0.1` scaling on 2026-07-08).

This directly rules out "batch size" and "G1-parity on the GP coefficient" as sufficient fixes: G1 itself, at the same coefficient, does not show the collapse SGK shows. The project's own earlier root-cause read (2026-07-20 discriminator-probe finding, same file, above) already named the likely reason: G1's regions are single atomic motions needing only coarse discrimination, while SGK's compositional double/triple-step target needs a much longer-lived, finer-grained discriminator signal than the same recipe naturally provides.

**Fix (user-directed experiment, explicit divergence from G1):** raised the discriminator's R1 gradient penalty from `lambda_=5 * 0.1` (effective 0.5, G1-matched) to `lambda_=100 * 0.1` (effective 10) in `MultiDiscAMPPPO.update()`. This targets the discriminator's own regularization strength directly, opposing "discriminator races ahead of the generator and gives no graded feedback" -- the AMP paper's own (Peng et al. 2021) recommended default is 10; G1 itself already diverges from the paper's own default downward. Notably, effective 10 was this port's *original* value before a 2026-07-08 fix reduced it to match G1 (see that FIX comment in the same file) -- this change consciously reverts that G1-parity correction in light of today's new evidence that matching G1's coefficient does not reproduce G1's non-collapsing behavior.

`src/simple_goalkeeper/rsl_rl_multi/multi_disc_amp_ppo.py` (the `grad_pen = discr.compute_grad_pen(...)` line in `update()`).

**Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v` -- 63/63 pass.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a live training run. This is explicitly an experiment, not a confirmed fix -- if AMP still saturates near ±1 within the first ~50 iterations and stays there, the task-complexity-mismatch hypothesis is the next thing to pursue (richer discriminator input features beyond joint_pos, discriminator capacity, or per-motion-type/finer-grained discriminator routing), not another hyperparameter sweep on this same axis.

## 2026-07-21 -- R1 experiment result: still saturates, just slower -- three fixes on the "discriminator training mechanics" axis have now all converged to the same floor, closing that axis

**Context:** ran `green_gradpen10_2026-07-21` (effective R1 coefficient 10, see entry above) standalone at full scale (6144 envs, G1 stopped to free the GPU) and watched it past the horizon where every prior fix had already re-settled.

**Result:** `AMP mean policy pred` was volatile for the first ~15 iterations (spiking to -1.57 at iteration 1, oscillating -0.74 to -1.08 through iteration 15 -- notably more turbulent than any prior run at this coefficient), but by iteration 164 it had fully re-settled to the same saturated floor as every previous attempt: policy pred -0.998 to -1.0007, expert pred 0.993 to 1.008, `AMP grad pen loss` flat at ~18-21, `AMP loss` flat at ~1.0-1.2. No NaN/Inf/traceback at any point (0 hits across the full log). Zero drift in either direction from iteration ~50 through 171 -- the stronger penalty changed the *transient* (took ~10x longer to converge, ~164 iterations vs ~15) but not the *destination*.

**Conclusion:** this is the third distinct fix attempted on the "discriminator training mechanics" axis (replay-buffer oversampling correction, `num_steps_per_env` batch-size increase, now R1 gradient-penalty strengthening) to leave the saturated ±1 floor completely unchanged. Per the project's own systematic-debugging standard: 3+ fixes converging to the identical failure mode is evidence against the axis, not grounds for a 4th hyperparameter sweep on the same mechanism. This closes the "it's a training-mechanics hyperparameter" hypothesis for SGK's AMP saturation.

**Not yet resolved -- next step is architectural, not another hyperparameter:** the task-complexity-mismatch hypothesis (2026-07-20 entry, above) is now the leading explanation: G1's regions are single atomic motions needing only coarse style discrimination and tolerate fast saturation; SGK's compositional double/triple-step regions plausibly need either (a) richer discriminator input features beyond `joint_pos` (e.g. velocities, end-effector positions), (b) more discriminator capacity, or (c) finer-grained (per-motion-phase rather than per-region) discriminator routing. None of these have been tried yet. Left `green_gradpen10_2026-07-21` running for continued observation; no further training-mechanics-axis changes should be made without first picking one of (a)/(b)/(c) and discussing scope with the user, since each is a more invasive change than anything tried so far.

**Discriminator input parity check (ground truth, via code read, not assumption):** compared G1's discriminator input (`legged_robot.py:159-163` `get_amp_observations()` -> `dof_pos.clone()` only, 29 dims x 2 frames = 58) against SGK's (`goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py:103-113`, `joint_pos` only via `joint_pos_abs_arms_masked_by_region`, 21 dims x 2 frames = 42). Both are joint-position-only, two-frame-concatenated vectors with identical hidden-layer sizes (`[512, 256]`) and identical init scheme -- this was already a deliberate 2026-07-08 G1-parity fix, not an accidental gap. The "richer discriminator input" candidate (velocities, end-effector positions, contact booleans) would therefore be a genuine SGK-only *addition* beyond what G1's own proven recipe uses, not a parity fix -- weaker justification than the other candidates, deprioritized for now. The only real structural difference found: SGK's `joint_pos_abs_arms_masked_by_region` freezes the 8 arm-joint columns to the default pose for "far"-region envs (`observations.py:306-321`), an SGK-only mechanism with no G1 equivalent, worth keeping in mind if per-region saturation behavior ever needs revisiting.

**CORRECTION (same day, later, more data collected):** the "converged to the floor and stopped moving" verdict above was based on only 171 iterations and was premature. Continued observation of the same run out to iteration ~1875 shows `AMP mean policy pred` did NOT stay pinned -- it is drifting steadily and monotonically off the floor: -0.999 (iter 300) -> -0.992 (450) -> -0.952 (600) -> -0.919 (750) -> -0.883 (900) -> -0.875 (1200) -> -0.844 (1500) -> -0.811 (1800) -> -0.797 (1875), moving roughly linearly at this timescale, not plateauing. `AMP mean expert pred` is moving symmetrically in the same direction (1.00 -> 0.796 over the same span) -- both predictions are converging toward each other/toward 0 together, rather than policy pred alone climbing toward expert pred's level. This symmetric-shrinkage pattern is consistent with (but not proven to be) the much stronger R1 penalty (effective 10 vs 0.5) globally softening the discriminator's output magnitude over training, rather than the generator specifically closing a style gap -- the distinction matters for whether this drift represents a genuinely more informative reward signal or just smaller numbers, and is not yet resolved. Either way, this is unambiguously different behavior from every prior fix attempt (all of which stayed pinned at the floor through 2600+ iterations with zero drift). The "three fixes closed this axis" verdict two entries above is retracted -- the R1 strengthening experiment is still active and looking more promising at the ~2000-iteration horizon than the ~200-iteration horizon suggested. No architectural change (richer input / more capacity / finer routing) has been started; continuing to monitor `green_gradpen10_2026-07-21` is the current plan instead.

## 2026-07-22 -- Two fixes after user review of green_gradpen10_2026-07-21 checkpoints around iteration ~10000: AMP discriminator gets joint_vel back, and a direct penalty for ball touching the wrong (non-leading) foot

**Context:** `AMP mean policy pred` had settled into a stable ~-0.78 to -0.80 plateau (see prior entries) and stayed there through iteration ~13000 -- real improvement over the old -0.99 floor, but not obviously still closing. Separately, the user reviewed checkpoints around iteration ~10000 directly and reported two concrete behavioral problems: (1) the policy still had not learned a genuine double-step approach for far balls, and (2) it had learned to save/stop the ball with its non-leading (wrong, non-task-assigned) foot to still farm `cleanstop`/`softstop`/`single_foot_save` reward. User instruction: implement two fixes, then stop `green_gradpen10_2026-07-21` and restart training with both applied.

**Fix 1 -- AMP discriminator observes joint_vel again (task-complexity-mismatch hypothesis, next lever after the discriminator-input parity check).** The 2026-07-21 discriminator-input parity check (entry above) found SGK's `joint_pos`-only AMP input exactly matches G1's -- not a bug, a deliberate 2026-07-08 G1-parity fix. But G1 never needed more than that because every G1 region is a single atomic motion (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-18-doublestep-research-and-plan.md`, finding #1); this project's far regions need a genuine multi-step gait, which `joint_pos` alone can't distinguish from a passive weight-shift through the same waypoints (see `foot_clearance`'s pre-existing docstring: "the robot sometimes shuffles without lifting feet... producing a slide rather than a committed step"). Confirmed via the running log that this isn't a curriculum-width problem either: `Curriculum/far_travel/far_inner` and `far_outer` were already fully saturated to the full `(0.5, 1.3)` range for thousands of iterations by iteration 10000 (the 2026-07-18 doc's recommendation B, `far_travel_curriculum` in `events.py`, was already implemented and had already maxed out) -- so a wider curriculum alone did not produce double-stepping either.

Re-added `joint_vel` to the multi-disc AMP observation group, deliberately reverting the 2026-07-08 G1-parity decision (that decision was correct as a parity statement, just not proven sufficient for a task G1 never had to solve). New `joint_vel_abs_arms_masked_by_region` (`observations.py`) mirrors `joint_pos_abs_arms_masked_by_region` exactly (softstop-gated to zero, arm columns zeroed for far regions instead of set to default pose -- zero being the natural "frozen" velocity). `_MULTIDISC_AMP_OBS_TERMS` updated to `["joint_pos", "joint_vel"]` (`goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`) -- the motion-dataset/expert side needed no other code change, `MotionDatasetCfg.freeze_joint_names` already zeroes whichever `amp_obs_terms` are configured generically. AMP observation dimensionality: 21 -> 42 per frame (84 total discriminator input after the runner's state/next_state concat, up from 42) -- confirmed live via `env.observation_manager.compute()["amp"]` on a real 4-env instantiation, shape `[4, 42]` as expected. Verified every motion `.npz` file already contains a `joint_vel` array at the correct `(frames, 21)` shape (checked `LeftStep_own_booster_t1.npz` and `new_doublestepleft_booster_t1.npz` directly) -- no data gap. All discriminator/runner dimension-dependent code reads `env.get_amp_observations().shape[-1]` dynamically (`him_amp_on_policy_runner.py:91`), no hardcoded-dimension sites found.

**Fix 2 -- new `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` reward, weight -30.0 (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, function in `rewards.py`).** The existing correct-foot gates on `stopball`/`softstop`/`cleanstop` (see their own 2026-07-14/07-15 FIX entries above) use `feet_contact`, a GROUND-contact sensor -- the assigned foot merely standing on the ground at the same instant a deflection happens satisfies the gate even when a DIFFERENT foot actually touched the ball. A prior attempt to tighten those gates directly with a ball-specific sensor (`ball_contact`) was tried and reverted (2026-07-15) because it made those rewards stop firing almost entirely -- too narrow a detection window for the primary positive-reward signal. Left those gates as-is per that prior finding, and instead added an independent, direct penalty using the same `ball_contact` sensor (already defined in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, just never used for gating after the revert): fires (binary, per-step, weight -30.0) whenever the foot NOT matching `_get_correct_foot_idx` is in genuine contact with the ball, regardless of what the existing correct-foot gates believe. For a penalty, the narrower/stricter detection window that made `ball_contact` unusable as the *positive*-reward gate is a non-issue -- false negatives here just mean the penalty under-fires sometimes, not that it incorrectly blocks the main training signal. Not gated by `_ball_is_behind`: any wrong-foot ball touch, before or after a save, is the behavior being discouraged.

**Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v` -- 63/63 pass. Live smoke test: built the real multi-disc env (`goalkeeper_multidisc_env_cfg_withoverlay()`, 4 envs, `cuda:0`), confirmed `observation_manager.compute()["amp"]` shape is `[4, 42]` and `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` appears in `reward_manager.active_terms`, stepped 20 zero-action steps with no errors (penalty correctly 0 with the robot standing still and the ball not yet in reach).

**Not yet resolved:** neither fix has live training evidence yet. `green_gradpen10_2026-07-21` was stopped (last checkpoint pushed) specifically to restart with both applied together -- see the run-launch entry for the new run's name. Because both fixes land in the same restart, a regression or improvement can't yet be attributed to one or the other individually; if the double-step/wrong-foot problems persist, the next diagnostic step is isolating which of the two (if either) is load-bearing before trying anything further.

## 2026-07-22 -- Post-save pose is "weird, not a default pose": G1 comparison found a genuine gap, not a hidden mechanism -- legs had no return-to-default reward at all

**Context:** user asked to check the running `green_jointvel_wrongfootpen_2026-07-22` training (healthy, iteration ~6072, both prior fixes' metrics stable -- AMP mean policy/expert pred holding ~-0.91, `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` firing at a small, non-runaway magnitude) and separately to compare against G1's post-success pose-recovery mechanism, since the policy settles into a "really weird pose, not a default pose" after a save. User's own guess was something like "amp motion set overwritten or a default pose" -- confirmed via grep that G1 does NOT switch/overwrite the AMP reference motion after a catch (searched `g1_utils.py`/`legged_robot.py` for any motion-switching logic post-catch -- none exists); G1's mechanism is pure reward shaping, not an AMP-side trick.

**Root cause found:** G1 has five post-success reward terms (`legged_robot.py:1480-1512`) -- `_reward_postorientation`, `_reward_postangvel`, `_reward_postlinvel`, `_reward_postupperdofpos`, `_reward_postwaistdofpos` -- all gated on `behind` (ball crossed the goal line or was decisively deflected) and already ported to SGK with matching gating and matching exponent coefficients (`postupperdofpos`: `exp(-1*err)`; `postwaistdofpos`: `exp(-3*err)`; confirmed line-for-line against `legged_robot.py:1502-1512`). But `_reward_postupperdofpos` only covers G1's `upper_body_joint_indices`, which is `elbow_joint_indices + wrist_joint_indices` ONLY (`legged_robot.py:1315`) -- not shoulders. `_reward_postwaistdofpos` covers the 3 waist joints. G1's `leg_joint_indices` (all 12 hip/knee/ankle joints per side, `legged_robot.py:1276-1284`) have **no post-success reward at all** -- confirmed by grep, no `_reward_post*` function references `leg_joint_indices` anywhere.

This is not a bug in G1 or a porting gap in the literal sense -- it's correct for G1's own task: G1 catches with its **hands**, so the catching limb (arms, via elbow+wrist) needs an explicit pull back to neutral after use; the legs are mostly just standing/braced throughout a hand-catch and don't need one. SGK's task structurally inverts this: SGK saves with its **feet**, so the legs are the limb thrown into an extreme, off-default configuration during a dive/step/reach -- and this project's port carried over G1's literal joint groupings (arms + waist get a return-to-default reward) without adding the role-equivalent term for the actually-active limb in this task. Until this fix, nothing pulled the legs back to a standing pose after a save -- exactly matching the reported "weird pose" symptom (only arms/waist had any incentive to return to default; legs, wherever the save left them, had none at all). This is precisely the kind of divergence the top-level `CLAUDE.md` flags: "check whether a decision... was designed for hands and needs adaptation for feet."

**Fix:** new `postlegdofpos` reward (`rewards.py`), mirroring `postupperdofpos`'s exact shape (`exp(-1*sum_sq_err) * behind`, same `_ball_is_behind` gate) but targeting the 12 leg joints (`Left/Right_Hip_Roll/Yaw/Pitch`, `Left/Right_Knee_Pitch`, `Left/Right_Ankle_Pitch/Roll` -- confirmed exact T1 joint names via `t1_constants.py`'s actuator `target_names_expr` patterns) instead of G1's elbow+wrist. Weight `1.0`, matched to `postupperdofpos`'s weight/exponent since G1 has no leg-specific value to copy and both play the analogous "catching limb, gentle pull to neutral" role for their respective tasks. Registered in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` (`_RECOVERY_LEG_CFG` alongside the existing `_RECOVERY_ARM_CFG`/`_RECOVERY_WAIST_CFG` pattern) and `mdp/__init__.py`.

**Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v` -- 63/63 pass. Live smoke test: built the real multi-disc env (4 envs, `cuda:0`), confirmed `postlegdofpos` appears in `reward_manager.active_terms`, stepped 20 zero-action steps with no errors.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a live training run -- this needs to go into the next restart alongside (or instead of, if the current run is left going) the joint_vel/wrong-foot fixes. Training was NOT stopped for this fix (unlike the joint_vel/wrong-foot pair) -- adding a reward term to a `RewardTermCfg` dict does not change the AMP discriminator's input shape (unlike the joint_vel fix, which was checkpoint-breaking), so whether to restart now or let `green_jointvel_wrongfootpen_2026-07-22` keep accumulating iterations without this term is a call for the user, not made unilaterally here.

## 2026-07-22 -- research instrumentation only, no reward/termination change: play.py now reports per-episode min base/shank height and which termination fired

**Context:** user dislikes the `shank_height` termination (min-shank-height 0.27, `shank_height_termination`) -- during play sessions, the live-printed shank height (`Lsh=`/`Rsh=`) sometimes reads low (e.g. 0.310) during what looks like a legitimate deep lunge, not a fall, and the live value scrolls past too fast to correlate with what actually happened. User's instinct: the root/torso height ("where the scrotum is") is probably a more reliable fall-vs-lunge signal, since a real athletic lunge keeps the torso relatively high over the standing leg while a genuine fall drops it too. This is exactly `mjlab_mdp.root_height_below_minimum` -- **already registered** as the `base_height` termination (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, `minimum_height=0.4`), running in parallel with `shank_height` the whole time; this is a request to research whether `shank_height` is causing false-positive terminations that `base_height` alone would avoid, not to add a missing mechanism.

**What was added (instrumentation only, `src/simple_goalkeeper/scripts/play.py`, no reward/termination values changed):**
- `AnalyticsPolicy` now tracks running per-episode minimums of base height and both shank heights (`self._min_base_h`/`_min_lsh_h`/`_min_rsh_h`), updated every step alongside the existing live `base_h`/`lsh_h`/`rsh_h` computation, reset at each episode boundary.
- On episode-end (reset detection), prints an `[EpEnd]` summary line: `terminated_by=<comma-separated fired termination names>` plus that episode's `min_base`/`min_Lsh`/`min_Rsh`. The termination cause is read from `env.termination_manager.get_term(name)` for every registered termination name -- confirmed via reading `mjlab`'s `TerminationManager.reset()` that it does NOT clear `_term_dones` (only `compute()`, called once per `env.step()`, does), so this buffer still reflects the exact step that caused the reset even though the read happens one policy-call later.

This directly gives the data needed to decide the actual question: for episodes where `shank_height` fired, was `min_base` still comfortably above 0.4 (real false positive -- torso never got close to its own termination) or also low (a genuine fall, both correctly fired)? No conclusion drawn yet -- this is instrumentation for the user to run interactively and read the results themselves; no reward weight, termination threshold, or termination registration was touched.

**Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v` -- 63/63 pass (unaffected, play.py has no test coverage but syntax-checked via `ast.parse`).

**Not yet resolved -- next step depends on what the user observes:** if `shank_height` false-positives are confirmed common (fires while `min_base` stays high), the follow-up is either loosening/removing `shank_height` and relying on the existing `base_height` termination alone, or retuning `base_height`'s own `minimum_height=0.4` using the same data. No G1-comparison needed here -- `mjlab_mdp.root_height_below_minimum`/`shank_height_termination` are both this project's own mechanisms with no direct G1 analog (G1 has no ball-driven lunge termination of this kind at all).

## 2026-07-22 -- Result of the base-height research: shank_height/penalize_kneeheight removed, replaced by penalize_baseheight (0.59) + retuned base_height termination (0.57)

**Context:** user ran `sgk_play` with the new per-episode min-height/`terminated_by` reporting (entry above) on `model_6000` of `green_jointvel_wrongfootpen_2026-07-22`, and reported back two threshold values: 590 (penalty) and 570 (termination) -- read as meters, 0.59 and 0.57, i.e. the user's own judgment call from watching real base-height values during legitimate deep lunges vs falls. Asked whether `shank_height`/`penalize_kneeheight` should be removed entirely, loosened, or left alone; user chose **remove entirely**.

**Change (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, `mdp/rewards.py`, `mdp/__init__.py`):**
- New `penalize_baseheight` reward (`rewards.py`) -- mirrors `penalize_kneeheight`'s exact graded shape (`clamp(min_height - z, 0)`, weight -100.0) but on the single root/Trunk body instead of the two shank bodies. `min_height=0.59`.
- `base_height` termination (`mjlab_mdp.root_height_below_minimum`, already existed) retuned `minimum_height` 0.4 -> 0.57 -- the prior 0.4 was an untuned starting value per its own original comment; 0.57 is the user's play-session-derived threshold. Keeps the same ~2.5cm graded-warning-before-hard-cutoff gap design as the old kneeheight(0.295)/shank_height(0.27) pair.
- `penalize_kneeheight` and `shank_height` **unregistered** from `cfg.rewards`/`cfg.terminations` (not deleted -- both functions still exist in `rewards.py`/`events.py`, kept in case shank-based gating is revisited; `_KNEE_BODY_CFG` in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` likewise left defined, now otherwise unused).

**Rationale:** for this foot-only task, a legitimate deep lunge/dive inherently brings one knee close to the floor even while the robot is fully balanced -- shank height can't distinguish that from an actual fall. Root/torso height stays high during an intentional lunge (weight stays over the standing leg) and only drops on a genuine loss of balance, which is exactly why the user suspected it as the better signal and why the play-session data apparently confirmed it. No G1 comparison applies here -- neither mechanism has a G1 analog (G1 has no ball-driven lunge/fall termination of this kind; its own fall-prevention is purely `bad_orientation`-style).

**Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v` -- 63/63 pass. Live smoke test on the real multi-disc env (4 envs, `cuda:0`, 20 zero-action steps): confirmed `penalize_baseheight` present and `penalize_kneeheight` absent from `reward_manager.active_terms`; `base_height` present and `shank_height` absent from `termination_manager.active_terms`.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a live training run -- the currently-running `green_jointvel_wrongfootpen_2026-07-22` still has the OLD shank-based gating (this change wasn't hot-applied to a running process); whether/when to stop and restart with this change included is a separate call from the user, not made unilaterally here.

## 2026-07-23 -- v2 branch: reimplemented the two-stage blue/green waypoint mechanism, removed from master's lineage on 2026-07-10

**Context:** master (`green-ball-baseline` lineage) never developed a genuine paced multi-step approach for far balls despite three weeks of AMP/reward-mechanics fixes (see the 2026-07-18 doublestep research doc and everything since). Separately, a `blue-ball-waypoint` branch had existed since before the 2026-07-10 "green-ball-baseline" simplification, containing a two-stage midpoint-then-full-target landing gate plus nine further mechanisms layered on through 2026-07-15, when it was abandoned (user judged the trained checkpoint "hadn't actually learned the task despite high landing-rate diagnostic readings"). User decided to retry the two-stage mechanism, but reimplemented fresh on top of current master rather than merged from the old branch -- master had diverged 74+ commits since the branch split (AMP fixes, reward-weight audits, region routing, wrong-foot gating, base-height gating, postlegdofpos, none of which the old branch has), making a direct merge/cherry-pick impractical.

**Process:** deleted the fully-merged local `green-ball-baseline` branch (0 unique commits vs. master, verified against `origin/green-ball-baseline` too). Created `v2-blue-ball-waypoint` from current master, developed in an isolated git worktree (`interceptV2DualDis-v2blueball/`) so the live `green_baseheight_postleg_2026-07-22` training run on master was never touched. Dispatched a research subagent to map every distinct mechanism the old branch added (10 total) with its own evidence trail, then selected a minimal-confound subset to reimplement based on that report's own assessment of what was well-evidenced vs. unvalidated/confounded/confirmed-negative.

**Ported (reimplemented fresh, not merged):**
- **`_get_reach_target_y`** (new function, `rewards.py`) -- the core two-stage mechanism: on wide crossings (`|crossing_y - start_y| > 0.5` OR `env._region_id in (1, 3)`, this project's own far-region convention, reimplemented in place of the old branch's pre-region-system `_REGION_IS_FAR` tensor), the reach target is the midpoint between robot and true crossing point until the assigned foot (`_get_correct_foot_idx`) genuinely lands there, then switches to the full point. Includes, ported and updated in place rather than as separate mechanisms:
  - **Leaky settle-count decrement** (the old branch's single best-evidenced fix -- 44.8% genuine landing rate at iteration 2000 of `blue_leaky_settle_count_2026-07-13`, vs. near-0% under a hard reset-to-zero counter): a missed frame decrements the settle counter by 1 (floored at 0) instead of zeroing it outright, since the reference clips' own "plant" phase is only ~11 frames wide and a stochastic policy has little margin for one dropped frame under a hard-reset counter.
  - **Curriculum-eased landing thresholds**: `landing_radius`/`landing_speed_threshold` lerp from loose (0.20m / 2.0 m/s) to strict (0.08m / 1.0 m/s) with `env._ball_difficulty`, so a policy that's never landed once has an easy version to discover first.
  - Settle-window memoization guard (this function is called up to 7x/step by different reward terms sharing one post-physics snapshot) and per-landing-event free-classification (`_blue_landed_was_free`, landings achieved before episode step 10 can't reflect real approach work).
- **`footreach`/`foot_proximity`** (`rewards.py`) -- restored two-stage targeting (`_get_reach_target_y` instead of the direct crossing point), plus the old branch's 2026-07-11 assigned-foot-targeting fix (`lateral_error`/`vel_toward` keyed off the actual assigned foot's position/velocity, not root -- a foot, unlike G1's root-relative hand-reach, can have the root sitting near target from stance width alone while the assigned foot itself lags) and the 2026-07-11/07-12 blue decel-zone (decays `footreach`'s speed bonus toward neutral as the foot closes on blue, reaching neutral at the SAME curriculum-eased radius `_get_reach_target_y` used, so nothing rewards carrying speed through the exact zone the foot should be planting in).
- **`blue_ball_landed`/`blue_overshoot_penalty`/`blue_stick_landing`** (new reward functions, `rewards.py`) -- one-shot landing bonus, a penalty for advancing past blue before landing (without it, ignoring the waypoint entirely earns the same zero reward as attempting and failing), and a dense "close AND slow" basin (the only thing giving graded credit for the exact joint condition the settle-window requires). Registered in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` with the same `reward_curriculum_ep_len` curriculum mechanism already used for `footreach`/`stopball`/`softstop` (confirmed master already has the bidirectional-ratchet/EMA-smoothing fix the old branch needed for this pattern to behave correctly -- `_update_smoothed_ep_len`, `events.py:550` -- so this needed no separate porting).
- **`stopball`/`softstop`** (`rewards.py`) -- added the `landing_ok` gate (`~env._blue_wide | (env._blue_landed & ~env._blue_landed_was_free)`) on top of, not replacing, the existing 2026-07-14/07-15 correct-foot-contact gating already on master -- both must hold.

**Explicitly NOT ported** (per the research subagent's own assessment, all considered unvalidated, confounded, or confirmed-negative in the original branch's history): RSI practice-fraction curriculum and RSI teleport-seeding (both never independently tested; teleport-seeding directly caused a reward leak the branch had to patch), landing-payoff scaling (entangled with that same leak for part of its life), AMP clip retiming to 1.5x/2x/2.5x pace (a confirmed NEGATIVE result -- genuine landing rate measurably got worse as retiming pace increased, and the actual fix that helped, leaky settle-count, was blocked by retiming rather than enabled by it), self-imitation learning (produced a headline 96.6%-genuine-landing diagnostic number, but the branch's own final finding cast real doubt on whether that reflected the task actually being learned, via a separate, unrelated wrong-foot-credit bug on narrow crossings that was live the whole time and never validated against a training run after being fixed).

**Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v` -- 63/63 pass. Live smoke test: built the real multi-disc env (4 envs, `cuda:0`), confirmed `blue_ball_landed`/`blue_overshoot_penalty`/`blue_stick_landing` all present in `reward_manager.active_terms`, stepped 30 zero-action steps with no errors.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a live training run -- this is the point of launching one next. Given the original branch's own headline lesson (measurement methodology itself was a repeated source of false signal -- deterministic vs. stochastic policy evaluation, diagnostics that structurally couldn't observe the step they were measuring, metrics mutated multiple times per step with no memoization, at least four separate instances across the branch's history), any new "genuine landing rate" measurement built for this run should get an equivalent audit before being trusted, not assumed correct by analogy to the old branch's (also eventually found to be partly unreliable) diagnostics.

## 2026-07-23 -- both-feet catching and weird post-save arm pose, ported from master: strengthened wrong-foot penalty and arm-recovery reward

**Context:** while `blue_v2_2026-07-23` was running, user reviewed checkpoints from both this run and master's last run (`green_baseheight_postleg_2026-07-22`) and reported post-save orientation/arm pose still "looks really weird" and the policy still catches with both feet. Checked master's own training log data directly: both symptoms were essentially identical between the two branches (`penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` logged value -0.0197 to -0.0222 on master vs. -0.0199 to -0.0206 on v2; `postupperdofpos` 0.016-0.066 on master vs. 0.066-0.066 on v2, both near floor) -- confirmed pre-existing on master, not something the v2 reimplementation introduced or worsened. Full diagnosis and rationale in master's own `docs/BugFixes.md`, same date -- this entry documents the identical fix applied here.

**Fix (ported from master, same commit content):**
- `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` weight -30.0 -> -100.0 (`rewards.py`, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`). No logic bug found (re-verified); -30 wasn't outweighing the save-quality benefit of planting both feet. No G1 equivalent, plain tuning call. Same severity tier as `penalize_self_collision`/`penalize_sharpcontact`/`penalize_baseheight`.
- `postupperdofpos` weight 1.0 -> 5.0 (`rewards.py`, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`). Deliberate G1 divergence (G1 also uses 1.0, confirmed `g1_29_config.py:317`) -- user explicitly confirmed proceeding with this divergence after being asked, since it's not a parity fix. G1's arms are the active/catching limb with task structure pulling them toward rest after use; SGK's arms have no equivalent pull in a foot-only task.

**Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v` -- 63/63 pass.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a live training run -- `blue_v2_2026-07-23` needs to be stopped (checkpoint pushed first) and restarted with this change included.

## 2026-07-23 -- blue_ball_landed never fired despite genuine plants: stopball/softstop read the wrong body entirely (unresolved SceneEntityCfg)

**Context:** user reported (via live `sgk_play` sessions, `--difficulty 0.0`/`--difficulty 0.0 --num-envs 1`) that `blue_ball_landed` would not fire even when the assigned foot visibly planted stationary at the blue midpoint for many consecutive ticks. Two earlier hypotheses were tested and ruled out first:
1. `env._blue_was_airborne` blocking a foot that never technically left the ground -- disabled the requirement, no change, ruled out.
2. `feet_contact`'s "fires on contact with ANYTHING, not just the ground" behavior (documented in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) letting a foot merely touching the ball register as grounded -- attempted a height-above-floor gate on `foot_in_contact`; this made settle stop accumulating **entirely**, even for cases that previously worked, so it was reverted (the exact reason it broke wasn't determined, but the regression was unambiguous and immediate).

**Root cause, found via a raw unconditional per-call print (temporary instrumentation in `rewards.py`/`play.py`, one print per actual call to `_get_reach_target_y` rather than the display-lagged debug fields):** every reward term calls `_get_reach_target_y`, but `stopball` and `softstop` were calling it as `_get_reach_target_y(env, ball_name)` -- no `asset_cfg` argument, silently falling back to that function's own Python-level default parameter object, `_DEFAULT_FEET_CFG`. mjlab's `SceneEntityCfg.body_ids` defaults to `slice(None)` ("all bodies") until `.resolve()` converts `body_names` into real integer IDs -- and mjlab's manager setup (`manager_base.py:_resolve_common_term_cfg`) only calls `.resolve()` on `SceneEntityCfg` objects it finds inside a term's **own registered `params` dict**. `_DEFAULT_FEET_CFG` never appeared in any term's `params` (every other blue-landing-related term registers its own `_FEET_CFG` object instead), so it was never resolved, for the entire run. `stopball`'s internal `body_link_pos_w[:, slice(None), :]` therefore selected every body in the robot, and indexing position 0/1 of that read some arbitrary early body (root/pelvis), not a foot -- a large, consistently-wrong `dist_to_blue` on every single call.

This mattered specifically because `stopball` is registered first in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s reward dict, making it the one call each tick `_get_reach_target_y`'s settle-counter memoization guard (`is_first_call_this_tick`) actually allows to update. Every other term (`footreach`, `foot_proximity`, `blue_ball_landed`, `blue_overshoot_penalty`, `blue_stick_landing`) registers `asset_cfg=_FEET_CFG` correctly and therefore computed the correct `dist_to_blue`/`candidate=True` on the same tick -- but since none of them are the first call, none of them were allowed to increment `settle_count`. Confirmed directly in the raw per-call log: same tick, `stopball` read `dist=0.2882` (candidate=False) while `footreach` read `dist=0.0797` (candidate=True) for what should have been the identical foot position.

**Fix:**
- `rewards.py`: added `asset_cfg: SceneEntityCfg = _DEFAULT_FEET_CFG` parameter to both `stopball` and `softstop`, threaded through to their internal `_get_reach_target_y(env, ball_name, asset_cfg=asset_cfg)` calls (was the bare 2-arg form).
- `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`: added `"asset_cfg": _FEET_CFG` to both `stopball`'s and `softstop`'s registered `params`, so mjlab's manager setup actually resolves it -- matching the pattern every other blue-landing term already used.
- `rewards.py`: `landing_speed_threshold` (in `_get_reach_target_y`) is no longer curriculum-eased (was 2.0 m/s at `ball_difficulty=0` down to 1.0 m/s at difficulty=1). Once the settle counter could genuinely accumulate, a *different* bug became visible: a foot merely striding through the wide, eased radius zone at under jogging pace could satisfy 3+ consecutive settle ticks without ever stopping, since 2.0 (and even 1.0) m/s is not "stopped." Every confirmed genuine plant measured in this investigation showed foot speed 0.002-0.05 m/s, so the threshold is now a flat, curriculum-independent 0.15 m/s -- costs nothing on real plants, closes the pass-through loophole. `landing_radius` is unchanged and still eases 0.20m -> 0.08m with difficulty.

**Evidence:** live `sgk_play` traces (temporary raw per-call prints, `[RAWSETTLE] ep_len=... caller=... cand=... first_call=... settle_before=... settle_after=...` plus a full component breakdown `wide/airborne/contact/distOk/dist/radius`) showing, across two independent runs (`--num-envs 1` and default batch size, ruling out batching as a factor): `stopball`'s call always `first_call=True` with `cand=False`, every subsequent same-tick call `cand=True`, `settle` frozen at its pre-episode value regardless. After the fix, settle correctly progressed 1->2->3 and `blue_ball_landed` fired on a genuine stationary plant.

**Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v` -- 63/63 pass. An isolated mock harness (calls the real `_get_reach_target_y` directly with scripted foot trajectories, 7x/tick matching the real reward-manager call pattern) confirmed the settle/memoization logic itself was always correct in isolation -- the bug only reproduced with the real task's actual `SceneEntityCfg` registration, which the mock didn't exercise.

**Not yet resolved:** temporary debug instrumentation (`env._blue_dbg_*` fields in `rewards.py`, the `BLUE ...`/`[RAWSETTLE]` printing in `play.py`) is still in place and should be removed once the fix is confirmed stable across more play sessions. Not yet validated against a live training run -- `blue_v2_2026-07-23` (or its successor) should be restarted with this fix included, since `stopball`/`softstop`'s landing-gate behavior during training was affected the same way as in play.

---

## 2026-07-23 (later same day) -- landing_speed_threshold's flat hardcode (previous entry) removed the curriculum entirely; restored with a narrower easy end

**Context:** the immediately preceding fix (this file, same date) correctly closed the pass-through loophole by making `landing_speed_threshold` a flat `0.15` m/s regardless of `env._ball_difficulty`, but in doing so also removed ANY curriculum easing for this threshold -- a policy at `ball_difficulty=0` now had to satisfy the exact same strict speed gate as a fully-difficulty-saturated one, with only `landing_radius` (0.20m -> 0.08m) still easing. User asked for this reimplemented: keep the hard-difficulty (d=1) end at the current, evidence-based `0.15` m/s, but do not restore the OLD easy end (2.0 m/s at d=0, easing to 1.0 m/s at d=1) -- that range is the exact one already confirmed to leak.

**Fix (`rewards.py:_get_reach_target_y`):**
- `landing_speed_threshold` now lerps again: `0.30 + (0.15 - 0.30) * d` -- i.e. 0.30 m/s at `ball_difficulty=0` down to 0.15 m/s at `difficulty=1`. 0.30 is a deliberately conservative choice, not re-derived from new evidence: it sits well below the 1.0-2.0 m/s band already confirmed to let a foot stride through the eased radius zone without stopping, while still giving an early policy a real (2x) easier bar than the strict end. Genuine plants measured 0.002-0.05 m/s in the original investigation, so both ends remain generous toward real plants.
- The function's own `landing_speed_threshold` default parameter (read by every caller that doesn't override it -- `stopball`/`softstop`/`blue_ball_landed`/`blue_overshoot_penalty`/`blue_stick_landing` all call with the 2-arg-plus-`asset_cfg` form) was also updated from a stale `1.0` (a leftover from the pre-flat-hardcode scheme) to `0.15`, since it's now read as the strict/hard-difficulty end of the restored lerp.
- Added `env._blue_landing_speed_threshold_current` (mirrors the existing `env._blue_landing_radius_current` cache), set unconditionally right after the lerp, before the robot/feet_contact-dependent block -- lets tests (and any future caller) read the resolved threshold without mocking the full contact-sensor/entity scene.

**Verification:** new `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_landing_speed_threshold_curriculum.py` (4 tests: strict value at d=1, eased value at d=0, monotonic lerp across d, regression guard that no difficulty level reaches the old 1.0/2.0 m/s leaky values). Full suite: `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -v` -- 67/67 pass (63 pre-existing + 4 new).

**Not yet resolved:** the 0.30 m/s easy-end choice is a conservative engineering judgment, not independently validated against the original pass-through failure mode -- if a live run still shows false-positive landings at low difficulty, the easy end should be tightened further. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-07-24 -- landing_speed_threshold/landing_radius reverted looser + `success` was bypassing the landing gate entirely

**Context:** `blue_v2_landinggatefix_2026-07-23` (the run launched with the previous two entries' fixes) was monitored over iterations 0-6700. `blue_ball_landed` peaked around 0.041 (iteration ~1700) then declined steadily to 0.007, while `blue_overshoot_penalty` worsened from ~0 to roughly -1.0 to -1.4 and `blue_stick_landing` kept climbing to 0.65-0.68 -- landed and stick-landing diverging rather than tracking together. User separately asked whether `success` (G1-ported continuing reward) could fire without visiting blue first.

**Investigation:**
1. Compared against `blue_v2_wrongfootarm_2026-07-23` (the prior run, pre-asset_cfg-fix, stopped at iteration 2058): that run showed the *same* landed/stick-landing/overshoot divergence pattern at a similar magnitude by its own final iteration (`blue_ball_landed` 0.025-0.028, `blue_overshoot_penalty` -0.74 to -0.77, `blue_stick_landing` 0.75-0.76) -- so the divergence predates the asset_cfg fix and isn't something introduced by `landinggatefix`'s changes.
2. Read `success` (`rewards.py`): `return (success_flag.float() + 1.0) * (dist < strict_th).float()`. `dist` targets the true/final `crossing_point` directly (frozen far `crossing_y`, or live ball position when close) -- never the blue midpoint -- and, unlike `stopball`/`softstop`, has no `landing_ok` check anywhere. Even with `success_flag` (`_sb_flag`) still `False`, the base term `1.0 * (dist < 0.15)` fires on its own, continuously, for any env whose assigned foot gets within 0.15m of the real target -- regardless of whether it ever visited blue. Weight is curriculum-ramped 5.0 -> 12.5. This is a real gap: the whole point of `stopball`/`softstop`'s `landing_ok` gate was to force genuine blue-first behavior, but `success` (ported from G1 in the same wave) never got the same gate, giving an ungated, continuously-accumulating alternate payoff for skipping straight to the final target on wide crossings.

**Fix (user-directed, three changes, `rewards.py`):**
1. **`success` landing gate:** added `_get_reach_target_y(env, ball_name, asset_cfg=asset_cfg)` at the top of `success` (mirrors `stopball`/`softstop`'s own call, memoized per physics tick so this is a cheap no-op on the settle counter) and multiplied the return by `landing_ok = ~env._blue_wide | (env._blue_landed & ~env._blue_landed_was_free)` -- the identical gate `stopball`/`softstop` use. Narrow crossings are unaffected (`landing_ok` always `True` there); on wide crossings, `success` can now only fire once a genuine blue landing has occurred, same as the save-trigger terms.
2. **`landing_radius` widened 0.08m -> 0.15m** (strict end, at `ball_difficulty=1`; easy end stays 0.20m at d=0): user reported the strict end was too tight to reliably land in even for a reasonably-competent approach. Affects `_get_reach_target_y`'s shared radius, so `blue_ball_landed`/`blue_overshoot_penalty`/`blue_stick_landing` all inherit the looser criterion consistently.
3. **`landing_speed_threshold` reverted to 2.0 m/s (d=0) -> 1.0 m/s (d=1)**, undoing the 2026-07-23 tightening to 0.30/0.15 m/s. This deliberately reintroduces the documented pass-through leak (a foot striding through the loose radius zone at up to 1.0 m/s can register as "landed" without a genuine stop) as a direct trade: with the 0.30/0.15 band, `blue_ball_landed` kept declining with no sign of recovery over ~6700 iterations, suggesting the strict end was suppressing the landing gradient outright rather than just filtering false positives. Combined with the radius widening above, the bet is that a looser combined gate gives the policy enough signal to discover genuine landings again; if `blue_ball_landed` still doesn't recover, or the pass-through failure mode reappears (foot striding through without stopping, visible via `sgk_play` traces), the next step should be a smoother curriculum shape (more difficulty steps between eased and strict ends) rather than re-tightening back to the exact 0.30/0.15 values.

**Verification:** `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_landing_speed_threshold_curriculum.py` rewritten in place (same 4 tests, values updated to match the reverted 2.0/1.0 m/s band; the old `never_reaches_old_leaky_values` regression guard is intentionally replaced with a range-pin test since 1.0/2.0 m/s is now the current, deliberate band). Full suite: `uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 67/67 pass. No other tests referenced the old hardcoded 0.08/0.15/0.30 values.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet validated against a live training run -- `blue_v2_landinggatefix_2026-07-23` needs to be stopped (checkpoint pushed first) and restarted with these three changes included. Whether the looser radius/speed combination actually recovers `blue_ball_landed` without reintroducing visible pass-through landings is an open question this restart is meant to answer.

---

## 2026-07-24 -- HOME_KEYFRAME default pose was a one-sided "right arm counterbalance" stance, not symmetric

**Context:** user noticed `sgk_play` resets the robot into a static, visibly asymmetric pose (right arm swung out ~61 deg) every single episode and asked why it wasn't a plain T-pose or symmetric stance, and whether it matched training's own reset.

**Investigation:** `t1_constants.py:HOME_KEYFRAME` (`joint_pos`) sets `Right_Shoulder_Roll=1.07` rad (~61 deg, arm abducted out to the side) vs `Left_Shoulder_Roll=-0.41` rad (~23 deg) -- a real magnitude asymmetry, not just a sign-convention artifact. Checked `t1_headless.xml` joint axes to rule out a sign-convention illusion: `Left_Shoulder_Pitch`/`Right_Shoulder_Pitch` and `Left_Elbow_Pitch`/`Right_Elbow_Pitch` share axis `"0 1 0"` with identical (non-mirrored) ranges on both sides, so equal numeric values already give a mirror-symmetric pose for those joints. `Shoulder_Roll` (axis `"1 0 0"`) and `Elbow_Yaw` (axis `"0 0 1"`) instead have ranges mirrored between sides (e.g. `Left_Shoulder_Roll` range `-1.74..1.57`, `Right_Shoulder_Roll` range `-1.57..1.74`), meaning a mirror-symmetric pose for those two joints requires equal magnitude but **opposite sign**, not equal value. Confirmed the asymmetry was real (not an encoding quirk): right arm's roll magnitude (1.07) is over 2.5x the left's (0.41), with no G1/upstream justification found for this project's feet-only track -- most likely a leftover from G1's hand-catching reach pose, which does not apply here since `SimpleGoalKeeper`/this track never uses hands (Phase 1 scope).

Separately confirmed this same base pose feeds BOTH training and play, not just play: `HOME_KEYFRAME` is the entity's `init_state`, which (a) sets the initial mjSpec keyframe, (b) is what `reset_robot_joints` (`mjlab`'s `velocity_env_cfg.py`, `reset_joints_by_offset` with `position_range=(0,0)`) resets to on every episode reset before any RSI event runs, and (c) is the `default_joint_pos` that `reset_from_motion_data`'s 20%-branch (non-RSI) scales/offsets around (`randomize_initial_joint_pos` mechanism, see the RSI mechanism divergence row in `CLAUDE.md`). So training's 20% fallback branch was also built on top of this same asymmetric base pose, just with `scale x U(0.5,1.5) + offset U(-0.1,0.1)` randomization layered on -- play (RSI off by default) shows it raw and identical every episode, training (RSI 80% of the time) mostly hides it behind mocap-frame resets.

**Fix (`t1_constants.py:HOME_KEYFRAME`):** mirrored the smaller-magnitude (left) arm values onto the right side rather than inventing new numbers, to minimize behavior change beyond removing the asymmetry itself:
- `Shoulder_Pitch`: both sides now `-0.21` (was Left=-0.21, Right=-0.11) -- combined into one regex entry since pitch is same-sign symmetric.
- `Shoulder_Roll`: `Left=-0.41`, `Right=+0.41` (was Right=1.07) -- equal magnitude, opposite sign per the mirrored-axis convention above.
- `Elbow_Pitch`: both sides now `-0.13` (was Left unset/0, Right=-0.13) -- combined into one regex entry.
- `Elbow_Yaw`: `Left=-0.21` (was unset/0), `Right=+0.21` (unchanged) -- equal magnitude, opposite sign.

This is a single-source-of-truth fix (`t1_constants.py` only) -- no changes needed in `play.py` or `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, since both the play-mode static reset and training's RSI-fallback branch already derive from this same `HOME_KEYFRAME`.

**Verification:** `python3 -c "import ast; ast.parse(...)"` syntax check only. Not yet visually confirmed in the mjlab viewer, and not yet validated against a live training run -- this changes the base pose training's 20%-RSI-fallback branch samples around, so an in-progress run's fallback-branch resets will look different (small pose shift only, not a distributional shift in RSI's dominant 80% mocap-frame path) once resumed; a fresh run is not required, but any currently-running job's checkpoint should be pushed before restarting if a clean comparison is wanted.
