## 2026-07-24 -- left_far double-step clips drifted ~4.5 deg toward +X instead of pure +Y

**Context:** user visually spotted (via the `-MultiDisc-WithOverlay` ghost overlay, per the far-region viz commands given earlier this session) that all 3 `left_far` reference clips travel slightly toward +X instead of staying purely lateral (+Y), and asked for an anti-clockwise rotation of roughly 10 deg to correct it. `right_far`'s clips were not reported as a problem.

**Investigation:** measured each clip's net Trunk (`body_pos_w[:,0,:]`) displacement from frame 0 to the last frame, before touching anything:
- `new_doublestepleft_short/base/wide_booster_t1.npz`: displacement angle from pure +Y = **+4.52 deg** (toward +X), identical across all 3 scale variants (expected -- `wide`/`short` are root-position-only rescales of `base`, per the 2026-07-18 clip-creation entry, so they inherit the same directional bias).
- `new_doublestepright_short/base/wide_booster_t1.npz`: displacement angle from pure -Y = **-0.5 deg** -- confirmed already clean, matching the user's report that only `left_far` needed correction.

Root cause not re-derived from the raw PKL pipeline (`pkl_to_npz.py` only snaps *frame-0 facing yaw* to exactly 0 deg -- `convert_one`'s "Snap frame-0 yaw" step, line ~179 -- which is a different quantity from the *net travel direction* over the whole clip; a mocap clip can start facing exactly +X and still walk on a slightly diagonal path if the underlying steps drifted). Fixed as a direct one-time correction on the existing NPZ data rather than by re-deriving/modifying the raw-PKL converter, since only these 3 already-generated files needed it and re-running the full leveling/cropping/hip-straightening pipeline that produced them (2026-07-18, commit `36fc902`) was out of scope.

**Fix (one-off script, not committed as a permanent tool -- ran inline, originals backed up to scratchpad first):** for each of the 3 `new_doublestepleft_*` NPZ files, computed the exact per-file correction angle as `atan2(disp_x, disp_y)` of the Trunk displacement (all 3 measured `+4.517 deg`, confirming the user's direction call and refining their `~10 deg` estimate to the precise zeroing value), then applied a rigid world-frame Z-axis rotation by that angle to every field that lives in world frame:
- `body_pos_w`: rotated the XY components about the frame-0 Trunk XY position as pivot (keeps the clip's start point fixed in place, only bends the travel direction) -- **not** about the world origin, which would have translated the whole clip sideways.
- `body_quat_w`: left-multiplied every body's quaternion by the same-angle Z-axis correction quaternion (matches `pkl_to_npz.py`'s own convention for its frame-0 yaw snap).
- `body_lin_vel_w`/`body_ang_vel_w`: rotated as free vectors (no pivot subtraction) by the same 2D (linear) / Z-invariant-3D (angular) rotation.
- `joint_pos`/`joint_vel`/`fps` untouched (local joint angles and timing are unaffected by a global root reorientation).

**Verification:** re-measured post-fix Trunk displacement for all 3 files -- X component now `+0.0000`/`+0.0000`/`-0.0000` m (was `+0.055`/`+0.072`/`+0.102` m), Y component unchanged in magnitude (`+0.70`/`+0.91`/`+1.30` m). Confirmed `body_quat_w` still unit-norm after the correction (min/max norm `0.9999999`/`1.0` across all 3 files) and that `fps`/array shapes/key set are unchanged. **Not yet visually re-confirmed in the mjlab viewer** -- user should re-run the far-region visualization commands to eyeball the corrected clips before this is considered fully verified.

---

## 2026-07-24 (later same day) -- `_ball_is_behind` never tripped on wide crossings that never satisfied the landing gate, letting footreach/foot_proximity/foot_inner_face_continuous/foot_clearance farm indefinitely

**Context:** user shared a raw `sgk_play` episode log (Ep 26, steps 34-48) and asked why total reward kept climbing (4.16 -> 23.96 over ~15 steps) on an episode that looked like the robot was ignoring the blue waypoint. The log showed `dvx` (ball delta-vx since spawn) already at +4.2 to +4.5 -- far past `stopball`'s own 0.6 m/s threshold -- with `contact=True`, `foot=0` (the assigned/correct foot), and `touchingBall=True` repeatedly, yet `SB·`/`SS·` (stopball/softstop) never fired the whole episode, and `footreach` alone climbed from 5.10 to 21.30 across the same steps while `blue dist` stayed 0.24-0.30 (never under the 0.15 `landing_radius`, `settle=0/3` the entire time).

**Root cause (`rewards.py`):** the 2026-07-23 v2 landing-gate work (`landing_ok = ~env._blue_wide | (env._blue_landed & ~env._blue_landed_was_free)`) was correctly added to `stopball`'s and `softstop`'s `fired`/`deflection_now` conditions, so the one-shot save bonus can only pay out after a genuine blue-waypoint landing on wide crossings -- that part works as designed. But `_ball_is_behind()` (the shutoff gate for every continuous approach reward: `footreach`, `foot_proximity`, `foot_inner_face_continuous`, `foot_clearance`) has no independent signal of its own -- it only reads `env._sb_flag`/`env._softstop_flag`, i.e. exactly the flags that are now landing_ok-gated. On a wide crossing that never satisfies the strict settle-window + 0.15m radius (common -- the criterion is intentionally strict), `landing_ok` stays `False` for the whole episode, so `_sb_flag`/`_softstop_flag` never set, so `_ball_is_behind()` never returns `True`, so the four continuous rewards above never shut off -- even though the ball is being hit hard and repeatedly by the correct foot. `footreach` in particular carries a velocity multiplier (`vel_sigma`, up to 10x) that spikes on every swing back toward the target, which is why it was the dominant runaway term (5.10 -> 21.30) while `foot_proximity`/`foot_inner_face_continuous`/`foot_clearance` stayed smaller per-step but still accumulated across the same un-gated steps. Net effect: a policy that repeatedly whacks the ball near (never landing at) the blue target earns far more reward per episode than one that lands cleanly and collects the one-shot save bonuses, which are capped and fire once.

Two different questions were incorrectly sharing one flag: "should the one-shot save bonus pay out" (correctly requires a genuine landing) vs. "should continuous approach reward stop" (should only require a genuine hard hit, landing or not -- this is what the pre-v2 code did, per `_ball_is_behind`'s own docstring: "stopping the farm-footreach-post-stopball exploit").

**Fix (`rewards.py`):**
1. `stopball` now also computes `raw_contact_now = (delta_vx > delta_vel_threshold) & in_front & correct_foot_contact` -- the same condition as before, but WITHOUT the `landing_ok` AND -- and latches it into a new sticky flag `env._raw_deflection_flag` (reset on `just_reset`, same pattern as `_sb_flag`). `deflection_now` (used for the actual one-shot bonus wiring, unchanged in effect) is now `raw_contact_now & landing_ok`.
2. `_ball_is_behind()` now ORs in `env._raw_deflection_flag` alongside the existing `_softstop_flag`/`_sb_flag` checks. Since `stopball` is registered first each tick (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, confirmed via the reward-term dict order -- same ordering fact already documented in `stopball`'s/`softstop`'s own docstrings for `_get_reach_target_y`'s settle counter), `_raw_deflection_flag` is fresh by the time `footreach`/`foot_proximity`/`foot_inner_face_continuous`/`foot_clearance` (all registered later, all calling `_ball_is_behind`) run in the same tick.

This restores "shut off once genuinely hit" as `_ball_is_behind`'s job while leaving `landing_ok` doing its own, separate job of gating the one-shot save bonus on a genuine blue landing -- no change to `stopball`/`softstop`'s own firing conditions or to the landing-gate mechanism itself.

**Verification:** `python3 -c "import ast; ast.parse(...)"` syntax check only -- no dedicated reward unit tests exist for `_ball_is_behind`/`stopball` in `tests/simple_goalkeeper/` to run. Not yet validated against a live training run or a fresh `sgk_play` session reproducing the original Ep 26 scenario.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet confirmed live that a wide-crossing episode with repeated correct-foot contact but no clean landing now correctly shows `footreach`/`foot_proximity`/`foot_inner_face_continuous`/`foot_clearance` dropping to 0 after the first hard hit, nor that this doesn't regress genuine multi-touch saves (e.g. a foot that legitimately needs 2-3 touches to fully arrest the ball before settling) by cutting their reward off too early. If multi-touch saves turn out to need continued `footreach` signal after the first hit, the fix should be narrowed (e.g. require `delta_vx` past a higher, more decisive threshold, or require `env._blue_wide` specifically) rather than reverted outright.

**REVERTED same day, before any training run used it.** Implemented without first walking the user through the exact mechanism (violates this project's own Change Approval Workflow -- list changes and get explicit sign-off before applying). On review, the user rejected the underlying design choice, not just the omission: landing at the blue waypoint is a hard task requirement, so a hard correct-foot hit that does NOT land cleanly should NOT be treated as "the approach is over" -- doing so removes all further reward pressure to actually go complete the landing after one bad touch, which undermines the "must land at blue" requirement even though it does also stop the runaway-farming symptom. `_ball_is_behind()` and `stopball()` were both reverted to their pre-fix form (this file's diff above described the now-reverted change; `_raw_deflection_flag` no longer exists). The underlying farming bug (footreach/foot_proximity/foot_inner_face_continuous/foot_clearance never shutting off on a wide crossing that never lands) is UNFIXED and still open -- next attempt should preserve continued landing pressure after a failed touch, e.g. only treat the ball as "behind" once it has physically left the blue zone without landing (not merely been touched), or bound `footreach`'s own reward magnitude (e.g. clip `vel_sigma`/add per-episode decay) rather than changing `_ball_is_behind`'s semantics at all.

---

## 2026-07-24 (later same day) -- `env._blue_landed`'s "free" (suspiciously-early) classification was only honored by the one-shot save bonus, not by the targeting/dense-reward logic that actually decides blue-vs-real-ball

**Context:** user re-shared the same Ep 26 log from the previous (reverted) entry and asked, more specifically, what reward mechanism is responsible for the observed EPISODE-LEVEL pattern: some episodes correctly double-step through the blue waypoint, others appear to skip it entirely and go straight for the real ball, with `SB·`/`SS·` never firing either way. Asked to check every reward term, not guess.

**Investigation:** re-read `_get_reach_target_y`, `footreach`, `foot_proximity`, `blue_ball_landed`, `blue_overshoot_penalty`, `blue_stick_landing`, and confirmed `_get_ball_crossing_y` (the full/real target) is frozen at episode reset via `env._rsi_cross_y`, not recomputed live from the ball's post-deflection trajectory -- ruling out a live-recompute explanation. In the specific pasted Ep 26 log, `target_xy` is numerically identical (blue midpoint) across every printed step, and `wide=True`/`landed=False` hold the whole window -- so for that specific episode, the earlier (reverted) `_ball_is_behind` farming theory was the operative mechanism, not this one. But the user's broader question (bimodal double-step-vs-skip-blue behavior across DIFFERENT episodes) pointed at something else.

Grepped every consumer of `env._blue_landed` across `rewards.py` and found: `env._blue_landed_was_free` (flags a landing achieved suspiciously early, `episode_length_buf < 10` -- almost certainly RSI seeding the reset with a donor foot trajectory already near the target from the mocap clip itself, not genuine approach work; RSI's own motion-pool selection is side/distance-matched to the target by design, making this plausible on a meaningful fraction of resets) was being read by exactly ONE consumer: `landing_ok` (`stopball`/`softstop`/`success`'s one-shot save-bonus gate). Every OTHER consumer of `env._blue_landed` -- `_get_reach_target_y`'s own `phase1_active` (the actual half_y-vs-full_y target switch), `footreach`'s `ball_close` and `blue_approach` (decel-zone), `foot_proximity`'s `ball_close`, `blue_overshoot_penalty`'s `phase1_active`, `blue_stick_landing`'s `phase1_active`, and `blue_ball_landed`'s own one-shot `fired` -- read the RAW `env._blue_landed` with no `was_free` exclusion. So a free landing (RSI luck, not skill) would permanently flip the entire episode's targeting to the real/full crossing point from as early as step ~3-9, skip the blue phase completely for the rest of the episode (blue_overshoot_penalty/blue_stick_landing going silent, footreach/foot_proximity switching to live-ball tracking), and even pay out `blue_ball_landed`'s own bonus for a landing that was never earned -- while `stopball`/`softstop` alone correctly kept refusing to pay the save bonus (matching the observed `SB·`/`SS·` never firing in "skip blue" episodes). This directly explains the reported bimodal pattern: which behavior an episode shows depends on whether that episode's particular reset happened to trip a free landing, not on anything the policy learned.

**Fix (`rewards.py`), user-approved:**
1. Added `env._blue_landed_genuine` (initialized alongside the other per-episode blue-mechanism state, reset on `just_reset`), computed once in `_get_reach_target_y` right before `phase1_active` as `env._blue_landed & ~env._blue_landed_was_free` -- single source of truth for "landed AND that landing wasn't free."
2. Replaced every bare `env._blue_landed` read in the six sites above with `env._blue_landed_genuine`: `_get_reach_target_y`'s `phase1_active`, `footreach`'s `ball_close`/`blue_approach`, `foot_proximity`'s `ball_close`, `blue_ball_landed`'s `fired`, `blue_overshoot_penalty`'s `phase1_active`, `blue_stick_landing`'s `phase1_active`.
3. Deduped `landing_ok` in `stopball`/`softstop`/`success` (previously each independently recomputed `env._blue_landed & ~env._blue_landed_was_free` inline) to read the same cached `env._blue_landed_genuine` instead -- identical value, no behavior change, just removes the triplicated expression now that it's computed once upstream (all three call `_get_reach_target_y` first each tick, same pattern already relied on elsewhere in this file).

Net effect: a free/illegitimate landing no longer switches targeting away from blue, no longer silences `blue_overshoot_penalty`/`blue_stick_landing`, and no longer pays `blue_ball_landed`'s bonus -- the entire blue mechanism (not just the save bonus) now requires a genuine, non-free landing before considering blue "done," consistent across all seven sites.

**Verification:** `python3 -c "import ast; ast.parse(...)"` syntax check only. Confirmed via grep that all seven `landing_ok`/`phase1_active`/`ball_close`/`blue_approach`/`fired` sites in `rewards.py` now read `env._blue_landed_genuine` and that every call site already invokes `_get_reach_target_y` (which sets it) before reading it -- no ordering hazard. No dedicated reward unit tests exist in `tests/simple_goalkeeper/` to run against this. Not yet validated against a live training run or a fresh `sgk_play` session confirming free-landing episodes now correctly stay in the blue phase.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet confirmed live that this actually eliminates the "skip blue and go for the real ball" episode pattern, nor whether a genuinely-landed-but-still-`was_free`-classified episode (i.e. a free landing that WOULD have been a real save had the robot kept going) is now handled reasonably -- `env._blue_landed_genuine` treats any free landing as equivalent to "not landed yet," so the robot still has to earn a fresh, non-free landing later in the same episode; if that turns out to be too strict (e.g. a free landing that's geometrically identical to a real one gets needlessly re-litigated), the fix should distinguish "free AND still there" from "free AND the foot then moved away," rather than being reverted outright. Separately, this fix is independent of and does not address the still-open, reverted farming bug from the previous entry (`_ball_is_behind` never tripping on a hard hit that doesn't land) -- both can be true in the same episode.

---

## 2026-07-24 -- new `blue_trunk_drive` reward: trunk (whole-body) velocity incentive missing between a genuine blue landing and the ball closing in

**Context:** user asked to add "something like an acceleration term after blue ball is landed... to lead the robot towards the green ball" -- framed as the same kind of horizontal-velocity incentive `footreach` already gives once the ball is close, but for the (potentially much longer) window right after a genuine blue landing on a wide crossing, before the ball actually gets close. Follow-up clarified this should be a **separate reward from `footreach`**, based on **trunk velocity**, not foot velocity, and should have three phases: accelerate toward blue, decelerate near blue, accelerate again toward green after landing.

**Investigation:** read `footreach` (`rewards.py`) fully. Its only velocity-based term is `vel_sigma` (`1.0 + 3.0*clamp(vel_toward, 0, 3)`), which multiplies the phase-2 sigmoid reach reward -- but phase 2 only activates when `ball_x_local <= 1.5m` (`phase1_mask = ball_x_local > 1.5`); before that, `footreach` uses `phase1_rew`, a pure static lateral-alignment reward with **no velocity term at all**. On a wide crossing, a genuine blue landing (`env._blue_landed_genuine`, see the immediately preceding entry) typically happens while the ball is still well past 1.5m out -- blue exists precisely so the assigned foot can plant early, then travel further to green -- so the entire "landed at blue, ball still far" window had zero velocity-based incentive pulling the robot toward green. Separately confirmed `blue_overshoot_penalty`/`blue_stick_landing` were NOT the blocker here -- both already correctly zero out once `env._blue_landed_genuine` is true (`phase1_active` gate), so nothing was actively holding the robot at blue after a real landing; the gap was purely an absence of a positive pull toward green, not an active penalty against moving.

Also confirmed `footreach`'s existing velocity term is explicitly foot-specific by design (its own docstring: uses the task-ASSIGNED foot's Y position/velocity, not root, because "the root can sit near the target purely from stance width/weight-shift while the assigned foot itself overshoots or lags"). The user's request for a **trunk**-based term is therefore a genuinely different signal, not a duplicate of `footreach`'s existing mechanism -- it drives whole-body translation (real walking) toward the target, independent of what any single foot is doing, addressing the failure mode where a foot can "reach" via leg extension without the trunk actually traveling (the exact failure mode far regions need to avoid for genuine multi-step gaits).

**Fix (`rewards.py:blue_trunk_drive`, new function; wired in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` and `mdp/__init__.py`), user-approved (recommended implementation option chosen from two presented):**
- Computes `robot.data.root_link_pos_w[:, 1]` / `root_link_lin_vel_w[:, 1]` (world-frame trunk Y position/velocity) against `_get_reach_target_y`'s own live target (blue while `wide & ~landed`, green once `wide & landed` -- same single source of truth `footreach`/`stopball`/`softstop` all share).
- `vel_toward` computed the same way as `footreach`'s (sign-flipped by which side the target is on), clamped to `[0, 3]` m/s -- same clamp range as `footreach`'s `vel_sigma`, but used as a direct additive reward (not a multiplier), since this term has no separate proximity reward underneath it to protect.
- **Blue decel-zone**: reuses `footreach`'s exact mechanism and constants (`_BLUE_DECEL_ZONE=0.30`, `env._blue_landing_radius_current`) to decay the velocity bonus as the trunk closes on blue during the unlanded approach phase -- but decays to **0**, not `footreach`'s neutral 1x floor, since there's no base proximity reward to preserve here; zero velocity bonus at zero distance is literally "stop completely at blue," matching the requested "then decelerate" phase directly.
- **After a genuine landing** (`env._blue_landed_genuine`): no decay applied -- full velocity-toward-green reward, mirroring `footreach`'s own phase-2 behavior (which also doesn't decay near the final target; carrying speed into the actual save is correct, only the artificial blue pause point needs a deliberate stop). This is the "accelerate again" phase.
- Zero on narrow crossings (`env._blue_wide` always False -- no two-stage target exists to drive toward; `footreach`'s own `vel_sigma` already covers near-region locomotion) and once `_ball_is_behind` (mirrors `footreach`'s own behind-gate).
- Registered with static `weight=5.0` and a `blue_trunk_drive_curriculum` entry using the same `reward_curriculum_ep_len` shape as the other `blue_*` terms (base 5.0 → max 10.0 at cu=3) -- without this curriculum, this term would stay fixed while `footreach`/`blue_ball_landed`/etc. all grow, the same asymmetry problem the `blue_ball_landed_curriculum` comment already documents for this term family. **The weight (5.0) is an initial, unvalidated guess, not derived from any tuning pass** -- picked as a moderate complementary-signal magnitude relative to `footreach`'s own 10→20, since this term is meant to supplement, not replace, the foot-specific incentive.

**Verification:** added to `mdp/__init__.py`'s explicit `rewards` import list (this project uses an explicit re-export list, not `import *` -- omitting this would have caused an `AttributeError` from `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s `gk_mdp.blue_trunk_drive` reference at env-build time, not at module-import time). Confirmed via a live Python import that `goalkeeper_multidisc_env_cfg(play=False)` builds successfully with `"blue_trunk_drive"` present in both `cfg.rewards` and `cfg.curriculum`. Full test suite (`uv run pytest tests/ -q`) -- 67/67 pass, no regressions (this project has no dedicated reward-value unit tests to specifically exercise the new term's math). **Not yet visually confirmed in `sgk_play`, and not yet validated against a live training run** -- this is a new mechanism and new weight with no empirical basis yet.

---

## 2026-07-24 (later same day) -- `footreach`'s `vel_sigma` rewards ball-collision impulse velocity as if it were deliberate reach speed, letting a foot that overshoots blue farm a reward spike on every dive/touch

**Context:** user shared a fresh `sgk_play` episode (ep_len 43-50, checkpoint-independent -- confirmed reproducible regardless of which model was loaded) showing `footreach` spiking 5.07 -> 12.97 -> 20.79 -> 28.12 across four consecutive ticks while `BLUE dist` stayed essentially flat (0.37 -> 0.38 -> 0.38 -> 0.37, i.e. NOT converging on blue at all), then crashing back to 8.60 the instant `contact=True` first appeared and `foot_ang_vel_xy` spiked to -10.58 (from a baseline of -0.1 to -0.5) with `feet_slippage` simultaneously crashing from 2.65 to 0.13 -- both independent signatures of a hard, uncontrolled physical impact (violent foot rotation, high slip velocity), not a controlled plant.

**Root cause (`rewards.py:footreach`):** `vel_sigma` (the up-to-10x speed multiplier on `reach_rew`) is built from `assigned_foot_vel_y`, read via `robot.data.body_link_lin_vel_w` -- this is the foot's POST-physics-step velocity, which unavoidably includes the impulse from any collision that happened during that step, not just the policy's own deliberate motion. Since `dist_to_blue` stayed flat across the spike (0.37-0.38 the whole window), the climbing `footreach` value could only be coming from `vel_sigma` inflating -- confirming the ramp was velocity-driven, not position-driven -- and the crash exactly coincident with two independent hard-impact signals (`foot_ang_vel_xy`, `feet_slippage`) confirms the peak (28.12) was the collision's own velocity spike getting picked up and amplified, not genuine fast approach. The existing "blue decel-zone" (which decays `vel_sigma` toward neutral near blue) doesn't catch this: at `dist_to_blue=0.37-0.38`, the foot was already OUTSIDE the decel zone's own `_BLUE_DECEL_ZONE=0.30` radius, so `decay_frac` was clamped to 1.0 (no decay applied at all) -- the exploit specifically lives in the band beyond the decel zone but still within `footreach`'s `reach_th=0.3` "close enough to matter" halo.

**G1 comparison (per this project's Design Rule -- verify against Humanoid-Goalkeeper before any reward change):** checked every one of G1's ~24 `_reward_*` functions in `legged_robot.py` for a zero-out-on-distance/overshoot pattern. None exists. `_reward_eereach`'s `behind` mask only sets a fixed `vel_sigma=2.0` (continues rewarding, never zeros); the `post*` reward family's `behind` gates turn rewards ON after a save, the opposite direction; `stop_flag`/`success_flag` are one-shot latches preventing a bonus from firing twice, not continuous-reward killers. G1 has no intermediate waypoint at all (single direct target), so an "overshot the waypoint" concept has no G1 analog to begin with -- consistent with this project's existing divergence-table entry for the whole two-stage blue/green mechanism (already documented as N/A in G1). This fix is therefore an SGK-only construct, same category as the rest of the blue mechanism, not a port.

**Fix (`rewards.py:footreach`):**
- Added `env._footreach_overshot_flag` (bool, one per env, reset on `episode_length_buf <= 1`, same reset pattern as `_sb_flag`/`_blue_landed`/etc.).
- Computes `signed_progress` using the IDENTICAL direction-aware formula `blue_overshoot_penalty` already uses (`direction = sign(full_y - start_y)`, `signed_progress = direction * (assigned_foot_y - half_y)`) -- so "overshot" means the same physical thing in both places, not two independently-tuned definitions.
- Latches the flag (sticky for the rest of the episode, never un-set except at reset) once `env._blue_wide & ~env._blue_landed_genuine & (signed_progress > 0.20)`. Threshold reasoning: `blue_overshoot_penalty`'s own deadband (0.08) is far too tight to use here -- it's meant to start a penalty ramp, not declare a miss, and would trip on ordinary exploration jitter. 0.20 was chosen as (a) comfortably below both observed exploit ranges (0.17-0.26 and 0.17-0.29 in the two shared logs, so it reliably catches them) and (b) matching the loosest/easy-curriculum end of the actual landing radius (`env._blue_landing_radius_current`'s 0.20m ceiling) -- i.e. "moved further past the target than even the most forgiving genuine-landing tolerance allows," not an arbitrary number.
- `footreach`'s final return is multiplied by `(~env._footreach_overshot_flag).float()`.
- Deliberately scoped to `footreach` ONLY -- `foot_proximity`, `blue_stick_landing`, `blue_ball_landed`'s one-shot bonus, and the `landing_ok`-gated save bonus (`stopball`/`softstop`/`success`) are all untouched, so a genuine recovery-and-land after a brief overshoot still earns real reward through those four channels; only `footreach`'s specifically-exploitable velocity multiplier goes silent for the rest of that episode.

This is a narrower-scoped design than the earlier (reverted, see two entries up) `_ball_is_behind` fix from the same day -- that one killed four continuous reward terms simultaneously off a single raw contact event with no path back; this one kills exactly one term, off a clear, direction-aware, position-based miss (not a mere touch), while leaving three other blue-specific reward channels fully live to reward correction.

**Verification:** `python3 -c "import ast; ast.parse(...)"` syntax check only. Not yet validated against a live training run or a fresh `sgk_play` session confirming the specific spike pattern (footreach climbing while `BLUE dist` stays flat, crashing in sync with `foot_ang_vel_xy`/`feet_slippage` impact signatures) no longer occurs.

**Not yet resolved:** the 0.20 threshold is a reasoned but untuned choice -- not derived from a sweep or ablation. If it turns out too loose (exploit still farms below 0.20) or too tight (genuinely recovering episodes get cut off too early, visible as `foot_proximity`/`blue_stick_landing` still active but noticeably lower total reward than a clean non-overshoot landing), it should be adjusted directly rather than reverting the whole mechanism. Also worth watching for a related but distinct failure mode this fix does NOT address: a foot that touches the ball hard while STILL short of blue (never overshoots, so this flag never trips) could in principle exhibit the same collision-impulse `vel_sigma` inflation on the near side -- not observed in either shared log (both were overshoot cases), but the underlying `vel_sigma`-picks-up-collision-velocity mechanism isn't inherently overshoot-specific, just gated to only matter there for now.

---

## 2026-07-25 -- the predicted "near side" gap above was real: footreach spiked from vel_sigma while the foot was still far from blue, not overshot

**Context:** user shared a fresh `sgk_play` episode log and directly asked why `footreach` spiked while the foot was still far from the blue target -- reporting they suspected `vel_sigma` specifically. This is exactly the failure mode flagged as unaddressed in the immediately-preceding 2026-07-24 entry's "Not yet resolved" note.

**Root cause (`rewards.py:footreach`):** `vel_sigma` is built from `assigned_foot_vel_y`, read via `robot.data.body_link_lin_vel_w` -- POST-physics-step velocity, which unavoidably includes any ball-collision impulse from that step, not just deliberate policy motion (same root cause as the 2026-07-24 entry above). The only existing guard, `env._footreach_overshot_flag`, is purely position-based: it only latches once the assigned foot has traveled more than 0.20m PAST the blue midpoint. A hard ball touch that happens while the foot is still short of/near blue -- never crossing that 0.20m overshoot threshold -- produces the same collision-impulse-inflated `vel_sigma` spike with no guard catching it, exactly as predicted.

**Fix (`rewards.py:footreach`):** added a second, position-independent guard alongside the existing overshoot flag: forces `vel_sigma` to neutral (1.0) whenever the assigned foot is in genuine ball contact THIS TICK, read from the "ball_contact" foot-vs-ball `ContactSensor` (`env.scene["ball_contact"]`) using the identical per-foot indexing pattern `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` already uses (`found[:, :4]`=left, `found[:, 4:]`=right, selected by `_get_correct_foot_idx`). Applied after the existing blue decel-zone computation, overriding both the raw and decayed `vel_sigma` values for any tick with genuine contact -- catches near, far, and overshoot cases alike since it keys off the actual contact event rather than a distance/position proxy for it. Does not touch the existing sticky overshoot flag (kept as a separate, still-useful post-hoc safety net for the case where contact happens on one tick but the foot keeps drifting past blue on subsequent ticks after contact ends).

**Verification:** `python3 -m pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/` -- 67/67 pass (no existing test exercises `footreach`/`vel_sigma` directly). Not yet validated against a live training run or a fresh `sgk_play` session confirming the specific "far from blue, footreach still spikes" pattern no longer occurs.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet confirmed whether zeroing `vel_sigma` to a flat 1.0 (rather than, say, scaling it down proportionally) on a contact tick is too aggressive for cases where the touch is light/incidental rather than a hard impact -- if genuine multi-touch saves that need continued approach-speed credit through a light touch turn out to be penalized, consider gating on an impact-severity signal (e.g. `delta_vx` magnitude) instead of a binary contact flag.

## 2026-07-25 -- right_far reference clips rotated 5 deg clockwise (deliberate experiment, not a correction); found the documented left_far correction was never actually written to the tracked data

**Context:** user asked to rotate all 3 `right_far` NPZ clips (`new_doublestepright_short/base/wide_booster_t1.npz`) 5 degrees clockwise, and separately asked whether the dataset currently being visualized (via `sgk_play ...-WithOverlay`) is the one pushed to GitHub.

**Investigation:** `git fetch origin` + byte comparison of local `HEAD`, the working tree, and `origin/v2-blue-ball-waypoint`'s tip (`b86fdea`, same commit as local `HEAD`) confirmed all 6 `new_doublestep*` NPZ files are identical across all three -- the dataset being visualized is exactly what's pushed. While verifying this, re-measured `left_far`'s net Trunk travel-direction angle (same `atan2(disp_x, disp_y)` metric as the 2026-07-24 left_far entry) and found it still reads ~-5.0 deg drift, not the ~0.0 deg the 2026-07-24 entry claims was verified post-fix. `git log` shows no commit has touched `motions/data/` since `36fc902` (which predates that entry) -- the documented correction was computed and described as verified but was apparently never written back to the tracked files (the entry's own script was explicitly "not committed as a permanent tool -- ran inline"; the write-back step evidently didn't persist). **Left_far's rotation is therefore still unresolved and separate from this entry** -- flagged for the user, not fixed here (out of scope of the specific request).

**Action:** rotated `right_far`'s 3 clips by 5 deg clockwise (viewed from above) using the same rigid world-frame Z-axis rotation method as the (apparently-lost) 2026-07-24 left_far script: `body_pos_w` XY rotated about the frame-0 Trunk XY pivot (keeps clip start point fixed), `body_quat_w` left-multiplied by the same-angle Z-axis quaternion `[cos(a/2),0,0,sin(a/2)]` (wxyz), `body_lin_vel_w`/`body_ang_vel_w` XY rotated as free vectors (no pivot), `joint_pos`/`joint_vel`/`fps` untouched. Sign convention (positive angle = counter-clockwise viewed from above) derived directly from `pkl_to_npz.py`'s own `_rotate_xy_minus90`/frame-0-yaw-snap code, not re-guessed -- cross-checked against the left_far entry's own account (matrix `+4.517°` there was independently confirmed anti-clockwise by the user in the viewer) before applying `-5°` (clockwise) here. Originals backed up to the session scratchpad before writing (`rotate_right_far_5deg_cw.py`, one-off script, not committed as a permanent tool, same pattern as the left_far precedent).

**This is a deliberate 5 deg bias, not a correction toward 0.** Right_far's pre-rotation drift measured ~-5.5 deg (not the ~-0.5 deg the 2026-07-24 entry reported as "already clean" -- another instance of that entry's numbers not matching current on-disk state, consistent with the left_far discrepancy above). The rotation was applied directly on top of the as-found data per explicit user instruction, not zeroed first.

**Verification:** measured Trunk displacement angle before/after for all 3 files -- delta exactly `+5.000°`/`+5.000°`/`+5.000°` in the bearing-style `atan2(disp_x,disp_y)` metric (this metric runs opposite the rotation matrix's own sign convention near the ±180° branch these right-side clips sit near, so a `+5°` metric delta is the expected signature of a `-5°` (clockwise) matrix rotation -- see the sign-convention note above). `body_quat_w` confirmed still unit-norm (min/max norm 0.99999994/1.0000001 across all 3 files) after the correction. Array shapes/keys unchanged. **Not yet visually re-confirmed in the mjlab viewer** -- re-run the far-region visualization commands to eyeball the rotated clips.

## 2026-07-25 (later same day) -- footreach still spiking after the ball-contact gate: overshoot-kill threshold was a stale fixed 0.20m, looser than the curriculum-tightened 0.15m landing radius

**Context:** user reported the footreach spike was still present after the same-day ball-contact `vel_sigma` gate (previous entry, "the underlying vel_sigma-picks-up-collision-velocity mechanism isn't inherently overshoot-specific" gap), and asked directly for the FULL `footreach` return (not just `vel_sigma`) to zero out once the assigned foot surpasses the blue midpoint by some distance -- for the rest of the episode.

**Root cause (`rewards.py:footreach`):** that mechanism already exists (`env._footreach_overshot_flag`, added 2026-07-24, zeros the entire `taskrew` -- both `reach_rew` and `vel_sigma` -- once `signed_progress` exceeds a threshold), but the threshold was a **fixed `0.20`**, chosen at the time to match the loosest/easy-curriculum end of the landing radius. `env._blue_landing_radius_current` (the actual curriculum-eased "at blue" tolerance used by `_get_reach_target_y`/`blue_ball_landed`/`blue_stick_landing`) tightens down to `0.15` as difficulty increases, but this fixed `0.20` never followed it -- at full difficulty a foot could overshoot by up to 0.20m (MORE than the real landing tolerance) before the kill-switch fired at all. The specific episode analyzed earlier the same day (footreach reaching 40+ with the foot up to ~0.15m past blue, curriculum-confirmed `radius=0.15` in the live debug trace) sat entirely inside this gap -- real overshoot, by every other measure, that the kill-switch's stale threshold didn't recognize as overshoot yet.

**Flagged separately (not fixed here, needs user confirmation):** the specific episode used as evidence for the ball-contact fix (previous entry) showed footreach already spiking to ~30-40 at `dist_to_blue = 0.23-0.25` -- while the foot was still APPROACHING blue, before any overshoot at all. Neither this fix nor the ball-contact gate addresses that case; it would require tightening `vel_sigma`'s blue decel-zone floor (currently decays to a 1.0x neutral floor, never to 0 -- see `footreach`'s own decel-zone comment) rather than anything overshoot-related. Not changed pending explicit confirmation this is still happening post-fix, since it's a more invasive behavior change than what was asked for.

**Fix (`rewards.py:footreach`):** `_FOOTREACH_OVERSHOOT_KILL` now reads `env._blue_landing_radius_current` (already set fresh by `_get_reach_target_y`, called earlier in the same function) instead of the fixed `0.20`, so the kill-switch threshold tracks the same curriculum-eased distance every other "at blue" consumer in this file uses.

**Verification:** `python3 -m pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/` -- 67/67 pass (no dedicated `footreach`/overshoot-flag test exists). Not yet validated against a live training run or a fresh `sgk_play` session confirming the specific spike pattern from the earlier log no longer occurs at the (now-tighter) threshold.

**REVERTED (same day) -- the ball-contact `vel_sigma` gate from the previous entry only, NOT this threshold fix.** User reported the ball-contact gate didn't fix the observed spike (consistent with the "flagged separately" approach-phase case above -- that spike didn't involve a ball touch at all, so the contact gate had nothing to catch) and asked for it to be removed as unnecessary compute. Removed the `ball_contact` sensor read + `assigned_foot_touching_ball` override from `footreach` (the block added in the previous entry, between the decel-zone `vel_sigma` computation and the phase1/phase2 combine). This threshold fix (tracking `env._blue_landing_radius_current`) is unrelated to the reverted block and stays in place. `python3 -m pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/` -- 67/67 pass after the revert.

**Not yet resolved:** left_far's rotation fix is still not actually applied to the tracked data despite being documented as done (see Investigation above) -- needs to be redone and this time actually committed. Not committed/pushed here -- working-tree change only, pending user confirmation.

## 2026-07-26 -- near-region footreach oscillation: `vel_sigma` had no decel-zone outside wide/blue crossings, letting the policy farm velocity-toward-target by bouncing across the near-region target before the ball arrives

**Context:** user reported (watching `model_13750.pt`, run `blue_v2_overshootradius_2026-07-25`) that the policy oscillates rapidly back and forth over the near-region ("green ball") target point well before the ball arrives, and hypothesized this was a `footreach` `vel_sigma` farming exploit -- asking whether G1 has a zero-out mechanism for this that SGK's near region is missing.

**Investigation:** confirmed G1's `_reward_eereach` (`legged_robot.py:1361-1400`) has no such mechanism at all -- its only structural gate is `phase1` (pure static alignment, zero velocity term, while the ball is farther than 1.5m out); once inside 1.5m, `vel_sigma` is uncapped and never decays, and `behind` only sets a *fixed* `vel_sigma=2.0` rather than zeroing it. The zero-out/decel-zone concept the user was thinking of is not a G1 port -- it's this project's own `blue_approach` mechanism (2026-07-24/07-25 fixes, this file), built specifically for wide/far crossings' two-stage blue-midpoint landing concept, which has no G1 equivalent. That mechanism is gated entirely on `env._blue_wide`, which is always `False` for near-region envs (`region_id` 0/2) -- so it structurally never applies there.

**Root cause (`rewards.py:footreach`):** in the exploitable window (`0.5m < ball_x_local <= 1.5m`, i.e. phase2 but before the live-ball switch at `ball_close`), `reach_rew` is already near-saturated (foot pre-positioned at the frozen near-region target) and `vel_sigma = 1.0 + 3.0*vel_toward.clamp(0,3)` is fully live with no cap. `vel_toward` flips sign every time the assigned foot crosses the target, so each oscillation pass generated a fresh 1-4x reward burst with no ball interaction required -- exactly the same exploit class the wide-crossing `blue_approach` decel-zone already guards against, just never extended to the narrow case.

**Fix v1 (`rewards.py:footreach`), REVISED same day -- see below:** added a second decel-zone, `near_decel = (~env._blue_wide) & (~ball_close)`, applied via the same `torch.where` decay pattern as `blue_approach` (mutually exclusive with it by construction, since `env._blue_wide` can't be both), decaying `vel_sigma` toward neutral (1.0x) as `dist_to_crossing` shrinks from `_NEAR_DECEL_ZONE=0.30m` to `_NEAR_DECEL_FLOOR=0.10m`. Floor is a fixed constant, not curriculum-eased -- no near-region equivalent of `env._blue_landing_radius_current` exists, and introducing a new curriculum for this was judged unnecessary complexity before validating the simple version. Gated off the instant `ball_close` triggers (ball within 0.5m, live-ball tracking begins), so the genuine last-moment interception dive keeps full, uncapped `vel_sigma` credit -- this only removes the reward for fast motion near an empty target while waiting for the ball. `foot_proximity` needed no change (`exp(-sigma*dist)` only, no velocity term, confirmed not exploitable this way).

**Verification (v1):** `python3 -m pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/` -- 67/67 pass. Live smoke test (`uv run sgk_train Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc --num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`) run to confirm no runtime errors from the new code path.

**REVISED same day -- v1's distance gating was the wrong shape for this exploit.** User ran `sgk_play --force-region right_near` on `model_13750.pt` (the frozen, pre-fix checkpoint) to check. Two things came out of that:

1. **Expected, not a bug:** `sgk_play` on a frozen checkpoint cannot change the robot's physical motion -- actions come from the actor network's forward pass, which the reward manager never feeds back into during play. The observed oscillation is a fixed motor pattern baked into weights trained under the old reward code; only a training run (fresh or resumed) can change it. This was flagged as a caveat before the user ran the command.
2. **Real diagnostic signal from the printed analytics, independent of (1):** at `epLen=24-26`, `bx` (ball_x_local) read `+1.29` -> `+1.14` -- solidly inside the window `near_decel` should have been damping -- yet `footreach` still spiked `17.83` -> `25.76` -> `30.16`. Root cause: v1's decay only activates within `dist_to_crossing < 0.30m` of the target, but the actual oscillation's swing radius is evidently wider than that -- most of the velocity burst happens outside the 0.30m radius the decay even inspects, so the mask being "active" (`near_decel=True`) didn't mean the decay was doing anything at the moment that mattered. A distance-gated decay is the right shape for `blue_approach`'s converge-then-land dynamic (one approach, should slow at the very end); it's the wrong shape for an oscillation that has no single approach to slow into.

**Fix v2 (`rewards.py:footreach`), supersedes v1:** removed the distance gating entirely. `vel_sigma` is now held flat at neutral (`1.0`, no boost at all) for the entire `~ball_close` window on narrow crossings, regardless of `dist_to_crossing`: `vel_sigma = torch.where(near_decel, torch.ones_like(vel_sigma), vel_sigma)`. Same principle G1's own `phase1` already uses (zero velocity credit when there's nothing real to react to yet), extended further than G1 literally goes -- G1's `_reward_eereach` (`legged_robot.py:1361-1400`) leaves `vel_sigma` fully live and uncapped between 1.5m and arrival too (a real, unguarded gap in G1 itself; the user asked directly whether this was a G1 port and it is not -- G1 has no equivalent protection in this window). Deliberate divergence beyond G1, justified by the live exploit evidence above. Still gated off the instant `ball_close` triggers, so the genuine last-moment interception dive keeps full credit.

**Verification (v2):** `python3 -c "import ast; ast.parse(...)"` syntax check, `python3 -m pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/` -- 67/67 pass.

**Not yet resolved (superseded by v3 below):** v2 was checked against a fresh `sgk_play --force-region right_near` trace on the same frozen `model_13750.pt` and found insufficient -- see v3.

**v2 diagnostic (same checkpoint, new trace):** `bx=1.03 -> 0.56` (still pre-`ball_close`, `epLen=32-39`): `footreach` correctly damped to `4-7`, confirming v2 fixed the window it targeted. But at `epLen=41`, `bx=0.43` crosses below `0.5` (`ball_close` triggers) and `footreach` immediately spiked to `46.32`, then stayed in the `20-55` range for the next ~10 steps while `dvx` stayed flat (no actual contact -- that only happened at `epLen=53`) and `bx` itself barely moved (`0.49 -> 0.11` over a dozen steps, i.e. the ball dwells inside the "close" zone for a while before real contact). Root cause: `near_decel` unconditionally turns full `vel_sigma` back on the instant `ball_close` is `True` -- but `ball_close` is a purely spatial line (`ball_x_local < 0.5`) that the ball can sit inside for many steps before genuine contact, so the identical oscillation-farming mechanism is wide open again in this post-`ball_close`, pre-contact window. Same underlying issue as v1 (a threshold-based reactivation with a blind spot), just relocated.

**Why v1/v2 were both structurally the wrong shape (user's own diagnosis, confirmed by re-reading `blue_stick_landing`):** neither ever PENALIZES foot speed near the target -- they only ever stop AMPLIFYING it (decay/neutralize `vel_sigma`, floor at 1.0x = "no boost", never below). An oscillation doesn't need a boost to keep scoring; `reach_rew` alone (the static proximity term, unaffected by either fix) still pays out on every close pass. The user asked directly whether wide/far crossings avoid this same problem, and if so, whether the mechanism responsible could be ported instead of iterating further on footreach alone.

**Investigation:** wide crossings' own `footreach` decay (`blue_approach`) has the *identical* structural gap described above -- distance-gated within the same kind of radius, and it also fully re-enables `vel_sigma` once `env._blue_landed_genuine` (a state-based reactivation, same blind-spot shape as `ball_close`). Reading it in isolation, it should be just as exploitable. What actually prevents wide-crossing oscillation is a *different*, additional mechanism: `blue_stick_landing` (`rewards.py:903-958`) -- `exp(-dist_sigma*dist) * exp(-speed_sigma*speed)`, a dense reward peaking only at "close AND slow". This is structurally different from a decay: it directly rewards low speed near the target, so a fast oscillation pass scores near zero at the exact moment distance is near zero (the speed term collapses), rather than merely declining to reward it further. Near regions have no equivalent of this term -- confirmed by grep, nothing in the near-region reward stack rewards deceleration/stillness near an empty target.

**Fix v3 (`rewards.py`: new `near_stick_reach`; `mdp/__init__.py`; `tasks/goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`), additive to v2 (v2 kept, not reverted):** ported `blue_stick_landing` as a new near-region-only reward term, `near_stick_reach` -- identical `exp(-dist_sigma*dist) * exp(-speed_sigma*speed)` formula (same unretuned sigmas, `dist_sigma=8.0`/`speed_sigma=1.5`), targeting `_get_reach_target_y`'s return value (the true crossing point for narrow crossings, same target footreach itself resolves to before the live-ball switch) instead of blue's midpoint. Gated `(~env._blue_wide) & (~ball_close)` -- same formula as `near_decel`, active only during the pre-arrival window, zero once `ball_close` (genuine last-moment dives are never penalized for being fast) and zero unconditionally on every wide/far-region env by construction. Registered as `"near_stick_reach"` (`RewardTermCfg`, weight 8.0, mirroring `blue_stick_landing`'s own weight) and `"near_stick_reach_curriculum"` (`CurriculumTermCfg`, same `reward_curriculum_ep_len` shape and `base_weight=8.0` as `blue_stick_landing_curriculum`) in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`; exported from `mdp/__init__.py`.

**Scope guarantee (user explicitly requested near-region-only changes):** the new term's own `~env._blue_wide` gate makes its VALUE exactly `0.0` for every wide/far-region env regardless of registration -- it cannot influence far-region training signal. The registration itself does touch `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, the shared config file every reward term (near and far) lives in -- unavoidable, since that's how every reward term in this project is wired -- but it's a pure addition (a new dict key), not a modification to any existing far-region entry (`blue_stick_landing`, `blue_overshoot_penalty`, `blue_trunk_drive`, etc. are byte-for-byte untouched). `footreach`/`foot_proximity` (shared by both near and far) were also not touched by v3 -- only the new standalone function and its two new config entries were added.

**Verification (v3):** `python3 -c "import ast; ast.parse(...)"` syntax check on all three touched files. `python3 -m pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/` -- 67/67 pass. Live smoke test (`uv run sgk_train Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc --num-envs 64 --agent.max-iterations 5`) clean: `Episode_Reward/near_stick_reach` populated (0.2505 mean) at `Curriculum/near_stick_reach_curriculum/weight=8.0000` as configured; `blue_stick_landing` and every other far-region reward/curriculum entry unchanged in shape/magnitude from the pre-v3 smoke test, consistent with the zero-influence claim above.

**Not yet resolved:** not yet checked against a fresh `sgk_play` analytics trace on the frozen checkpoint (would show the new dense term's value in the printed breakdown, though as established throughout this entry that still can't demonstrate whether the *policy* stops oscillating -- only a real training run can). `dist_sigma=8.0`/`speed_sigma=1.5` are unretuned, carried directly from `blue_stick_landing` -- near-region target geometry (single fixed point vs. wide's midpoint-then-full two-stage schedule) may warrant different values once live training data exists.

## 2026-07-26 -- right_far reference clips had a ~24 deg trunk-yaw swing during the step (wrong-direction relative to left_far, likely contributing to right-side non-convergence); replaced with an exact mirror of left_far

**Context:** user reported the left side of the goalkeeper is converging in training but the right side is not, and asked to mirror the (visually confirmed-good, via `sgk_play ...-WithOverlay --motion-file .../new_doublestepleft_short_booster_t1.npz`) left_far reference clips onto right_far as a diagnostic experiment.

**Root cause, found before fixing anything:** measured the full trunk-yaw trajectory (not just endpoints) across all 3 `left_far`/`right_far` clip pairs (`new_doublestep{left,right}_{short,,wide}_booster_t1.npz`). `left_far` holds an almost perfectly constant yaw the entire step (+9.46 deg to +9.54 deg, 0.08-0.09 deg swing, consistent across all 3 clip lengths). `right_far` -- the actual data the region's AMP discriminator was scoring against -- swings from -14.3 deg to +9.5 deg, a ~23.7 deg swing, ending up yawed the *same sign* as left instead of mirrored negative. This is independent of the deliberate +5 deg CW bias rotation applied to `right_far` in the entry above (`db4bdf6`) -- a separate, pre-existing asymmetry between the two sides' underlying motion-capture sources. A discriminator scoring a large, wrong-direction trunk rotation as "natural" for the right side is a concrete, plausible contributor to the reported non-convergence.

**Fix:** new general-purpose `src/simple_goalkeeper/scripts/mirror_motion.py` (`sgk_mirror` CLI, self-inverse L<->R transform, added to `pyproject.toml`). Mirrors `joint_pos`/`joint_vel` via a per-joint sign/swap table derived from `t1_headless.xml`'s Pitch(Y-axis, unchanged)/Roll(X-axis, negate)/Yaw(Z-axis, negate) convention (cross-checked against `t1_constants.py`'s `HOME_KEYFRAME` mirror-symmetric-pose comment and the sign-mirrored joint ranges in the XML), negates root Y position, mirrors the root quaternion as `(w,x,y,z) -> (w,-x,y,-z)` (derived via `q'=n*q*n`, `n`=pure quaternion for the Y axis), then recomputes `body_pos_w`/`body_quat_w`/`body_lin_vel_w`/`body_ang_vel_w` for every body from scratch via forward kinematics (reusing `pkl_to_npz.py`'s own FK/finite-diff helpers) rather than hand-mirroring per-body arrays -- avoids needing to manually swap e.g. `left_foot_link`<->`right_foot_link` body indices. Full derivation in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-26-motion-mirror-tool-design.md`.

Ran `sgk_mirror` on the 3 `left_far` clips and overwrote the 3 `right_far` NPZ files in `motions/data/` with the output. The pre-mirror (+5 deg-CW-biased) `right_far` files were already fully pushed to `origin/v2-blue-ball-waypoint` at `HEAD` (`ece0247`) before this change, so they remain recoverable via `git checkout ece0247 -- <path>` if ever needed; a local convenience backup was made and used to visually re-confirm the mirror in the `-WithOverlay` viewer, then deleted once confirmed. **User visually confirmed the mirrored right_far clips look correct** (clean sideways mirror of left_far, steady inward yaw, no wobble) via `sgk_play ...-WithOverlay --motion-file .../new_doublestepright_*.npz`, and asked to make the swap permanent -- committed as of this entry (previously left uncommitted pending that visual check).

**Verification:** (1) re-measured trunk yaw on the mirrored output: flat -9.54 deg to -9.46 deg (0.08-0.09 deg swing) for all 3 clips -- exact sign-flipped match of `left_far`, the 24 deg swing is gone. (2) Cross-checked the full mirror pipeline (joint table + FK) by comparing the mirrored right_foot_link trajectory against the Y-negated source left_foot_link trajectory directly: max abs error 2.4e-7 (float32 precision), i.e. exact. Not yet validated against a live training run -- next step is to launch/resume training against the mirrored `right_far` data and compare convergence to the pre-mirror baseline.

## 2026-07-27 -- Unify all 3 joint-position post-save recovery rewards around one stance target; waist weight bump

**Context:** post-save pose was still visibly unstable in the currently-running 15k-iteration run (`blue_v2_nearstickreach_2026-07-26`). An approved-but-unimplemented spec (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-25-post-save-stance-design.md`, 2026-07-25) already diagnosed part of this: `postupperdofpos`/`postlegdofpos` reward returning to `robot.data.default_joint_pos` -- the crouched, bent-knee `HOME_KEYFRAME` pose -- which requires continuous active torque to hold and the policy was not reliably settling into post-save. That spec deliberately scoped out `postwaistdofpos`/`postorientation`/`postangvel`/`postlinvel`. User separately reported the waist specifically rotating post-save and asked to unify all of the post-save recovery terms around one stance concept.

Live reward data from the running training run confirmed a clean split: `postorientation` (2.29), `postupperdofpos` (2.00, after its 2026-07-23 weight bump), `postangvel` (0.63), and `postlinvel` (0.48) are all converging reasonably; `postwaistdofpos` (0.31) and `postlegdofpos` (0.07) are stuck near their floor. `postorientation`/`postangvel`/`postlinvel` read root state (`projected_gravity_b`/`root_link_ang_vel_b`/`root_link_lin_vel_b`), not joint positions -- they have no "stance" to retarget (upright/zero-velocity targets are already stance-independent) and their values don't show the same floor-stuck symptom, so they were deliberately left unchanged. `postwaistdofpos` and `postlegdofpos` are both joint-position terms compared against `default_joint_pos`, same mechanism as the two the spec already targeted -- `Waist` (`t1_headless.xml`) is a pure yaw joint (`axis="0 0 1"`, range +/-1.57 rad), exactly the joint that would visibly present as "the robot rotating" if under-converged, and `postwaistdofpos` had never received the weight bump `postupperdofpos` got for the identical symptom on 2026-07-23 (that fix's own comment named `postwaistdofpos` as one of the terms `postupperdofpos` was compared against at the time -- that comparison is now stale).

**Fix (`rewards.py`, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`):**
- New `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP: dict[str, float]` (rewards.py, placed after `_T1_VEL_LIMIT_MAP`) extends the spec's straight-leg/45-deg-arms-out stance to also cover the waist joint (`"Waist": 0.0`, unchanged from its current `default_joint_pos` value). Resolved once via the same lazy-cache pattern already used 3x in this file (`_t1_vel_limits`/`_t1_kp_inv`/`_t1_effort_limits`), cached as `env._post_save_stance_target` -- the first cache in this file shared across more than one reward function (`postupperdofpos`, `postlegdofpos`, `postwaistdofpos` all read it); safe regardless of call order since the guard is `hasattr`-based.
- `postupperdofpos`/`postlegdofpos`/`postwaistdofpos` all now compute their error against `env._post_save_stance_target[asset_cfg.joint_ids]` instead of `robot.data.default_joint_pos[:, asset_cfg.joint_ids]`. Reward shape/weight/curriculum unchanged for the arm/leg terms, per the original spec's explicit scope.
- `postwaistdofpos` weight raised 1.0 -> 3.0 (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) -- the actual behavioral fix for the rotating-waist complaint (the retarget alone changes nothing for waist, since its target value didn't move). 3.0 is a deliberate, evidence-based tuning choice (no G1 equivalent to size against), below `postupperdofpos`'s 5.0 since that term controls 8 joints across 2 limbs vs. this term's 1.
- `postorientation`/`postangvel`/`postlinvel`: no code or weight change, deliberately -- see Context above.

**Verification:** new `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_post_save_stance.py` (5 tests: stance-map resolution in declared and shuffled joint order, cache sharing across all 3 functions with a mutation-survives check, a regression guard confirming the old `default_joint_pos` values no longer score near 1.0 for legs/arms while waist -- whose target didn't move -- still does, and the unchanged `_ball_is_behind` gate). Full suite: `uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass (67 pre-existing + 5 new).

**Not yet resolved:** not validated against a live training run -- this is a reward-shaping change to already-curriculum-scheduled terms, same validation posture as other same-day fixes in this file. Whether to stop the currently-running `blue_v2_nearstickreach_2026-07-26` run and restart with this fix is a separate decision, not made unilaterally here. The original spec's suggested visual sanity check (mjlab viewer, setting `robot.data.joint_pos` to the new target and rendering) was not performed as part of this change.

## 2026-07-27 -- `feet_slippage` counted genuine foot-ball contact as ground slippage (feet-vs-hands divergence from G1, never previously identified as this specific term's bug)

**Context:** while adding raw per-foot slip-speed plots to `sgk_play`'s P-panel (viewer-only change, `interceptV2DualDis/src/simple_goalkeeper/scripts/play.py`) for visually checking `feet_slippage`'s behavior, the user noticed a foot reported nonzero slip speed while visibly airborne relative to the ground, and asked whether this is what's preventing `feet_slippage` from converging, and whether it should be excluded.

**Root cause:** `feet_slippage` (`rewards.py:2225-2318`) determines "in contact" from the `feet_contact` sensor, which has `secondary=None` (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py:107`) -- it fires on contact with **anything**, not specifically the ground, as already documented on a neighboring sensor's own comment (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py:112-119`). G1's original `_reward_feet_slippage` (`legged_robot.py:1469-1473`) makes the identical "any contact" assumption via `self.contact_forces`, but it's harmless there because G1 catches with its **hands** -- feet never touch the ball in G1's task at all, so "any contact" is unambiguously "ground contact." This project's feet ARE the ball-contact effector (the entire save mechanic is a foot touching the ball), so a genuine foot-ball impact was being counted as ground slippage -- exactly the class of hands-vs-feet divergence this project's CLAUDE.md calls out as needing adaptation, never previously caught for this specific term. Corroborating evidence already existed in this file's own history without being connected to this cause: the 2026-07-23 entry above (line ~1263) recorded `feet_slippage` "crashing from 2.65 to 0.13" in the exact same step `foot_ang_vel_xy` spiked to -10.58 -- both flagged there as signatures of a hard physical impact, i.e. genuine foot-ball contact, not ground sliding.

The existing partial mitigation (`rewards.py:2299-2309`, `suppress` block) only zeroes this for the **assigned/correct** foot, and only by ball-proximity (<0.5m), not actual touch. The **trailing** (non-assigned) foot got no such protection at all -- if it grazed the ball, that counted as full-strength slippage penalty for a foot that wasn't sliding on anything.

**Fix:** reused the existing `ball_contact` sensor (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py:120-129`, same primary geom pattern/layout as `feet_contact`, built in an earlier fix specifically to distinguish genuine foot-vs-ball contact from generic "touching anything") to exclude real foot-ball contact from `in_contact` for **both** feet, independent of foot assignment:
```python
ball_found = env.scene["ball_contact"].data.found  # same [B,8] primary layout as feet_contact
left_touching_ball  = (ball_found[:, :4] > 0).any(dim=-1)
right_touching_ball = (ball_found[:, 4:] > 0).any(dim=-1)
left_in_contact  = left_in_contact & ~left_touching_ball
right_in_contact = right_in_contact & ~right_touching_ball
```
The pre-existing proximity-based `suppress` block (assigned foot only, <0.5m) is left untouched and still applies on top -- it exists to let the *assigned* foot sweep into the ball at speed even before contact registers, a different purpose than this fix (excluding contact-confirmed ball touches for *either* foot).

**Known limitation (not fully solved, disclosed rather than silently assumed complete):** an earlier fix on `stopball`/`softstop` (2026-07-15 entry, line ~1128) tried using this same `ball_contact` sensor for a correct-foot gate and reverted it, because "MuJoCo contact detection for a small rolling ball vs. foot geoms is apparently too narrow a window to reliably catch at the exact step" a one-shot event needs. That requirement (catching one precise step) is stricter than this fix's requirement (suppressing across however many steps a contact episode spans) -- a few missed frames at the start/end of a graze still leave most of the contact duration correctly excluded here, unlike a one-shot trigger that needs the single right instant. Still, this fix should be read as *reducing*, not guaranteeing elimination of, ball-contact false positives in `feet_slippage`.

**Divergence documented:** added a row to `interceptV2DualDis/CLAUDE.md`'s "Divergences from G1 Upstream" table.

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -q` -- 67/67 pass. Live smoke test: built the real multi-disc env (`goalkeeper_multidisc_env_cfg_withoverlay()`, 4 envs, `cuda:0`), confirmed `feet_slippage` present in `reward_manager.active_terms`, stepped 20 zero-action steps with no errors, `feet_slippage` read 2.97 (near its 3.0 ceiling, consistent with near-zero slip while standing). Not yet validated against a live training run, and not yet re-confirmed in `sgk_play`'s P-panel that the airborne-with-ball-contact spike specifically is gone (the reported symptom) -- next step is watching the panel through a save sequence on the fixed code.

**Follow-up same day:** `_compute_foot_slip` in `play.py` (the P-panel's raw slip-speed debug plot, added earlier this session) was NOT updated alongside the reward fix above, so the user still saw a slip spike on foot-ball contact in that plot -- this was expected (the plot used only the plain `feet_contact` sensor, no ball exclusion) but looked like the reward fix hadn't worked. Mirrored the identical `ball_contact` exclusion into `_compute_foot_slip` so the debug plot and the `feet_slippage` reward now agree on what counts as "in contact."

## 2026-07-27 -- `foot_clearance` capped at 1.0 for any height >= 10cm, giving no incentive to keep foot-lift near the target height

**Context:** user asked whether `foot_clearance`'s reward decreases again once a foot goes above the 10cm target height, or if it's simply zeroed out above that -- neither was true: it was a linear ramp `clamp(height, 0, 0.10) / 0.10` that plateaus at exactly 1.0 for 10cm and stays there unchanged no matter how much higher the foot goes (20cm, 1m, all identical). User asked for the reward to go back down above 10cm instead.

**No G1 equivalent:** grepped `legged_robot.py` for any `_reward_feet_clearance`-style function -- none exists. This term (weight +2.0) is a pure SGK addition with no upstream reference to check against, and had never been added to `CLAUDE.md`'s Reward Design table at all (a pre-existing documentation gap, unrelated to this fix, closed alongside it).

**Fix (`rewards.py:foot_clearance`):** replaced the linear-ramp-then-clamp with a smooth bump matching this reward table's existing `exp(-k*err)` kernel convention (`feetorientation`, `footreach`'s phase1, etc.):
```python
foot_z_above_floor = (foot_pos_w[:, :, 2] - floor_z[:, None]).clamp(0.0, None)  # min-clamp only now
max_foot_height = foot_z_above_floor.max(dim=-1).values
reward = torch.exp(-clearance_sigma * (max_foot_height - target_height) ** 2)  # clearance_sigma=300.0
```
Peaks at exactly `1.0` at `target_height` (10cm) and decays symmetrically both below and above it -- user picked this smooth-bump shape over a piecewise-linear triangle (both options presented) since it matches the codebase's existing kernel style with no sharp corners.

`clearance_sigma=300.0` was chosen (not derived from any reference) so the new shape closely tracks the old linear ramp's values below the peak -- `h=0 -> 0.0498` (old: `0.0`), `h=0.05 -> 0.4724` (old: `0.5`) -- while now mirroring the same falloff above the peak (`h=0.15 -> 0.4724`, `h=0.20 -> 0.0498`, `h=0.30 -> ~0.0`), instead of staying flat at `1.0` past 10cm.

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -q` -- 67/67 pass. Live smoke test: built the real multi-disc env (4 envs, `cuda:0`), confirmed `foot_clearance` present in `reward_manager.active_terms`, stepped 20 zero-action steps with no errors, read `0.4598` (consistent with a foot a few cm off the target height while settling from reset). Standalone numeric check of the formula confirms the exact bump shape described above (`h=0.00→0.0498, 0.05→0.4724, 0.10→1.0000, 0.15→0.4724, 0.20→0.0498, 0.30→0.0000`). Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-07-27 -- trailing foot had zero orientation shaping anywhere in the reward table; `success`'s doubling was tied to the easiest save event, not the desired end result

**Context:** while investigating the trailing-foot-orientation symptom via the P-panel foot-yaw plots added earlier this session, user asked whether `foot_inner_face_continuous`/`inner_face_orientation_save` shape both feet or just one, and what G1 did for this. Separately, user asked whether `success`'s doubling should key off `softstop`/`cleanstop` (the actual desired outcomes) instead of `stopball` (the loosest/easiest event).

**Investigation 1 -- foot orientation coverage:** confirmed by re-reading the code that both `foot_inner_face_continuous` and `inner_face_orientation_save` pick exactly ONE foot via `_get_correct_foot_idx` (`torch.where(foot_idx==0, left, right)`) and never touch the other foot at all. Grepped `legged_robot.py`/`g1_29_config.py` for any hand-orientation-during-catch reward ("palm", "hand_orient", `_reward.*hand`) -- found nothing. These two SGK-only reward terms were single-foot (leading/assigned only) from their original design, not because of any G1 pattern; there was never a G1 baseline to check "both feet" against. Net finding: the TRAILING foot has zero orientation shaping anywhere in the episode -- not during the approach (only the leading foot gets `foot_inner_face_continuous`), not at the save moment (only the leading foot gets `inner_face_orientation_save`), and only indirectly post-save (`postlegdofpos`'s joint-space `Hip_Yaw` pull, not a direct heading target). This is the likely root cause of the visually odd trailing-foot orientation originally reported.

**Fix 1 -- two new reward terms (`rewards.py`, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, `mdp/__init__.py`):**
- `trailing_foot_forward_continuous` (weight +3.0, always active, no `~behind` gate): rewards the trailing foot's alignment with the robot's own forward (local +X) axis via `trailing_foot_long_axis_w · robot_forward_axis_w`, `[-1,1]`, unclamped. No left/right sign flip needed (unlike the leading foot's Y-axis target) since forward is the same direction for both feet. Runs the entire episode per user's explicit choice (offered three gating options: always-on, `~behind`-gated like the leading foot, or behind-only; user picked always-on since the reported symptom is specifically post-save).
- `postleadfootorientation` (weight +2.0, `behind`-gated): rewards the LEADING foot rotating back toward forward once the ball is behind, even while still airborne recovering from an in-the-air save (no ground-contact requirement). Deliberately complementary to `foot_inner_face_continuous`'s `(~behind)` gate -- `behind` and `~behind` are mutually exclusive by construction, so the two terms can never fight over the same step; no change to `foot_inner_face_continuous` was needed or made.

No G1 equivalent for either (see Investigation 1) -- both are pure SGK design additions.

**Investigation 2 -- `success`'s flag source:** `success`'s doubling reads `env._sb_flag`, set by `stopball` on its own `delta_vx > 0.6` deflection check -- the loosest, easiest-to-trigger event in the save-family reward group. This matches G1's own literal design (G1's `success_flag` is likewise set inside `_reward_stopball`, G1's only stop event), so it was never a porting bug -- but the user wants the doubling tied to the actual desired training outcome (a genuine stop, ideally a clean trap), which this project splits into three distinct, increasingly strict events G1 doesn't have at all: `stopball` (initial deflection) → `softstop` (full velocity reversal) → `cleanstop` (ball nearly dead + correct foot).

**Fix 2 (`rewards.py:success`, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`):** retiered to a 3-level ladder:
```python
multiplier = 1.0 + softstop_flag.float() + cleanstop_flag.float()
```
`cleanstop`'s own gate already requires `softstop_fired` (`cleanstop()` reads `env._softstop_flag`), so `cleanstop_flag=True` implies `softstop_flag=True` -- clean `1.0x -> 2.0x -> 3.0x` tiers with no double-counting, no extra branching. `stopball`/`env._sb_flag` dropped from `success` entirely -- a deliberate divergence beyond G1's literal design, not a parity fix.

**Ordering fix required alongside:** `success` was registered 3rd in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s rewards dict, `cleanstop` 5th -- reading `env._cleanstop_flag` from `success` in that order would see last-tick's stale value on the exact step `cleanstop` first fires (the same class of staleness bug this project has hit and fixed before, e.g. the 2026-07-23 asset_cfg staleness entry). Moved `success`'s registration to directly after `cleanstop`. Confirmed the reward-weight curriculum lookup (`reward_curriculum_ep_len`, `correct_foot_save_curriculum`) resolves reward terms by NAME (`env.reward_manager.get_term_cfg(reward_name)`), not by dict position, so this reorder is safe and doesn't affect `success_curriculum` or any other curriculum entry.

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -q` -- 67/67 pass. Live smoke test: built the real multi-disc env (4 envs, `cuda:0`), confirmed all three touched/new terms (`trailing_foot_forward_continuous`, `postleadfootorientation`, `success`) present in `reward_manager.active_terms`, confirmed via `active_terms.index(...)` that `success` (index 4) now runs after `cleanstop` (index 3) as intended, stepped 30 zero-action steps with no errors. Values at that point: `trailing_foot_forward_continuous=2.997` (near its 3.0 ceiling -- foot pointing forward at a normal standing pose, as expected), `postleadfootorientation=0.0` and `success=0.0` (both correctly zero -- no save has happened yet in a brief zero-action rollout, so `behind` is False and the foot is nowhere near the crossing point). Not yet validated against a live training run, and not yet re-confirmed visually in `sgk_play`'s viewer that the trailing foot's orientation genuinely improves.

**Post-merge note (same day):** this entry's `postleadfootorientation`/`trailing_foot_forward_continuous` and the separately-landed "Unify all 3 joint-position post-save recovery rewards" entry above both touch leading/trailing-limb post-save behavior but via orthogonal mechanisms -- see the merge reconciliation note at the end of this file for the full analysis of why they don't conflict or duplicate.

## 2026-07-27 -- Merge reconciliation: `origin/v2-blue-ball-waypoint` (post-save-stance unification) x this session's local work (feet_slippage/foot_clearance/foot-orientation/success-retiering)

**Context:** this session's local work and a separately-landed remote commit (`def6226`, "unify all 3 joint-position post-save recovery rewards around one stance target") were developed in parallel and both touch `rewards.py`/`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`/`CLAUDE.md`/this file. User explicitly asked to verify nothing "serves the same thing" before merging, given both sets of changes concern post-save leading/trailing-limb behavior.

**Textual merge:** `git pull --no-rebase` auto-merged `rewards.py`, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, and `CLAUDE.md` cleanly (confirmed by inspecting each diff's line ranges beforehand: remote's hunks are at `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP`/`postupperdofpos`/`postwaistdofpos`/`postlegdofpos`, ~lines 105-1990; this session's are at `success` (~1340-1470) and new functions after `foot_inner_face_continuous` (~2298+) -- no overlapping line ranges). Only this file (`docs/BugFixes.md`) conflicted, because both sides appended a new dated entry immediately after the same anchor line (the end of the 2026-07-26 mirror-motion entry) -- resolved by keeping both entries in full, remote's placed first.

**Semantic reconciliation (the actual question asked):** re-read the merged `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP` directly (`rewards.py:121-133`) to check for real overlap, not just textual non-conflict:
- `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP["Left_Hip_Yaw"]`/`["Right_Hip_Yaw"]` are both `0.0` -- **identical to their old `default_joint_pos` value** (the map's own comment confirms this: "including ones that don't change from default_joint_pos (Hip_Roll, Hip_Yaw, Ankle_Roll, Waist, all already 0.0)"). The remote fix retargets hip/knee/ankle PITCH (crouched -> straight) and shoulder ROLL (arms out 45deg) -- it does NOT change what `postlegdofpos` was already pulling foot/leg YAW toward, before or after. This session's `postleadfootorientation`/`trailing_foot_forward_continuous` were designed and verified against the PRE-merge `postlegdofpos` (see the entry above) -- the merge changes nothing about that relationship.
- Net finding: **not a duplicate, but a genuine (mild, intentional) reinforcement, not a conflict.** Post-merge, the leading foot's heading is now pushed toward "forward" by three simultaneously-active, `behind`-gated mechanisms: `postlegdofpos`'s `Hip_Yaw` component (indirect, diluted across all 12 leg joints, weight 1.0 total), `postorientation`-adjacent joint-space pitch correction from the same term (unrelated axis, no overlap), and this session's `postleadfootorientation` (direct world-frame heading, weight 2.0, dedicated). These three do not compete for the same behavior via different targets -- they all agree on the same direction (forward) via different signal types (indirect joint-space vs. direct world-frame), which is reinforcement, not redundancy in the wasteful sense: the original symptom (odd trailing/leading foot orientation) was reported precisely because the sole pre-existing indirect signal (`postlegdofpos`'s diluted `Hip_Yaw` pull) wasn't sufficient on its own.
- The trailing foot's `trailing_foot_forward_continuous` (always active, not `behind`-gated) has a genuinely distinct role from anything in the remote commit -- the remote fix's `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP` still only ever applies through `behind`-gated terms (`postlegdofpos` covers both legs, but only post-save); nothing in either change set shapes the trailing foot's heading during the approach.
- Weight-scale sanity check: remote's `postwaistdofpos` bump (1.0 -> 3.0) and this session's new terms (+2.0, +3.0) are independent, unrelated joints/quantities (waist yaw vs. foot heading) -- no double-counting risk there either.

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` after merge -- expect 72 tests (67 pre-existing + 5 new `test_post_save_stance.py`, none from this session added new test files). Live smoke test on the fully merged env to confirm all touched/new terms from both change sets coexist and compute without error. See the next tool-call evidence in this session's conversation log for the actual numbers (not duplicated here to avoid this file drifting out of sync with a specific run's output).
