## 2026-07-27 -- `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP`'s arm pose retargeted a second time (same day): from a 45-deg pure-roll T-pose to `HOME_KEYFRAME`'s exact arm values

**Context:** user found the merged-in post-save stance target's arms "really far out" and asked to reduce the angle (first suggestion: 45deg -> 25deg), then separately asked whether the target could just reuse `HOME_KEYFRAME`'s own arm stance so there's one consistent pose to reason about, and whether resetting the env in `sgk_play` would let them preview it.

**Root cause of "really far out" (not just the angle):** `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP`'s arm portion was `Shoulder_Pitch=0.0, Shoulder_Roll=∓0.785 (45deg), Elbow_Pitch=0.0, Elbow_Yaw=0.0` -- a flat, fully-extended pure-roll pose. `HOME_KEYFRAME` (`t1_constants.py:98-103`) uses `Shoulder_Pitch=-0.21, Shoulder_Roll=∓0.41 (23.5deg), Elbow_Pitch=-0.13, Elbow_Yaw=∓0.21` -- a smaller roll angle, but critically also a forward shoulder-pitch and elbow bend that visually tucks the arm down and in. Zeroing pitch/elbow (as the original T-pose did) reads as far more extended than the roll-angle difference alone (45 vs 23.5deg) would suggest.

**Clarified for the user (not a code change, a conceptual correction):** resetting the env in `sgk_play` shows `HOME_KEYFRAME` (the literal per-episode reset pose), NOT `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP` (a reward *target* a trained policy only gradually learns to approach -- never a pose the env is ever forced into). These are two independently-justified poses for different purposes and were never the same thing, so "reset to check" only ever previews `HOME_KEYFRAME`, coincidentally already close to the initially-requested 25deg (`HOME_KEYFRAME`'s own roll is 23.5deg).

**Option considered and rejected:** copying `HOME_KEYFRAME` wholesale (arms AND legs) into `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP`. Explicitly rejected because `HOME_KEYFRAME`'s legs are the crouched pose (`Hip_Pitch=-0.3/Knee_Pitch=0.6/Ankle_Pitch=-0.3`) that motivated the entire stance-retarget fix in the first place (see the "Unify all 3 joint-position post-save recovery rewards" entry above) -- reverting legs would risk reintroducing the exact measured "policy won't settle" problem (`postlegdofpos` stuck ~0.07) that fix addressed. User confirmed (via an explicit two-option choice) to keep the straight-leg fix and only change the arm values.

**Fix (`rewards.py:_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP`):** arm entries changed to `HOME_KEYFRAME`'s exact values --
```python
"Left_Shoulder_Pitch": -0.21,  "Right_Shoulder_Pitch": -0.21,
"Left_Shoulder_Roll": -0.41,   "Right_Shoulder_Roll": 0.41,
"Left_Elbow_Pitch": -0.13,     "Right_Elbow_Pitch": -0.13,
"Left_Elbow_Yaw": -0.21,       "Right_Elbow_Yaw": 0.21,
```
Legs and waist unchanged (straight legs, waist 0.0). Since the arm target now numerically equals `HOME_KEYFRAME`/the old `default_joint_pos` for those 8 joints, `postupperdofpos`'s old-pose-vs-new-target comparison no longer shows an arm-specific improvement -- only legs still do. Updated `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_post_save_stance.py` accordingly: two hardcoded `-0.785`/`0.785` assertions changed to `-0.41`/`0.41`, and `test_reward_lower_at_old_default_pose_than_at_new_target`'s arm assertions changed from "old scores worse than new" to "both old and new score ~1.0 for arms" (legs retain the original "old scores worse" assertion, since legs are the part that actually changed).

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass (all 5 `test_post_save_stance.py` tests updated and passing under the new values). Live smoke test on the real multi-disc env: confirmed `env._post_save_stance_target[Left_Shoulder_Roll] == -0.41` / `[Right_Shoulder_Roll] == 0.41` (was `∓0.785`), `postupperdofpos` still present and computing without error. Not yet validated against a live training run.

## 2026-07-27 -- Arm angle reduced again (23.5deg -> 10deg), this time also in `HOME_KEYFRAME` itself, not just the post-save target

**Context:** user reported the reset pose in `sgk_play` was *still* "really far out" even after the previous fix matched the post-save target to `HOME_KEYFRAME`'s existing 23.5deg. Asked to reduce it further, to "something like 10%" -- clarified via explicit options that this meant 10 degrees, not 10% of the current angle or of a full T-pose.

**This is a different kind of change than the previous two same-day fixes:** those only touched `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP` (a reward target, never physically forced on the robot). This one changes `HOME_KEYFRAME` itself (`t1_constants.py:81-108`) -- the literal per-episode reset pose. Checked the blast radius before changing it (`grep -rn "default_joint_pos|HOME_KEYFRAME"` across `mdp/`): `HOME_KEYFRAME` feeds `robot.data.default_joint_pos` directly, which is also read by (1) RSI's 20% "continue_keep"-else branch (`events.py:285`, scales/offsets `default_joint_pos` for that fraction of resets), (2) the actor-obs AMP-freezing mechanism (`observations.py:230-318`, freezes actor obs to `default_joint_pos` once `softstop_fired`, feeding the AMP discriminator's post-save naturalness signal), and (3) `deviation_waist_joint`/`posture` (waist-only and dead/unregistered respectively -- neither affected by an arm-angle change). Net: a real, if modest, change to RSI's arm-reset distribution and the AMP post-save reference pose, not purely cosmetic -- disclosed rather than treated as risk-free.

**Fix:**
- `t1_constants.py:HOME_KEYFRAME`: `Left_Shoulder_Roll`/`Right_Shoulder_Roll` changed `∓0.41 -> ∓0.1745` rad (23.5deg -> 10deg). Only `Shoulder_Roll` changed -- `Shoulder_Pitch`/`Elbow_Pitch`/`Elbow_Yaw` (not part of the "arms out" complaint) are untouched.
- `rewards.py:_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP`: mirrored to the same `∓0.1745` value, keeping the two poses identical as the previous fix intended.
- `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_post_save_stance.py`: the two hardcoded `-0.41`/`0.41` target-resolution assertions updated to `-0.1745`/`0.1745`. `test_reward_lower_at_old_default_pose_than_at_new_target`'s arm comparison flipped back to "old scores measurably worse than new" (`0.85 < arm_old < 0.95`, computed exactly from the now-real 0.41-vs-0.1745 Shoulder_Roll delta) -- the previous same-day fix's "arms score ~1.0 under both" assertion was only valid while the target matched the OLD 0.41 default; now that the target itself moved again, that's no longer true. `old_row`'s hardcoded arm values deliberately stayed at the original 0.41 (a frozen historical fixture, not meant to track live constants).

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live smoke test on the real multi-disc env: confirmed the ACTUAL joint position at reset (`robot.data.joint_pos`), `robot.data.default_joint_pos`, and `env._post_save_stance_target` all read `∓0.1745` for `Shoulder_Roll` (all three, not just the reward target this time). Not yet validated against a live training run, and the RSI/AMP-freeze downstream effects noted above haven't been separately measured.

## 2026-07-27 -- Arm angle reduced a third time (10deg -> ~2.9deg); user tested with a stale checkpoint and found no visible change (expected, not a bug); zero-action dynamics found to swing well past the configured target

**Context:** user loaded `model_15000.pt` (a checkpoint trained days before any of today's fixes) via `sgk_play` and reported the standing pose "doesn't change" despite the config edits, and asked to iterate until the upper arm is "almost straight pointing down."

**Root cause of "doesn't change": not a bug, a fundamental checkpoint/config distinction.** `HOME_KEYFRAME`/`_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP` only affect the environment's config -- they have zero effect on an already-trained checkpoint's behavior, since the checkpoint is a frozen neural network whose weights were fixed at that training run's completion. `HOME_KEYFRAME` only sets the pose for the single instant of reset; the loaded policy's own learned actions take over from the very next physics step, driving the arms wherever *that specific policy* learned to hold them -- entirely independent of any code changes made after its training finished. No config edit can ever retroactively change a specific `.pt` file's behavior; only a NEW training run under the updated config would reflect it. Explained to the user, with `--agent zero --num-envs 1` given as the correct way to see the current raw config live (PD holds `default_joint_pos` directly with no policy override).

**Fix (arm angle, third iteration):** `Left_Shoulder_Roll`/`Right_Shoulder_Roll` reduced `∓0.1745 -> ∓0.05` rad (10deg -> ~2.9deg) in both `t1_constants.py:HOME_KEYFRAME` and `rewards.py:_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP`, kept in sync as established by the prior two fixes. Chose a small nonzero margin (not literal `0.0`) as a precaution against unverified self-collision with the torso at the true mechanical zero -- joint range (`t1_headless.xml`: Left `-1.74..1.57`, Right `-1.57..1.74`) has ample room either way, so this wasn't a limits concern, just an unverified-collision one.

**Important physics finding while verifying (not a bug, a real dynamics observation worth flagging):** confirmed via a live smoke test that even under **zero policy action** (PD servo alone, target = `default_joint_pos` exactly), `Left_Shoulder_Roll` does NOT stay pinned at the configured `-0.05` -- it drifted to `-0.127` by step 50, then to `+0.026` by step 80 (confirmed via `episode_length_buf` that no reset occurred in between -- this is continuous single-episode drift, not an RSI donor-copy artifact). This rules out treating it as a simple fixed gravity-droop offset that could be numerically compensated for (e.g. "configure -0.05, expect it to settle at -0.13, so bias the config by +0.08") -- the swing crossed zero between steps 50 and 80, meaning it's oscillating under whatever full-body dynamics a zero-action, non-balancing stance produces (with `cfg.terminations = {}`, nothing stops an inherently unstable zero-action stance from swaying/falling over across a couple seconds). Not investigated further (PD gains/damping for this joint, whether the pose is statically stable at all under pure PD hold, or whether this is specific to the `-WithOverlay` ghost-motion config used for the test) -- flagged here rather than chased, since headless single-frame snapshots during an evolving trajectory aren't a reliable way to "tune to a number" and the user's own visual judgment in the interactive viewer is the correct verification method for this, not a further round of numeric probing.

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass (`test_post_save_stance.py`'s hardcoded values and `arm_old` bound updated to the new `0.05` target: `0.70 < arm_old < 0.85`, computed from the real `0.41`-vs-`0.05` Shoulder_Roll delta). Live smoke test confirms the reset-instant pose reads `∓0.05` exactly as configured; the subsequent drift described above is a separate, disclosed, NOT-yet-investigated dynamics question, not a config-application bug. Not yet visually confirmed by the user in the interactive viewer -- that's the real next step, not another headless probe.

## 2026-07-27 -- MISDIAGNOSIS CORRECTED: `Shoulder_Roll` near 0.0 is this joint's T-POSE reference, not "hanging down" -- all three same-day reductions had been moving the arm the WRONG direction

**Context:** user paused the viewer immediately after reset (`--agent zero`, Space to pause, Enter to reset -- eliminating the drift/timing questions from the previous two entries entirely) and reported the frozen pose was "fully T-posed", explicitly not what they wanted, and asked how to actually verify this properly.

**Root cause of the whole day's confusion:** every verification up to this point checked `robot.data.joint_pos`/`env._post_save_stance_target` NUMBERS and confirmed they matched the configured value exactly -- which they did, every time, with no exceptions. What was never checked was what those numbers actually LOOK LIKE rendered. Built a minimal offscreen-rendering script (`mujoco.Renderer` against the same `sim.mj_model`/`mj_data` `NativeMujocoViewer` uses, synced the same way -- `mjd.qpos[:] = sim_data.qpos[0].cpu().numpy()` then `mujoco.mj_forward`) and looked at the actual image for the first time this session. Confirmed: at `Shoulder_Roll = ±0.05` (this session's then-current value), the render is an unambiguous, full T-pose -- arms straight out horizontally. This directly contradicts the entire session's operating assumption (small `Shoulder_Roll` = arms near the body, large `Shoulder_Roll` = arms out) -- the axis convention is backwards from what was assumed: **`Shoulder_Roll ≈ 0` IS this joint's T-pose/horizontal reference**, not its hanging-down reference.

This retroactively explains the user's entire feedback trail: "first 45 degrees [0.785 rad, closer to the true hang-down end of the range than the later values], now T-pose [0.05 rad, right next to the actual T-pose reference]" -- every one of the day's three "reductions" (`0.41 -> 0.1745 -> 0.05`) moved the value CLOSER to 0.0, which moved the rendered pose CLOSER to a T-pose, i.e. every fix this session made the visual result *worse*, not better, despite all of them being verified "correct" by the numeric checks that were actually being run.

**Confirmed the correct direction empirically, not by further guessing:** rendered test images at `Shoulder_Roll = 1.4`, `1.5`, and `1.55` rad (i.e. toward this joint's actual range limit -- `t1_headless.xml`: Left `-1.74..1.57`, Right `-1.57..1.74` -- rather than toward its center) -- all three show arms hanging naturally at the sides. `1.5 rad (~86deg)` chosen as the final value: comfortably within both the hard range and the `0.9` soft-limit factor used elsewhere in training (soft band for Left: `[-1.575, 1.405]`; `-1.5` sits inside it).

**Fix:** `Left_Shoulder_Roll`/`Right_Shoulder_Roll` changed `∓0.05 -> ∓1.5` rad in both `t1_constants.py:HOME_KEYFRAME` and `rewards.py:_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP`, kept in sync as established by prior fixes. Re-rendered the ACTUAL config end-to-end (via `load_env_cfg('Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc', play=True)`, not a manual `qpos` override this time) to confirm the real fix produces the same natural arms-at-sides result as the manual test -- image reviewed directly, not inferred from numbers.

**Process lesson, stated plainly:** every previous verification this session (multiple live smoke tests, `pytest`, direct `joint_pos` reads) was internally consistent and passed -- and every one of them still missed the actual bug, because none of them rendered anything. For a *visual/pose* request, numeric agreement with a config value is necessary but not sufficient verification; an actual image (or the user's own eyes) is the only thing that checks whether the configured number means what it's assumed to mean.

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass (`test_post_save_stance.py` updated: `-1.5`/`1.5` hardcoded target assertions, `arm_old` bound recomputed to `0.05 < arm_old < 0.15` from the real `0.41`-vs-`1.5` delta). Two independent renders confirm the visual result: a manual `qpos` override at `1.5`/`1.55` rad, and the actual `HOME_KEYFRAME`/`_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP` config loaded end-to-end through the same code path `sgk_play` uses. Not yet confirmed by the user's own eyes in the live interactive viewer -- that confirmation is still the real next step, but this is the first fix today backed by an actual image rather than a number.

## 2026-07-27 -- Arm pose fine-tuned in the live viewer, then matched exactly to Booster's official T1 walk-policy pose

**Context:** user confirmed `1.5 rad` looked correct in the live interactive viewer (the first fix today actually confirmed by their own eyes, not just a render on this end), then asked for two further tuning passes, then asked to compare against Booster Robotics' own official walking controller as a reference.

**Tuning pass 1 -- "10 degrees":** user asked for "10 degrees" of arm angle; clarified this meant 10deg of outward flare FROM straight-down, not 10deg from the `0.0` T-pose reference (the axis convention the whole session had been confused about earlier). `90deg - 10deg = 80deg = 1.3963 rad`. Applied to both `HOME_KEYFRAME` and `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP`, re-rendered the real config end-to-end to confirm.

**Tuning pass 2 -- "a bit bigger gap":** user asked for more visible separation between the arm and torso. Rendered three candidate flare angles side by side (15deg/20deg/25deg) rather than guessing one value -- 20deg picked as clearly visible but still natural-looking (`90deg - 20deg = 70deg = 1.2217 rad`). Applied and re-rendered to confirm.

**Reference comparison -- Booster's official pose:** user asked to check what pose Booster Robotics' own official T1 walking policy uses, for comparison. Investigated `/home/isaak/BEPImitationlearning/booster_deploy` (their official deployment SDK, untracked in this repo) via a dispatched Explore agent: `tasks/locomotion/locomotion.py:205-214`, `T1WalkControllerCfg.robot.default_joint_pos` -- the actual bias pose their production walk policy (`t1_walk.pt`) adds actions on top of every step. Found: `Shoulder_Pitch=0.2, Shoulder_Roll=∓1.3, Elbow_Pitch=0.0, Elbow_Yaw=∓0.5` -- same Left-negative/Right-positive sign convention already used in this project (confirmed, no remapping needed). Our hand-tuned `Shoulder_Roll=1.2217` was already close to their `1.3` (20deg vs ~15.5deg of flare) -- independent validation that the direction/magnitude arrived at by rendering matched a real production reference.

**User asked to just match it exactly, "to see how it looks."** Applied Booster's official values for all four arm joint types (not just Shoulder_Roll this time -- `Shoulder_Pitch` and `Elbow_Yaw` also changed, `Elbow_Pitch` went to exactly `0.0`) to both `HOME_KEYFRAME` and `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP`. Re-rendered the real end-to-end config to confirm: natural relaxed standing pose, arms at the sides with a slight rotation from the larger `Elbow_Yaw`.

**Consistency check (user asked "do we use this pose for everything"):** confirmed `HOME_KEYFRAME` -> `robot.data.default_joint_pos` is also read by RSI's 20%-branch (`events.py:285`, `reset_from_motion_data`/`MotionResetManager.reset` -- verified directly against the live code, not just the docs summary: `torch.rand(1) > 0.2` picks the 80% donor-copy-from-another-live-env branch, else the 20% branch reads `robot.data.default_joint_pos[env_ids]` and randomizes it `×U(0.5,1.5) + U(-0.1,0.1)`, clamped to soft joint limits) and the AMP post-save obs-freeze mechanism (`observations.py`) -- both automatically inherit the new pose with no separate change needed. Also cross-checked the actual AMP reference motion `.npz` clips (independent of anything changed today) -- their first-frame `Shoulder_Roll` values (`~-1.05`/`~0.84-0.94` across 3 clips checked) already sit in the same large-magnitude range as Booster's official `∓1.3`, nowhere near the near-zero values mistakenly used earlier this session -- meaning today's correction makes `HOME_KEYFRAME` MORE consistent with the existing training data, not a new inconsistency.

**Fix (`t1_constants.py:HOME_KEYFRAME`, `rewards.py:_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP`):** final arm values, matching Booster's official T1 walk-policy default exactly:
```python
"Left_Shoulder_Pitch": 0.2,   "Right_Shoulder_Pitch": 0.2,
"Left_Shoulder_Roll": -1.3,   "Right_Shoulder_Roll": 1.3,
"Left_Elbow_Pitch": 0.0,      "Right_Elbow_Pitch": 0.0,
"Left_Elbow_Yaw": -0.5,       "Right_Elbow_Yaw": 0.5,
```

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass at every intermediate value (1.3963, 1.2217, and the final Booster-matched set) and at the end -- `test_post_save_stance.py`'s hardcoded target assertions and `arm_old` bound recomputed each time from the actual deltas (final: `arm_old` computed across all four arm joint types now differing from the frozen historical fixture, not just `Shoulder_Roll`). Config values confirmed exact via direct `robot.data.joint_pos` reads at every step, and the final Booster-matched pose re-rendered end-to-end through the real `load_env_cfg` + play-mode path to visually confirm before reporting back. Not yet re-confirmed by the user in the live viewer at this final value (they confirmed `1.5 rad` live, but the two tuning passes and the Booster-match were verified via renders on this end, pending the user's own look).

## 2026-07-28 -- `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` viewer plot showed no reaction to genuine wrong-foot contact

**Context:** added a new P-panel plot (`_patch_viewer_wrong_foot_contact_plot`, `play.py`) intended to promote the existing `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` reward term into the viewer's visible plots (following the `feet_slippage`/`postlegdofpos` promotion pattern already in the file). User reported it showed no visible reaction even when watching the trailing (wrong) foot clearly touch the ball, while the sibling `feet_slippage` plot did visibly react to the same contact (a dip, since `feet_slippage` excludes genuine foot-ball contact from its slip calculation).

**Root cause:** NOT a reward-computation bug. `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` is a binary signal that is 0 almost always and spikes only for the 1-3 physics steps (dt=0.02s, ~20-60ms) a brief foot-vs-ball graze lasts. mjlab's native viewer (`viewer.py:_write_history_to_figure`) auto-scales each plot's y-axis from the 2nd/98th percentile of its last 300 samples (`PlotCfg`: `p_lo=2.0, p_hi=98.0`). Reproduced the exact formula standalone:
```
n_spike=1 (0.33%): range=(-0.0000, 0.0000)  spike_visible=False
n_spike=6 (2.00%): range=(-0.0500, 0.0100)  spike_visible=False
n_spike=7 (2.33%): range=(-2.5000, 0.5000)  spike_visible=True
```
A contact event needs to hold for at least ~7 of the rolling 300 samples (~2.33%, ~140ms) before the auto-scale widens past `[0,0]` at all -- below that floor the reward value is computed correctly but the plotted y-range never widens enough to show it, so the line is indistinguishable from a genuine 0. `feet_slippage` "worked" because its ball-contact exclusion produces a *sustained* multi-step dip (real contact duration against an already-nonzero walking-slip baseline), not a rare spike from a flat-zero baseline -- a fundamentally easier case for this particular auto-scale algorithm.

**Fix:** replaced the reward-term promotion with a viewer-only "flash" signal (`_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash`, `_patch_viewer_wrong_foot_contact_plot`, `play.py`). Independently recomputes the identical detection `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` uses (same `ball_contact` sensor + `_get_correct_foot_idx`, imported directly from `rewards.py` -- not duplicated logic) and latches the plotted value at 1.0 for `_WRONG_FOOT_FLASH_STEPS=15` steps (~0.3s) after each detected touch, decrementing back to 0 otherwise, with a reset guard (`episode_length_buf <= 1`) so a flash active at episode end doesn't bleed into the next episode's early frames. This is purely a display-layer latch in `play.py` -- `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact`'s actual per-step value and weight, used for training, are completely untouched.

**Verification:** `python -m py_compile src/simple_goalkeeper/scripts/play.py` -- passes. Percentile-autoscale hypothesis confirmed via a standalone numpy reproduction of the exact `_write_history_to_figure` formula (above), not guessed. Not yet confirmed live in the viewer by the user -- that's the next step.

**Follow-up same day:** user reported the flash still didn't react and asked whether the ball might be hitting the chin/head instead of a foot. Investigated `t1_headless.xml`: confirmed a `head_collision` sphere geom exists on body `H2` (fixed since head joints were removed for this "headless" project -- `H1`/`H2` never move, so the sphere sits at a static offset above `Trunk`). Grepped the whole `src/simple_goalkeeper/` tree for `head_collision` -- it is referenced nowhere in any sensor or reward (`feet_contact`/`ball_contact` both match only `^(left|right)_foot[1-4]_collision$`), confirming a ball-vs-head touch is completely invisible to the entire reward stack, not just to the new plot. Physically plausible: an aggressive forward dive/lunge pitches the Trunk (and the fixed head atop it) down toward the ball's height.

**Fix (2nd iteration):** added a new, purely additive `ContactSensorCfg(name="head_ball_contact", primary=head_collision, secondary=ball_geom)` in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` (shared base config, so all task variants including the multidisc one in use inherit it) -- does not touch `feet_contact`/`ball_contact`'s geom set or shape, so no existing reward is affected. Extended `_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash` (`play.py`) to also read this sensor and latch a second plot, `head_ball_contact`, using the same flash mechanism. Both plots are still purely viewer-side; no reward weights or training behavior changed.

**Verification:** `python -m py_compile` on both changed files -- passes. Live smoke test: built the real task (`Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc`, `play=True`, 2 envs, `cuda:0`) end-to-end through `load_env_cfg`/`ManagerBasedRlEnv` -- confirmed `head_ball_contact` appears in `env.scene.sensors` with `data.found` shape `[2, 1]`, and the reward manager still shows all 43 terms at unchanged weights (`penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` still -100.0). `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Not yet confirmed live in the viewer against an actual episode where the chin touches the ball -- that's the next step.

**Follow-up same day (3rd iteration):** user reported the analytics stdout line (e.g. `REW total=... [... penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact=-0.00 ...]`) still read 0.00 even while apparently watching wrong-foot contact happen. The pasted excerpt was from `epLen=101-103` with `SB✓ SS✓ CS✓` all already latched true and the ball essentially at rest (`spd=0.01`) -- i.e. well after the actual save, ball settled near the feet with no active touch, where `-0.00` is very likely the CORRECT value, not a detection failure.

**Verified the detection mechanism directly, not by inference:** wrote a standalone script that resets the real env, reads `_get_correct_foot_idx`, teleports the ball (`write_root_state_to_sim`) directly onto the wrong foot's world position, then steps and reads `ball_contact.data.found`, `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact(...)`, and `_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash(...)` every step:
```
step 0: ball_contact found = [0,0,0,0, 1,1,1,1]   (wrong/right foot's 4 geoms)
  penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact = [1.0]
  flash wf=1.0
step 1-2: flash wf=1.0 (ball already bounced off; latch correctly holding)
```
Confirms the sensor, the reward function, and the flash/latch all fire correctly on genuine contact -- the whole chain works. The contact itself lasted exactly 1 physics tick before the ball bounced away, consistent with the sensor's already-documented brief-contact-window limitation for a small rigid ball.

**Fix (3rd iteration, decoupling + visibility):** added `WF✓`/`HD✓` tags to `AnalyticsPolicy`'s printed flag line (`play.py`, next to the existing `SB✓ SS✓ CS✓`), computed by calling `_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash` unconditionally every step (before the `self.enabled` early-return) so the shared per-env latch counters keep advancing regardless of whether analytics printing or the viewer's P-panel plots happen to be toggled on -- previously the counters were only ever updated from inside the viewer's `_show_plots`-gated update path, an unnecessary and easy-to-miss dependency for a diagnostic meant to be trustworthy on its own.

**Verification:** `python -m py_compile src/simple_goalkeeper/scripts/play.py` -- passes. Live scripted test (above) confirms real detection end-to-end via a genuine physics contact, not a mocked one. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Not yet confirmed by the user watching the new `WF✓`/`HD✓` console tags during an actual save.

**Follow-up same day (4th iteration) -- real bug found, not a visualization/detection artifact:** user watched the interactive viewer with MuJoCo's native contact-point overlay enabled (`C` key) and confirmed an orange contact dot appearing between the trailing leg and the ball at the exact moment `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` still read 0 -- ruling out "near-miss, no real contact" and pointing at a genuine detection gap.

**Root cause:** `t1_headless.xml` has `left_shin_collision`/`right_shin_collision` (capsule) and `left_knee_collision`/`right_knee_collision` (sphere) geoms on each leg -- none matched by `ball_contact`'s `^(left|right)_foot[1-4]_collision$` pattern. Confirmed via a probe sensor (`leg_ball_contact`, pattern `^(left|right)_(shin|knee)_collision$`) replayed against the real trained checkpoint (`model_39750.pt`, 16 envs, 500 steps): 17 genuine shin/knee-vs-ball contact events found, none visible to `ball_contact`. Verified the sensor's primary resolves in geom-index order `[left_shin, left_knee, right_knee, right_shin]` (via `env.scene["leg_ball_contact"].primary_names`, NOT alphabetical -- right side's knee is declared before its shin in the XML) -- confirmed the left/right split (`found[:, :2]`/`found[:, 2:]`) is still valid regardless of the shin/knee sub-order, matching the existing `ball_contact`/`feet_contact` convention.

**Fix:** added `leg_ball_contact` `ContactSensorCfg` (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, same primary/secondary shape as `ball_contact`, purely additive). Extended `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` (`rewards.py`) to OR the wrong-side leg touch into the existing wrong-side foot touch -- same `-100` weight, no new tuning. Scope, per user's explicit choice among three options (foot+leg wrong-side only / any-side leg / viewer-only): only the WRONG side's leg counts; the correct side's shin/knee touching the ball is NOT penalized here (that's a separate save-quality question). Mirrored the identical OR into `_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash` (`play.py`) so the viewer plot and console `WF✓` tag can never drift out of sync with what the reward actually penalizes.

**Verification:** `python -m py_compile` on all three changed files -- passes. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live scripted replay of the real checkpoint confirms the fix directly: at step 44, env 9, `correct_foot=R` with `leg_found` showing the LEFT side's leg in contact -- `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` now correctly returns `1.0` (was `0.0` before this fix) and the flash correctly reports `1.0`. Same-side leg touches (the other 16 sampled events) correctly remain `0.0`, confirming the fix doesn't over-fire. Not yet confirmed by the user watching the live viewer plot/console tags react during an actual session.

**Process note:** this took four rounds to find because the first three (autoscale visibility, decoupling from `_show_plots`, direct sensor/reward verification via forced + real-checkpoint tests) all correctly proved the *foot*-specific mechanism worked -- none of them could have caught this, since the actual contact was happening through a completely different, unmonitored body part (the shin) that no prior test exercised. The user's own use of MuJoCo's native contact-point overlay was the decisive piece of evidence that finally distinguished "code bug" from "visual near-miss" -- ground-truth from the physics engine itself, not another layer of inference.

---

## 2026-07-28 -- Two new reward terms added: whole-body post-save heading recovery, and an arm-specific velocity penalty for counterbalance swinging

**Context:** user reported two more live-checkpoint observations (same `6144_postpull_2026-07-27` lineage as the shin/knee finding above): (1) the whole body -- not just a foot -- drifts into a left/right yaw after a save, and (2) the arms visibly swing for counterbalance more than desired.

**Why no existing term covers (1):** `postorientation` (`rewards.py`) only tracks `projected_gravity_b[:, :2]` -- roll/pitch tilt -- which is YAW-INVARIANT (a robot upright but rotated 90 deg scores identically to one facing forward), so it structurally cannot supply a heading signal. `ang_vel_z` (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, -0.5) only penalizes yaw ANGULAR VELOCITY, not final heading -- a robot that yaws once then holds still pays nothing there. `postlegdofpos`'s `Hip_Yaw` joint-space pull is a weak, indirect proxy (joint angle, not the actual world-frame heading) -- already flagged as such in its own docstring and `CLAUDE.md`'s divergence table. No G1 equivalent exists (checked -- G1 is a static hand-catch task that never needs to reface).

**Fix:** added `postheadingorientation` (`rewards.py`), `behind`-gated like the other post-save recovery terms. Reuses the same world-forward-axis `quat_apply` construction already used by `trailing_foot_forward_continuous`/`postleadfootorientation` (2026-07-27), but applied to the ROOT quaternion instead of a foot quaternion, and shaped `exp(-1.5 * 2*(1-alignment))` to match `postorientation`/`postangvel`'s bounded `[0,1]` style rather than those two foot terms' raw `[-1,1]` dot product. Registered in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, weight 2.5 (first empirical guess, sized similarly to sibling post-* terms -- no G1 value exists to match).

**Why no existing term covers (2):** `dof_vel` (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, -5e-4) already penalizes joint velocity, but it's summed across all ~21 joints via `_ALL_JOINTS_CFG` -- any single arm joint's contribution to the total is heavily diluted. Checked G1 (`g1_29_config.py`) for a curriculum on action-rate/smoothness that could be ported instead, per the user's suggestion this might exist: **it does not** -- G1's `smoothness` (`legged_robot.py:1532`, 2nd-order, matches SGK's `action_acc_l2`) is a flat `-0.1` for the entire run, never curriculum-scaled, and SGK's own `action_rate_l2`/`action_acc_l2` are likewise flat with no curriculum on either side. Did find `g1_29_config.py:168`'s `curriculum_joints` list (waist_yaw + all 4 shoulder joints) -- coincidentally the same joint group -- but confirmed via `legged_robot.py` that this is a **domain-randomization curriculum** (ramps actuator noise/offset injection for those joints), not a reward-smoothness mechanism, so nothing to port from it.

**Fix:** added `arm_dof_vel` (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`), reusing mjlab's existing `joint_vel_l2` function (same one `dof_vel` uses) scoped to `_RECOVERY_ARM_CFG` (already-defined 8-joint arm group) instead of `_ALL_JOINTS_CFG` -- a concentrated, undiluted arm-only velocity penalty. Always active (not gated -- the swinging isn't a post-save-only issue). Weight -5e-3 (10x `dof_vel`'s diluted per-joint share, since now scoped to 8 joints instead of ~21) -- a first empirical guess, not a G1-matched value (nothing to match).

**Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live smoke test: real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` construction (`Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc`, 4 envs, `cuda:0`), reset + 50 zero-action steps -- no NaN/Inf in returned rewards, both `postheadingorientation` and `arm_dof_vel` confirmed present in `reward_manager.active_terms` and firing (45 total reward terms, up from 43). Not yet validated against a live training run -- next checkpoint should be compared against the pre-this-fix run for whether hip drift/arm swing actually decrease, and whether `arm_dof_vel`'s weight fights necessary in-dive counterbalance (a real, not-yet-tuned risk this weight was only guessed for).

---

## 2026-07-28 (same day, follow-up) -- continuous cleanstop payout, arm-scoped torque_limits, and a stale-doc correction

**Context:** user asked to (1) research whether `cleanstop`'s binary threshold could become a continuous reward gradient (worst at 1 m/s, best at 0.2 m/s) and whether that's learnable given ball speed "isn't something you can change", (2) check whether the arm-swinging fixes from the previous entry (`postheadingorientation`/`arm_dof_vel`) should also cover action-rate/acceleration and torque limits specifically for the arms, and (3) re-verify G1's curriculum behavior on fast-movement penalties via an independent subagent rather than trusting the existing CLAUDE.md claim at face value.

**Research finding (cleanstop):** post-deflection ball speed IS controllable -- it's a direct consequence of the foot's contact velocity/orientation at impact, the same mechanism `stopball`/`softstop` already exploit (their nonzero fire rates in prior runs prove the policy already has influence over it). A continuous payout in place of a binary cliff is standard dense-reward shaping and should be at least as learnable, likely easier (gradient exists across the whole 0.10-1.0 m/s range instead of only at the exact threshold). User then explicitly redirected the design away from a continuous *per-step* reward ("just measure end velocity, keep it singular but on a scale") specifically because an ungated continuous term would pay out for pure ball-rolling-friction deceleration with no real foot contact -- a valid concern independently confirmed by this file's own 2026-06-20 rolling-friction/spawn-angular-velocity entries (friction alone can shed meaningful ball speed).

**Fix (`rewards.py:cleanstop`):** kept the existing one-time latch (`env._cleanstop_flag`) and both gates (`softstop_fired`, `correct_foot`) completely unchanged -- these are exactly what rules out the friction-only exploit the user flagged. Widened the FIRE condition from `speed < 0.10` to `speed < speed_threshold=1.0` (new default), and replaced the flat `1.0` payout with `clamp(exp(-decay_rate*(speed-best_speed)), 0, 1)`, `best_speed=0.2`, `decay_rate=3.75` (solves `exp(-3.75*0.8) ~= 0.05` at the old threshold edge, so a marginal deflection still earns a small non-zero credit instead of nothing). `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s `cleanstop` params updated to pass all three new kwargs explicitly; weight/curriculum (17.36->34.72) untouched.

**Research finding (arm action-rate/torque) + G1 re-verification:** dispatched a fresh Explore subagent against the frozen `Humanoid-Goalkeeper/legged_gym` reference (not reusing the prior conversation's conclusions) to independently re-check the claim already in CLAUDE.md that G1 has no curriculum on fast-movement penalties. Confirmed: `_reward_dof_acc`/`_reward_smoothness`/`_reward_dof_vel`/`_reward_torques` (`legged_robot.py:1527-1542`) all read fixed scalars from `g1_29_config.py:324-328`, untouched by the curriculum-update method (`legged_robot.py:359-372`, which only ramps `eereach`/`success`/`stopball`/`dof_pos_limits`/`torque_limits`). All four also operate with `dim=1` over the FULL DOF vector -- never arm-scoped; the only arm-indexed reward functions in G1 are position-recovery terms (`_reward_postupperdofpos`), unrelated to velocity/torque. `curriculum_joints` (`g1_29_config.py:168`) reconfirmed as actuator-noise DR scoping only, no reward connection. G1 has no `action_rate` reward at all (only 2nd-order `_reward_smoothness`).

Separately confirmed via source read that mjlab's built-in `action_rate_l2`/`action_acc_l2` (`mjlab/envs/mdp/rewards.py:58-79`) take NO `asset_cfg` parameter -- they always sum over the entire action vector, with no way to scope to arms using the built-in functions. An arm-scoped variant would need new custom functions indexing `env.action_manager.action`/`prev_action`/`prev_prev_action` directly.

**Decision (user request):** implement the torque-limits variant now (simpler, reuses an already-`asset_cfg`-correct existing function) and defer the arm action-rate/acc variant, since it needs brand-new indexing logic and the user specifically flagged the `footreach`/`_get_reach_target_y` `SceneEntityCfg`-resolution bug (this file, ~line 1107: a `SceneEntityCfg` not explicitly passed through a term's own registered `params` dict never gets `.resolve()` called by mjlab, silently leaving `joint_ids`/`body_ids` at `slice(None)` = "all") as a real risk for a new hand-written indexing term. Doing the lower-risk reuse-based term first, arm action-rate/acc last.

**Fix (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`):** added `arm_torque_limits`, reusing the existing `gk_mdp.torque_limits` function (unchanged) with `params={"asset_cfg": _RECOVERY_ARM_CFG}` instead of `_ALL_JOINTS_CFG` -- explicitly passed through `params` (not a bare default arg) specifically to avoid the exact `SceneEntityCfg`-resolution bug flagged above. Weight -6.0 (2x whole-body's -3.0), deliberately NOT `arm_dof_vel`'s 10x-for-8-joints convention -- torque-limit excess is a rectified, unnormalized Nm quantity (not a small squared velocity term), and a real dive/save legitimately saturates arm effort briefly, so a blind 10x risked punishing genuine athletic motion rather than idle swinging.

**Fix (doc correction, `CLAUDE.md`):** the Reward Design table's `torque_limits` row claimed a `-3.0->-9.0` curriculum ramp; `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py:261`'s own comment ("torque_limits and dof_pos_limits intentionally NOT in curriculum") and the actual `RewardTermCfg` registration (flat `weight=-3.0`, no matching `CurriculumTermCfg`) both confirm this was stale -- corrected to "flat, not curriculum", no code change.

**Verification:** `python3 -m py_compile src/simple_goalkeeper/mdp/rewards.py src/simple_goalkeeper/tasks/goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` -- passes. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass (unchanged from before this fix). Live smoke test: real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` construction (`Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc`, 4 envs, `cuda:0`), reset + 50 zero-action steps -- no NaN/Inf in returned rewards, both `cleanstop` and `arm_torque_limits` confirmed present and active (46 total reward terms, up from 45). Directly verified `cleanstop`'s new payout shape via an isolated call with mocked ball velocities: speed=1.0 -> fires=False (boundary, strictly `<` not `<=`) -> 0.0; speed=0.6 -> 0.223 (matches `exp(-3.75*0.4)=exp(-1.5)` exactly); speed=0.2 -> 1.0. Not yet validated against a live training run -- the arm action-rate/acc variant remains the deferred, higher-risk item for a follow-up.

## 2026-07-28 (same day, 3rd follow-up) -- arm-scoped action-rate/acceleration, the deferred piece

**Context:** completing the arm-swinging fix pair from the previous two entries. This one was explicitly deferred and implemented last per user instruction, because it needed genuinely new indexing logic rather than reuse of an existing `asset_cfg`-parameterized function -- the user specifically flagged this as the same risk class as a prior bug in `footreach`/`_get_reach_target_y` (a `SceneEntityCfg` silently never getting `.resolve()`'d because it wasn't passed through a term's own `params` dict, leaving `joint_ids`/`body_ids` at `slice(None)` = "all").

**Why a new mechanism was required:** confirmed via source read that mjlab's built-in `action_rate_l2`/`action_acc_l2` (`mjlab/envs/mdp/rewards.py:58-79`) take no `asset_cfg` parameter at all -- unlike `torque_limits`/`dof_vel_limits`/`joint_vel_l2`, there was no existing asset-scoped function to reuse. Arm-scoping requires indexing `env.action_manager.action`/`prev_action`/`prev_prev_action` directly.

**Mitigation for the flagged risk class:** rather than resolve arm columns via a `SceneEntityCfg` (which would reproduce the exact failure mode if the object weren't threaded through `params` correctly), `rewards.py:_resolve_arm_action_indices` resolves indices by NAME against the "joint_pos" action term's own `target_names` list (`env.action_manager.get_term("joint_pos").target_names`) -- a fundamentally different resolution path than `SceneEntityCfg.joint_ids`, sidestepping the bug class entirely rather than just being careful with the same mechanism. Indices are cached on first call (`env._arm_action_indices_joint_pos`).

**Fix:** added `arm_action_rate_l2`/`arm_action_acc_l2` (`rewards.py`), same formulas as mjlab's whole-body versions (`action-prev_action` / `action-2*prev_action+prev_prev_action`, squared, summed) restricted to the 8 arm columns. Registered in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` right after the whole-body `action_rate_l2`/`action_acc_l2` terms, weights -0.5/-1.0 (10x the whole-body -0.05/-0.1, same `arm_dof_vel`-style ratio-of-joints convention). Exported via `mdp/__init__.py`. Confirmed via a fresh subagent re-check (same one that verified the `arm_torque_limits` G1 claim in the prior entry) that G1 has no `action_rate` reward at all, and its `action_acc`-equivalent `_reward_smoothness` is flat/whole-body -- no G1 arm-specific value exists to match either weight.

**Verification:** `python3 -m py_compile` on all three changed files -- passes. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live smoke test: real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` construction (`Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc`, 4 envs, `cuda:0`), both new terms confirmed present and active (48 total reward terms, up from 46). Specifically verified the index-resolution correctness (not just "didn't crash"): cross-checked `_resolve_arm_action_indices`'s output against the action term's own `target_names` list directly -- the 8 resolved names matched `_ARM_ACTION_JOINT_NAMES` exactly, in order. Drove 10 steps of random (not zero) varying actions and confirmed `arm_action_rate_l2`/`arm_action_acc_l2` are strictly <= the whole-body `action_rate_l2`/`action_acc_l2` on every sampled env (proves the slice is a genuine subset, not silently resolving to "all" the way the footreach bug did) and are nonzero (proves the slice isn't silently resolving to "none" either). Not yet validated against a live training run.

## 2026-07-28 (same day, 4th follow-up) -- viewer: swapped the 3 feet-slippage P-panel plots for the day's 4 new reward terms

**Context:** user asked for the viewer's P-panel plots to be updated the same way prior sessions did when new reward terms were added (e.g. the `postlegdofpos`/`arm_dof_vel` promotions, and the wrong-foot-contact flash plots) -- specifically to make `cleanstop`, `arm_torque_limits`, `arm_action_rate_l2`, `arm_action_acc_l2` visible in the viewer, and to remove the 3 feet-slippage plots to make room.

**Mechanism (established by prior entries, restated for this change):** `NativeMujocoViewer._update_reward_figures` only renders `_term_names[:max_viewports]` (12). This task registers ~48 active reward terms, so anything past index 12 in declaration order is silently never shown regardless of `_show_plots`. Each `_patch_viewer_*` function in `play.py` works around this by prepending specific term names to the front of `_term_names` at `setup()` time.

**What was removed:** `_patch_viewer_feet_slip_plots` (`play.py`) and its `_compute_foot_slip` helper -- this was 3 plots total occupying 3 of the always-visible front slots: two custom raw per-foot slip-speed plots (`left_foot_slip`/`right_foot_slip`) plus the promoted `feet_slippage` reward-term plot itself.

**What replaced them:** a new `_patch_viewer_new_reward_plots` function, following the simpler "promotion-only" pattern (no custom raw plot needed, since all 4 are already ordinary scalar reward terms with their own auto-created figures -- same pattern `postlegdofpos`'s promotion already used inside `_patch_viewer_foot_orientation_plots`). Promotes `cleanstop`, `arm_torque_limits`, `arm_action_rate_l2`, `arm_action_acc_l2` to the front of `_term_names`. Call site in `run_play`/viewer setup updated from `_patch_viewer_feet_slip_plots(...)` to `_patch_viewer_new_reward_plots(...)`; the `_patch_viewer_wrong_foot_contact_plot` docstring's stale cross-reference to the removed function updated to point at its replacement.

## 2026-07-29 -- reverted arm_torque_limits/arm_action_rate_l2/arm_action_acc_l2; postupperdofpos's during_scale 0.3 -> 0.5

**Context:** a live run of `6144_armtorquefix_2026-07-28` (the run launched with the previous four entries' changes) was compared against the immediately-prior run (`6144_headingarmfix_2026-07-28`, same task/hardware, only this pull differs) at matched iteration counts. User asked whether the regression could be fixed by reverting the three dynamics-based arm penalties and instead raising `postupperdofpos`'s pre-save strength -- their actual goal was narrower than the full penalty stack suggested: prevent the arm ending up in a bad static position (e.g. behind the body), not dampen all arm motion generally.

**Evidence the three reverted terms were the problem, not `postupperdofpos` itself:** matched-iteration comparison (10000+ points each side) showed `footreach` (6.42->5.96 at iter ~10000), `ball_exit` rate (0.0008->0.0037/env), episode length (135.7->107.4), and `softstop` (0.38->0.25) all regressed after the pull -- and `postupperdofpos`'s OWN reward collapsed ~10x (2.16->0.18 at the same point) despite being the term meant to fix arm pose. Diagnosis: `arm_torque_limits`/`arm_action_rate_l2`/`arm_action_acc_l2` penalize movement/effort *regardless of resulting pose* -- none of them care whether the arm ends up correctly placed, only that it moves smoothly and with low torque. Stacked with `postupperdofpos` now always-on and `arm_dof_vel` (existing), the arms were under 5 simultaneous constraints, collectively over-suppressing the counterbalance motion a dive legitimately needs -- degrading save performance broadly, not just damping idle post-save swinging as intended.

**Fix:** removed `arm_torque_limits`/`arm_action_rate_l2`/`arm_action_acc_l2` `RewardTermCfg` registrations (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`). Deleted the now-unused `arm_action_rate_l2`/`arm_action_acc_l2` functions and their `_resolve_arm_action_indices` helper (`rewards.py`) -- nothing else called them. `arm_torque_limits` needed no function deletion (reused the existing whole-body `torque_limits`, just scoped via `asset_cfg`). `arm_dof_vel` (goalkeeper_env_cfg.py, from 2026-07-28) is kept unchanged, per explicit user request: same movement-penalty family as the three reverted terms, but a real G1-matched mechanism (`dof_vel`) at a small weight (`arm_dof_vel=-0.12` vs `arm_action_acc_l2=-4.14` in the last logged run -- an order of magnitude smaller), judged a minor contributor to the regression rather than the driver. `postupperdofpos`'s `during_scale` default raised 0.3 -> 0.5 (`rewards.py`) -- keeps the pose-matching mechanism as the sole arm-posture lever, at moderately higher pre-save strength, without reintroducing the movement-agnostic side penalties.

**play.py note:** `_patch_viewer_new_reward_plots`'s promotion list still references the three removed term names -- left as-is since it degrades safely (`promoted = [n for n in _PROMOTED_REWARD_TERMS if n in native_viewer._term_names]` filters to terms that actually exist, so this doesn't crash, it just silently promotes one fewer plot than intended). Cosmetic-only, not fixed as part of this change since it doesn't affect training.

**Verification:** updated `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_post_save_stance.py::test_gated_off_when_ball_not_behind` (hardcoded the old `during_scale=0.3` expected value; changed to 0.5). `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live smoke test: real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` construction (`Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc`, 4 envs, `cuda:0`), reset + 50 zero-action steps, finite rewards, confirmed the 3 terms are gone and `arm_dof_vel`/`postupperdofpos` remain active (45 total reward terms, down from 48). Not yet validated against a live training run -- next checkpoint should be compared against both this run and the pre-2026-07-28 baseline to confirm footreach/ball_exit/episode-length actually recover and `postupperdofpos` no longer collapses, without the arm ending up in a bad static pose again.

**Verification:** `python3 -m py_compile src/simple_goalkeeper/scripts/play.py` -- passes. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass (no test exercises the native viewer directly, which needs a display -- confirmed no test file references `_patch_viewer_*`/`feet_slip`, so nothing to update there). Grepped the file after the edit to confirm zero remaining references to the removed `_patch_viewer_feet_slip_plots`/`_compute_foot_slip`/`_SLIP_TERM_NAMES`. Not confirmed live in the interactive viewer (no `DISPLAY` in this environment) -- next `sgk_play` session with a display should confirm the 4 new plots actually render and the 3 old slip plots are gone.

## 2026-07-29 (same day, follow-up) -- postupperdofpos's exp(-err) kernel was saturated in FAR regions specifically, not fixed by the during_scale change

**Context:** user reported the `during_scale` fix above only seemed to help near-region episodes -- far-region arm movement still looked "really weird," both during and after the save.

**Root-caused via direct checkpoint replay, not guessed:** wrote a standalone probe script loading the live `model_19000.pt` checkpoint, using `--force-region`-equivalent pinning (`pin_region_on_reset`) to run 64-env rollouts of `left_near` and `left_far` separately, and read `env._post_save_stance_target` vs the actual arm joint positions directly every step, split by whether `_softstop_flag` (a `behind` proxy) was set:

```
              pre-save err   post-save err   post-save exp(-1.0*err)
left_near     0.334          0.155           0.856
left_far      1.336          7.810           0.0004
```

Far-region dives require a ~50x larger arm excursion than near-region ones, and critically **the error was not recovering after the save either** -- postupperdofpos is at FULL strength (not `during_scale`) post-save, yet still returned essentially zero. At the kernel's implicit steepness of 1.0, `exp(-1.0*7.81) = 0.0004` -- already fully saturated to the kernel's zero-gradient floor, so this term provided no learning signal for far-region recovery regardless of `during_scale` (which only touches the pre-save multiplier and can't fix a post-save-only saturation). This is the exact "exp(-err) vanishing-gradient-far-from-target" limitation flagged (but not fixed) in the 2026-07-28 entry above -- not something the `during_scale` change introduced, just now empirically localized to far regions specifically, where the error magnitude happens to fall in the kernel's dead zone.

**Fix:** added a `kernel_scale` parameter to `postupperdofpos` (`rewards.py`), lowered from an implicit `1.0` to `0.15`. Checked against the measured values above: `exp(-0.15*0.155)=0.977` (near, barely changed) vs `exp(-0.15*7.81)=0.310` (far, real signal restored, was `0.0004`). 0.15 is an empirical choice balancing far-region gradient recovery against not degrading near-region's existing fine pose discrimination -- not G1-matched (no G1 recovery-quality kernel exists to size against). Kept the same `exp(-k*err)` kernel family already used throughout this reward table (`postorientation`/`postangvel`/`postlinvel`/`postlegdofpos`/`postwaistdofpos`/`foot_clearance` all use this shape) rather than switching families, per user request to stay within the established convention. Confirmed the target itself is already `HOME_KEYFRAME`'s exact arm pose (`t1_constants.py` lines 115-120 == `_POST_SAVE_STANCE_MAP` lines 153-156, byte-for-byte) -- user asked to verify this rather than assume it, since a stable target was the other half of their proposed fix; it was already correct, no change needed there.

**Scope note:** only `postupperdofpos` was touched. `postlegdofpos`/`postwaistdofpos` use the identical `exp(-k*err)` shape (`k=1.0`/`3.0` respectively) and likely have the same latent far-region saturation risk, unverified -- out of scope for this fix, worth a follow-up probe using the same methodology.

**Verification:** updated `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_post_save_stance.py::test_reward_lower_at_old_default_pose_than_at_new_target` (hardcoded `exp(-1.0*err)` for the arm case; changed exponent to 0.15 and recomputed the expected numeric range, `0.09-0.15 -> 0.7-0.75`, for the same underlying joint-space error -- the error itself didn't change, only how the kernel scores it). `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live smoke test: real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` construction, reset + 50 zero-action steps, finite rewards. Not yet validated against a live training run -- next checkpoint should be re-probed with the same near/far `--force-region` methodology to confirm far-region post-save error actually produces a non-trivial reward now, not just that the math checks out standalone.

## 2026-07-30 -- penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact widened to cover chin + either-side knee; viewer P-panel: postupperdofpos promoted, yaw/postlegdofpos plots removed

**Context:** user asked to (1) confirm/restore the chin+trailing-knee "bad ball contact" penalty in the `sgk_play` viewer the way commit `f7856a2` originally did, and (2) fold the LEADING foot's knee/shin touching the ball into the same penalty (not a separate reward term). Clarified via back-and-forth: `head_ball_contact` (added `f7856a2`, 2026-07-28) was a viewer-only diagnostic sensor the whole time -- never wired into any reward, contrary to the user's belief that chin contact was already penalized. `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact`'s existing `leg_ball_contact` OR (also `f7856a2`) only covered the WRONG side's knee/shin, not the leading side's.

**Fix (`rewards.py`):** rewrote `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` so the single -100 penalty now fires on any ball contact except the correct/leading foot's own sole/toe geoms:
- wrong (trailing) foot's sole -- unchanged (`ball_contact` sensor, wrong-side only)
- **either** foot's knee/shin -- widened from wrong-side-only to both sides (`leg_ball_contact` sensor, `(found > 0).any(dim=-1)` with no side split)
- chin/head -- newly OR'd in (`head_ball_contact` sensor, previously unused by any reward)

No weight change (-100, unchanged) -- this is a scope widening of an existing SGK-only penalty (no G1 equivalent to check parity against, already documented as such), not a new tuning pass. Updated the function's summary docstring line (previously "Binary penalty when the ball touches the WRONG (non-assigned) foot", now describes the full "anything except the leading foot's own sole" condition) and the stale `head_ball_contact`/`leg_ball_contact` sensor comments in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` that claimed these sensors fed no reward.

**Fix (`play.py`):** `_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash`'s "wrong_touch" signal (feeds the `wrong_foot_ball_contact` P-panel plot) widened identically -- `any_leg_touch` now checks both sides instead of gating through `_get_correct_foot_idx`, so the plot can't drift out of sync with what the reward actually penalizes (same discipline the 2026-07-28 entries established). The `head_ball_contact` plot is left as a separate signal from `wrong_foot_ball_contact` even though both now feed the same underlying reward, so the two failure modes (foot/knee-based vs chin-based) stay visually distinguishable in the viewer.

**Fix (`play.py`, viewer P-panel promotion):** per user request, replaced `_patch_viewer_foot_orientation_plots` (which promoted `left_foot_yaw_deg`/`right_foot_yaw_deg` custom plots + `postlegdofpos`) with a new, smaller `_patch_viewer_postupperdofpos_plot` that promotes only `postupperdofpos` -- same promotion-only pattern as `_patch_viewer_new_reward_plots` (`postupperdofpos` is already an ordinary reward term with its own auto-created figure, no custom raw plot needed). `_compute_foot_yaw_error` (only caller was the removed function) deleted along with its now-unused `quat_apply` import. Call site in `run_play` updated; stale cross-reference in `_patch_viewer_new_reward_plots`'s docstring fixed.

**Verification:** `python3 -m ast.parse`-equivalent syntax check on all three changed files -- passes. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass (no test exercised the changed reward logic or viewer patches directly; none needed updating). Live smoke test: real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` construction (`Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc`, 1 env, `cuda:0`, zero-action policy, native viewer with a real `DISPLAY`), ran ~45s / 16+ episodes with `--analytics True` -- `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` confirmed present and active at -100 in the reward-term table (45 terms), `WF`/`HD` console flags updating every step with no exceptions, no `AttributeError`/`KeyError` from the `head_ball_contact`/`leg_ball_contact` sensor lookups. Not yet confirmed which specific plots render in the P-panel via a visual screenshot (headless grep of stdout can't see the OpenGL window contents) -- next interactive session should visually confirm `postupperdofpos`/`wrong_foot_ball_contact`/`head_ball_contact` appear and the yaw/`postlegdofpos` plots are gone. Not yet validated against a live training run -- the widened penalty (leading-foot knee now penalized) is expected to increase this term's firing rate; watch `Episode_Reward/penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` on the next run for a bigger magnitude than prior runs at the same weight.

## 2026-07-30 (same day, 3rd follow-up) -- diagnosed "leading leg still fires" as a mislabeled sensor, then re-asymmetrized the penalty per user's actual intent

**Context:** user reported the widened penalty (above) was "still not working" -- it kept firing on the leading/correct leg, which they said wasn't what they wanted. Investigated via a live-checkpoint replay (64 envs, real `model_18250.pt`) instrumented to log, at every firing, which specific geom in `leg_ball_contact` (`primary_names`: `[left_shin_collision, left_knee_collision, right_knee_collision, right_shin_collision]`) triggered, plus a manual surface-gap calculation (ball surface to geom surface, accounting for the shin capsule's local offset/orientation via `quat_apply_inverse`, minus ball radius 0.10m, knee radius 0.06m, shin radius 0.05m).

**Finding:** every single sampled firing (25/25) was the SHIN (`left_shin_collision`/`right_shin_collision`), never the knee sphere -- shin surface gap ~0.0003-0.003m (genuinely touching) vs knee surface gap ~0.10-0.13m (genuinely not touching, confirming the user's own visual observation that the ball wasn't near the knee). Root cause: `leg_ball_contact`'s combined "knee/shin" label (and this doc's prior entries, which called the whole sensor "the knee") was misleading -- the shin sits lower on the leg, closer to a rolling ball's height, so it fires on nearly every save; the knee sphere (positioned at the joint, higher up) almost never does. Not a code bug -- the sensor was reporting real, physically-verified contact the whole time; the earlier "add the leading leg's knee" change (previous entry) had inadvertently included the leading leg's SHIN too (since `any_leg_touch` didn't distinguish shin from knee), which is what was actually firing on nearly every save.

**User's actual intent, clarified:** wrong (trailing) side -- penalize the WHOLE leg (sole OR shin OR knee), same as already-widened. Leading (correct) side -- penalize the KNEE ONLY, explicitly excluding the shin (which fires on essentially every save and isn't a useful "bad technique" signal there, same "unavoidable contact" problem class as chin).

**Fix (`rewards.py`):** rewrote `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` with explicit per-geom indexing instead of the previous side-only split: `wrong_sole_touch` (unchanged) OR `wrong_leg_touch` (either shin or knee on the wrong side, via `leg_touch = stack([left_leg_touch, right_leg_touch])` gathered at `wrong_foot_idx`) OR `leading_knee_touch` (knee geom only, indices 1/2 of `leg_found`, gathered at `foot_idx` -- the correct side). Same -100 weight.

**Fix (`play.py`):** `_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash`'s `wrong_touch` signal rewritten identically (wrong-side whole-leg OR leading-side knee-only) so the P-panel plot can't drift out of sync with what the reward actually penalizes, same discipline as every prior entry in this chain.

**Verification:** syntax checks pass. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live verification script (64 envs, real checkpoint, 1500 steps): computed the expected condition independently by reading raw sensor geoms and comparing against `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact`'s actual output every step -- **0 mismatches** across all 96000 env-steps. Separately confirmed 150 genuine leading-side shin-only touches occurred during the run and none of them fired the reward (explicit assertion, zero violations) -- proves the shin-exclusion on the leading side actually works, not just that the code compiles.

## 2026-07-30 (same day, 4th follow-up) -- postupperdofpos during_scale 0.5->0.8; new penalize_arm_above_shoulder; live-checkpoint balance-correlation probe

**Context:** user reported the trained checkpoint's arms still "fly everywhere" despite the earlier kernel_scale fix, and pushed back hard on two successive theories (AMP-reference-mismatch, then "some other reward is outweighing postupperdofpos") -- both investigated with real data and refuted (see the two entries above/below this one covering the wandb correlation pull and the AMP-clip check). User then asked: (1) raise `postupperdofpos`'s pre-save pull in case the post-save starting error is too large to recover from, (2) a live-checkpoint test for whether raised arms correlate with genuine balance instability (a real mechanism, not a bug) before adding a new penalty, (3) if supported, a new reward specifically targeting "hand above its own shoulder."

**(1) during_scale 0.5 -> 0.8:** `postupperdofpos`'s default (`rewards.py`) raised again (0.3->0.5 was 2026-07-29, this is the second raise) -- still below 1.0 to avoid re-fighting legitimate dive counterbalance motion (the exact regression `arm_torque_limits` et al. caused when they suppressed arm motion unconditionally, reverted 2026-07-29). Speculative, not yet validated against a live run -- a direct, testable lever on the POST-save starting error, distinct from `kernel_scale` (which reshapes sensitivity to a given error, not the error itself). Test updated: `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_post_save_stance.py::test_gated_off_when_ball_not_behind` (hardcoded 0.5 -> 0.8).

**(2) Balance-correlation probe:** live-replayed both the mature `armrevert` (model_19250) and the newer `armkernelfix` (model_14750) checkpoints, correlating raw arm-pose error against body tilt (`projected_gravity_b[:, :2]` norm) in the STEADY post-save window (`behind` for >20 consecutive steps). Result: **far region shows a real, consistent positive correlation across both checkpoints** (armkernelfix r=+0.589, armrevert r=+0.218; quartile breakdown: armkernelfix's most-tilted quartile has 2.3x the arm error of its most-upright quartile, 15.6 vs 6.9) -- bigger instability genuinely coincides with bigger arm deviation in hard far-region saves. **Near region contradicts itself between checkpoints** (armkernelfix +0.365 vs armrevert -0.542) -- no consistent mechanism there, likely noise. Also confirmed (all checkpoints/regions): during the DIVE itself, arm error strongly anti-correlates with base height (-0.21 to -0.64) -- lower crouch = bigger arm swing, matching the already-established legitimate counterbalance motion.

**(3) New `penalize_arm_above_shoulder` (rewards.py, weight -2.0):** given (2)'s finding, a flat "arms above shoulder = penalty" would repeat the `arm_torque_limits` mistake for far-region dives specifically. Designed as a supplementary, narrowly-targeted penalty: `clamp(hand_z - shoulder_z, min=0)²` per side (`AL2`/`AR2` shoulder bodies vs `left_hand_link`/`right_hand_link`), summed, gated to fire ONLY in the STEADY post-save window (same `behind`-run-length->20 convention as the probe) -- never during the dive or immediate post-save recovery, directly avoiding the balance-recovery motion (2) found evidence for. New `_ARM_HEIGHT_CFG` SceneEntityCfg added in both `rewards.py` (function default) and `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` (registration) -- mirrors the pattern other body-based reward defaults already use in this file.

**Bug caught before shipping (validates this session's own new skill):** initial implementation indexed `body_pos_w[:, 0/1/2/3, :]` assuming `SceneEntityCfg.body_ids` preserves declared `body_names` order. Live verification (per `.claude/skills/debugging-mujoco-contact-sensors/SKILL.md`'s "never assume order, verify against the live object") caught that it does NOT -- `body_ids` resolves in MODEL kinematic-tree index order, and since the left-arm chain (AL1->AL2->AL3->left_hand_link) is fully declared before the right-arm chain begins, `(AL2, AR2, left_hand_link, right_hand_link)` resolved as `(AL2, left_hand_link, AR2, right_hand_link)` -- a naive positional read would have silently paired the LEFT hand against the RIGHT shoulder. Fixed by resolving each body's actual position via `robot.find_bodies(...)`'s returned `(ids, names)` pair, looked up by name and cached (`env._arm_above_shoulder_body_idx`), not assumed by position.

**Viewer (`play.py`):** `_patch_viewer_postupperdofpos_plot` now also promotes `penalize_arm_above_shoulder` and `trailing_foot_forward_continuous` (the latter existed since 2026-07-27 but was never promoted, silently unrendered past the top-12-viewport cap) alongside `postupperdofpos`, per user request to watch all three arm/posture terms together.

**Verification:** syntax checks pass. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live checks: (a) confirmed the body-order bug and its fix directly (printed resolved vs declared order, asserted the fixed version resolves correctly, checked hand-below-shoulder at the default standing pose reads 0 excess as expected); (b) 1000-step/32-env real-checkpoint replay: term fires on ~89% of sampled steps (28443/32000), raw excess maxes at 0.063 (~0.245m above shoulder), no non-finite values; (c) full `ManagerBasedRlEnv` construction with the reward manager confirms the term is registered and produces finite dt-scaled values over 300 zero-action steps. Not yet validated against a live training run -- next checkpoint should be compared against this session's balance-correlation baseline (far-region tilt-vs-arm-error correlation) to confirm the new penalty doesn't reintroduce the over-suppression regression this session went out of its way to avoid.
