## 2026-07-31 -- far-region AMP reference clips (all 6 `new_doublestep{left,right}_{short,base,wide}_booster_t1.npz`) had excessive swing-leg hip/knee flexion; damped toward standing neutral

**Context:** user reviewed the `postupperdofpos` post-save arm investigation (previous two entries) and, while visually inspecting the far-region reference motions via the real mjlab viewer (`sgk_play ...-WithOverlay --agent zero --no-terminations True --motion-file <clip>`, not the bare passive `sgk_view`), reported the stepping leg visibly "points towards the field" during the reach/plant phase of `new_doublestepleft_short_booster_t1.npz` and asked for it to look "more upright." This is a separate root cause from the arm-pose investigation above (arm columns are region-masked to a frozen default for far regions in the AMP observation, per `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py:91-102` -- the discriminator supplies zero gradient on arm pose there either way), but was raised in the same session while inspecting the same reference data.

**Investigation (before any edit, per this project's Change Approval Workflow):** loaded `new_doublestepleft_short_booster_t1.npz` directly and measured joint angles against the T1 standing neutral (`HOME_KEYFRAME`: `Hip_Pitch=-0.3 rad` / -17.2°, `Knee_Pitch=0.6 rad` / +34.4°). Root/pelvis pitch itself averages ~0° across the clip (already upright on average) -- the visual lean is not a whole-body tilt. At the peak of the swing-leg reach, `Left_Hip_Pitch` bottoms out at -39.2° (far past neutral) at the SAME frame `Left_Knee_Pitch` also nearly straightens (+4.1°, far below its +34.4° neutral bend) -- i.e. the front leg goes almost fully extended and swung forward at the top of the step, which reads as "leg pointing at the field." User confirmed this diagnosis matched what they saw and chose a damping strength (`AskUserQuestion`: 30/50/70% offered, 50% picked initially, then this message asked to push it further -- see below).

**Fix -- swing-leg-only joint-space damping, FK-refit (one-off correction script, not a permanent codebase tool):**
- Detected each leg's own swing vs. stance frames from the clip's OWN foot-height trajectory (`body_pos_w`; stance sits at exactly 0.0300 m in this file's convention, so `z > 0.032 m` = airborne), independently per leg -- the weight-bearing/stance leg's frames are never touched, since altering a planted leg's hip/knee angle (with the root trajectory held fixed) would move the foot out of its recorded ground-contact position via forward kinematics.
- For airborne frames only, damped `Hip_Pitch` and `Knee_Pitch` toward the T1 standing-neutral values: `new = neutral + (1-DAMPING)*(old-neutral)`. Blended in/out over a 2-frame box-filtered ramp at every swing/stance transition so no discontinuity hits the finite-difference velocity computation.
- Recomputed `joint_vel` via the same central-difference method `pkl_to_npz.py` uses, and refit `body_pos_w`/`body_quat_w`/`body_lin_vel_w`/`body_ang_vel_w` via `mj_kinematics` FK from the ORIGINAL, untouched root trajectory (position + quaternion) plus the new joint angles -- exactly mirroring `pkl_to_npz.py`'s own Pass 2. A final uniform vertical re-level shift (same idea as that script's Pass 1 z-correction, a rigid translation so it's safe to apply directly to the already-computed positions) restores the lowest foot-contact point to exactly 0.0300 m, since editing swing-adjacent blended frames can move the stance foot's z by a couple mm.
- Applied identically to all 6 far-region clips (`new_doublestepleft_short/base/wide`, `new_doublestepright_short/base/wide`) -- the right-side clips are mirror images of the left-side ones and produced numerically identical (L/R-swapped) results, confirming the swing detection generalizes correctly across the mirrored dataset.

**Damping strength -- iterated three times, all user-directed:**
1. First pass: `DAMPING=0.5`. Peak swing Hip_Pitch: -39.2° -> -32.6° (dominant stepping leg), -31.8°/-32.1°/-32.4° -> -26.0°/-25.0°/-24.8° (other leg, short/base/wide respectively). Written to scratchpad drafts only, previewed via the real mjlab viewer (ghost overlay, `-MultiDisc-WithOverlay --agent zero --no-terminations True --motion-file <draft path>`) against the originals before touching the repo.
2. User asked to push it further ("a bit more... a little bit straighter"). Re-ran from the ORIGINAL untouched files (not stacked on top of pass 1) at `DAMPING=0.65`, overwriting the 6 tracked NPZ files directly. Peak swing Hip_Pitch: -39.2° -> -30.6°, -31.8°/-32.1°/-32.4° -> -24.2°/-23.4°/-23.0° (other leg). Committed (`91f7953`) and pushed.
3. User asked for "even more like 0.8". True originals recovered from git (`91f7953~1`, since `DATA_DIR` itself now held the 0.65-damped result -- re-reading from there would have stacked/compounded the damping instead of applying one clean pass from the source mocap) and re-run at `DAMPING=0.8`. Peak swing Hip_Pitch: -39.2° -> -28.6° (dominant leg, identical across all 6 clips -- same source capture, only retimed/mirrored per clip), -31.8°/-32.1°/-32.4° -> -23.2°/-22.4°/-22.0° (other leg). Mean swing-phase knee bend up from ~16-17° (original) to ~22° (0.8 pass).

**Verification (all 6 files, all three passes):** no NaNs in `joint_pos`/`body_pos_w`; post-fix minimum foot-contact height exactly 0.0300 m (no floor penetration) after the re-level shift (+3.4 to +5.2 mm depending on clip/pass); peak `Hip_Pitch`/`Knee_Pitch` joint velocity DECREASED at every damping level relative to the original (damping a swing naturally slows it, no spike introduced by the blend). Not yet validated against a live training run -- next checkpoint trained against these 6 clips should be compared for whether the far-region stepping gait still reads as natural (not stiff) at 0.8, since this is now well past the original mocap's own hip/knee excursion range.

---

## 2026-07-31 (same day, follow-up) -- the hip/knee damping pass above silently un-flattened the feet; fixed with a per-frame ankle Newton-solve, new `editing-amp-motion-data` skill written

**Context:** user reported the feet were "angled again" in all 6 corrected clips. This is a DIFFERENT bug from the arm-pose investigation two entries above (which found far-region AMP arm columns are frozen/uninformative, so AMP wasn't the culprit there) -- this is a direct, mechanical consequence of the hip/knee damping fix just above, missed because every sanity check run at the time (NaN, foot-contact-Z floor penetration, joint-velocity spikes) checks POSITION-related quantities only, never orientation.

**Root cause, confirmed by direct measurement:** computed the same gravity-projected tilt metric `rewards.py:feetorientation` uses (`quat_apply(quat_inv(foot_quat_w), [0,0,-1])`'s XY components -- `(0,0)` = perfectly flat) on the TRUE original mocap vs. the 0.8-damped result, both feet, every frame. **The true original reads `|tilt| ~ 0.000` (machine precision) on literally every frame of every clip** -- the original "ankle flatness" leveling pass (commit `36fc902`, 2026-07-18) really did hold the foot dead level throughout the whole clip, not just at footstrike. After the hip/knee damping pass, `|tilt|` reaches up to **0.41 (~24 deg)** exactly on the frames where hip/knee were edited (swing frames) -- Ankle_Pitch/Ankle_Roll's own joint VALUES were never touched by that pass, but the foot's WORLD orientation still moved, because it's downstream of hip+knee in the same serial kinematic chain. Editing an ancestor joint always changes a descendant body's world orientation, independent of whether the descendant's own joint value changed.

**Fix (`rewards.py`/`t1_constants.py` unaffected -- this is purely a data fix, same as the parent entry):** re-ran the hip/knee damping pass (same `DAMPING=0.8`, sourced from the TRUE original recovered via git, not the already-corrected file) and added a per-frame, per-foot Newton solve for `Ankle_Pitch`/`Ankle_Roll` that drives `tilt_xy` back to exactly `(0, 0)`: FK the foot's current world quat, build a 2x2 Jacobian from two finite-difference perturbations (`Ankle_Pitch`, `Ankle_Roll`, `1e-3` rad each), solve `J @ delta = -tilt`, clip to the joint's real range (`model.jnt_range`, not hardcoded), iterate 4 times. Deliberately NOT a hand-derived formula (e.g. "Hip_Pitch+Knee_Pitch+Ankle_Pitch=0" holds at the standing-neutral pose but doesn't account for `Hip_Roll`/`Hip_Yaw`'s non-commutative coupling) -- solved numerically against real FK so it's correct regardless of how much roll/yaw is present. Runs on every frame unconditionally (not gated to the swing frames the hip/knee pass touched) since it's a correct no-op wherever the foot is already flat -- cheap enough (a few dozen frames x 2 feet x 4 iters x 3 FK calls) not to bother special-casing.

**Result (all 6 clips):** max tilt magnitude 0.41 -> 0.000001 (effectively exact) on every clip. `Ankle_Pitch` mean shifted from the original's +8.1°/+4.1° (short clip, dominant/other leg) to -0.6°/-2.3° to compensate for the straighter hip/knee; `Ankle_Roll` barely moved (original mocap's roll component was already near the small residual the tilt metric showed before this fix, confirming the tilt was almost entirely a pitch-axis error as the Kinematic Chain Rule would predict for joints sharing the same rotation axis). All resulting ankle angles stay comfortably inside `model.jnt_range` (`Ankle_Pitch: [-0.87, 0.35]`, `Ankle_Roll: [-0.44, 0.44]`) -- no clipping triggered.

**Verification:** no NaNs in `joint_pos`/`body_pos_w` for any of the 6 files; foot-contact-Z minimum still exactly 0.0300 m after re-leveling; `Ankle_Pitch`/`Ankle_Roll` peak joint velocity stayed in the same ~3-6 rad/s range as the other leg joints (no spike from the per-frame Newton solve, since the correction varies smoothly frame-to-frame). Not yet validated against a live training run.

**New skill: `.claude/skills/editing-amp-motion-data/SKILL.md`** (+ `flatten_and_damp_template.py`, adapted from this fix's actual script) -- captures the Kinematic Chain Rule, the flat-foot detection/Newton-solve method, the full edit-in-place pipeline (recover true original -> stance/swing detect -> blend -> edit -> flatten -> FK-refit -> re-level -> verify including orientation), and the `sgk_view` (bare skeleton, wrong tool) vs. `sgk_play ...-WithOverlay` (real scene, right tool) viewer distinction. Referenced from `CLAUDE.md`'s Key Files table per this project's "turn repeated debugging into a skill" rule -- this is the second such skill after `debugging-mujoco-contact-sensors`, and the second time in this project's history that a data/sensor correctness bug required going back to first-principles verification instead of trusting that passing the existing checks meant the fix was complete.

## 2026-08-01 -- far-region AMP reference clips (same 6 `new_doublestep{left,right}_{short,base,wide}_booster_t1.npz`): added +8° constant forward root/torso tilt, re-flattened feet

**Context:** user reviewed the 2026-07-31 hip/knee-straightening + flat-foot fix in the real mjlab viewer and reported the robot "tends to fall back over" -- asked, as a further pose edit in the same session, to tilt the torso forward a little.

**Important caveat surfaced before editing:** root/torso orientation in these NPZ files is not read by the live training reset path (`MotionResetManager.reset()`, registered as `reset_from_motion_data`, only ever copies `joint_pos`/`joint_vel` -- see its own docstring in `events.py`) nor by the AMP discriminator (`joint_pos`+`joint_vel` only, no root term -- see the "AMP discriminator observation content" divergence-table row in `CLAUDE.md`). Root quat from these files is consumed only by the legacy tier-pool `_write_rsi_state`/`sgk_play_rsi` diagnostic script and by the `-WithOverlay` ghost viewer. This edit is therefore a reference-clip visual/data-quality change, same category as the 2026-07-31 leg-straightening -- it will not by itself change the trained policy's real fall-back behavior; that would require a reward/balance-side fix instead.

**Fix -- constant root-quat rotation, ankle re-flattened (one-off script, `flatten_and_damp_template.py`-derived, not a permanent tool):**
- Source: the CURRENT committed (already hip/knee-damped + flat-footed, commit `344a254`) files -- correct to build on top of here since this is a NEW edit layered on an approved prior fix, not a re-tuning of that fix (unlike damping-strength re-tuning, which must always re-source from the true original mocap).
- For every frame, left-multiplied the root (`Trunk`) quaternion by a fixed +8° rotation about the body's own local Y axis (`new_root_quat = root_quat * delta_quat`, body-frame right... corrected to body-local left-multiply so the tilt is relative to the robot's own facing direction regardless of yaw wobble, not a fixed world-Y assumption). Root position unchanged -- pure rotation. Verified sign convention numerically first (a positive rotation moves the trunk's local +Z/up axis toward local +X/forward in world frame when at identity orientation, i.e. genuinely "leans forward").
- Per the Kinematic Chain Rule (same as the 2026-07-31 fix): rotating the root also rotates every descendant body in world frame, including both feet, even though `Ankle_Pitch`/`Ankle_Roll`'s own values were untouched by the root edit itself. Re-ran the identical per-frame Newton solve (FK + 2x2 Jacobian on `Ankle_Pitch`/`Ankle_Roll`, 4 iterations) to drive `tilt_xy` back to exactly `(0,0)` at the new root orientation.
- Recomputed `joint_vel` via central differences, refit `body_pos_w`/`body_quat_w` via `mj_kinematics` from the (unchanged) root position + new root quat + new joint_pos, recomputed `body_lin_vel_w`/`body_ang_vel_w`, then re-leveled with a single constant Z shift so the lowest foot-contact point returned to exactly 0.0300 m.
- User separately asked whether the hip/knee looked "straightened" by this edit too -- verified numerically: `Left_Hip_Pitch`/`Left_Knee_Pitch`/etc. joint angles are byte-identical before/after (`0.00e+00` max diff, this script only ever touches root quat + the two ankle joints). The visual straightening is a real but separate effect of the SAME Kinematic Chain Rule: `Hip_Pitch_Left`/`Shank_Left` body *world*-frame pitch shifted +7.94°/+7.28° (inherited from the root rotation, same mechanism as the feet), stacking with the already-committed 2026-07-31 hip/knee joint-space damping to read as an even straighter leg, even though no additional joint-space edit was made to the legs in this pass.

**Result (all 6 clips, measured):** root pitch mean 0.05° → 8.05°, full-clip range [-2.67°,+4.74°] → [+5.33°,+12.74°] (short clip; other 5 near-identical, same source capture). Max foot tilt introduced by the root rotation before the ankle fix: 0.1392 (~24°, same order as the 2026-07-31 finding); after: `0.000000` on every clip. `Ankle_Pitch` mean shifted roughly -8° (more dorsiflexion) to compensate; no ankle value approached `model.jnt_range` (`Ankle_Pitch [-0.87,0.35]`, `Ankle_Roll [-0.44,0.44]`). Ankle joint-velocity peaks essentially unchanged (e.g. 6.175→6.538 rad/s) -- no spike introduced.

**Verification:** no NaNs in `joint_pos`/`body_pos_w` for any of the 6 files; foot-contact-Z minimum restored to exactly 0.0300 m after re-leveling on all 6; previewed via the real mjlab viewer (`-WithOverlay --agent zero --no-terminations True`, before vs. after paths) before overwriting `motions/data/` -- user confirmed the result looked right. Not yet validated against a live training run (expected: no training-time effect at all, per the caveat above, since this data path isn't read by `reset_from_motion_data` or the AMP observation).

## 2026-08-01 (same day, follow-up) -- postupperdofpos's during_scale raised 0.8 -> 1.0, removing the pre-/post-save distinction entirely

**Context:** user asked to raise `postupperdofpos`'s pre-save multiplier so it has the "same weight as before and after saving the ball" -- i.e. remove the `during_scale` discount pre-save entirely, not just narrow it further.

**Fix:** `during_scale` default changed `0.8 -> 1.0` (`rewards.py:postupperdofpos`). At 1.0, `torch.where(behind, 1.0, during_scale)` evaluates to `1.0` on both branches -- a literal no-op, so the term now pulls toward the target arm pose at constant, full strength for the entire episode, not just a reduced pull pre-save. Kept as a parameter (not hardcoded/removed) since this value has now been retuned four times (`0.3 -> 0.5 -> 0.8 -> 1.0`) and the original reason `during_scale` existed below 1.0 at all -- avoiding a fight with legitimate dive/counterbalance arm motion, the exact regression that got `arm_torque_limits`/`arm_action_rate_l2`/`arm_action_acc_l2` reverted on 2026-07-29 -- is a real, documented risk if this proves too strong once retrained.

**Verification:** updated `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_post_save_stance.py::test_gated_off_when_ball_not_behind` (hardcoded the old `during_scale=0.8` expected value at the target pose; changed to `1.0` -- the test now correctly shows no distinction between the `behind`/`not behind` cases, which is the intended effect of this fix, not a loss of coverage). `uv run pytest tests/simple_goalkeeper/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live smoke test: real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` construction, reset + 50 zero-action steps, finite rewards. Not yet validated against a live training run -- watch whether this reintroduces the 2026-07-28 "arms fighting dive motion" regression (footreach/ball_exit/episode-length dropping), since that's the specific risk of removing the pre-save discount entirely.

## 2026-08-01 (same day, follow-up) -- added forward hip-flexion joint-space edit (genuinely AMP-visible), new `amp_eye_view.py` diagnostic tool, new Visualization Honesty Rule in CLAUDE.md

**Context:** after the root-tilt push above, user asked whether the same effect could be made to actually influence AMP training (not just the ghost-overlay visual) and whether `play.py` could visualize "how AMP sees" a clip -- with an explicit instruction to never let `play.py` show something that misrepresents what training actually does.

**Root/torso lean is structurally invisible to AMP, proven directly:** `joint_pos` (byte-identical between a root-tilted and root-level rendering) drove `Trunk` world-pitch to `+8.05deg` with the real root quat vs. exactly `0.00deg` when the root quat was blanked -- confirms the root-tilt edit could never be training signal, only a viewer/diagnostic-script visual (`MotionResetManager.reset()`'s live path never reads root from NPZ; AMP's observation is `joint_pos`/`joint_vel` only).

**Fix 1 -- genuinely AMP-visible edit (Option 1, joint-space):** added +5deg extra forward `Hip_Pitch` flexion to the SWING leg only (same swing/stance detection + box-filtered blend as the 2026-07-31 fix, applied in the opposite direction), on top of the already-committed root tilt, across all 6 far-region clips. Ankle Newton-solve re-run afterward (Kinematic Chain Rule) to keep both feet flat. This is a real `joint_pos` change and IS sampled by AMP's `MotionDataset`/`WeightedMotionDataset` as expert data for these clips (confirmed these exact 6 files are hardcoded in `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py:274-282`). Caveat restated for the record: since `Hip_Pitch` is downstream of the (untouched) root, this cannot make the TRUNK itself visually lean -- it deepens the swing leg's forward reach under a torso AMP still can't perceive as tilting. Verified: swing-leg hip/knee joint angles byte-identical is NOT the claim here (this edit DOES touch `Hip_Pitch`); ankle re-flattened to `tilt_xy` `0.000000` on all 6 clips; no NaNs; re-level shift near-zero (<0.5mm).

**Fix 2 -- `scripts/amp_eye_view.py` (new tool, `sgk_amp_eye_view` CLI + `play.py --amp-eye-view True`):** renders any clip re-anchored, per frame, to whichever foot is planted, canonical flat, instead of the file's real root frame. Mathematically: re-expressing every body relative to ANY single body in the kinematic chain (not just the root) cancels the root transform out completely, leaving a rendering driven purely by relative joint angles -- i.e. what AMP's `joint_pos`/`joint_vel` actually implies, made visible. First implementation attempt (forcing root orientation to literal identity) was WRONG and is documented in the module docstring as "do not resurrect": it broke this project's own feet-always-flat invariant (every frame, both feet, `tilt_xy ~ 0`, established since the 2026-07-18 leveling pass) because the recorded joint angles were tuned assuming the real root, producing a nonsensical "leaning back" render that had nothing to do with the edit being checked. Corrected by anchoring to the foot instead (already flat/grounded by construction) -- verified both feet stay exactly flat (`tilt_xy` max `0.00000`) in the corrected render, and the hip-flex edit's real effect shows up correctly at the swing foot's position relative to the anchor (~8mm delta at peak reach) while correctly NOT appearing in trunk-relative-to-anchor pitch (expected: the edit only touches the swing leg, and the anchor at any instant is the stance leg, untouched by the edit).

**Fix 2b -- anchor-selection bug found and fixed same day:** initial anchor logic used a fixed absolute-height threshold (0.032m) to decide "is this foot grounded." On `new_doublestepleft_short_booster_t1.npz`, neither foot ever dipped below that threshold for the first ~16 frames (both hovering 0.030-0.040m), so the "keep previous anchor on ambiguity" fallback froze onto whichever foot an initial tie-break happened to pick -- which turned out to be the swinging (not stance) foot for that whole stretch, confirmed by measuring the anchor foot's rendered position landing exactly on the canonical target `(0,0,0.03)` at a frame independently known to be near-peak swing height. Fixed by anchoring to whichever foot is simply LOWER at each frame (no absolute threshold), with a 0.015m hysteresis band to avoid pure-noise flicker at genuine crossover frames. Re-verified: both feet flat throughout on all 6 clips under the corrected method.

**Fix 3 -- `CLAUDE.md` "Visualization Honesty Rule" (new section):** codifies never letting `play.py` (or any viewer/diagnostic tool) render something that could be mistaken for actual training-observed behavior without a clearly labeled distinction; documents the root=identity mistake and its foot-anchored fix as the canonical example; points at `--amp-eye-view` as the tool to check "does this edit reach AMP" before concluding a reference-motion edit will affect training.

**Verification:** all 6 clips -- ankle `tilt_xy` restored to `0.000000`; no NaNs in `joint_pos`/`body_pos_w`; AMP-eye-view before/after confirmed the root-tilt-only portion produces zero delta under the foot-anchored lens (as expected) while the hip-flex portion produces a real, measurable delta (as expected); previewed via real viewer + AMP-eye viewer (`commands_data.txt`) before overwriting `motions/data/` -- user confirmed before push. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-08-01 (same day, follow-up) -- near/far range revert, leading-foot orientation retarget (90deg->60deg off forward), airborne-only gate on postleadfootorientation, new foot-angle viewer plot

**Context:** user requested four related tweaks in one session: (1) narrow the near/far region boundary back to 0.5m (was widened to 0.65m on 2026-07-23), (2) narrow the far-region outer bound back to 1.1m (was 1.3m), (3) check the far-region difficulty-convergence gap still makes sense once (1)/(2) land, (4) retarget the leading foot's "block posture" save orientation from fully sideways (parallel to world/robot Y, 90deg off forward) to 60deg off forward (30deg off Y) -- a shallower stance. A follow-up in the same session added: (5) the reward that pulls the leading foot back to forward post-save (`postleadfootorientation`, already existed, found via code search rather than being missing) needed to stop firing once the foot is actually planted, since it was found to be causing ground slippage by continuing to pull a grounded foot to rotate; and (6) a MuJoCo-viewer plot to let the user visually verify the new 60deg target is actually being reached.

**Fix 1/2 -- near/far boundary 0.65->0.5, far outer bound 1.3->1.1:** reverted in every location the 2026-07-23/earlier widenings touched, kept in sync per `regions.py`'s own cross-reference comment:
- `mdp/regions.py`: `_REGION_Y_END_RANGE` near ends `(0.15,0.65)`/`(-0.65,-0.15)` -> `(0.15,0.5)`/`(-0.5,-0.15)`; far bounds `(0.65,1.3)`/`(-1.3,-0.65)` -> `(0.5,1.1)`/`(-1.1,-0.5)`.
- `mdp/rewards.py`: `_get_reach_target_y`'s `wide_threshold` default `0.65` -> `0.5`.
- `mdp/events.py`: `far_travel_curriculum.__init__`'s `_lo`/`_hi` defaults `0.65`/`1.3` -> `0.5`/`1.1` (no explicit override at the one call site, `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`, so this alone repoints the curriculum); docstring's worked-example numbers recomputed to match (`~0.722/~1.083` -> `~0.567/~0.900`).
- `tasks/goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`: single-disc task's training AND play `reset_ball` `y_end_range` `(-1.3,1.3)` -> `(-1.1,1.1)` (training/play parity rule) -- this is a separate, non-region-conditioned task from the multi-disc one, previously not covered by the 2026-07-23 widening's own audit trail since that fix only touched the region system.
- `scripts/play.py`: comment referencing the old `0.65` value (viewer blue/green sphere docstring) updated to `0.5`.

**Fix 3 -- difficulty-convergence gap, no code change:** `far_travel_curriculum` seeds `env._far_inner` at cu=0 to `lo + 0.1111*(hi-lo)` (G1 STEP's `inner_frac`), a proportional formula, not a literal `+0.05m`. With the old `lo=0.65,hi=1.3` that was `~0.722` (`+0.072m` gap above the boundary); with the new `lo=0.5,hi=1.1` it's `~0.567` (`+0.067m` gap) automatically, no override needed -- the mechanism the user remembered already exists and self-adapts to the new bounds.

**Fix 4 -- leading-foot orientation target 90deg->60deg off forward:** `inner_face_orientation_save` (one-shot bonus at `softstop`) and `foot_inner_face_continuous` (dense per-step, `~behind`-gated) both previously measured alignment against a pure coordinate axis (world Y / robot-local Y respectively -- a 90deg-off-forward target). Both retargeted to a constructed target vector `(cos60deg, +-sin60deg, 0)` (new module constants `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG=60.0`/`_FOOT_TARGET_COS`/`_FOOT_TARGET_SIN`, `rewards.py`), sign-flipped per assigned foot side, each function keeping its own pre-existing frame choice (world for the one-shot term, robot-local for the continuous term -- a pre-existing inconsistency between the two, not introduced or fixed by this change, flagged for a possible future pass). `inner_face_orientation_save`'s `alignment_threshold=0.7` (~45deg tolerance) left numerically unchanged; it now measures a cone around the new 60deg target instead of the old 90deg one, per explicit user confirmation.

**Fix 5 -- `postleadfootorientation` airborne-only gate:** was unconditional through the entire post-save (`behind`) window, rewarding rotation-to-forward whether the foot was airborne or already planted. User found this was pulling a grounded foot to keep rotating, which slips against the floor instead of turning cleanly (the same "active rotation + ground contact = slippage" problem `feet_slippage` already guards against). Added a ground-contact gate using the identical `feet_contact`+`ball_contact` sensor pattern `feet_slippage` uses (ball-contact excluded from the "grounded" read, so a lingering foot-ball touch at the save moment isn't misread as planted): reward now pays out only while the assigned foot is genuinely airborne, zero once grounded. No new persistent state needed (unlike the blue-waypoint's `_blue_was_airborne` latch) since this only needs the current-frame contact state, not a transition/edge detector.

**Fix 6 -- new `assigned_foot_angle_deg` viewer plot (`scripts/play.py`):** `_compute_assigned_foot_angle_deg` reuses `foot_inner_face_continuous`'s exact geometry (not a fresh reimplementation) to compute the assigned foot's unsigned angle off robot-forward in degrees for the single tracked env; `_patch_viewer_foot_orientation_plot` wires it into a new always-visible-front P-panel figure (same raw-custom-plot pattern as `_patch_viewer_wrong_foot_contact_plot`), title-annotated with the target (60deg) and tolerance band (+-45.57deg = degrees(acos(0.7))) so the user can watch the value converge toward ~60deg instead of the old ~90deg during play.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on all 5 changed files (`rewards.py`, `regions.py`, `events.py`, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, `play.py`) -- no syntax errors. Live smoke test: `sgk_play Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc --agent zero --num-envs 4` for ~15s / 5 episodes -- no exceptions, all reward terms (including the three rewritten ones) logging finite values every step (`foot_inner_face_continuous` producing non-zero partial-alignment values, `postleadfootorientation` correctly reading 0.00 under a zero-action policy that never saves). Native viewer opened without error, confirming the new plot patch doesn't break viewer setup. Not yet validated against a live training run -- the range narrowing changes the region/difficulty distribution and the orientation retarget changes what a "correct" save looks like, both are training-time-relevant.

## 2026-08-01 (same day, follow-up) -- new `postsave_foot_airtime` reward: time-boxed airborne bonus after the save

**Context:** user asked whether an existing airborne-related reward could be extended a bit past the save so the leading foot has more hangtime to rotate before `postleadfootorientation` (the reward that pulls it back toward forward, just made airborne-only earlier this session) actually has something to reward. `foot_clearance` (+2.0) is the only existing airborne-related term, but its own docstring explicitly deactivates it once `behind` fires "to avoid rewarding post-save hopping" -- removing that gate would reopen the exact failure mode it exists to prevent, and it scores whichever of the two feet is higher (not specifically the leading foot) toward a fixed 10cm target that has no natural meaning post-dive.

**Fix -- new `postsave_foot_airtime` reward (`rewards.py`, registered in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`), weight +2.0 (lowered to +1.0 same day, see below):** flat (not height-shaped) bonus for the assigned/leading foot staying airborne, active only for a short fixed window starting the instant `behind` first flips true (`window_steps=10`, ~0.2s at dt=0.02s, user-confirmed window length). Window tracked via two new latched buffers (`env._psa_prev_behind`, `env._psa_window_start`), edge-detecting the `behind` transition and recording the triggering step, reset on `episode_length_buf <= 1` same as this file's other latch-based terms (`_ifos_ss_prev`/`_ifos_flag`, `_blue_was_airborne`, etc.). Ground-contact detection reuses `postleadfootorientation`'s exact `feet_contact`+`ball_contact` sensor pattern (ball-contact excluded from the "grounded" read). Time-boxing (rather than `foot_clearance`'s unconditional-until-`behind` shape) is the deliberate difference: after the window elapses the term goes to zero regardless of contact state, so it cannot turn into indefinite post-save hopping the way removing `foot_clearance`'s gate would.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `rewards.py`, `mdp/__init__.py` (added to the explicit re-export list, required for `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s `gk_mdp.postsave_foot_airtime` reference to resolve), `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` -- no syntax errors. Live smoke test: `sgk_play Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc --agent zero --num-envs 4 --viewer viser` for ~40s -- reward-term table confirms `postsave_foot_airtime` registered at weight 2.0, no exceptions across ~3000 log lines. Term reads 0.00 throughout (690/690 samples), matching `postleadfootorientation`'s identical 0.00 reading in the same run -- both share the `behind` precondition, which a frozen zero-action policy never satisfies (confirmed via `SB·` never showing a fired stopball flag in the same log), so this is the expected null result for this test, not evidence of a bug. Not yet validated against a live training run.

**FIX 2026-08-01 (user request, same day):** weight lowered `2.0 -> 1.0` -- user judged 2.0 too strong relative to the rest of the post-save recovery stack (`postleadfootorientation`+2.0, `postorientation`+3.0, `postupperdofpos`+5.0, etc.) before any training data existed to confirm either way; a same-magnitude bonus purely for hovering risked competing with those stabilization terms. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`.

## 2026-08-03 -- native viewer: swap the `stopball` P-panel plot for `postsave_foot_airtime`

**Context:** user replaying `model_39750` (`6144_footorientfix_2026-08-01` run) in the native MuJoCo viewer asked to see `postsave_foot_airtime` (the 2026-08-01 airborne-hangtime bonus) live, in place of `stopball`'s plot -- `stopball` already fires reliably and doesn't need dedicated screen space, and `postsave_foot_airtime` was registered late enough in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s `RewardTermCfg` dict (after ~40 other terms) that it falls outside `max_viewports` (12) and is silently never rendered, same class of problem `_patch_viewer_postupperdofpos_plot`/`_patch_viewer_new_reward_plots` already solved for other terms.

**Fix:** new `_patch_viewer_postsave_airtime_plot` (`scripts/play.py`), same `_term_names` front-promotion mechanism as the existing patches, plus an explicit demote step (`stopball` moved to the back of the list) -- promoting `postsave_foot_airtime` alone would only guarantee something drops off the front-12, not specifically `stopball`. Wired into the native-viewer patch chain in `run_play` after the other P-panel patches.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `play.py` -- no syntax errors. Standalone logic test of the swap (fake `_term_names` list including both terms) confirms `postsave_foot_airtime` ends up first and `stopball` ends up last after `setup()` runs. Not run against the real native viewer in this session (headless environment, no display) -- user should confirm visually with `sgk_play Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc-WithOverlay --checkpoint-file logs/rsl_rl/intercept_simple_goalkeeper_multidisc/2026-08-01_17-14-57_6144_footorientfix_2026-08-01/model_39750.pt`.

## 2026-08-03 -- `postsave_foot_airtime` window could reopen mid-episode via a wrong-foot deflection, undermining its own time-box

**Context:** user asked (before seeing any actual bad behavior in the current checkpoint) whether the policy could learn to exploit `postsave_foot_airtime` by lifting the foot again post-save, and specifically whether a "fires once, never again once it returns to 0" gate already existed.

**Investigation found a real, previously-unguarded gap:** `_ball_is_behind(env, ball_name)` = `(ball_x_local < 0) | _sb_flag | _softstop_flag`. `_sb_flag`/`_softstop_flag` (set in `stopball`/`softstop`, both `rewards.py`) are genuine one-way per-episode latches (`env._sb_flag |= fired`, only reset at episode start) -- but only fire when `correct_foot_contact` holds. The raw `ball_x_local < 0` component is NOT latched, recomputed fresh every step. `postsave_foot_airtime`'s window previously restarted on every `behind` rising edge (`just_became_behind = behind & ~env._psa_prev_behind`), not just the first ever. Sequence that reopens it: ball crosses the goal line untouched (`behind`->True, window opens, no flag set yet) -> a WRONG-foot touch knocks it back to x>=0 (`correct_foot_contact` false, so neither sticky flag sets, `behind`->False) -> ball rolls behind again (`behind`->True again) -> window reopens, handing out another ~0.2s airborne bonus disconnected from any genuine correct-foot save. This project already has a documented history of wrong-foot deflections actually occurring in practice (see `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact`'s fix history above), so this isn't purely theoretical. Not observed yet in the current checkpoint, per the user's own report -- caught proactively via code reading, not from an observed failure.

**Fix:** new `env._psa_used` per-episode one-way latch (`rewards.py:postsave_foot_airtime`), reset only at `episode_length_buf <= 1`. `just_became_behind` now additionally requires `~env._psa_used`, and `env._psa_used |= just_became_behind` -- so only the very first `behind` rising edge each episode can ever set `_psa_window_start`; every subsequent rising edge (however `behind` got toggled) is ignored for the rest of that episode. Scope note: this closes the cross-episode re-trigger gap only -- the smaller secondary issue (within one open 10-step window, every airborne step is paid, not just the first liftoff) was explicitly left as-is per user request, since it's already tightly time-boxed (~0.2s) by design.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `rewards.py` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass (no existing test covered this function; none needed updating). Standalone tensor-level simulation of the state machine (behind: False x4 -> True -> True x2 -> False x4 [simulated wrong-foot toggle] -> True x3 -> True [window should have just elapsed]) confirms `_psa_window_start` latches at its first value and does NOT move on the later False->True transition, and correctly resets to fresh on the next episode. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-08-03 -- `postupperdofpos` fought shoulder's legitimate dive-reach excursion (root cause, G1-comparison); new `angular_momentum_penalty` ported from BoosterT1mjlab

**Context:** live-checkpoint replay this session (comparing `2026-08-01_01-55-04_6144_ampvisiblehipflex` vs `2026-08-01_17-14-57_6144_footorientfix`) found `postupperdofpos` badly non-convergent on the newer checkpoint -- near-region post-save arm error 8.4x worse than the older checkpoint (1.48 -> 12.44), with elbow/shoulder joints pinning near their extreme values and never recovering, `arm_dof_vel` in the steady window ~12x higher (arms still actively diverging, not settled-then-static). Several hypotheses (`during_scale`, the foot-orientation retarget, airborne-hover pressure from `postsave_foot_airtime`) were investigated and each ruled out or downgraded to secondary/unproven as the ROOT cause (see the three preceding investigation-report entries this session, plus the conversation's own git-timestamp correction that `during_scale` was actually unchanged between the two compared checkpoints). User asked to re-derive the answer from G1's actual code (this project's proven reference) rather than keep guessing, since "G1 didn't experience this bug."

**Root cause, found via a skeptical, cited-line-by-line G1 comparison (dispatched as an independent subagent):** G1's own post-catch upper-body recovery reward, `_reward_postupperdofpos` (`Humanoid-Goalkeeper/legged_gym/legged_gym/envs/base/legged_robot.py:1502-1507`), is a near-line-for-line match to this port's mechanism (same `exp(-k*sum_sq_err)` kernel, same `behind`-style gating) -- EXCEPT for joint scope. G1's `upper_body_joint_indices = cat(elbow_joint_indices, wrist_joint_indices)` (`legged_robot.py:1315`, built from `cfg.control.elbow_joints`/`wrist_joints`, `g1_29_config.py:175,177`) -- **shoulder is never included**. SGK's `_ARM_JOINT_CFG` (`rewards.py`) included `Shoulder_Pitch`/`Shoulder_Roll` alongside `Elbow_Pitch`/`Elbow_Yaw` -- the shoulder being by far the largest-excursion joint during a dive/reach. This directly explains the pre-existing, already-documented saturation finding (post-save error 0.155 near vs 7.81 far, ~50x, saturating `exp(-kernel_scale*err)` to near-zero gradient regardless of `kernel_scale` tuning): shoulder's large natural swing dominates the shared sum-of-squares. Compounding this, every OTHER arm-related term (`arm_dof_vel`, `action_rate_l2`, `action_acc_l2`) penalizes MOVEMENT, not position -- once a joint goes still at a static extreme, all of those read ~0, so nothing pulls a pinned joint back. G1 never faces this because it never asks the reward to recover the one joint that legitimately needs to travel far. The subagent also ruled out (with citations) actuator stiffness, a post-catch arm freeze, episode-length mismatch, and curriculum as alternative G1 mechanisms -- joint scope is the one real, concrete difference.

**Also corrected:** an existing, never-actually-checked claim in this function's own docstring (FIX 2026-07-23, "G1's arms are the active catching limb... SGK's arms have no such structural pull") is refuted by the above -- annotated in place (not rewritten) as a correction, since the weight history it documents (1.0 -> 5.0) is still accurate, only its causal reasoning was wrong.

**Fix 1 -- narrow `_ARM_JOINT_CFG`** (`rewards.py` -- confirmed via grep this is `postupperdofpos`'s ONLY consumer, so a direct edit is safe) from 8 joints (`Shoulder_Pitch/Roll + Elbow_Pitch/Yaw` x2 sides) to 4 (`Elbow_Pitch/Yaw` x2 sides only), matching G1's scope exactly. `postupperdofpos` no longer tries to recover shoulder at all -- matching G1's actual design, not a literal-but-wrong joint-count port. Weight (5.0), `kernel_scale` (0.15), `during_scale` (1.0) all left unchanged; only the joint scope moved.

**Fix 2 -- new `angular_momentum_penalty` reward** (`rewards.py`, registered `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, weight -0.02), ported near-verbatim from the sibling `BoosterT1mjlab` project's `tasks/velocity/mdp/rewards.py:angular_momentum_penalty` (identical weight there), whose own docstring states its exact purpose: "Penalize whole-body angular momentum to encourage natural arm swing." Reads MuJoCo's native `subtreeangmom` sensor (`root_angmom`, body=`Trunk`) -- **already present in this project's own `t1.xml`/`t1_headless.xml`** (confirmed via grep it was carried over with the shared robot asset lineage) but never read by any Python code here before this fix; `BuiltinSensor.from_existing`-style auto-discovery means no scene-config registration was needed, only the reward function + its `RewardTermCfg`. Physically adaptive by construction: a genuine dive/counterbalance swing legitimately produces high momentum (so this term doesn't fight it, unlike `postupperdofpos`'s fixed-pose pull), and only meaningfully penalizes once the body should already be stable and momentum is still nonzero. Small/gentle by design (matching BoosterT1mjlab's own weight), a complement to `postupperdofpos`, not a replacement. Independently corroborated by external literature surveyed the same session (arXiv 2507.04140, "Learning Humanoid Arm Motion via Centroidal Momentum Regularized Multi-Agent RL" -- a "CAM damping reward" for the identical underlying problem) and by `booster_deploy`'s real deployed T1 walk policy (`tasks/locomotion/locomotion.py`), which gives the policy full, only moderately-damped control over shoulder+elbow rather than constraining them via a fixed-pose reward.

**Considered and deliberately NOT changed:** arm actuator gains (`t1_constants.py`). `booster_deploy`'s real deployed arm actuators use `stiffness=50, damping=1.0`; this project's physically-derived (reflected-inertia + 5Hz natural-frequency model) arm actuator computes `kp=27.88, kd=3.55` -- not a clean "booster uses lower gains" story (their stiffness is HIGHER than ours, their damping is 3.5x LOWER), and the two are derived by different methodologies (real motor physics vs. empirical walking-specific tuning) that aren't directly comparable. Left untouched pending evidence the above two fixes aren't sufficient.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `rewards.py`, `mdp/__init__.py`, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live checks: confirmed `env.scene["robot/root_angmom"]` resolves to a `BuiltinSensor` with shape `(N,3)`, auto-discovered with no scene-config changes; confirmed `angular_momentum_penalty` is registered (`weight=-0.02`) and produces finite, nonzero values that scale up under randomized (vs. zero) actions, as physically expected; confirmed `postupperdofpos`'s resolved joint scope is now exactly `['Left_Elbow_Pitch', 'Left_Elbow_Yaw', 'Right_Elbow_Pitch', 'Right_Elbow_Yaw']` (no shoulder); 30-step real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` rollout with both zero and randomized actions produced finite rewards throughout. Not yet validated against a live training run -- next checkpoint should be compared against the `footorientfix`/`ampvisiblehipflex` baselines using this session's own steady-post-save-window arm-error methodology.

## 2026-08-03 (same day, follow-up) -- post-shoulder-scope-fix training run still shows a slow post-save arm drift; traced to a correlated whole-body yaw drift; new P-panel "post-recovery" view

**Context:** user watched a fresh training run (`2026-08-03_13-47-54_6144_shoulderscopefix_2026-08-03`, `model_13250.pt` -- the first checkpoint trained under the shoulder-scope + `angular_momentum_penalty` fixes above) in the native viewer's analytics text dump and reported "all the arm movement is after the save event occurred" -- i.e. the arms look fine through the entire approach/dive and only start drifting once the save actually happens, ruling out (for this specific observation) any theory that blames the dive/approach phase itself.

**Reading the pasted analytics dump directly (no probe needed for this part):** `postupperdofpos` sits at a stable ~4.5-4.7 (out of a max 5.0) for the ENTIRE pre-save approach, confirming pre-save arm tracking is genuinely fine. The instant `SB✓`/`SS✓` fire (save event), `postupperdofpos` holds steady for a few steps then begins a smooth, monotonic decline over roughly the next 60 steps (~1.2s at dt=0.02s) down to a low plateau (~0.9-1.0) -- not a sudden spike/chaotic jump, a slow, steady drift away from the target that eventually levels off.

**Root-cause dig (live checkpoint replay of this exact checkpoint, `probe_postsave_drift.py`, synchronous run, no backgrounding):** traced elbow error, shoulder error (unrewarded now, but still measured), whole-body yaw heading, and `root_angmom` magnitude together through a real post-save window on the same checkpoint. Real numbers (t = steps since `behind` first true):

| t | elbow_err | shoulder_err | yaw (deg) | angmom |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0.152 | 0.411 | -4.9 | 0.98 |
| 29 | 0.339 | 1.207 | -15.7 | 0.17 |
| 59 | 0.557 | 2.296 | -18.6 | 0.15 |
| 69 | 0.646 | 2.383 | -18.0 | 0.23 |
| 99 | 1.613 | 1.703 | -12.6 | 0.39 |

Elbow error climbs steadily the whole window (matches the pasted log's `postupperdofpos` decline exactly). Shoulder error climbs even faster, PEAKS around t+60-70, then partially recovers. **Whole-body yaw follows the identical time-course**: drifts from -4.9° at the save moment out to -18.6° by t+59, holds through t+69, then starts recovering back toward -12.6° by t+99 -- the same rise-peak-partial-recovery shape as shoulder error, on the same timescale.

**Interpretation (not yet confirmed as fully causal, but a strong, non-obvious correlation worth flagging directly):** the arm drift may be substantially a DOWNSTREAM SYMPTOM of a ~15-19° whole-body yaw drift that develops after the save and takes on the order of 100+ steps to correct, rather than purely an arm-reward-design problem. `ang_vel_z` (weight -0.5, penalizes yaw rate) and `postheadingorientation` (weight 2.5, pulls yaw back to world +X, active only post-save) are the two existing mechanisms that should be fighting this, and the data shows they eventually do win, just slowly. Whether the yaw drift itself is a genuine necessary consequence of shedding the dive's angular momentum, an artifact of some other reward pulling the body to turn, or under-tuned yaw-recovery gains, is NOT yet determined -- this is a real, freshly-found lead, not a diagnosed root cause. The shoulder-scope fix above remains correct and necessary (it fixes a real gradient-saturation bug verified independently of this finding) but is evidently not sufficient by itself to eliminate all post-save arm movement, since the arms are plausibly reacting to (or helping execute) a whole-body rotation that has its own, still-unidentified cause.

**New viewer feature (user request): P-panel now shows the full post-recovery reward family.** `KEY_P` already toggles the P-panel's visibility natively (mjlab's own `TOGGLE_PLOTS` binding, not something added here) -- previously the front-12 visible slots were an accumulated mix from several unrelated promotion patches (`cleanstop`, arm-posture terms, wrong-foot/head flash, foot-orientation angle, the `postsave_foot_airtime`-for-`stopball` swap). New `_patch_viewer_post_recovery_plots` (`scripts/play.py`) supersedes all of that, setting the front-12 to exactly the 12 reward terms relevant to post-save recovery, built directly from the code (every function that reads `_ball_is_behind` and gates ON with it -- `postangvel`, `postlinvel`, `postupperdofpos`, `postwaistdofpos`, `postlegdofpos`, `postleadfootorientation`, `postsave_foot_airtime`, `postheadingorientation`, `penalize_arm_above_shoulder` -- plus three always-active terms from the same family directly relevant to this investigation: `postorientation`, `angular_momentum_penalty`, `arm_dof_vel`). Exactly 12 terms, so all are guaranteed visible with nothing pushed off-cap.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `play.py` -- no syntax errors. Standalone logic test confirms all 12 post-recovery terms occupy the front-12 `_term_names` slots after `setup()` runs. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. The drift-tracing probe (`probe_postsave_drift.py`, scratchpad, not committed) ran synchronously to completion against the real `model_13250.pt` checkpoint via the exact same env-construction/checkpoint-loading path `play.py`'s `run_play` uses (`AMPEnvWrapper`, `HimAMPOnPolicyRunner`, `_get_actor_current_obs`) -- not run against the native viewer itself (headless environment, no display) -- user should confirm the new P-panel content visually. Root cause of the yaw drift itself is NOT yet found -- flagged as the concrete next investigation thread, not resolved here.

## 2026-08-03 (same day, follow-up) -- root cause of the post-save yaw drift found (Hip_Yaw reaction torque); `postleadfootorientation` capped to the same window as `postsave_foot_airtime`

**Root-cause dig (live checkpoint replay, `probe_hipyaw_causal.py`, 40 real post-save episodes on `model_13250.pt`, run synchronously):** confirmed directly in `t1_headless.xml` that T1's ankle has ONLY `Ankle_Pitch`/`Ankle_Roll` -- no ankle-yaw joint exists anywhere in the leg. The only joint that can change which direction a foot points is that leg's own `Hip_Yaw`. Since `postleadfootorientation` rewards rotating the assigned foot from its ~60deg-off-forward block-posture angle back to forward, and the ankle physically cannot do this, the ONLY way to execute it is via the assigned leg's `Hip_Yaw` -- which reaction-torques the trunk (basic conservation of angular momentum through the kinematic chain).

Measured directly, split by which foot was assigned to the save:

| | yaw drift @ t+40 | mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| left-foot saves (n=24) | **+11.3deg** | `Left_Hip_Yaw` unwinds 0.83 -> 0.22 rad over the window |
| right-foot saves (n=16) | **-5.1deg** | `Right_Hip_Yaw` unwinds -0.75 -> -0.34 rad (mirror image) |

The mirror symmetry (left-assigned saves yaw the trunk positive, right-assigned saves yaw it negative, in both cases exactly timed with that leg's own Hip_Yaw unwinding) is decisive: this is a direct mechanical consequence of `postleadfootorientation` doing its job, not noise or an unrelated bug. The arms are very plausibly reacting to (or helping manage, per `angular_momentum_penalty`'s own rationale) this whole-body yaw disturbance, which would explain why arm error tracks the same rise-peak-recover timescale documented in the entry above.

**Fix (user request):** `postleadfootorientation` was previously gated only by `behind & airborne`, with NO time limit -- it could keep paying out (and therefore keep incentivizing the Hip_Yaw reaction torque) for the entire remainder of the post-save episode. Capped it to the exact same fixed window `postsave_foot_airtime` already uses (`window_steps=10`, ~0.2s), via a new shared helper `_postsave_airtime_window` (`rewards.py`) that both functions now call -- factored out of `postsave_foot_airtime`'s existing one-shot-latch window logic (added earlier today), no behavior change to that function beyond the extraction. `postleadfootorientation` gained a `window_steps: int = 10` parameter (unregistered in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, so both terms share the same default and stay in sync automatically without needing an explicit config override). This doesn't change the rotation itself or its airborne-only gate -- it only bounds how long the policy is incentivized to keep applying the reaction torque once the window elapses, mirroring the same "don't let a good mechanism run open-ended" reasoning `postsave_foot_airtime` itself was built with (2026-08-01) and `foot_clearance`'s pre-existing post-save deactivation.

**Safety of the shared helper (verified, not just asserted):** calling `_postsave_airtime_window` from two different reward functions in the same tick is idempotent regardless of which is registered first in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s RewardManager -- the state-mutating part only reacts to a `behind` rising edge, and `_psa_prev_behind` is overwritten to the current `behind` value on every call, so a same-tick second call always sees `just_became_behind` as False and only recomputes the (unchanged) `in_window` result.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `rewards.py` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live check: forced `_sb_flag` true on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` (4 envs, zero-action policy) and stepped 15 times -- `_postsave_airtime_window` reads `True` for steps 0-9 and `False` from step 10 onward exactly as configured (`window_steps=10`), and both `postleadfootorientation` and `postsave_foot_airtime` correctly read 0.0 once the window elapses. Not yet validated against a live training run -- the yaw-drift magnitude/duration should be re-measured against this same checkpoint lineage once retrained.

## 2026-08-04 -- postsave_foot_airtime window 10->20 steps; reshaped flat bonus to a linear time-ramp

**Context:** user reviewed `2026-08-03_23-38-41_6144_yawwindowfix_2026-08-03/model_14500.pt` (the first checkpoint trained under the shared-window fix above) and requested two changes: (1) the airtime window (~0.2s) felt too short, wanted ~0.4s; (2) the flat 1.0-whenever-airborne-and-in-window bonus gives no incentive to stay up LONGER within the window specifically -- landing at step 1 and landing at step 19 of the old 10-step window scored identically -- wanted a gradient that rewards more hangtime with more reward.

**Fix 1 -- window_steps 10 -> 20:** changed the default on all three functions that share this window (`_postsave_airtime_window`, `postsave_foot_airtime`, `postleadfootorientation` -- kept in sync since none of the three is overridden with an explicit `window_steps` in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s registration, so all three always share whatever the shared default is).

**Fix 2 -- linear time-ramp:** `postsave_foot_airtime`'s payout is now `(elapsed_steps_since_window_open + 1) / window_steps` (masked by the existing `in_window & airborne` gate, unchanged) instead of a flat `1.0`. At `window_steps=20`: scores `0.05` at the first in-window step, climbing linearly to exactly `1.0` at the last (step 19), then `0.0` once the window elapses (unchanged cutoff behavior). Peak magnitude is unchanged (still tops out at `1.0 x weight`) -- this reshapes WHERE the reward concentrates within the window (rewarding sustained hangtime specifically), not the total budget available. `postleadfootorientation`'s own shape (continuous alignment-based, not flat) was left untouched -- only its shared WINDOW length changed via fix 1, not its payout formula, since the user's gradient request was specifically about the airtime term.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `rewards.py` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live checks on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv`: (a) confirmed `_postsave_airtime_window` now reads `True` for steps 0-19 and `False` from step 20 onward (was 0-9/10+); (b) confirmed the ramp formula directly (`elapsed`/`env._psa_window_start` read straight off the env) produces `0.05, 0.10, 0.15, ..., 1.00` across steps 0-19 and `0.0` (masked by `in_window`) from step 20 on, matching the intended shape exactly. Not yet validated against a live training run.

## 2026-08-04 (same day, follow-up) -- far-region arm columns un-masked in AMP, matching near (both sides now live)

**Context:** this session's near-vs-far investigation found near-region post-save arm recovery measurably better than far's (see the "root-cause dig" entry above), and confirmed the AMP live-vs-masked arm asymmetry (near=live, far=masked, from the 2026-07-16 fix) does NOT explain the difference during the post-save recovery phase specifically -- direct measurement showed AMP's arm columns are frozen for BOTH regions the instant softstop fires (~k=5-15), so the near/far AMP difference only ever mattered pre-save/during the approach. User's request: since near performs better and near is the one with live AMP arm supervision, try giving far the same live supervision during the approach too, as an experiment -- a combination (unmask everyone) not previously tested. The 2026-07-15 attempt that WAS previously tried and reverted was the opposite direction (mask everyone), found to make arm swinging worse; this is a genuinely new configuration, not a re-run of that reverted attempt.

**Fix:** `far_region_ids` default changed `(1, 3) -> ()` in both `joint_pos_abs_arms_masked_by_region` and `joint_vel_abs_arms_masked_by_region` (`observations.py`) -- no region's arm columns get masked now, so far regions keep real, live arm `joint_pos`/`joint_vel` in their AMP input during the pre-save/approach phase, matching near. Expert/reference-clip side updated to match in the same commit: `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_runner_cfg`'s `freeze_joint_names` (`goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py`) changed from conditionally freezing far-region reference clips' arm columns to always `None` -- both policy and expert sides must agree per region, same invariant the original 2026-07-16 fix established, now pointing the other way (unmasked instead of far-masked). `_FAR_REGION_NAMES`/`ARM_JOINT_NAMES` constants kept, not deleted -- the former is still used for an unrelated purpose (`motion_weights`), the latter left available for any future re-enable.

The separate softstop-gated freeze (all 21 joints, both regions, the instant a save is confirmed) is completely unaffected by this change -- this only touches the pre-save arm-masking layer.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on both changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live check: pinned a real env to `left_far`, stepped with random actions, called `joint_pos_abs_arms_masked_by_region` directly and confirmed its elbow-column output is now byte-identical to the real `robot.data.joint_pos` (previously would have read frozen `default_joint_pos`). Not yet validated against a live training run -- this is an explicit experiment per user request; the original 2026-07-16 fix exists because *some* far-region AMP arm masking was found necessary at the time (pre-save "weird arm swinging"), so this should be watched for a regression on that specific symptom, not just improvement on post-save recovery.

## 2026-08-05 (same day, follow-up) -- foot_inner_face_continuous: steepened the overshoot-side gradient, foot_clearance-style

**Context:** user asked why the 2026-08-01 leading-foot orientation retarget (90deg fully-sideways -> 60deg off forward, see that date's entry) appeared to only show up in far-region play, not near. Investigated live via a headless checkpoint-replay probe (`probe_foot_angle_near_far.py`, scratchpad, not committed) against `model_39750.pt` (`2026-08-04_16-43-37_6144_airtimerampfix_2026-08-04`), forcing each of the 4 static regions in turn and logging the assigned foot's angle off robot-forward at every correct-foot `softstop` rising edge (128 near-region saves/side, ~115-127 far-region saves/side):

| region | mean foot angle (target=60deg) | mean ball approach angle off pure -X |
|---|---|---|
| left_near | 84.7deg | 24.1deg |
| right_near | 82.4deg | 30.9deg |
| left_far | 68.6deg | 50.0deg |
| right_far | 68.9deg | 46.0deg |

Confirmed via `git show` on the 2026-08-01 retarget commit (`036222f`) that the reward code itself has no region branch at all -- both `inner_face_orientation_save` and `foot_inner_face_continuous` were edited together in that commit and share the same module-level `_FOOT_TARGET_COS`/`_FOOT_TARGET_SIN` constants regardless of region. So the near/far split above is not a code gate. Root cause, found by also logging the ball's own world-frame velocity angle at each save: near-region crossings (`|cross_y|<0.5m`, `regions.py`) travel a much smaller lateral offset over the same forward `dist_range` as far-region crossings, so they arrive far more head-on (~24-31deg off pure -X) than far-region's oblique approach (~46-50deg off -X). Physically, killing/reversing a near-head-on ball's velocity (what `stopball`/`softstop`, weight 36->91, actually require to fire) needs a foot much closer to perpendicular than a 60deg-off-forward block posture provides -- so `stopball`/`softstop`'s much heavier pull was winning against `foot_inner_face_continuous`'s comparatively weak gradient specifically for near-region saves, dragging the achieved angle back toward the old ~90deg value even though the target itself never changed.

Measured why that gradient was weak: `foot_inner_face_continuous`'s metric is a raw `cos(angle-target)` dot product -- symmetric, but very flat near its own peak (a 30deg overshoot back to the old 90deg value only cost `cos(30deg)=0.87`, ~13% off peak). The one-shot `inner_face_orientation_save` bonus is even less informative here -- a hard `alignment>0.7` threshold gate (~45.6deg tolerance cone), giving literally zero gradient for overshoot anywhere inside that cone (61deg and 90deg score identically).

**Fix (user request):** `foot_inner_face_continuous` now splices in a `foot_clearance`-style Gaussian, but ONLY on the overshoot side (foot rotated past the 60deg target, on the correct/assigned side) -- new `_FOOT_OVERSHOOT_SIGMA=0.00333` constant (`rewards.py`), `reward = exp(-_FOOT_OVERSHOOT_SIGMA * (angle_from_forward_deg - 60)^2)` when `side_sign>0 & angle_from_forward_deg>60`, else the original `cos(angle-target)` unchanged. Calibrated the same way `foot_clearance`'s own `clearance_sigma=300` was: `error = target_magnitude` (foot_clearance: 0.10m; here: 30deg, the exact gap back to the old 90deg value) scores ~0.05, half that error (75deg) scores ~0.47 -- verified numerically (`60->1.000, 75->0.473, 90->0.050, 105->0.001`). The undershoot/wrong-side branch was deliberately left untouched, not replaced with a fully symmetric signed Gaussian (`2*exp(-k*err^2)-1`) considered and rejected during planning: that form is symmetric around the target, so it would score "foot never rotated" (60deg short of target) nearly identically to "foot rotated to the genuinely wrong/mirror side" -- exactly the collapse the 2026-07-10 `.abs()` fix (see that date's entry) was written to prevent. The two pieces agree exactly at the target (both equal 1.0), a continuous but not smooth splice.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `rewards.py` -- no syntax errors. Standalone numeric check of the piecewise formula at phi=0/30/45/60/67.5/75/90/105 confirms the calibration table above. Re-ran the same near/far checkpoint-replay probe against `model_39750.pt` under the new reward code -- angles came back unchanged (~84-85deg near, ~69-70deg far), which is expected and not a failure of the fix: replaying an already-trained checkpoint only re-runs its frozen policy through the new reward function for logging purposes, it cannot retroactively change weights that were already trained under the old reward shape. Whether near-region saves actually converge closer to 60deg under this reward requires a fresh training run, not yet launched (user requested logging only, no training run this session). `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` not re-run this entry (no test changes); should be run before/alongside the next training launch touching this file.

## 2026-08-05 (later same day) -- world-frame rotate fix on the 8 far-region double-step clips (supersedes the reverted ankle-flatness attempt above)

**Context:** the earlier same-day entry above ("trailing/support-foot ankle flatness fixed in 10 Safe*/Step_own reference clips") was scoped wrong -- user had asked specifically for a world-frame rotate/translate fix on the far-region-active double-step dataset, not a joint-level ankle edit on the near-region Safe*/Step_own clips. That entire attempt was reverted (`git checkout` on all 10 files, confirmed clean via `git status`) and this entry supersedes it.

**Correct scope:** the 8 double-step (far-region) clips -- `LeftDoubleStep_own_booster_t1_2p5x`, `RightDoubleStep_own_booster_t1_2p5x`, and the 6 `new_doublestepleft`/`new_doublestepright` variants (`booster_t1`, `_short`, `_wide`). All showed the same diagonal world-frame drift pattern as the original `LeftSafeFar1` complaint: trunk trajectory drifting off pure lateral (Y) travel by up to 0.12m in X.

**Fix:** pure world-frame rigid rotation about the trunk's own frame-0 position (the world origin in every one of these clips) -- `theta = arctan(x_end / y_end)` (the small-angle branch of the trunk's final resting XY position, NOT `atan2`, see bug below), applied to `body_pos_w`/`body_quat_w`/`body_lin_vel_w`/`body_ang_vel_w` for every body at every frame. `joint_pos`/`joint_vel` are mathematically invariant under any such transform (confirmed byte-identical before/after on every file), which is exactly why this fix cannot disrupt the double-step gait itself -- footfall timing and leg kinematics are untouched by construction, only the recorded root's world orientation/position changes.

**Bug caught mid-fix (first attempt, immediately reverted before inspection):** the first version computed `theta = atan2(x_end, y_end)`. For the 4 "right"-side clips (trunk ends at negative Y), `atan2` picked the ~178-180deg branch instead of the ~2-5deg small-angle branch -- both zero the X-component algebraically, but the large-angle branch also flips the sign of Y, i.e. it rotated a rightward step into ending up on the LEFT side while `joint_pos` (unchanged) still executed a right-step gait -- a direct root-trajectory/joint-trajectory mismatch, the same class of breakage the user flagged on the previous (ankle-edit) attempt. Caught by inspecting the printed before/after trunk-end values (Y sign flipped) before ever looking at the render; reverted via `git checkout` (files were clean at HEAD, trivial revert) and refit using `arctan(x_end/y_end)` (plain arctan, not atan2), which always resolves to the small-angle solution regardless of the sign of `y_end`. Re-verified: all 4 "right" files now preserve their original negative-Y sign after rotation. Left/right mirror pairs among the `new_doublestep*` files came out with mirrored angles as an independent sanity check (`new_doublestepleft_*`: -5.00deg every variant, `new_doublestepright_*`: +5.00deg every variant -- exactly mirrored, as expected for genuine left/right mirror clips); the two `*DoubleStep_own_*_2p5x` files are not mirror pairs of each other (18.71deg vs -2.30deg) which is consistent with them not being simple mirrors to begin with.

**Verification:** all 8 files' trunk end-of-clip X now reads ~0.000000 (was 0.017-0.113), Y sign/magnitude preserved in every case. `joint_pos`/`joint_vel` confirmed byte-identical before/after (`np.array_equal`) on every file. Z column confirmed unchanged (`np.allclose`) on every file -- expected, since rotation is about the Z axis only and translation was never applied (rotation-about-origin alone zeroed the target). Foot flatness (`tilt_xy` while grounded) re-scanned across all 8 files post-fix: unchanged from pre-fix (all still exactly `0.0000`), as expected since a pure yaw rotation cannot affect gravity-relative tilt. No NaN in any output array. Visual check: rendered `new_doublestepleft_wide` (the largest correction, 5deg) at its final frame (frame 60) old vs. new, both loaded via `mj_forward` -- the leg/foot landing pose is visually identical between the two (confirms the double-step gait pose is fully preserved), only the ~5deg whole-body reorientation differs, which is imperceptible at this rotation magnitude in a static render. `git status` confirms exactly these 8 files modified, nothing else. This fix is cosmetic-only for training purposes (established earlier this session: RSI never reads these NPZ files, AMP never observes root position/orientation) -- it only affects the ghost-overlay's visual appearance in `sgk_play ...-WithOverlay`, per explicit user request. Not yet re-verified in the live viewer by the user.

## 2026-08-05 (later same day) -- moved non-AMP-active clips to backupnotusedamp/; trailing-foot trunk-relative-X pulled to ~0 on the 6 real far-region clips

**Scope audit (user request):** cross-checked exactly which of the 8 rotated files are the real "AMP far region active dataset" by reading `REGION_MOTION_FILES` in `goalkeeper_multidisc_amp_cfg.py:261` (the config that actually feeds the 4 region discriminators in this project's documented active architecture). Result: only 8 files total are referenced -- `LeftStep_own_booster_t1.npz`/`Rightstep_own_booster_t1.npz` (near) and the 6 `new_doublestepleft`/`new_doublestepright` `{short,booster_t1,wide}` files (far). The 2 `*DoubleStep_own_*_2p5x` files touched by the previous rotation fix are NOT in this list -- a comment at that same location confirms they were the OLD far-region reference clips, replaced by the `new_doublestep*` files on 2026-07-18 (commit `36fc902`). HEAD checked against: `39f69ba` (2026-08-05); confirmed via each file's own last-touching commit (`e461f1a` for the 2p5x pair, `2cd6729` for the 6 `new_doublestep*` files) that no commit in between altered them, so `git show HEAD:<path>` is a valid recovery of the true pre-session state.

**Reorganization:** moved the 12 files not in `REGION_MOTION_FILES` (`LeftDoubleStep_own_booster_t1_2p5x`, `RightDoubleStep_own_booster_t1_2p5x`, all 4 `*TripleStep_own*_2p5x`, all 6 `*Safe*` files) from `motions/data/` to a new `motions/backupnotusedamp/` folder via `git mv` (history preserved). `motions/data/` now holds exactly the 8 files `REGION_MOTION_FILES` uses. Side effect flagged to user: this also shrinks what the single-disc task's flat glob (`goalkeeper_amp_cfg.py`, not the active architecture) and the `sgk_play_rsi` diagnostic script's combined pool can see -- accepted as expected, not investigated further this session.

**Double-step gait re-verified via foot POSITION (not just height) on the 6 real far-region files**, per user follow-up request, since world-frame rotation touches X/Y (unlike Z, already confirmed identical) -- computed 3 rotation-invariant quantities per frame: inter-foot distance, each foot's distance from the trunk, and total path length per foot. All matched old-vs-rotated to ~1e-7 (float32 noise floor) on every one of the 6 files. Top-down (bird's-eye) render of `new_doublestepleft_wide`'s full trunk/left-foot/right-foot trace, old vs. new: the double-step shape (right/trailing foot's outward step first, then left/leading foot's larger reach past it) is visually identical between the two; only the trunk's world-frame track differs (drifts off X=0 in old, tracks X=0 in new), exactly as intended.

**New finding (user observation) -- rotation alone doesn't fix the trailing foot's OWN position:** user correctly pointed out the trailing foot still looked wrong after the rotation fix. Measured directly: trunk-relative-X of the trailing foot while grounded was **+0.087 to +0.104m** on all 6 far-region files (vs. only +0.02 to +0.04m on the 2 near-region files, for comparison) -- i.e. the trailing foot sits ~9-10cm forward of the trunk while planted, and this is untouched by any world-frame rigid transform since it's purely a function of `joint_pos` (Hip/Knee/Ankle), which rotation cannot change by construction. This offset DOES reach AMP (unlike the root-frame drift, `joint_pos` is exactly what AMP observes).

**Fix:** Newton-solve `Hip_Pitch`+`Knee_Pitch` (primary X-position DOF + height-preserving compensation) to pull the trailing foot's trunk-relative-X toward 0 while holding its Z at the original (already-grounded) height, followed by a second Newton-solve on `Ankle_Pitch`/`Ankle_Roll` to restore exact flatness (disturbed by the Hip/Knee change, same Kinematic Chain Rule as the reverted ankle-only fix earlier). Applied only near/at the trailing foot's own grounded frames (`z<0.033`), smoothly blended in/out over the swing/stance transition (`smooth_mask`, `BLEND_FRAMES=2`) so no velocity discontinuity is introduced. Leading foot and the opposite leg's joints are completely untouched (confirmed `np.allclose` before/after). Layered directly on top of the already-rotated files in `motions/data/` -- valid because rotation never touched `joint_pos`, so the two fixes compose cleanly.

**Bug caught before applying to real files (first attempt, immediately reverted):** a plain full-step Newton solve diverged on 2 of ~6-7 grounded frames per file, right at the swing/stance blend boundary -- a joint hit its range clip mid-iteration, and the next linearization from that clipped pose produced a wildly wrong step (e.g. `Hip_Pitch` slammed to its `+1.57` limit), giving trunk-relative-X results like `-0.42m` (i.e. WORSE than the original `+0.09m`, and on the wrong side of 0) instead of converging. This also spiked `max|joint_vel|` from the original ~4.7-6.4 rad/s to **46 rad/s** on the `_wide` files -- caught from the printed before/after diagnostic (a value moving further from the target, not closer) before ever rendering or looking at the viewer, and reverted immediately from a pre-fix backup (files were not yet committed at this stage, so a plain file copy served as the revert point). Root-caused by printing per-frame convergence directly (same "verify, don't assume convergence" discipline as the ankle-flatness skill's Kinematic Chain Rule lesson, applied here to a numerical-divergence failure mode instead of a silently-wrong-target one). Fixed by rewriting `solve_position` with a damped/backtracking line search (halve the step until it actually reduces the error, matching `flatten_and_damp_template.py`'s general Newton-solve pattern but hardened against the clip-boundary divergence this 2-target/2-DOF problem hit and the simpler 2026-08-05 ankle-only solve did not) and an explicit convergence check -- a frame that still fails to converge (combined position+height error under 5mm) is left at its ORIGINAL joint values rather than have a divergent result applied, by design (verified zero skipped frames on the real data once the line search was in place, but the safety fallback stays for any future re-run on different clips).

**Verification:** re-ran a standalone convergence check across every grounded frame of all 6 files BEFORE touching real data -- worst combined error 4.7mm, 0 non-converged frames. Applied fix: trunk-relative-X while grounded went from `[+0.087,+0.104]` to `[+0.002,+0.043]` on every file (the small residual is expected and by design -- it's the blended transition frames at partial weight, not the fully-grounded ones, which land at 0.0004-0.0018m). `max|joint_vel|` **unchanged** from the pre-fix value on every file (5.03, 6.42, 4.67 rad/s respectively) -- confirms the earlier divergence is fully gone, not just improved. Foot flatness re-checked post-fix: max tilt 0.0012-0.0023 (still effectively flat, negligible residual from the ankle solve's own convergence tolerance). No NaN. Re-level shift ≤0.00124m on every file. Leading foot's `body_quat_w` and the opposite leg's 4 joint columns confirmed unchanged (`np.allclose`) on every file. Visual check: rendered the trailing foot at a fully-grounded frame (frame 26 of `new_doublestepleft_booster_t1`), old vs. new -- old clearly shows the foot extended well ahead of the leg/ankle; new shows the leg coming straight down onto the foot, planted directly underneath, matching the intended fix. Not yet validated against a live training run (this changes `joint_pos` on grounded frames of the AMP-active far-region clips, unlike the earlier rotation-only fix which was confirmed cosmetic).
