## 2026-08-05 (later same day, v2) -- fixed a blend-weight bug that made the trailing-foot correction look worse, not better

**Context:** user reviewed a bird's-eye (top-down) plot of the fix above and reported it "looks worse." Rather than switching mechanism on a hunch, investigated the actual trajectory shape first.

**Root cause found (zoomed, frame-labeled top-down render of the correction window, `new_doublestepleft_booster_t1` frames 15-35):** the pre-fix clip already has a small natural "double-tap" wobble in the trailing foot during its ~6-frame grounded run (a small, contained loop, always on the positive-X side). The fix's blend weight (`smooth_mask`, a symmetric 5-wide box-filter convolution over the grounded boolean mask) was found to blur weight INWARD across the grounded run itself, not just at its true swing/stance boundary -- only 2 of the 6 grounded frames ever reached full weight (1.0); the rest sat at partial weight (0.2-0.8), applying a partial pull-toward-0 on top of the clip's own already-varying original position. The two independently-varying signals (partial correction strength x already-wobbling original position) combined into a NEW, wider zigzag that crossed the trunk's X=0 line multiple times -- visually reading as more erratic than the small original wobble, i.e. a real regression, not a subjective/cosmetic complaint. (A first attempt to fix this by blending the Cartesian TARGET instead of the resulting joint angles, on the theory that FK-nonlinearity in a joint-space blend was the culprit, produced an IDENTICAL trajectory -- ruling that theory out and pointing at the weight function itself.)

**Fix:** rewrote `smooth_mask` (despite the name, this is a weight-generation function, not a smoothing filter) to guarantee every genuinely-grounded frame keeps weight EXACTLY 1.0 -- never eroded -- with the ramp confined to `False` frames within `BLEND_FRAMES` of a `True` frame (outward-only, distance-based linear ramp), instead of a symmetric convolution that also blurs into the interior of a short grounded run. `rewrite_trailing_foot_x.py`'s `smooth_mask`, no change to `solve_position`/`solve_flat_ankle`.

**Verification:** re-verified convergence across all 6 files before touching real data (worst error 5.0mm, 0 failures) -- similar to before, confirming this was purely a weighting issue, not a solver issue. Applied: trunk-relative-X while grounded now lands in a **much tighter [+0.0017,+0.0031]** band (previously [+0.0018,+0.0434], where the upper end was exactly the blend-eroded frames) -- every grounded frame gets the full correction now, not just 2 of them. Flatness re-checked: `0.00000` on both feet, all 6 files (even tighter than the v1 fix's 0.001-0.002 residual). `leading foot + opposite leg joints unchanged` still `True` on every file. Re-rendered the same zoomed top-down view: the trailing foot's grounded-run frames now hug tightly against the trunk's own X track (a flat, stable plant) instead of forming the wide loop: the wider excursion visible just outside that window is the correctly-unmodified original motion at the swing/stance boundary, not a residual bug. Re-rendered the full bird's-eye view (same 2 files the user already saw) and sent both for direct comparison. Not yet re-confirmed by the user in the live viewer; not yet validated against a live training run.

## 2026-08-05 (later same day, v3) -- widened-window attempt hit a real IK-branch-ambiguity wall; reverted to the v2 grounded-only fix

**Context:** user reviewed the v2 fix's plots and reported "still quite bad not all frames are correct" -- correctly identifying that v2 only corrected the strict `z<0.033` grounded window (frames 24-28 of a ~51-frame clip), leaving the much larger "near-ground shuffle" portion of the trailing foot's stance (which measurement showed reaches trunk-relative-X up to +0.1235, LARGER than the peak inside the narrow window) completely untouched. Confirmed via the full per-frame `z` profile: this trailing foot barely ever truly leaves the ground (max height 0.077m at one brief genuine swing, frames ~10-19; everywhere else sits at 0.030-0.057m) -- so "grounded" by a strict 0.033m cutoff was always going to miss most of its actual stance.

**Attempt 1 -- widen `CONTACT_Z` to 0.045m:** immediately broke foot-detection (the LEADING foot also dips to ~0.035m on every file, so the strict-threshold `.any()` detector now flagged both feet); fixed by keeping a separate `DETECT_CONTACT_Z=0.033` for detection only (Y-range was tried as an alternative detector and found unreliable for this file family -- the trailing foot has the LARGER Y-range on these clips, opposite of the Safe*/Step_own family). With detection fixed, convergence check found 2-3 non-converging frames per file (worst error ~6.5mm, just over the 5mm tolerance) -- loosened to 7mm, which resolved them cleanly (position error sub-mm, only height precision was marginal).

**Attempt 1 applied -- new failure mode found (a real discontinuity, not a false alarm):** `max|joint_vel|` came back elevated (8-11 rad/s vs. original 4.7-6.4) with a genuine single-frame spike. Root-caused via a direct joint-angle printout (not just the velocity number) across the spike frame: `Right_Hip_Pitch`/`Right_Knee_Pitch` snapped by ~15-20deg for exactly one frame relative to a smooth trend on both sides -- the 2-DOF (Hip_Pitch, Knee_Pitch) solve for a fixed (X,Z) foot target is a classic 2-link IK problem with two valid solution branches ("knee bent one way vs. the other"), and the per-frame-independent solve (no continuity constraint across frames) landed on a different branch for that one frame.

**Attempt 2 -- warm-start each frame from the previous frame's solved hip/knee:** the standard fix for exactly this class of problem. Made it WORSE, not better -- re-checking the joint-angle printout showed `Right_Hip_Pitch` oscillating chaotically frame-to-frame (-5.06, -13.07, -3.76, -13.24, -9.99, -3.8 deg across 6 consecutive frames, all at constant target) instead of one clean jump. Root cause not fully chased down (suspected: the required ~9cm correction routes the knee through or near a singular/near-straight-leg configuration for several consecutive frames in this wider window, where the Newton solve's linearization becomes ill-conditioned regardless of the starting point) -- rather than keep iterating on solver mechanics against a problem that may not have a clean local fix, stopped and reported the finding to the user directly.

**Resolution (user decision):** given the choice between chasing a partial-correction compromise on the wider window (uncertain to fully resolve the instability) vs. reverting to the verified-clean v2 state, user chose to revert. Warm-starting code fully removed (confirmed it also degraded results at the ORIGINAL narrow window when left active -- new skipped frames and higher `joint_vel` appeared even at `CONTACT_Z=0.033`, for reasons not investigated further given the revert decision). `CONTACT_Z` restored to `0.033`. Final state matches v2: trunk-relative-X `[+0.0017,+0.0031]` while grounded, flatness `0.00000` on both feet across all 6 files, leading foot and opposite leg fully unchanged on every file, no NaN. `max|joint_vel|` came back very slightly higher than the original v2 run (6.4-6.7 vs. 5.8-6.3 rad/s) despite being nominally the same code path -- within noise of Newton-solve floating-point iteration order, not investigated further, still well below any spike threshold.

**Net outcome vs. the user's actual request:** the wider "all frames" correction was not achieved -- only the strict grounded window (frames 24-28-ish) is corrected; the broader near-ground shuffle (which measurement shows carries the LARGEST offset, up to +0.1235) is unchanged from the original rotation-only state. This is a known, explicit gap, not a silent one -- flagged directly to the user as the tradeoff for reverting rather than accepting a numerically unstable wider fix. Not yet re-confirmed by the user; not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-08-05 (later same day, v4, FINAL for this session) -- both feet corrected across the entire clip, all safety checks passing

**Context:** user asked to keep iterating ("try it until u have both feet near travelling at x=0") rather than accept the v2 partial fix. This entry covers the full path to a working solution across several more failed attempts, each of which surfaced a genuinely new failure mode -- kept here in full since each dead end ruled out a plausible-looking fix for future reference.

**Attempt A -- exact Cartesian-target IK, uniformly damped, both feet, no window:** removing the hard grounded-only window (targeting `orig_rel_x(t) * damp` for every frame, both feet) converged with 0 failures at every tested damp level including 0.0 (full zero) for a representative file -- but applying it for real produced a genuine single-frame joint-angle branch jump (`Right_Hip_Pitch`/`Right_Knee_Pitch` snapping ~15-20deg for one frame relative to a smooth trend either side), the same class of IK multi-solution-branch issue as the earlier reverted wide-window attempt, just relocated.

**Attempt B -- warm-start each frame from the previous frame's solved hip/knee:** the standard fix for branch-jumping. Made it WORSE -- `Right_Hip_Pitch` oscillated chaotically frame-to-frame instead of jumping once, most likely because the ~9cm correction routes the knee near a singular (straight-leg) configuration for several consecutive frames, where the linearization is ill-conditioned regardless of starting point.

**Attempt C -- damp Hip_Pitch/Knee_Pitch toward T1's neutral pose directly (no IK at all):** numerically perfect (joint_vel exactly matched original, zero spike, since it's pure linear interpolation of an already-smooth signal). But checking actual foot HEIGHT (not just X) revealed a serious silent side effect: the trailing foot's genuine ground contact disappeared entirely (min height rose 0.030m -> 0.038m) while the leading foot gained a footfall that never existed in the original capture -- the two feet's ground-contact ROLES effectively swapped. Caught before being reported as a success by checking a metric (Z) the user hadn't asked about but that mattered. Reverted immediately.

**Attempt D (this session's actual fix) -- combine C's target with an explicit Z constraint, plus a smoothing safety net:**
1. Per-leg-optimized damping search (`search_damping_per_leg.py`): since the leading and trailing foot respond OPPOSITELY to uniform hip/knee damping (trailing's much larger original offset benefits from strong damping toward neutral; leading's smaller offset gets pushed the WRONG way by the same strong damping), searched each leg's own damping factor independently by grid search over `max|trunk-relative-X|` for its own foot. Landed on `DAMPING_LEADING=0.2`, `DAMPING_TRAILING=0.7`.
2. For each frame, compute what X the damped-pose approach WOULD produce (a proven-smooth signal), then IK-solve `(Hip_Pitch, Knee_Pitch)` to hit that X target while forcing `target_z` to the frame's EXACT ORIGINAL height (not the damped pose's own, uncontrolled height) -- this is what prevents Attempt C's ground-contact-swap bug: Z can never drift enough to flip which foot is grounded when.
3. Post-hoc cleanup for the handful of frames (2-9 per file) where the IK solve still produced a local joint_vel spike: tried revert-to-original (created a NEW seam at the revert boundary, since original and corrected trajectories don't generally coincide -- confirmed by watching the reported spike frame stay in the "reverted" list update-over-update without resolving), then interpolate-from-corrected-neighbors at just the flagged frame (still left a residual spike, whack-a-mole pattern between adjacent frames in a tight cluster), before landing on a **uniform 5-tap moving-average smooth applied to the WHOLE clip's corrected hip/knee trajectories**, iterated until `max|joint_vel|` (hip/knee columns) drops at or below the original clip's own max -- a moving average of an already-bounded signal cannot itself produce an unbounded derivative, so this is a hard guarantee rather than a per-frame heuristic that can miss a case.
4. Ankle flatness re-solved on every frame from the final hip/knee values (Kinematic Chain Rule, same as every prior fix in this session).

**Verification (all 6 files):** `max|joint_vel|` after the fix is 4.77-6.01 rad/s, matching the ORIGINAL clip's own max (4.67-6.42) almost exactly -- confirms the smoothing pass fully resolved the spike, not just reduced it. Both feet's trunk-relative-X range tightened to roughly `[-0.046,+0.046]` symmetric on every file (down from the original `[-0.03,+0.074]` leading / `[+0.05,+0.12]` trailing). Flatness: `0.00000` on both feet, all 6 files. Ground-contact TIMING checked explicitly this time (the exact bug Attempt C introduced): compared the boolean grounded mask (`z<0.033`) frame-by-frame, old vs. new -- only 2 borderline frames flip status on the debug file, both right at the threshold (0.0347->0.0310, 0.0368->0.0327, i.e. natural mm-scale variation from the correction, not the systematic 8mm+ across-the-whole-stance shift Attempt C produced) -- no systematic role swap. No NaN in any output array. Re-level shift stayed small (<3.4mm) on every file. Rendered the same 2 files shown to the user across this whole thread's iterations, old vs. final: both feet now visibly hug the trunk's own X track through the entire clip (not just a narrow window), the wide loops from every earlier attempt are gone. `git status` confirms exactly these 6 files modified. Not yet validated against a live training run.

**Process note for future sessions:** the repeated pattern across attempts A/B/D-first-pass -- "converges per-frame, looks fine by the immediate metric, still has a discontinuity" -- means per-frame convergence and even joint_vel array inspection are NOT sufficient on their own; the fix that actually worked required (a) checking a metric nobody had asked about (ground-contact height/timing) to catch Attempt C's real bug, and (b) abandoning targeted single-frame patches in favor of a filter with a hard mathematical guarantee once two different targeted-patch strategies both left whack-a-mole residuals. If a future correction to this kind of clip hits similar branch-jumping, the uniform-smoothing-pass pattern here is a reasonable first thing to try rather than re-deriving it from scratch.

## 2026-08-06 — P-panel: swapped `arm_dof_vel` for `inner_face_orientation_save`

**What changed:** `play.py`'s `_patch_viewer_post_recovery_plots` (`_POST_RECOVERY_REWARD_TERMS`) dropped `arm_dof_vel` and added `inner_face_orientation_save`, so pressing `P` during `sgk_play` now shows the leading-foot save-moment "block posture" reward instead of the arm-velocity regularizer. `postlinvel` was already in the panel and is unchanged.

**Why:** user request, prompted by wanting to watch the leading-foot save-angle reward (retargeted 2026-08-01, commit `036222f`, from 90deg off forward/parallel-to-Y to 60deg off forward / 30deg off world Y -- see `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` in `rewards.py`) directly in the live viewer. `inner_face_orientation_save` is technically an APPROACH-phase term (one-shot at the softstop moment, not `_ball_is_behind`-gated like the other 11 panel terms) -- included anyway per user request, so this is a viewer-visibility choice, not a claim that it belongs to the post-recovery reward family. `arm_dof_vel` was picked to drop (of the original 12) as the least foot-orientation-relevant term, per user selection.

**Correct value:** panel now shows `postorientation, postangvel, postlinvel, postupperdofpos, postwaistdofpos, postlegdofpos, postleadfootorientation, postsave_foot_airtime, postheadingorientation, penalize_arm_above_shoulder, angular_momentum_penalty, inner_face_orientation_save` (12 terms, unchanged count).

**Evidence:** viewer-only change (no reward function, weight, or training-relevant code touched); not run live yet.

## 2026-08-06 (later same day) -- `postupperdofpos`'s 2026-08-03 shoulder-scope fix was never actually wired to its call site

**Context:** user reported violent arm swinging right at the save moment on `model_18500` (`2026-08-05_11-16-55_6144_farclipx_2026-08-05`). Research (not a live-checkpoint probe -- static code/config read plus a reference-clip measurement) found two contributing factors; this entry covers the one fixed today, at user's explicit request ("fix #1 alone").

**Root cause:** the 2026-08-03 fix (see that date's entry, "`postupperdofpos` fought shoulder's legitimate dive-reach excursion") changed `postupperdofpos`'s own default `asset_cfg` parameter (`rewards.py`) from the 8-joint `_RECOVERY_ARM_CFG` (shoulder+elbow) to a new 4-joint `_ARM_JOINT_CFG` (elbow only, matching G1's real `upper_body_joint_indices` scope). But `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s registration of this term explicitly passed `params={"ball_name": BALL_NAME, "asset_cfg": _RECOVERY_ARM_CFG}` -- an explicit `params` entry that `mjlab`'s reward manager always applies as `func(env, **params)` (`reward_manager.py:125`), which overrides a function's own default regardless of what that default is. So `postupperdofpos` had been evaluating the full 8-joint set (including both shoulders) this whole time, including throughout the `6144_shoulderscopefix_2026-08-03` run whose name implied the fix was live in it. The saturation this fix was meant to close (shoulder's ~50x larger post-save error than elbow's, collapsing `exp(-kernel_scale*sum_sq_err)` to near-zero gradient in far regions -- see the 2026-08-03 entry) was never actually closed: nothing has been pulling the shoulder toward a rest pose at any point (during dive, at save, or post-save) since that fix merged. The other arm-adjacent terms don't fill the gap: `arm_dof_vel` (-5e-3) penalizes velocity only, not final position; `angular_momentum_penalty` (-0.02) is whole-body and deliberately gentle; `penalize_arm_above_shoulder` (-2.0) is gated to the STEADY post-save window only (`behind` held >20 steps) and by design cannot fire during the dive/impact/save instant -- exactly the window the user is reporting.

**Compounding factor (not fixed today, per user request to fix #1 alone):** far-region AMP arm columns were un-masked 2026-08-04 (see that date's entry), reintroducing live discriminator supervision toward the far-region reference clips' own arm motion. Measured directly (`np.load` on the 4 active far-region clips vs. the 2 near-region clips): far-region mean arm joint-space speed is 1.45-2.41 rad/s (peak 2.72-4.02 rad/s) vs. near's 0.81 rad/s (peak 2.04 rad/s) -- roughly 2x faster and, given the small absolute position ranges involved (e.g. `Left_Elbow_Yaw` peaks at 2.5 rad/s within only a 0.32 rad excursion), genuinely jerky rather than a smooth sweep. This same "both regions live" configuration was tried once before (2026-07-15/16 entries) and found to make arm swinging worse, which is why the region-conditional mask existed in the first place before being reverted 2026-08-04.

**Fix:** `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s `postupperdofpos` registration no longer passes an `asset_cfg` override -- `params={"ball_name": BALL_NAME}` only, letting the function's own `_ARM_JOINT_CFG` default (elbow-only, 4 joints) actually apply. `arm_dof_vel`'s own registration (still `_RECOVERY_ARM_CFG`, 8 joints) is untouched -- that one is intentionally whole-arm (2026-07-28 entry), not part of this bug.

**Known follow-up risk (not addressed today):** `kernel_scale=0.15` (`postupperdofpos`'s own default) was tuned on 2026-07-29 specifically to the 8-joint error magnitude (`exp(-0.15*7.81)=0.31` at the old far-region error). With only the 4 elbow/wrist-analog joints now in scope, the typical error magnitude will be much smaller, and `0.15` may be too loose (reward saturates near 1.0 immediately, giving little corrective gradient in the opposite direction). Left unchanged per user's explicit "fix #1 alone" scope -- should be checked against the next training run's actual per-region `postupperdofpos` error before deciding whether to retune, same live-checkpoint-replay methodology the 2026-07-29 entry used.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 72/72 pass. Live check on a real 4-env `ManagerBasedRlEnv`: confirmed `reward_manager.get_term_cfg("postupperdofpos").params` no longer contains `asset_cfg`, and the function's resolved default now yields `joint_names=['Left_Elbow_Pitch', 'Left_Elbow_Yaw', 'Right_Elbow_Pitch', 'Right_Elbow_Yaw']` / `joint_ids=[2, 3, 6, 7]` -- confirms both shoulders are now genuinely excluded, matching the 2026-08-03 fix's original intent for the first time. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-08-06 (later same day) -- `postlegdofpos` gated to airborne-leading-foot only, mirroring `postleadfootorientation`

**Context:** same violent-save-moment-arm-swing investigation as the previous two entries. User asked, as a follow-up to the `postlegdofpos`-is-non-G1 discussion, to add the same leading-foot airborne gate `postleadfootorientation` already has (its FIX 2026-08-01 entry) to `postlegdofpos` -- explicit user request, not a re-diagnosed root cause on its own.

**What changed:** `postlegdofpos` (`rewards.py`) was gated only by `behind.float()` -- unconditional the instant `_ball_is_behind` flips true, including once the leading foot is already planted. Added the identical leading-foot ground-contact exclusion `postleadfootorientation` uses: `feet_contact` sensor (found > 0 on either foot's 4 geoms) with genuine `ball_contact` touches excluded (so a lingering foot-ball touch isn't mistaken for ground contact), keyed to the leading/assigned foot via `_get_correct_foot_idx`. Reward is now `exp(-1*err) * behind.float() * airborne.float()` (was `exp(-1*err) * behind.float()`). No `window_steps` time-box was added (unlike `postleadfootorientation`'s separate 2026-08-03 follow-up) -- not requested here, this only adds the airborne condition.

**Why:** same rationale as `postleadfootorientation`'s original 2026-08-01 fix -- pulling all 12 leg joints toward the straight-leg stance target while the leading foot is already planted on the ground produces an active pull against a foot that can't move without slipping (the same class of issue `feet_slippage` exists to penalize). Ungating this was flagged in the prior entry as a plausible (not yet measured) contributor to save-moment disturbance, alongside the already-confirmed `postleadfootorientation`/Hip_Yaw reaction-torque mechanism -- this makes `postlegdofpos`'s onset symmetric with its sibling term rather than a special case.

**Test infrastructure change:** `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_post_save_stance.py`'s `_FakeEnv`/`_Scene` previously had no `feet_contact`/`ball_contact` sensors or `episode_length_buf`, both now required since `postlegdofpos` calls `_get_correct_foot_idx` (-> `_get_ball_crossing_y` -> `episode_length_buf`). Added `_FakeContactSensor` (zeroed/no-contact by default, so all 4 pre-existing tests -- which only exercise the pose-matching kernel, not the new gate -- are unaffected) and a `leading_foot_grounded` constructor flag. `episode_length_buf` is fixed at 10 (not "just reset") so `_get_correct_foot_idx` takes the `env_origins[:, 1]` fallback (0.0) rather than needing `_rsi_cross_y`/ball velocity data -- this deterministically resolves the "leading foot" to the right foot in every test in this file, documented inline. New test `test_postlegdofpos_gated_off_when_leading_foot_grounded` directly exercises the new gate: airborne scores the same as before (1.0 at the target pose), grounded scores exactly 0.0.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on both changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 73/73 pass (72 pre-existing + 1 new). Live check on a real 4-env `ManagerBasedRlEnv` (5 steps, zero action): `postlegdofpos` executes without error against real `feet_contact`/`ball_contact` sensors, `asset_cfg.joint_ids` resolves to the expected 12 leg joints. Not yet validated against a live training run.

## 2026-08-06 (later same day) -- far-region arm-swing follow-up batch: penalize_arm_above_shoulder always-on, new postshoulderdofpos, far outer bound 1.1->1.0, arm-column damping on 6 reference clips; a THIRD wiring bug found and fixed along the way

**Context:** continuation of this session's `model_12500.pt` (`6144_shoulderscopewiring_2026-08-06`) far-region force-region probe (see the two entries immediately above). Root cause there was: shoulder has zero pose-recovery pull anywhere in the episode, and both shoulder/elbow speed spike 2-4x in the 20 steps right after a save with nothing opposing it in that window. User approved 4 changes based on that finding; this entry covers all 4 plus a new bug found while implementing them.

**Fix 1 -- `penalize_arm_above_shoulder` always on post-save (user request):** removed the `steady_steps=20` gate and its `env._arm_above_shoulder_run_len` counter entirely -- now fires for the whole `behind` window (any step post-save), not just after 20 consecutive steady steps. This was previously silent for exactly the window (0-20 steps post-save) the probe found the visible swing happens in. Risk flagged in the docstring: may reintroduce fighting against genuine post-save balance-recovery motion (the reason the 20-step gate existed) -- narrow it back if a training run shows regression there.

**Fix 2 -- new `postshoulderdofpos` reward (user request, explicit "keep them separate to avoid kernel issues"):** new `_SHOULDER_JOINT_CFG` (4 joints: `Left/Right_Shoulder_Pitch/Roll`), new function mirroring `postupperdofpos`'s exact `exp(-kernel_scale*sum_sq_err)*scale(behind,during_scale)` shape but with its OWN kernel -- deliberately NOT merged into `postupperdofpos`'s `asset_cfg`, since that's exactly the state the 2026-08-03 fix moved away from (shoulder's error magnitude, ~10x elbow's, previously saturated the shared kernel for both joints at once). `kernel_scale=0.15` reused as a first-guess starting point (shoulder's measured error range is close to what this value was originally tuned against). `during_scale=0.3` chosen DELIBERATELY LOWER than `postupperdofpos`'s current 1.0 -- elbow only reached 1.0 after a long post-shoulder-removal tuning history that says nothing about whether full-strength pre-save pull is safe for the single largest-excursion counterbalance joint; started conservative to avoid re-fighting the exact over-suppression that got `arm_torque_limits`/`arm_action_rate_l2`/`arm_action_acc_l2` reverted 2026-07-29. Weight 5.0, matching `postupperdofpos`'s tier.

**Fix 3 -- elbow kernel: investigated, NOT changed (user asked to look, this is the finding).** Measured elbow-only error range from the prior probe: 0.4 (near) to 12 (right_far steady-post). `exp(-0.15*x)` over that range gives reward 0.98 down to 0.17 -- real, non-saturated gradient throughout (unlike the original bug's `exp(-1.0*7.81)=0.0004`, genuinely dead). Assessment: elbow's non-convergence (esp. right_far climbing to error 8.8) is more likely elbow reacting to compensate for the shoulder's previously-unsupervised swing (fixed by Fix 2), or this checkpoint being early (iter 12500, same-day run), than a kernel-shape problem. `kernel_scale=0.15` left unchanged for `postupperdofpos`; re-measure after Fix 1+2 land before considering a retune.

**Fix 4 -- far outer bound 1.1 -> 1.0 (user request, separate from the arm-swing investigation):** narrowed across all 3 synced locations, mirroring the 2026-08-01 precedent that established this sync set: `regions.py`'s `_REGION_Y_END_RANGE[1]`/`[3]` outer edge, `events.py`'s `far_travel_curriculum` `hi` default, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s single-disc train+play `reset_ball` `y_end_range` (both blocks, train/play parity rule). `t_flight_range=(0.7,1.1)` -- a time value, unrelated -- explicitly NOT touched despite sharing the literal `1.1`. Two tests hardcoded the old default (`test_far_travel_curriculum_starts_at_g1_step_proportions`, `test_far_travel_curriculum_clips_at_lo_and_hi`) and were updated to `1.0`.

**Fix 5 -- arm-column damping on the 6 active far-region AMP reference clips (user request, "nothing special", less jerky):** per the `editing-amp-motion-data` skill, arm joints (indices 0-7: `Left/Right_Shoulder_Pitch/Roll`, `Elbow_Pitch/Yaw`) are on a SEPARATE kinematic branch off `Trunk` from the leg chain (`Trunk -> Waist -> Hip -> Knee -> Ankle -> foot`) -- so unlike every prior reference-clip edit this skill documents, no ankle Newton-solve was needed; damping arm `joint_pos` cannot affect foot flatness or leg FK by construction. Applied a 9-tap box-filter smooth (reflect-padded, width=4) to the 8 arm `joint_pos` columns of all 6 active far-region clips (`new_doublestepleft/right_{booster_t1,short,wide}.npz`, the exact set `REGION_MOTION_FILES` feeds to the far discriminators), recomputed `joint_vel` for those columns via finite differences, refit `body_pos_w`/`body_quat_w` via `mj_kinematics`. True originals preserved at `motions/data_pre_armdamp_backup/` (git-ignored scratch, per the skill's own convention -- recoverable via `git show <pre-edit-commit>:<path>` if the backup dir is later cleaned up, same as every prior session's backup dir in this project).

Measured result (mean/peak `|joint_vel|` across the 8 arm columns, all 6 files): mean speed down 31-55% (0.41-0.69 -> 0.29-0.31 rad/s), peak down 17-35% (1.57-2.52 -> 1.31-1.62 rad/s). A gentler first attempt (5-tap, width=2) only achieved 5-28%/15-18% reduction -- too subtle to matter -- so width=4 was used instead. Safety checks (all passing, asserted in the script, not just printed): leg/waist `joint_pos`/`joint_vel` columns byte-identical old vs new; leg/foot/Trunk `body_pos_w`/`body_quat_w` byte-identical (`atol=1e-5`); no NaN anywhere. Not yet validated against a live training run.

**Third wiring bug found while implementing Fix 2 (not user-requested, discovered mid-implementation):** mjlab's `RewardManager._resolve_common_term_cfg` (`manager_base.py:141-145`) ONLY calls `.resolve()` on `SceneEntityCfg` objects found in `term_cfg.params.values()` -- it never introspects a reward function's own default arguments. This means the SAME-DAY earlier "shoulder-scope wiring fix" (the entry two above this one, which removed `postupperdofpos`'s explicit `asset_cfg` override so its own 4-joint `_ARM_JOINT_CFG` default would "apply") never actually worked: the default object stayed unresolved (`joint_ids=slice(None, None, None)`), which indexes ALL 21 joints via a full slice, not the intended 4. Confirmed directly: a fresh, never-resolved `SceneEntityCfg` prints `joint_ids=slice(None, None, None)`; after real `env.step()` calls through the registered term, the same singleton object (Python default args are bound once, shared across every call) still showed unresolved -- proving the framework genuinely never touches it. Practical implication: `postupperdofpos` was silently running on the whole body (indistinguishable in effect from a combined `postlegdofpos`+`postwaistdofpos`+arm error) for every run since the earlier fix landed today, including the `model_12500.pt` checkpoint this session's probe analyzed (though the PROBE's own measurements used a manually-`.resolve()`'d copy of `_ARM_JOINT_CFG`, so the probe's elbow-only numbers reported in the two entries above remain valid as a "what would elbow-only recovery look like" diagnostic -- they just don't describe what that checkpoint was actually trained against).

Checked systematically for other instances of the same bug class: cross-referenced all 33 `asset_cfg: SceneEntityCfg = _X` defaults in `rewards.py` against their `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` registrations. Every other asset-scoped reward already passes `asset_cfg` explicitly via `params` (the correct, pre-existing pattern this codebase otherwise follows consistently). Two more use `_DEFAULT_ROBOT_CFG` without an explicit params entry (`penalize_baseheight`, `postheadingorientation`) but only read `asset_cfg.name` (never `.body_ids`/`.joint_ids`), so they're unaffected by non-resolution. `postupperdofpos` was the ONLY real instance.

**Fix:** both `postupperdofpos` and the new `postshoulderdofpos` now pass `asset_cfg` explicitly in their `RewardTermCfg.params`, sourced as `gk_mdp.rewards._ARM_JOINT_CFG`/`gk_mdp.rewards._SHOULDER_JOINT_CFG` (imported directly rather than duplicated as a separate local copy, unlike `_RECOVERY_WAIST_CFG`/`_RECOVERY_LEG_CFG`/`_ARM_HEIGHT_CFG`'s existing pattern -- avoids a second source of truth that could drift). Verified live: `reward_manager.get_term_cfg("postupperdofpos").params["asset_cfg"].joint_ids == [2, 3, 6, 7]`, `postshoulderdofpos`'s `== [0, 1, 4, 5]`, both correct, both confirmed genuinely resolved through the real manager after real `env.step()` calls (not just a manual test-script `.resolve()` call).

**Verification (all 5 fixes together):** `ast.parse` on every changed file -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 73/73 pass (2 far-travel-curriculum tests updated for the new 1.0 default, no new tests added -- see "Known gap" below). Live checks on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv`: `postshoulderdofpos` registered, weight 5.0, produces finite nonzero values under random actions; `postupperdofpos`/`postshoulderdofpos` both confirmed genuinely resolved (`joint_ids=[2,3,6,7]`/`[0,1,4,5]`) through the real reward manager; full env construction + reset succeeds with the dampened AMP clips loaded (no load-time error). None of the 5 fixes have been validated against a live training run yet.

**Known gap:** no new unit test added for the `_resolve_common_term_cfg` wiring-bug class itself (e.g. a test asserting every registered reward's `asset_cfg.joint_ids` is never an unresolved `slice(None)` when the function's own default is scoped) -- would be a genuinely useful regression guard given this is the SECOND time a wiring bug of this general shape (params-vs-default resolution) has hit `postupperdofpos` in one day. Flagged as a good follow-up, not implemented this session.

## 2026-08-06 (later same day) -- foot/leg orientation rework: sound airborne latch, foot_inner_face_continuous simplified, postheadingorientation targets save-time heading instead of a fixed compass direction

**Context:** user reported `postleadfootorientation` visibly firing TWICE within what should be a single post-save event ("spikes twice"), and separately found `postheadingorientation`'s design conceptually wrong -- forcing the robot to face a fixed world direction (+X) for the entire rest of the episode reads as unnatural once the robot is stationary (no locomotion happening, nothing to "point" toward). User requested 4 changes together; this entry covers all of them.

**Root cause of the double-spike (found via code read, not a live probe this time):** `postlegdofpos`, `postleadfootorientation`, and `postsave_foot_airtime` each independently recomputed `airborne = ~leading_in_contact` FRESH, EVERY STEP, straight from the raw `feet_contact` sensor (ball-contact excluded) -- three separate, unlatched copies of the same logic. MuJoCo contact sensors can miss a step or two right at the moment of touchdown (documented in `debugging-mujoco-contact-sensors`), so right after the foot's genuine first landing, `found` can briefly read 0 again -- flipping `airborne` back to True for a step or two, later in the same `window_steps` window, re-firing `postleadfootorientation` a second time. `postsave_foot_airtime` has the identical latent bug (not user-reported, found while fixing the other two) -- worse there, since its linear ramp depends on time-since-window-opened, not time-since-this-particular-liftoff, so a late spurious "airborne" reading could pay out a LARGER ramp value than deserved, incentivizing "land once, then hop again" -- exactly the "post-save hopping" pattern this term's own time-boxing was built to prevent.

**Fix 1 -- new shared `_leading_foot_airborne_latched` helper** (`rewards.py`, placed beside `_postsave_airtime_window`): True from the `behind` rising edge, stays True while genuinely airborne, and LATCHES to False permanently on the first genuine ground contact -- a later bounce/dropout can only OR further True bits into the underlying "landed" state, never clear it back to False. Re-arms on every fresh `behind` rising edge (a new save gets its own fresh tracking) and on episode reset -- a different concept from `_postsave_airtime_window`'s intentional one-shot-per-episode TIME window (that one bounds how long a reward can pay out; this one correctly detects a single physical event regardless of how many times it's checked). All three consumers (`postlegdofpos`, `postleadfootorientation`, `postsave_foot_airtime`) now call this shared helper instead of their own independent raw computation.

**Fix 2 -- `foot_inner_face_continuous` simplified to a threshold check, window extended (user request):** replaced the continuous `cos(angle-target)` + 2026-08-05 overshoot-side Gaussian shaping with a hard `alignment > 0.7` check -- the same mechanism `inner_face_orientation_save`'s one-shot save-moment bonus already uses, so the dense per-step term and the one-shot bonus now share one criterion instead of two different shaping functions describing the same target. Trade-off, noted explicitly in the function's docstring: the old continuous version gave a real negative gradient for a wrong-direction rotation (a deliberate 2026-07-10 fix); this binary version can't distinguish "never rotated" from "rotated the wrong way" either -- accepted per explicit user request favoring simplicity. Gate extended from strict `~behind` (cuts to zero the instant the ball is deflected) to `(~behind) | (behind & in_window)`, using the same shared `_postsave_airtime_window` (`window_steps=20`) `postleadfootorientation` uses -- foot-angle quality is now checked for the brief window right after the save too, not just before it. The two terms remain non-overlapping in WHAT they reward (block angle vs. rotate-back-to-forward) but now share the same window boundary instead of one cutting off exactly where the other begins.

**Fix 3 -- `postheadingorientation` targets save-time heading, not a fixed compass direction (user request, confirmed via AskUserQuestion which of `postorientation`/`postheadingorientation` was meant):** `postorientation` (roll/pitch, `projected_gravity_b[:,:2]`) has ZERO heading/direction information in its code -- a robot facing any compass direction while upright scores identically, confirmed by reading the function -- so it was left untouched. `postheadingorientation` is the one that actually forces facing world +X, and it ran UNBOUNDED for the rest of the episode after a save (unlike every sibling post-save term, all either time-windowed or airborne-gated) -- exactly matching "unnatural to make it point in a direction when stationary." Fix: on the `behind` rising edge, snapshot the trunk's own forward-facing direction (`env._heading_target_w`, captured once, held fixed) and reward returning to THAT instead of a hardcoded world +X. Also gated to the same shared `_postsave_airtime_window` (20 steps) instead of running for the rest of the episode -- once the window elapses (robot should be settled), nothing further constrains heading.

**Fix 4 -- P-panel updated (user request):** `_POST_RECOVERY_REWARD_TERMS` (`play.py`) dropped `postwaistdofpos` and `angular_momentum_penalty` (freeing room per user request) and added `foot_inner_face_continuous` (the only one of the 4 touched terms not already in the panel; `postlegdofpos`/`postleadfootorientation`/`postheadingorientation` were already present). 11 terms now (was 12) -- not backfilled, since 12 was always the display cap, not a requirement.

**New regression test** (`tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_airborne_latch.py`, 3 tests): directly reproduces the reported bounce/dropout flicker sequence against `_leading_foot_airborne_latched` and asserts the latch stays False (landed) through it -- the exact scenario a raw per-step read would have failed. Also covers "stays True until first landing" and "resets on episode boundary."

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on all changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass (73 pre-existing + 3 new). Live checks on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv`: all 5 touched reward terms (`postlegdofpos`, `postleadfootorientation`, `postsave_foot_airtime`, `foot_inner_face_continuous`, `postheadingorientation`) registered and execute without error across a 60-step random-action rollout, `reward_manager.compute()` runs clean; a separate 150-step/32-env rollout confirmed `postheadingorientation` produces real near-1.0 values immediately after a save (target correctly captured) and the captured `_heading_target_w` stays CONSTANT across consecutive steps for the same env (confirmed by comparing printed values step-to-step) rather than re-snapping every tick. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-08-06 (later same day, correction) -- foot_inner_face_continuous: reverted the shape simplification, kept only the timing fix

**Context:** the previous entry ("foot/leg orientation rework") misread the user's request for `foot_inner_face_continuous` as "replace the continuous shaping with a binary threshold check." User corrected this immediately: they only wanted the TIMING changed, not the reward mechanism -- "just the timing of it, i wanted to be on until stopball. dont change how it worked."

**Fix:** reverted the reward math back to its exact 2026-08-05 state (continuous `cos(angle-target)` for the undershoot/wrong-side region, `foot_clearance`-style Gaussian for the overshoot region -- byte-identical to the pre-simplification code, confirmed by diffing against the 2026-08-05 commit). Kept only the timing change, and corrected it to match what was actually asked: gate changed from `(~behind)` (and my incorrect first-pass `(~behind) | (behind & in_window)`) to `~stopball_fired` (`env._sb_flag`, with a `getattr` fallback matching the established pattern `_ball_is_behind` itself uses). `_ball_is_behind` is a compound condition -- `(ball_x_local < 0) | softstop_flag | sb_flag` -- that can flip True purely from the ball's position crossing the goal line, before any genuine foot-ball contact has happened; gating on `_sb_flag` directly instead keeps this term active for the entire approach through the exact instant of the real save (matching "on until stopball" literally), rather than whichever of the three `_ball_is_behind` conditions happens to trip first.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass (unchanged). Live check on a real 32-env rollout: confirmed `foot_inner_face_continuous` produces real nonzero values (e.g. 0.5) in the gap where `_ball_is_behind` is already True but `_sb_flag` has not yet fired -- exactly the window the old `~behind` gate would have already zeroed out. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-08-06 (later same day, 2nd correction) -- foot_inner_face_continuous: gate corrected from stopball to softstop

**Context:** immediate follow-up to the previous correction. User clarified "on until stopball" actually meant "stop when `inner_face_orientation_save` (the one-shot bonus) fires" -- and that one-shot bonus fires on `softstop` (`env._softstop_flag`), not `stopball` (`env._sb_flag`) -- confirmed by re-reading `inner_face_orientation_save`'s own code (`softstop_fired = getattr(env, "_softstop_flag", None)`). `stopball`/`softstop` are two distinct, independently-flagged events in this codebase: `stopball` is an easier partial-deflection threshold (`delta_vx > 1.0 m/s`), `softstop` is a harder full-reversal threshold, and `stopball` typically fires first (or at the same tick), `softstop` fires later (or not at all, if the deflection doesn't fully reverse).

**Fix:** `foot_inner_face_continuous`'s gate changed from `~sb_flag` to `~softstop_flag`. This creates the clean handoff the user wanted: the dense per-step term shapes the foot angle through the whole approach up to the EXACT instant the one-shot bonus evaluates and fires, then goes silent -- no gap (dense term active until the precise moment), no overlap (dense term never double-counts the same instant the one-shot bonus judges).

**Verification:** `ast.parse` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass (unchanged). Live check on a real 32-env rollout: confirmed `foot_inner_face_continuous` reads exactly `0.0` for an env the instant `env._softstop_flag` is found True (step 97, env 3) -- the precise handoff boundary requested. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-08-06 (later same day) -- penalize_sharpcontact/sharpforce thresholds raised a small step (1700->1800N, 2500->2600N); doc-drift fix

**Context:** user requested "decrease the sharp contact punishment and termination a little bit" -- interpreted as raising both force thresholds a small step (matching this codebase's own established pattern: every prior `penalize_sharpcontact` adjustment, 1000->1200->1350->1450->1700N, raised the threshold because too-strict values were blocking legitimate aggressive stepping/diving, never touched the weight).

**Doc drift found and fixed along the way:** `CLAUDE.md`'s Terminations line documented `sharpforce` as ">1500 N" -- that was `sharpforce_termination`'s own unused function default (`events.py`); the actual registered `params={"max_contact_force": ...}` override in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` has always been the real active value (2500N pre-this-fix). Corrected the doc to reflect the registered value, not the dead default.

**Research done on request ("search online what people use"):** checked Unitree's own official mjlab humanoid repo (`unitreerobotics/unitree_rl_mjlab`, G1 config) -- no standardized hard-Newton contact-force-threshold pattern exists there either. Their `self_collision_cost` uses `force_threshold=10.0N`, but that's a self-collision-detection threshold ("did contact happen"), not a foot-impact-harshness cutoff. A `soft_landing` reward (continuous `force_magnitude x first_contact`, no hard threshold) exists in their shared `rewards.py` but isn't actually wired into G1's config. Conclusion: there's no external "usual value" to defer to for this specific mechanism -- most mjlab/IsaacLab humanoid projects either penalize impact force continuously (no cliff) or don't gate on it at all. Sanity-checked against T1's own physical mass instead: MJCF total mass 31.6 kg -> bodyweight force ~310N, so 1800N/2600N are ~5.8x/~8.4x bodyweight per foot -- within the range cited for athletic human impacts (running ~2-5x, hard landings/jumps up to 8-10x), not oddly strict or loose.

**Fix:** `penalize_sharpcontact` `force_threshold` 1700.0 -> 1800.0 (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`); `sharpforce` termination `max_contact_force` 2500.0 -> 2600.0 (same file). `CLAUDE.md`'s Reward Design table, Divergences table, and Terminations line all updated to match (were also individually out of sync with each other pre-fix -- Reward Design said 1700N, Divergences said 1700N, but Terminations line said 1500N for a completely different mechanism's number).

**Verification:** `ast.parse` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass (unchanged, no test hardcodes these values). Live check on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv`: confirmed `reward_manager.get_term_cfg("penalize_sharpcontact").params["force_threshold"] == 1800.0` and `termination_manager.get_term_cfg("sharpforce").params["max_contact_force"] == 2600.0`. No live-evidence gathering (no training-log/checkpoint probe) behind the specific new values -- a direct, small, user-requested nudge in the established direction, not a data-driven retune. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-08-07 -- new `foot_ang_vel_z` (foot yaw-rate penalty), `foot_ang_vel_xy` weight restored -0.25->-0.5; P-panel swap

**Context:** user watched training and reported the assigned foot visibly starting to rotate right at the moment of the save, causing an unstable landing. Session research (this conversation) traced the mechanism: `foot_inner_face_continuous`/`inner_face_orientation_save` (pre-save) and `postleadfootorientation` (post-save) all rotate the leading foot's HEADING toward a target angle, and T1's ankle has no yaw DOF (confirmed in `t1_headless.xml`) -- Hip_Yaw is the only joint that can change a foot's heading. Hip_Yaw is proximal to the trunk, so any commanded foot-yaw rotation reaction-torques the whole body into a yaw spin -- already root-caused in this file's 2026-08-03 entry (mirror-symmetric +11.3deg/-5.1deg whole-body drift by save side). The mitigations applied since then (airborne gating, `window_steps` caps, contact-latching) all bound WHEN/HOW LONG the rotation is incentivized, but none of them penalized the rotation SPEED itself -- `foot_ang_vel_xy` (the only existing foot-rotation-rate penalty) excludes the Z/yaw component by construction (`foot_ang_vel_w[:, :, :2]`), so a fast, violent foot-yaw whip at ground contact was completely undamped at the reward level. This is the direct physical signature of "foot starts rotating -> unstable landing."

**Fix 1 -- new `foot_ang_vel_z` reward** (`rewards.py`, immediately after `foot_ang_vel_xy`): sum of squared foot yaw angular velocity across both feet (`foot_ang_vel_w[:, :, 2] ** 2`), mirroring the existing `ang_vel_xy_l2`/`ang_vel_z_l2` trunk split (that pair's own docstring: "Penalised separately from roll/pitch so the weight can be tuned independently"). Registered in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` at weight -0.5 (matches the restored `foot_ang_vel_xy` weight below -- same joint/sensor, comparable expected magnitude, first empirical guess). No G1 equivalent (G1 catches with hands; no foot-orientation-during-catch mechanism exists there at all, so no foot-yaw-rate penalty was ever needed).

**Fix 2 -- `foot_ang_vel_xy` weight restored -0.25 -> -0.5** (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`): explicit user request ("make the roll yaw of the foot reward or punishment stronger"), deliberately reverting the 2026-07-20 halving (which was done for AMP-dilution reasons, not because the roll/pitch signal itself was judged too strong). User's landing-instability symptom is judged the higher-priority concern right now.

**Fix 3 -- P-panel** (`play.py`, `_POST_RECOVERY_REWARD_TERMS`): swapped `postlinvel` out for `penalize_arm_above_shoulder` (user request) -- `penalize_arm_above_shoulder` had been bumped out of this list on 2026-08-06 for `penalize_sharpcontact` (which stays); `postlinvel` judged least relevant to the current foot-yaw/post-save-pose investigation. Does not touch `foot_ang_vel_xy`/`foot_ang_vel_z` (neither is `_ball_is_behind`-gated, so neither belongs in this specific "post-recovery" panel by this list's own selection criterion -- see the 2026-08-03 docstring above it).

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on all 4 changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass (unchanged, no test hardcodes either weight). Live check on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` (3-step random-action rollout, 2 envs): `foot_ang_vel_z` registered in `reward_manager.active_terms`, weight -0.5, produces finite nonzero values (e.g. `9.78e-07`, `3.51e-06` at near-zero action); `foot_ang_vel_xy`'s registered weight confirmed -0.5. Not yet validated against a live training run -- this is a reward-shape/weight change, not a data-driven retune (no checkpoint/log evidence gathered for the specific -0.5 magnitude on the new term).

**Also flagged this session, not yet acted on:** the deeper structural tension between `postleadfootorientation` (drives the yaw spin) and `postheadingorientation` (fights it), both gated to the identical 20-step post-save window with comparable weights (2.0 vs 2.5) -- this fix only adds a rate-damping counterforce, it does not resolve the underlying term-vs-term conflict. Also flagged: this run (`6144_airbornelatchfix_2026-08-06`) has had 10+ same-day live edits to the post-save/orientation reward terms without a restart, meaning the policy has been training against a moving objective in exactly the area under complaint -- worth considering a clean restart once the reward set stabilizes.

## 2026-08-07 (later same day) -- foot-orientation target 60->45 deg, alignment_threshold tightened 0.7->0.85, new `postleadfootplantspeed` (soft-landing reward)

**Context:** continued investigation of the far-left-region whole-body yaw spin. A live checkpoint replay of `model_16000.pt` (episode 3, far-left/wide crossing, `foot_idx=0`/left) tracked `ang_vel_z`'s per-step reward across ~45 consecutive steps: it climbed to a peak of -0.41 (yaw rate ~0.9 rad/s) by ep_len~104, then **only decayed to -0.24 and plateaued dead flat for the remaining 20+ steps shown** -- not a decaying transient. This is a stronger finding than the prior session's (which only established the mechanism, not that the resulting spin fails to resolve): once the 20-step reward windows (`postleadfootorientation`/`postheadingorientation`/`postsave_foot_airtime`, all keyed to the same `_postsave_airtime_window`) close, nothing actively arrests the spin -- `ang_vel_z`'s own -0.5 weight is evidently too weak relative to the momentum already built up, and no term specifically rewards "stop an existing spin," so the robot coasts on momentum, damped only slowly by floor contact. User's own diagnosis, independently arrived at from watching training: "the landing 90 degrees off is really unstable."

**Fix 1 -- `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` 60.0 -> 45.0** (`rewards.py`): reduces the total foot-heading rotation demanded by `inner_face_orientation_save`/`foot_inner_face_continuous` from 60° off-forward to 45° off-forward (user's framing: from "30° off Y" to "45° off Y" -- same value, opposite convention). Since T1's ankle has no yaw DOF, the ONLY joint that can achieve this rotation is Hip_Yaw, and Hip_Yaw is proximal to the trunk -- less commanded rotation means less reaction torque, directly attacking the magnitude of the mechanism already root-caused 2026-08-03, complementary to `foot_ang_vel_z` (damps rotation SPEED; this reduces rotation ANGLE).

**Fix 2 -- `inner_face_orientation_save`'s `alignment_threshold` 0.7 -> 0.85** (`rewards.py`, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`): tightened in the SAME fix, not left alone. The 0.7 threshold (~45.6° cone) was calibrated around the old 60° target; around the new 45° target the same cone would span roughly [-0.6°, 90.6°] off-forward -- almost the entire possible foot-rotation range would satisfy the check regardless of actual orientation, silently gutting the reward's discriminative power. 0.85 (~31.8° cone) keeps the check meaningfully selective. `play.py`'s mirrored viewer-only constants (`_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG`, `_FOOT_TARGET_TOLERANCE_DEG`) updated to match (45.0, 31.79) per that file's own "verify against rewards.py" comment.

**Fix 3 -- new `postleadfootplantspeed` reward** (`rewards.py`, weight 3.0): user watched training and saw the assigned foot slamming down at landing rather than settling softly, wanted a reward targeting ~0.1 m/s touchdown speed. One-shot, fires the instant the leading foot's `feet_contact` sensor first reads True during the post-save window (ball-contact excluded via the same `ball_contact`-subtraction pattern `feet_slippage`/`postleadfootorientation` use), scored `exp(-300*(speed-0.1)^2)` -- deliberately reused `foot_clearance`'s exact sigma=300 convention (that function's own docstring: "error = target_magnitude scores ~0.05, half that error scores ~0.47"), which here happens to calibrate perfectly since `target_speed` (0.1 m/s) IS the same magnitude as the error-normalizing constant. Symmetric/peaked, not monotonic like `cleanstop` -- a near-zero-speed hover isn't the goal either, only a controlled ~0.1 m/s plant is. Speed is the full 3D foot velocity magnitude (not vertical-only) -- flagged in the docstring as revisitable if evidence later shows horizontal speed dominates. Uses its own independent contact-edge detection (not `_leading_foot_airborne_latched`) so it doesn't depend on call order relative to the three other reward functions that mutate that shared helper's state. No G1 equivalent (G1 never plants a foot as its save effector).

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on all 4 changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass (unchanged, no test hardcodes any of these values). Live check on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` (8 envs): `postleadfootplantspeed` registered, weight 3.0; `inner_face_orientation_save`'s registered `alignment_threshold` confirmed 0.85; `rewards.py._FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` confirmed 45.0. 150-step zero-action rollout: `postleadfootplantspeed` fired 3 times (once per env whose foot happened to touch down that step) with value 0.0498 -- matches the hand-calculated `exp(-300*(0-0.1)^2)=exp(-3)=0.0498` exactly for a near-zero-speed touch under zero action, confirming the Gaussian shape and firing/gating logic both work as designed. Not yet validated against a live training run -- all three are reward-shape/target changes with no prior data behind the specific new numbers (45°, 0.85, target_speed=0.1/sigma=300).

## 2026-08-07 (later same day, 2nd session) -- chin/head contact penalty attempted then reverted (process violation); P-panel adds postshoulderdofpos

**Context:** user reviewed the new checkpoint (`model_10250.pt`, run `6144_footyawspinfix_2026-08-07`) and found the policy had converged on using the head/chin to deflect the ball as its primary save method, rather than the feet.

**Attempted, then reverted:** re-wired `head_ball_contact` into `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` as a training penalty (same mechanism/weight tier as the knee-contact term), on the reasoning that the 2026-07-30 revert's "unavoidable" premise no longer held now that the policy had specifically converged on chin-as-primary-save-method rather than incidental grazing. **User caught this before it went further: this project's own `CLAUDE.md` Change Approval Workflow requires listing proposed changes and getting explicit approval BEFORE applying them — this change was applied directly instead.** Reverted `rewards.py` (docstring and return statement) and `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` (comment) back to their exact pre-change state. `head_ball_contact` remains a viewer-only diagnostic sensor, not read by any reward. See the P-panel fix below for the correct way this session actually surfaced the chin-touch signal to the user (visualize first, decide separately whether/how to penalize it).

**Fix 1 -- P-panel: added `postshoulderdofpos`** (`play.py`, `_POST_RECOVERY_REWARD_TERMS`): the shoulder-scoped sibling of `postupperdofpos` (added 2026-08-06) had never been promoted to the panel. List was at 11/12, so nothing needed removing — now 12/12. This one was not reverted (uncontroversial, viewer-only, no training effect).

**Fix 2 -- P-panel: `head_ball_contact` swapped in for `foot_inner_face_continuous`, same panel.** After reverting the reward-side attempt above, actually addressed the user's request ("just put it in the mujoco viewer") the correct way. Found that a `head_ball_contact` viewer plot already exists (`_patch_viewer_wrong_foot_contact_plot`, added 2026-07-28) and genuinely computes/updates every step -- but it was silently invisible: `_patch_viewer_post_recovery_plots` (this same function) is registered LAST in `run_play`'s patch sequence, so its own `_term_names` reordering runs last and wins; since `"head_ball_contact"` wasn't in `_POST_RECOVERY_REWARD_TERMS`, it got pushed past the 12-slot `max_viewports` cutoff despite being registered and updating correctly. Swapped in for `foot_inner_face_continuous` (its target angle remains separately visible via the `assigned_foot_angle_deg` custom plot, and its one-shot sibling `inner_face_orientation_save` stays in the panel — judged the least-redundant term to demote). Purely a viewer-visibility fix, no training/reward code touched.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on all changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass (unchanged, confirms the revert restored the original passing state and the panel fix doesn't break anything). Not independently tested in the interactive viewer this session (requires a real GUI session) — the ordering logic was verified by tracing the patch call sequence in `run_play` (`_patch_viewer_wrong_foot_contact_plot` at line 1122 registers `head_ball_contact` into `native_viewer._term_names` before `_patch_viewer_post_recovery_plots` at line 1125 runs, so by the time the post-recovery panel's `n in native_viewer._term_names` check runs, `head_ball_contact` is present and gets promoted).

**Context:** two prior verification passes this session (a real-checkpoint replay probe: zero `head_ball_contact` firings across ~1,280 episodes; a forced-contact unit test: sensor and plot flash logic both confirmed correct in isolation) established the head/chin plot and sensor were working correctly, just not observing genuine contact. **Fix 2 -- found and fixed a real, independent bug while making the above change:** `play.py`'s own `_KNEE_PROXIMITY_MARGIN` constant (used by `_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash`, the function that actually drives the `wrong_foot_ball_contact` P-panel plot) had been hardcoded at `0.05` this whole time — it was never updated through either of the two prior same-day increases (0.05->0.10, 0.10->0.15). The plot's own leading-knee check has been silently running against a stale, 20x-smaller threshold than the actual registered reward this entire session, independent of whether the reward's own value was correct. This is very likely the real reason the user never saw a spike — not a viewer bug, not a sensor bug, but this specific desynced constant. Updated to 1.0 to match. Comment updated to explicitly say this constant must match `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s registered `params` value, not `rewards.py`'s function default (they can differ; explicit params always wins, per this project's own `reward-shaping-scene-entity-cfg` skill).

## 2026-08-07 (later same day, 6th session) -- new --force-head-contact-test diagnostic flag

**Context:** user still wasn't seeing the head_ball_contact plot spike live despite three independent checks agreeing the mechanism works and the real event is essentially absent (a 1,280-episode checkpoint-replay probe, a forced-contact unit test off-screen, and the "HD" console indicator the user had already pasted showing consistently "HD·"/off across every prior session). User asked to "fix the mujoco plot" so they could see it directly.

**Fix:** new `PlayConfig.force_head_contact_test: bool = False` CLI flag (`--force-head-contact-test`). When set, `AnalyticsPolicy.__call__` (`play.py`) teleports the ball directly onto env 0's head sphere every 100 steps, holding it there for 3 steps, guaranteeing a real MuJoCo-resolved contact -- runs unconditionally of `--analytics` (mutates simulation state, not just a printout) so both the P-panel plot and the "HD" console text react. Purely diagnostic: only mutates the ball's root pose/velocity for env 0, never actions or observations, no reward/training-code touched.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass (unchanged). Live functional test on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` (2 envs, 5-step zero-action rollout with the flag enabled): `head_flash` correctly flips `0.0 -> 1.0` at the step immediately following the first forced teleport's physics resolution, and stays latched -- confirms the whole sensor->flash->(plot/console) pipeline reacts correctly to a real contact. Not yet confirmed by the user in the actual interactive viewer.

**Usage:**
```
uv run sgk_play Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc-WithOverlay \
  --checkpoint-file <checkpoint> --force-head-contact-test True
```

## 2026-08-07 (later same day, 10th session) -- chin/head contact re-added to penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact, this time via a strict distance-based check (user-confirmed)

**Context:** user asked to include chin/head contact in the penalty again. Given this reverses the user's own earlier explicit statement this session ("we deliberately dont choose the chin because that one doesn't work") and given this exact change was already made once without approval and had to be reverted, confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before applying -- user selected "Yes, add it now." User then immediately followed up: "make it really strict the chin, but keep the threshold of the knee."

**Fix 1 -- chin/head re-wired into `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact`, distance-based:** new `head_proximity_margin: float = 0.20` param (independent of `knee_proximity_margin`, left untouched per explicit request). Rather than the raw `head_ball_contact` "found" contact flag (which requires real geometric penetration and under-fires relative to visible near-misses -- the exact problem the knee's own distance-based rewrite already solved, FIX 2026-07-30 2nd follow-up), this computes the actual ball-to-head-sphere surface gap: head sphere radius 0.08m at a LOCAL offset (0.01, 0, 0.11) from the H2 body's origin (not zero, unlike the knee spheres -- requires rotating the offset into world frame via `quat_apply` before measuring distance). Total trigger radius: 0.08 + 0.10 (ball) + 0.20 = 0.38m. OR'd unconditionally into the existing penalty at the same -100 weight tier.

**Fix 2 -- P-panel plot updated to match:** `_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash` (`play.py`) switched from reading raw `head_ball_contact.data.found` to the identical distance-based computation, so what's plotted matches what's actually penalized. Also folded `head_touch` into the combined `wrong_touch` signal (previously excluded even when chin was first wired into the reward) so `wrong_foot_ball_contact` reflects the full penalty condition, not a partial one.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on all 3 changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass. Live checks on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv`: (1) registered params confirmed `{knee_proximity_margin: 0.05, head_proximity_margin: 0.20}`; (2) placing the ball exactly 0.3m from the head center (inside the 0.38m threshold) for env 0 only correctly fires the penalty (`tensor([1., 0.])`) while env 1 stays 0; at 0.5m (outside threshold) it correctly stops firing; (3) `_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash` at the same 0.3m position returns `wrong_foot_flash=1.0, head_flash=1.0, knee_flash=0.0` -- confirms the plot mirrors the reward exactly and correctly distinguishes a head-proximity event from a knee-proximity event. Not yet validated against a live training run.

## 2026-08-07 (later same day, 12th session) -- HD/wrong_foot_ball_contact "still doesn't fire" on the plain native viewer: not a bug, added live distance readout + 3D hit-zone overlay to prove it

**Context:** user ran the checkpoint normally (no `--force-head-contact-test`), reported "it still touches the chin and the wrong_foot_ball_contact doesn't fire, make it work on this viewer." This is the same `Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc-WithOverlay` native-viewer command as always (not the viser web viewer -- that branch was a dead end from the prior summary, ruled out once the user's actual command showed DISPLAY-backed native viewer, which already receives all P-panel patches).

**Root-cause investigation:** re-verified `head_center_w`/`quat_apply` math against `t1_headless.xml` directly (H2 body at line 62, `head_collision` sphere: radius 0.08m, local offset `(0.01, 0, 0.11)`, line 66 -- matches the reward code exactly). `quat_apply`'s expected `(w,x,y,z)` convention matches `body_link_quat_w`'s storage (MuJoCo native `xquat` convention, unconverted) -- no convention mismatch. Confirmed via `git`/code read that no reward term or figure is named `head_ball_contact` other than the ContactSensorCfg and the custom viewer plot (no name collision double-writing the figure). Re-derived the earlier "closest real approach ever measured: 0.736m" entry (2026-08-07, 11th session) from its source script (`probe_head_contact.py`) -- confirmed it is the raw `dist_to_head`, not a surface gap, and it is the single closest sample across an entire 1,280-episode replay, i.e. a rare outlier, not a typical distance at the moment a human perceives an on-screen "touch." Conclusion: the detector is not broken -- at `head_proximity_margin=0.65` (threshold 0.83m > 0.736m) it CAN fire, but genuine chin/head proximity is rare enough that a live few-minute play session is unlikely to hit it, while a 2D camera view can make a ball passing in front of/behind the head look like contact even when the true 3D distance is much larger (the same illusion hypothesis raised earlier this session, now the leading explanation for this report too).

**Fix (diagnostic-only, no reward/training-code change -- explicitly the safe category per this file's CLAUDE.md Change Approval Workflow addendum, so applied directly without an approval round-trip):**
1. `_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash` now caches `env._head_dbg_dist` (per-env raw ball-to-head-center distance), `env._head_dbg_threshold` (the scalar 0.83m trigger radius), and `env._head_dbg_center_w` (per-env head-sphere world position) every step -- it already ran unconditionally each frame, so this is free.
2. `AnalyticsPolicy`'s console flags line now prints `HD·(d=X.XX<0.83)` / `HD✓(d=X.XX<0.83)` instead of a bare flag, using the exact cached numbers -- the live measured distance is now visible on every printed line, not just a boolean.
3. New `_patch_viewer_head_proximity_vis` draws a translucent sphere in the native 3D viewer at the exact head-threshold radius around the currently-viewed env's head (yellow when clear, red the instant the ball's center enters it -- exactly when HD would flash). Registered in `run_play`'s native-viewer patch chain right after `_patch_viewer_intercept_vis`. Lets the user rotate the camera and see directly whether the ball ever actually enters the real 3D hit-zone, resolving the on-screen-illusion-vs-real-contact question by inspection instead of by further threshold tuning.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `play.py` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass. Live check on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` (2 envs, zero action): ball placed 0.5m from the head center reads `_head_dbg_dist=0.50 < _head_dbg_threshold=0.83`, `wf=1.0 hd=1.0 kn=0.0` -- cached values agree with the flags exactly; ball placed far away reads a distance safely above threshold with no flag firing; `_head_dbg_center_w` confirmed shape `(num_envs, 3)` so the overlay's `native_viewer.env_idx` indexing is valid. Not yet visually confirmed in the actual interactive viewer (requires the user to run it) -- `knee_proximity_margin`/`head_proximity_margin` themselves were NOT changed this entry.

## 2026-08-07 (later same day, 13th session) -- chin marker sphere removed; head_proximity_margin widened again 0.65 -> 1.0

**Context 1 (viewer):** user watched the prior entry's 0.83m sphere overlay live and reported it read as centered on "the head", not "the chin" -- asked for the yellow radius to be moved onto the chin specifically. Investigated by loading `t1_headless.xml`'s `H2.stl` mesh (trimesh) in H2's local frame (+X=forward, confirmed no `quat=` on H1/H2) and found the frontmost, lower-face vertex band (z in [0.0,0.05), |y|<0.015) centers around local `(0.065, 0, 0.03)` -- about 8cm lower / 5.5cm more forward than the real reward's reference point `(0.01, 0, 0.11)`. Added a small (0.08m, un-wired-to-HD) marker sphere there as a positioning aid, deliberately not touching `rewards.py`'s real offset (reward-affecting, needs confirmation first per this file's Change Approval Workflow addendum). User's next message: "remove that visualiser on the head, it doesn't sreve a purpose" -- removed `_patch_viewer_head_proximity_vis` and `_CHIN_LOCAL_OFFSET_CANDIDATE` entirely (and the now-unused `_head_dbg_center_w` cache field) rather than iterating further on marker placement; `_head_dbg_dist`/`_head_dbg_threshold` (the console `HDdist` readout) stay, since the user only asked to remove the 3D sphere, not the numeric console readout added the same session.

**Context 2 (threshold):** same message, second half: "could u increase the treshold again for the chin contact it is to narrow." This is `head_proximity_margin` (0.65, trigger radius 0.83m) -- there is no separate "chin" parameter; `head_proximity_margin`/`_HEAD_LOCAL_OFFSET` IS the mechanism the user calls "the chin" throughout this session (rewards.py's function/param names predate this terminology distinction and were not renamed). Asked via `AskUserQuestion` for a specific target value (0.65->1.0 vs 0.65->1.5 vs a custom number) before editing, per this file's Change Approval Workflow addendum; user's reply did not select an option but expressed frustration at the repeated "head" phrasing rather than objecting to proceeding, so applied the first (moderate) option rather than re-prompting again.

**Fix:** `head_proximity_margin` 0.65m -> 1.0m (total trigger radius 0.83m -> 1.18m) across all three synced locations (`rewards.py` default line ~2622, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` params line ~997, `play.py`'s `_HEAD_PROXIMITY_MARGIN` mirror line ~677). `knee_proximity_margin` untouched (0.05).

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on all 3 changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass (both before and after the sphere removal, and again after the margin change). Live check on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv`: registered params confirmed `{knee_proximity_margin: 0.05, head_proximity_margin: 1.0}`; ball placed 1.0m from the head center (inside the new 1.18m threshold, outside the old 0.83m one) correctly fires `wf=1.0 hd=1.0`, and `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` called directly with the exact registered params agrees (`1.0`). Not yet validated against a live training run. Given the trigger radius (1.18m) is now a large fraction of the robot's own height, watch for over-firing on ball proximity to the torso/shoulders/arms, not just genuine head/chin contact, if this doesn't resolve the user's report.

## 2026-08-07 (later same day, 14th session) -- head/chin sub-condition removed entirely from penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact

**Context:** user's next message, visibly frustrated after the repeated "head" vs "chin" terminology back-and-forth: "why are you being so dumb, i only want the treshold of the chin so revert back again, and ignore the whole head thing, even remove the whole head touch penalty for now." Read as: stop tuning/discussing the head/chin mechanism at all -- remove it from the actual training penalty, not just adjust its threshold again.

**Fix:** `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` (rewards.py) reverted to exactly its pre-2026-08-07-session form -- `head_proximity_margin` param, `_HEAD_GEOM_RADIUS`/`_HEAD_LOCAL_OFFSET`/`head_center_w`/`dist_to_head`/`head_threshold`/`head_near` all removed; return value back to `(wrong_sole_touch | wrong_leg_touch | leading_knee_touch).float()`. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s registered params for this term dropped `head_proximity_margin` entirely (now just `ball_name`/`knee_proximity_margin`). `knee_proximity_margin` (0.05) untouched throughout.

`play.py`'s `_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash` mirrored the same removal (it must always match the reward exactly, per its own docstring): dropped the head-distance computation, the `_head_dbg_dist`/`_head_dbg_threshold` console-readout cache, and the `_head_contact_flash_counter` latch; return type changed from a 3-tuple `(wrong_foot, head, knee)` to a 2-tuple `(wrong_foot, knee)`. Every caller updated to match: `AnalyticsPolicy` (dropped `self._head_flash`, the `HD`/`HDdist` console fields, and the entire `--force-head-contact-test` mechanism -- `PlayConfig.force_head_contact_test`, the forced-teleport block in `__call__`, and the `AnalyticsPolicy(...)` call-site kwarg -- since it only existed to verify the now-removed HD indicator/plot), `_patch_viewer_wrong_foot_contact_plot` (dropped the `"head_ball_contact"` figure, now only `"wrong_foot_ball_contact"`/`"knee_distance_contact"`), and `_POST_RECOVERY_REWARD_TERMS` (dropped `"head_ball_contact"`, 11 terms now, not backfilled). The `head_ball_contact` `ContactSensorCfg` in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` itself was left in place (it predates this session's 2026-08-07 chin work, from 2026-07-28, and is inert/harmless now that nothing reads it -- consistent with a minimal, targeted revert of only what this session added).

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on all 3 changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass. Live checks on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv`: (1) registered params confirmed `{ball_name: 'ball', knee_proximity_margin: 0.05}` -- no `head_proximity_margin` key; (2) ball placed exactly on the H2 head-center point (previously well inside any of this session's thresholds) now correctly produces `wf_flash=0.0`, `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact=0.0` -- head/chin contact no longer penalized at all; (3) knee-proximity mechanism reverified unaffected -- ball placed 0.19m from the leading knee (inside the unchanged 0.21m threshold, outside the solid-geometry overlap boundary of ~0.16m) correctly fires `wf_flash=1.0 kn_flash=1.0 reward=1.0`. (Initial knee reverification attempt placed the ball exactly AT the knee's body origin -- deep geometric interpenetration caused MuJoCo's contact solver to violently eject the ball in a single physics step, ~14 m/s, before the flags could be read; not a code bug, a test-methodology mistake, fixed by placing the ball in the genuine "near miss" gap between the solid-overlap boundary and the proximity threshold instead.)
