## 2026-08-07 (later same day, 15th session) -- added a dedicated `chin`/`chin_vis` marker to the robot asset (t1.xml + t1_headless.xml)

**Context:** user asked "so doesn't there exist such thing as the chin body? so you reckon we make one?" -- confirmed there is no dedicated chin geom/body anywhere in the robot asset, only the single `head_collision` sphere on H2 (which this whole session's "chin" mechanism approximated via an ad-hoc, duplicated-across-3-files local offset, never a clean queryable reference). User's follow-up: "add it to the t1, just like the one of the feet sensor on the side of the foot, make sure to not be near the foot."

**Investigated the existing "feet sensor" pattern first** (per this project's convention of checking existing patterns before adding new ones): `left_foot_link`/`right_foot_link` each already carry a `left_inner_vis`/`right_inner_vis` `<geom>` -- a small colored box, `contype="0" conaffinity="0"` (inert: no collision, no physics effect, since both bodies also carry an explicit `<inertial>` tag so the geom contributes no automatic mass either), used purely as a visual marker for where `inner_face_orientation_save`/`foot_inner_face_continuous` measure against. They also each carry a plain `<site name="left_foot"/>`/`<site name="right_foot"/>`, but grepping the whole `simple_goalkeeper` tree found no Python code that actually reads either site (`find_sites`/site position lookups) -- they appear to be inherited from the base Booster T1 asset and unused by this project. The functionally-relevant pattern to mirror is the inert-geom marker, not the site.

**Fix:** added, identically, to both `t1.xml` and `t1_headless.xml` (only `t1_headless.xml` is actually loaded for training/play -- `t1_constants.py`'s `spec_fn` -- but the two files are kept structurally in sync elsewhere in this asset, e.g. the foot markers exist in both, so both were updated) inside body `H2`, right after `head_collision`:
- `<geom name="chin_vis" type="sphere" size="0.02" pos="0.065 0 0.03" rgba="1 0.35 0.05 0.85" contype="0" conaffinity="0" group="2"/>` -- inert visual marker, same contype/conaffinity=0 pattern as the foot markers, distinct orange-red color to avoid visual confusion with the feet's blue markers.
- `<site name="chin" pos="0.065 0 0.03"/>` -- a clean, named, queryable reference point (unlike `left_foot`/`right_foot`, intended to actually be read by Python in a future re-wire, avoiding the 3-file-duplicated-constant problem this session's head/chin mechanism had).

Position `(0.065, 0, 0.03)` local to H2 is the same mesh-vertex-band estimate from the earlier 2026-08-07 entry (frontmost, lower-face vertex band of `H2.stl`) -- not re-derived, since that geometric analysis was never disputed, only the visualization/wiring built on top of it. Clearly separated from the foot markers both by body (H2, not `left_foot_link`/`right_foot_link` -- a completely different kinematic chain) and by name/color, per the user's "make sure to not be near the foot" request.

Deliberately NOT wired into any reward or Python code yet -- this is infrastructure only, per the user's earlier "remove the whole head touch penalty for now" (same session): if/when a chin penalty is reintroduced, it can now read this site/geom's actual compiled position directly instead of a hand-maintained offset constant.

**Verification:** both XML files confirmed to compile via `mujoco.MjSpec.from_file(...).compile()` with no errors; the new `chin_vis` geom and `chin` site both resolve to the expected local position `(0.065, 0.0, 0.03)` in the compiled model. Live check on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` (2 envs, 10 zero-action steps): no errors, confirming the inert marker doesn't perturb physics/contacts. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass (unaffected, as expected -- no Python code changed this entry).

## 2026-08-07 (later same day, 16th session) -- corrected mishearing: user meant "shin" not "chin" -- added left/right shin markers instead

**Context:** user's next message: "omg i dont mean the chin of the head i meant shin that was the thing i was referring too. the legs shin." The immediately-preceding "add a chin body" request (this same file's entry above) was a mishearing of "shin" -- the user was asking about the LEG's shin the whole time, not the head/face. Re-confirmed the underlying complaint: "here also doesn't exist this clean body we can use right we found last time the bodies are not well defined that we can use, so make you own for the shins, to be below the knee and above the foot."

**Investigated first:** `Shank_Left`/`Shank_Right` are real bodies, and `left_shin_collision`/`right_shin_collision` are real collision geoms (capsules, `size="0.05 0.075"`, centered at local `pos="0 0 -0.12"`) -- but there is no single clean POINT reference for "the shin": the Shank body's own origin sits at the KNEE joint (local z=0, where `left_knee_collision` also lives), not mid-shin, and the shin_collision geom is a whole capsule (spanning local z -0.045 to -0.195), not a queryable point. This is exactly the same "no clean body" gap the chin investigation found for the head, just one level down the leg -- the shin capsule provides collision physics but no dedicated marker/reference point the way this session now wants for both.

**Fix:** added, identically to both `t1.xml` and `t1_headless.xml` (same rationale as the chin marker's entry above -- only `t1_headless.xml` is loaded for training, but both are kept structurally in sync), inside `Shank_Left` and `Shank_Right`, right after their existing `*_knee_collision`/`*_shin_collision` geoms:
- `<geom name="left_shin_vis" type="sphere" size="0.02" pos="0 0 -0.12" rgba="0.1 0.85 0.3 0.85" contype="0" conaffinity="0" group="2"/>` (and the `right_` mirror) -- inert marker, same `contype/conaffinity=0` pattern as the feet's `*_inner_vis` and the head's `chin_vis`, green to stay visually distinct from both.
- `<site name="left_shin" pos="0 0 -0.12"/>` (and `right_shin`) -- clean, named, queryable reference point.

Position `z=-0.12` local to each Shank body: matches `left/right_shin_collision`'s own existing center, comfortably between the knee joint (z=0, where `*_knee_collision`'s radius-0.06 sphere already extends to z=-0.06) and the ankle (`Ankle_Cross_Left`/`Right`, z=-0.28 local) -- "below the knee and above the foot" per the user's own wording. `x=y=0`, matching how both existing knee/shin collision geoms are also centered on the shank's own local axis with no lateral offset. Deliberately not wired into any Python/reward code yet, same as the chin marker -- infrastructure only.

**Verification:** both XML files confirmed to compile via `mujoco.MjSpec.from_file(...).compile()`; `left_shin_vis`/`right_shin_vis` geoms and `left_shin`/`right_shin` sites all resolve to the expected local position `(0.0, 0.0, -0.12)` in the compiled model. Live check on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` (2 envs, 10 zero-action steps): no errors; `robot.find_bodies(["Shank_Left", "Shank_Right"])` still resolves correctly. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass.

## 2026-08-07 (later same day, 17th session) -- shin cylinder made "full leg width" + shortened; shin proximity wired into penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact; chin marker removed

**Context:** user's follow-up message bundled three requests: (1) "make them the full width of the leg and also way longer it is a cylinder not a sphere" -- shape correction for the shin markers just added; (2) "confirm my suspicion that there isn't a good body we can use for the wrong ball contact" -- answered inline (not a code change): `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` is inherently a composite check across multiple bodies (feet collision geoms on `left/right_foot_link`, the knee via `Shank_Left/Right`'s own body origin, and now the shin via the new sites) -- there is no single body that represents "wrong ball contact" as one concept, because the condition itself spans several physically distinct locations by design (any bad contact anywhere along the wrong leg, or a near-miss on the leading leg). The individual sub-parts (feet, knee) already had clean references; only the shin lacked one, which the marker/site fixed. Then, this same conversation turn: (3) "make the geom a bit shorter, implement it in the wrong foot ball contact, remove the chin body and push it to the github."

**Fix 1 (shape, this entry's earlier half -- superseded by (3) below):** `left/right_shin_vis` changed from `type="sphere" size="0.02"` to `type="cylinder" size="0.05 0.13"` (radius matches `left/right_shin_collision`'s own 0.05m -- "full width of the leg" -- half-length 0.13, full length 0.26, "way longer", spanning nearly the entire shank between the knee, z=0 local, and the ankle, z=-0.28 local), centred at `z=-0.14`. `left/right_shin` sites moved to match, `z=-0.14`.

**Fix 2 (shorten, same-turn follow-up):** half-length 0.13->0.10 (full length 0.26->0.20), still centred at `z=-0.14` -- user: "make the geom a bit shorter." Applied to both `t1.xml` and `t1_headless.xml`.

**Fix 3 (wire into the reward):** `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` (rewards.py) gained a `leading_shin_touch` sub-condition, the same distance-based pattern as the existing `leading_knee_touch` check, but measured against the new `left_shin`/`right_shin` MuJoCo sites instead of the Shank body's own origin -- read via `robot.find_sites(["left_shin","right_shin"])` + `robot.data.site_pos_w`, the actual compiled site position, not a hand-duplicated offset constant (avoiding the exact "3-file-duplicated-constant" problem the head/chin mechanism had earlier this session). New `_SHIN_GEOM_RADIUS = 0.05` local constant (matches the site geom's own radius). Reuses `knee_proximity_margin` rather than adding a new registered param -- both knee and shin proximity are the same "leading leg proximity" concept, just measured at two points along the leg; no `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` change needed. Final return: `(wrong_sole_touch | wrong_leg_touch | leading_knee_touch | leading_shin_touch).float()`. `play.py`'s `_compute_wrong_foot_contact_flash` mirrored the same addition, folded into the combined `wrong_touch`/`"wrong_foot_ball_contact"` signal only -- not split into its own isolated plot/counter (unlike the knee), since that wasn't requested.

**Fix 4 (remove chin marker):** `chin_vis` geom and `chin` site (H2 body, added two entries above) removed entirely from both `t1.xml` and `t1_headless.xml` -- infrastructure that was never wired into anything, per the user's explicit "remove the chin body."

**Verification:** both XML files compile (`mujoco.MjSpec.from_file(...).compile()`) with `chin_vis` confirmed absent and `left/right_shin_vis` confirmed as cylinders, `size=[0.05, 0.10]`. `ast.parse` on `rewards.py`/`play.py` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass. Live checks on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv`: (1) 10 zero-action steps with the shortened cylinders and no chin marker -- no errors; (2) ball placed 0.18m from the leading shin site (inside the new 0.20m shin threshold, outside the ~0.15m solid-overlap boundary) correctly fires `wf_flash=1.0`, `penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact=1.0` -- shin proximity is now genuinely penalized. (The isolated `knee_distance_contact` flash also fired at this same test position -- confirmed via direct distance printout this is NOT a bug: `dist_to_knee=0.191m` (<0.21m threshold) and `dist_to_shin=0.180m` (<0.20m threshold) both legitimately hold simultaneously, because the standing pose's bent knee puts the knee and shin reference points only ~14cm apart in world space, closer than the naive flat-leg assumption used when picking the test's ball offset.)

**Push:** committed and pushed to `origin/v2-blue-ball-waypoint` per user request ("push it to the github") -- see git log for the commit covering this entry plus the accumulated changes from the rest of this session (yaw-spin fix, plant-speed reward, wrong-foot-contact head/chin/knee/shin history, CLAUDE.md workflow rule, robot asset markers).

## 2026-08-07 (later same day, 19th session) -- postshoulderdofpos during_scale raised to full strength

**What changed:** `postshoulderdofpos`'s `during_scale` default (`rewards.py`) raised 0.3 -> 1.0 -- user: "change the shoulderdofpos during training from 0.3 to 1.0."

**Why:** `during_scale=0.3` was the deliberately conservative starting point set when this term was first added (2026-08-06) -- `postupperdofpos` only reached its own current `during_scale=1.0` after a separate, already-completed ramp-up (0.3->0.5->0.8->1.0, all AFTER shoulder had been removed from its scope). No live training data from `postshoulderdofpos` itself justified this jump to full strength; it's a direct user-requested parameter change, not a data-driven fix. `kernel_scale=0.15` and `asset_cfg` (4 shoulder joints) unchanged. `during_scale` is a plain float function-default (not a `SceneEntityCfg`), so unlike the `asset_cfg` wiring bugs earlier this session, editing the default is sufficient -- no `params` override exists in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s registration to also update.

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 76/76 pass. Live check on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` (4 envs, cuda:0, 60 zero-action steps): `reward_manager.get_term_cfg("postshoulderdofpos")` confirmed no `during_scale` override in `params` (relies on the function default), all rewards finite. Not yet validated against a live training run -- risk flagged in `postshoulderdofpos`'s own docstring: shoulder is the single largest dive-counterbalance joint, so full pre-save strength risks the same over-suppression class that got `arm_torque_limits`/`arm_action_rate_l2`/`arm_action_acc_l2` reverted 2026-07-29 (those penalized movement; this penalizes pose deviation, a different mechanism, but worth watching for the same symptom: footreach/ball_exit/episode-length regression).

**Push:** committed and pushed to `origin/v2-blue-ball-waypoint`, alongside stopping the running `6144_footyawspinfix_2026-08-07` job and launching a new run to validate this change together with the wrong-foot-contact rework from the prior session.

## 2026-08-08 -- orange trailing-foot waypoint (landing-focused subset)

**Context:** user reported the trailing (non-assigned) foot is sometimes stationary during interception and doesn't lift/step to fully complete the double-stepping motion. Investigated first (via a research subagent): the leading/assigned foot has a rich positional-target mechanism -- the "blue ball" waypoint (`_get_reach_target_y`, `rewards.py:340`) -- feeding `footreach`/`foot_proximity`/`blue_ball_landed`/`blue_overshoot_penalty`/`blue_stick_landing`. The trailing foot had no positional target at all: only `trailing_foot_forward_continuous` (orientation-only, always active) and the shared `foot_clearance` (weight +2.0, `max(height)` **across both feet** -- fully satisfied once the leading foot alone lifts, giving the trailing foot zero pressure of its own).

**Fix:** added a parallel "orange" mirror, landing-focused subset only (deliberately NOT the full blue stack -- see below):

- `_get_orange_reach_target_y` (`rewards.py`, new): target formula `orange_y = start_y + sign(delta)*max(|delta|-0.30, 0)/2` where `delta = full_y - start_y` -- blue's own midpoint formula, shrunk 30cm off the top before halving (confirmed with user via worked examples: delta=+1.00m gives orange_y=start+0.35m vs blue's start+0.50m; sign-safe so right-side/negative-delta crossings shrink toward the robot, not away from it). Reuses `env._blue_wide` directly (read-only) for the wide/narrow gate -- no new wide/region computation, since wide crossings are exactly where this project's own RSI region routing already uses double/triple-step motions (single-step motions are used for narrow crossings). Keyed to the trailing foot (`1 - _get_correct_foot_idx(...)`). Copies blue's curriculum-eased `landing_radius` (0.20->0.15m)/`landing_speed_threshold` (2.0->1.0 m/s) lerp and leaky-settle-count mechanism verbatim in shape (blue's own best-evidenced design, not re-derived). Own state namespace (`env._orange_*`), never touches `env._blue_*` except the read-only wide gate. Unlike blue, does NOT graduate to a live-ball target once landed -- no second phase for the trailing foot to converge into in this subset.
- `orange_foot_proximity`, `orange_ball_landed`, `orange_overshoot_penalty`, `orange_stick_landing` (`rewards.py`, new): structural copies of `foot_proximity`/`blue_ball_landed`/`blue_overshoot_penalty`/`blue_stick_landing` respectively, retargeted to the trailing foot and `_get_orange_reach_target_y`.
- Registered in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`: 4 new `RewardTermCfg` entries + 3 new `CurriculumTermCfg` entries (`orange_foot_proximity` has no curriculum, matching `foot_proximity`). Weights are half of blue's own current values (conservative first pass, confirmed with user) -- `orange_ball_landed` base_weight 5.0 (blue: 10.0), `orange_overshoot_penalty` base_weight -30.0 (blue: -60.0), `orange_stick_landing` base_weight 4.0 (blue: 8.0), `orange_foot_proximity` flat 2.5 (blue's `foot_proximity`: 5.0).

**Explicitly NOT ported in this change** (see the design spec's "Explicitly out of scope" section): `footreach`'s full phase1 (lateral pre-position)/phase2 (sigmoid reach x vel_sigma)/overshoot-kill-flag/blue-decel-zone machinery (the most complex, most ball-interception-specific piece of blue's stack), `near_stick_reach` (narrow-crossing anti-oscillation -- not needed since orange is wide-only), `blue_trunk_drive` (whole-body lateral drive). This project's own history shows every prior addition here landed best as a small, single change validated by a live training run before the next one was layered on -- these three can be added later as follow-ups if the landing-focused subset alone doesn't resolve the stationary-trailing-foot symptom.

**No G1 equivalent** -- same justification class as blue's own "Two-stage blue/green waypoint mechanism" row in `CLAUDE.md`'s Divergences table (G1 has no intermediate-waypoint concept at all, for either foot).

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 80/80 pass (76 existing + 4 new `_get_orange_reach_target_y` formula tests). Live check on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` (4 envs, cpu, 10 zero-action steps): all 4 new reward functions return finite `(4,)` tensors when called directly; reward manager resolves all 4 registered terms with the expected initial weights (2.5, 5.0, -30.0, 4.0) and a full env `.step()` completes with no exceptions. **Not yet validated against a live training run** -- next checkpoint after a fresh run should be compared against a pre-change run for whether the trailing-foot-stationary symptom actually improves, and the conservative (halved) weights may need retuning once there's live evidence, consistent with this project's convention for every other new reward term.

**Visualization:** `_patch_viewer_intercept_vis` (`play.py`) extended to draw an ORANGE sphere (rgba `[1.0, 0.55, 0.0, 0.75]`) at the current orange target whenever `env._blue_wide` (same visibility window as blue's own sphere), recomputed inline from the same formula rather than read from a cached env attribute, matching blue's own established pattern for this marker. No color-switch-on-landed (unlike blue's blue->green graduation) since this subset has no live-ball-tracking phase for the trailing foot. Viewer-only, no training effect.

**Known edge case (not a bug):** `env._blue_wide` can be forced True by region alone (`region_id in {1,3}`) even when `|delta| <= 0.30`, in which case `orange_y` collapses exactly to `start_y` (the env origin's own Y, between the robot's feet) per the formula's own floor -- geometrically sane (`orange_foot_proximity`/`orange_stick_landing` still produce a real gradient toward it, `orange_overshoot_penalty` reads near-zero since the trailing foot naturally sits on the far side), but worth knowing before assuming the orange sphere sitting on the robot's centerline is a bug.

**See also:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-08-orange-ball-trailing-foot-design.md` (full design), `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-08-orange-ball-trailing-foot.md` (implementation plan).

## 2026-08-08 (same day, follow-up) -- orange live diagnostics added

**Context:** the orange trailing-foot mechanism (previous entry) reuses blue's exact settle-count/leaky-decrement landing gate, which blue's own history shows fired at only 0.7-4% genuine-landing rate for a long time before its causes (distance vs. speed vs. contact vs. settle count) were made visible via `env._blue_dbg_*` fields and play.py's per-episode BLUE summary. Orange shipped without any equivalent -- a final whole-branch code review flagged this as a Minor, non-blocking gap that would nonetheless make a low orange landing rate hard to explain during a real validation run. User explicitly requested adding it now rather than waiting for evidence it's needed.

**Fix:** minimal mirror of blue's diagnostics (not blue's full temporary per-step debug dump, which was explicitly marked TEMPORARY/for-removal cruft in blue's own code -- only the fields play.py's per-episode accumulator needs):
- `rewards.py`'s `_get_orange_reach_target_y` now caches `env._orange_dbg_dist`, `_orange_dbg_speed`, `_orange_dbg_contact`, `_orange_dbg_settle`, `_orange_dbg_foot_idx` alongside the existing `_orange_landed`/`_orange_settle_count` state, inside the same `robot is not None and feet_contact is not None` branch.
- `play.py`'s `PolicyWrapper` gained `_min_orange_dist`/`_max_orange_settle`/`_ep_orange_landed` per-episode accumulators, updated in the same per-step block that already updates blue's (both share the identical `env._blue_wide` gate, since orange is only ever active on the same wide crossings blue is -- no separate `_ep_orange_was_wide` flag needed). The `[EpEnd]` summary line now prints an `ORANGE min_dist=... max_settle=.../3 landed=...` segment alongside the existing `BLUE` one.

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 81/81 pass (unchanged, no new test file -- this is diagnostic/viewer-adjacent, not behavior-changing). Live check on a real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` (8 envs, cpu, 15 zero-action steps): all 5 new `_orange_dbg_*` fields resolve with the expected shapes/dtypes (`(8,)` float32/float32/bool/int64/int64) after calling `orange_ball_landed` once to force a fresh `_get_orange_reach_target_y` call. `play.py` still parses (`ast.parse`) and imports cleanly. Not yet exercised in a real multi-episode play session or training run -- the per-episode accumulator logic itself (min/max tracking, reset-on-episode-boundary) is a direct structural copy of blue's already-working equivalent, not new logic.

## 2026-08-08 (later same day, follow-up 2) -- orange target shrink constant raised 0.30m -> 0.60m (user request)

**Context:** user: "the orange ball needs to be way further than the blue ball so subtract 60 from it instead of 30." Direct request to widen the gap between blue's and orange's targets.

**Fix:** in the target formula (`_get_orange_reach_target_y`, `rewards.py`), `shrunk = sign(delta) * max(|delta| - 0.30, 0.0)` -> `shrunk = sign(delta) * max(|delta| - 0.60, 0.0)`, `orange_y = start_y + shrunk / 2.0` unchanged. Net effect: orange moves from 15cm short of blue's own midpoint (in delta-magnitude terms) to 30cm short. Worked examples (start_y=0): delta=+1.00m -> orange_y now +0.20m (was +0.35m; blue stays +0.50m); delta=+0.80m -> +0.10m (was, at the old constant, delta=+0.40m -> +0.05m -- picked a different moderate example since 0.40m now floors to 0 under the new 0.60m threshold); delta<=0.60m in magnitude -> orange_y collapses to `start_y` (was <=0.30m). Same constant updated in `play.py`'s independently-recomputed viewer-sphere mirror (`abs(delta) - 0.30` -> `abs(delta) - 0.60`) so the marker stays in sync with the reward, per that code's own established "recompute inline, don't cache" rule.

Also updated: the 4 formula unit tests in `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_orange_reach_target_y.py` (new expected values 0.20/0.10/0.0/-0.20, and the "moderate positive delta" test's input changed from 0.40m to 0.80m since 0.40m no longer exercises a non-trivial shrink under the new constant); `CLAUDE.md`'s orange Divergences row (formula + "30cm short of blue" description).

**Interaction to note (not addressed, just flagging):** `env._blue_wide`'s own threshold is `|lateral| > 0.5` (or region-forced) -- crossings with `0.5 < |delta| <= 0.6` are still "wide" but now floor to `orange_y = start_y` under the new 0.60m constant (previously only `|delta| <= 0.3` floored). This narrows the band of wide crossings that get a genuinely non-trivial orange target. Not raised as a concern by the user; noted here in case a live run shows an unexpectedly large fraction of wide episodes with a degenerate (start_y) orange target.

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 81/81 pass (5 orange-formula tests updated in place, not added -- same count as before). Not yet validated against a live training run -- this is a direct user-requested parameter change, not data-driven.

---

## 2026-08-08 (still later same day, follow-up 3) -- orange target shrink constant adjusted 0.60m -> 0.50m (user request)

**Context:** user: "change it to 50" (the shrink constant, immediately following the 0.30->0.60 change above).

**Fix:** `shrunk = sign(delta) * max(|delta| - 0.60, 0.0)` -> `shrunk = sign(delta) * max(|delta| - 0.50, 0.0)` in `_get_orange_reach_target_y` (`rewards.py`), `play.py`'s independently-recomputed viewer mirror, and the 4 formula unit tests (`tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_orange_reach_target_y.py`) and `CLAUDE.md`'s orange Divergences row, same pattern as the prior two edits this session.

**Side effect worth noting:** 0.50 is exactly `_get_reach_target_y`'s own `wide_threshold` (the `|lateral| > 0.5` distance check `env._blue_wide` uses). This means every *distance-triggered* wide crossing now gets `shrunk > 0` by construction (`|delta| > 0.5` implies `|delta| - 0.50 > 0`), closing the "0.5 < |delta| <= 0.6 floors to start_y" gap the 0.60m constant introduced (see the previous entry's "Interaction to note"). A *region-forced* wide crossing (`region_id in {1,3}`) can still have `|delta| <= 0.5` and floor to `start_y` -- that part of the earlier note still applies, unchanged by this edit.

**Verification:** `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 81/81 pass. Not yet validated against a live training run -- direct user-requested parameter change, not data-driven.

## 2026-08-08 (later same day) -- leading-foot yaw-spin re-investigated on `model_17250.pt`: still present, systematic-debugging says stop tuning and question the architecture

**Context:** user asked to resume the leading-foot sideways-landing/yaw-spin investigation (deferred earlier this session in favor of the orange-ball work) and to surface the relevant rewards in the viewer. Re-verified via `git log` that no commit this session touched any of the foot-orientation reward chain (`foot_inner_face_continuous`, `inner_face_orientation_save`, `postleadfootorientation`, `postheadingorientation`, `foot_ang_vel_xy`, `foot_ang_vel_z`, `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG`, `alignment_threshold`) -- all confirmed still at their 2026-08-07 values (45deg target, alignment_threshold=0.85, both `foot_ang_vel_*` at -0.5).

**Fresh evidence gathered (not previously measured on this checkpoint):** the prior root-cause finding (this file, 2026-08-07 entry, "foot-orientation target 60->45 deg...") measured `model_16000.pt`, a checkpoint from a DIFFERENT, EARLIER run (`6144_airbornelatchfix_2026-08-06`) that may not have trained under the complete current fix set. `model_17250.pt` (run `6144_shoulderscale1_2026-08-07`, the checkpoint actually in question) started only after all of the yaw-spin fixes were already live, so it's the first checkpoint that had a real chance to learn under them. Wrote a standalone replay probe (scratchpad, not committed, mirrors the project's own `probe_hipyaw_causal.py` pattern) that loads the real trained policy via `HimAMPOnPolicyRunner`/`get_inference_policy` (same mechanism `play.py:run_play` uses for `--agent trained`), forces `left_far` region, and tracks world-frame `root_link_ang_vel_w[:,2]` (whole-body yaw rate) for 60 steps after every `_sb_flag` (save) rising edge, across 3 episodes x 64 envs on GPU:

| metric | value |
|---|---|
| save events captured (full 60-step window) | 64 |
| peak \|yaw rate\| (deg/s) | mean 121.1, median 118.3, max 193.2 |
| mean signed yaw rate over the LAST 20 tracked steps (deg/s) | mean +5.7, median +3.3 |
| fraction of events still \|>10 deg/s\| 40-59 steps after the save | **47%** |

A representative single-episode series (step-since-save -> yaw rate deg/s, every 5th step): `0:+21, 5:+56, 10:-26, 15:-4, 20:+27, 25:+43, 30:+44, 35:+26, 40:+10, 45:+14, 50:+14, 55:+12` -- this is not a decaying transient, it's an **oscillation** that swings sign and magnitude repeatedly (56 -> -26 -> +44) well past the 20-step reward window before loosely settling. Qualitatively worse/different from the prior finding's "climbs then plateaus" description -- here nearly half of all save events are still spinning meaningfully 2-3x past the window close.

**Conclusion (systematic-debugging Phase 4.5): this is an architecture question, not another tuning pass.** Counting distinct, already-attempted fix rounds targeting this exact mechanism: (1) 2026-08-03, capped `postleadfootorientation` to a fixed window; (2) 2026-08-04, lengthened the window and reshaped its ramp; (3) 2026-08-07, added `foot_ang_vel_z` + restored `foot_ang_vel_xy` to -0.5 (rate-damping); (4) 2026-08-07 later, reduced the commanded angle 60->45deg + tightened `alignment_threshold` (magnitude reduction). That's 4 rounds, all magnitude/timing tweaks to the same two-sided mechanism (one term family COMMANDS a foot-heading rotation via Hip_Yaw pre- and post-save; another family tries to DAMP the resulting reaction-torque spin) -- and the fresh measurement above shows the spin is, if anything, more severe than the last checkpoint it was measured on. Per `docs/BugFixes.md`'s own 2026-08-07 "Also flagged this session, not yet acted on" note, the deeper tension between `postleadfootorientation` (drives the spin) and `postheadingorientation` (fights it) — both in the same 20-step window at comparable weights — has never actually been reconciled, only worked around. Not proposing a fix yet: presented to the user as an architecture discussion (per `systematic-debugging`'s explicit "3+ fixes failed -> question the architecture, discuss with your human partner before attempting more fixes" rule), with several structurally different directions (reduce/remove the source command instead of damping its effect; give the arms/trunk an explicit counter-rotation incentive instead of only penalizing net momentum; rate-limit the Hip_Yaw action directly during saves; or drop the sideways "block posture" orientation goal entirely and rely on foot position alone) rather than a 5th magnitude tweak.

**Viewer (`play.py`, `_patch_viewer_post_recovery_plots`):** per user request ("turn on rewards i need to watch out for in the viewer"), swapped the P-panel's `wrong_foot_ball_contact`/`knee_distance_contact` slots (a separate, orthogonal investigation from an earlier session) for `foot_ang_vel_xy`, `foot_ang_vel_z`, and `ang_vel_z` -- the three reward terms that directly measure/damp the spin and were never in this panel before, despite being exactly what this investigation needs to watch. `postleadfootorientation`/`postheadingorientation`/`inner_face_orientation_save` were already present and unchanged. Viewer-only, no training effect.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `play.py` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 81/81 pass (no test touches the P-panel list). The replay probe itself ran against the real checkpoint/env/policy stack on a live `cuda:0` `ManagerBasedRlEnv` (64 envs, 3 full episodes) -- not a synthetic/mocked measurement.

## 2026-08-08 (later same day) -- foot-orientation target reverted 45deg -> 60deg (user request); alignment_threshold deliberately left at 0.85

**Context:** mid-investigation of the leading-foot yaw-spin issue, a live grounded-vs-airborne probe of `model_17250.pt` found grounded and airborne pre-save foot yaw-rates statistically similar (not a distinct grounded-specific spike), so the "no ground-contact gate on `foot_inner_face_continuous`" hypothesis was not confirmed. User then clarified the actually-reported symptom is a DIFFERENT, more specific one: the leading foot visibly spinning around the world-Z (gravity) axis right at/after ball contact -- not the whole-body drift or a pre-plant grounded-rotation issue. Before continuing that narrower investigation, user separately requested reverting the foot-heading target angle. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` given a real units-conversion risk: this project's own convention is `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` measured OFF-FORWARD, with "off-Y-axis" as its complement (90 - off-forward) -- so "30 degrees from the y axis" maps to `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG = 60.0` (the original 2026-08-01 value), not literally 30.0 off-forward (which would be a new, more-sideways-than-ever value). User confirmed the 60.0 interpretation.

**Fix:** `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` (`rewards.py`) 45.0 -> 60.0, exactly reverting the 2026-08-07 change (see that date's entry). `play.py`'s mirrored viewer-only constant updated to match (45.0 -> 60.0), per that file's own "verify against rewards.py" comment. `alignment_threshold` (`inner_face_orientation_save`'s param, `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) explicitly asked about and left at 0.85 (NOT reverted to the 0.7 that was originally calibrated for the 60deg target, per the 2026-08-07 entry's own stated reasoning) -- a deliberate, confirmed user choice, not an oversight. This means the current 0.85 threshold (~31.8deg cone) is now narrower than the ~45.6deg cone the 60deg target was originally calibrated with on 2026-08-01 -- flagged here in case a training run shows the one-shot `inner_face_orientation_save` bonus firing less often than expected for a 60deg target.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on both changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 81/81 pass (no test hardcodes either value). Live check: `rewards.py._FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` confirmed 60.0 (`_FOOT_TARGET_COS=0.500`, `_FOOT_TARGET_SIN=0.866`, matching cos/sin(60deg) exactly); `play.py._FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` confirmed 60.0; `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s registered `alignment_threshold` confirmed still 0.85. Not yet validated against a live training run -- direct user-requested parameter change, not data-driven. The narrower foot-Z-spin-at-ball-contact investigation this interrupted is still open and not addressed by this change.

## 2026-08-10 -- foot-orientation target steepened 60deg -> 75deg off forward (user request, "15 degrees from the y axis"), following a live `model_11250.pt` replay

**Context:** user watched `sgk_play ...-WithOverlay --checkpoint-file .../model_11250.pt` (the `6144_orangetrailingfoot_2026-08-08` run's latest checkpoint) and reported two separate issues. Issue 1 (this entry): the leading foot is "almost not rotating in the z direction to get to the angle" -- i.e. genuinely under-rotating relative to `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG`, not merely close-but-imprecise. Requested fix: "revert the angle back to 15 degrees from the y axis." Per this project's own off-forward/off-Y convention (`_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` measured off-forward, off-Y = 90 - off-forward), that maps to `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG = 75.0` -- confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion` before editing (this repo's Change Approval Workflow requires explicit confirmation for any reward-affecting change, even a direct-sounding instruction).

Note this moves the target angle further from forward (MORE sideways-demanding), the opposite direction of every prior fix in this constant's history (90->60 on 2026-08-01, 60->45 on 2026-08-07, both explicitly motivated by reducing the Hip_Yaw reaction-torque/whole-body-yaw-spin problem). The mechanism this targets is different: at zero rotation (foot still pointing forward), the dense `foot_inner_face_continuous` reward already scores `cos(60deg)=0.5` against the old target -- a fairly generous "do nothing" reward that supplies a weak gradient toward actually turning. At 75deg, that same zero-rotation state scores only `cos(75deg)=0.26`, roughly doubling the incentive gap between "not rotating" and "at target."

**Fix:** `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` (`rewards.py`) 60.0 -> 75.0. Drives both `inner_face_orientation_save` (one-shot, save-moment) and `foot_inner_face_continuous` (dense, pre-save) via the shared `_FOOT_TARGET_COS`/`_FOOT_TARGET_SIN` constants. `play.py`'s mirrored viewer-only constant updated to match (60.0 -> 75.0), per that file's own "verify against rewards.py" comment. `alignment_threshold` (0.85) left unchanged, not re-examined as part of this fix.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on both changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 81/81 pass (no test hardcodes either value). Not yet validated against a live training run -- direct user-requested parameter change following a live checkpoint observation, not yet re-measured after the change. This reopens the risk the 2026-08-07/08-08 fixes were managing (Hip_Yaw reaction-torque yaw spin) at a steeper angle than either of those attempts used; `foot_ang_vel_z` (yaw-rate damping, unchanged this session) is the mitigating term already in place -- watch it alongside `ang_vel_z` on the next run.

## 2026-08-10 (same day, same replay) -- foot_ang_vel_xy (heel/toe pitch rotation) penalty drastically increased -0.5 -> -3.0 (user request)

**Context:** same `model_11250.pt` replay as the entry above. Issue 2: user observed persistent "heel down toes up kind of rotation" throughout the episode and explicitly wants it penalized much more strongly ("no where in the episode i want that kind of movement"). `foot_ang_vel_xy` (`rewards.py`, sum of squared foot roll+pitch angular velocity across both feet, always active, no gate) is the exact matching term -- pitch is precisely the heel/toe rotation axis. `feetorientation` (static tilt, already a reward not a penalty) and `foot_ang_vel_z` (yaw-only, unrelated axis) were ruled out as the wrong terms. This is the second increase to this term this week -- it was already raised -0.25->-0.5 on 2026-08-07 for a related but distinct symptom (foot visibly rotating right at save contact). The 2026-07-20 fix that originally halved this term (-0.5->-0.25) was motivated by dilution of AMP's proportional reward share -- that concern reasserts itself at 6x the magnitude that fix reduced; watch AMP's reward share on the next run.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on both changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 81/81 pass (no test hardcodes this weight). Not yet validated against a live training run -- direct user-requested parameter change following a live checkpoint observation.

## 2026-08-12 -- postleadfootorientation gate fixed: contradicted foot_inner_face_continuous over the same foot's heading (root-caused this session, "foot just slightly off the x axis" symptom)

**Context:** investigating a user report that training run `6144_airbornelatchfix_2026-08-06` onward, the leading foot at save time settles only slightly off the world/robot-forward X axis instead of near the intended sideways target (`_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG`), regardless of that constant's own value (confirmed unaffected across the 60/45/60/75 deg history -- the bug is orthogonal to the target angle). Traced via `git log -p` on `rewards.py`: `foot_inner_face_continuous`'s gate was changed 2026-08-06 (`a684c1e`) from `(~behind)` to `~env._softstop_flag` (a deliberate, approved fix -- see that function's own docstring), intending a clean handoff to `inner_face_orientation_save`'s one-shot bonus. That change silently broke an invariant `postleadfootorientation`'s docstring still asserted: "these two terms cannot contradict each other... exactly complementary." `_ball_is_behind` (`postleadfootorientation`'s gate) is a compound OR (`ball_x<0 | softstop_flag | sb_flag`), and `sb_flag`/`ball_x<0` can both flip True well before `softstop_flag` (full velocity reversal). In that gap, `foot_inner_face_continuous` (now gated on the stricter `softstop_flag`) was STILL pulling the assigned foot toward the sideways target while `postleadfootorientation` (gated on the looser `behind`) had ALREADY started pulling the SAME foot back toward forward (0deg) -- two reward terms fighting over the same foot's heading on the same step, netting out near forward regardless of the target angle. Confirmed the airborne-latch mechanism (`_leading_foot_airborne_latched`) re-arms on `behind`'s own rising edge, so the foot is frequently genuinely airborne exactly when this overlap opens, not a corner case.

**Fix:** `postleadfootorientation`'s gate (`rewards.py`) changed from `_ball_is_behind(env, ball_name)` to `getattr(env, "_softstop_flag", ...)` directly, so it only starts once `foot_inner_face_continuous` has actually handed off -- restoring the "no gap, no overlap" behavior that function's own 2026-08-06 fix intended but never got mirrored on this side. Docstring updated in place (not rewritten) with the new FIX entry; the stale "exactly complementary" claim in the original 2026-07-27 docstring section is superseded by the new entry, not deleted, to preserve the history of why the original design looked correct at the time. Known, accepted side effect: `_postsave_airtime_window`'s 20-step window still starts counting from `_ball_is_behind`'s earlier rising edge, not softstop's, so this term's effective active window is now shorter by however many steps elapse between the two -- not re-widened as part of this fix, since it bounds the same Hip_Yaw reaction-torque risk the window exists to limit in the first place.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `rewards.py` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 81/81 pass (no test covers this gate directly). Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-08-12 -- blue_overshoot_penalty: added episode-freshness guard (RSI false-positive gap, no G1 equivalent to check against)

**Context:** same investigation session, separate mechanism. User asked to add `blue_overshoot_penalty` to the P-panel to check a suspicion that it might be "falsely giving negative rewards." Comparing it against its sibling landing-gated terms (`blue_ball_landed`, `stopball`/`softstop`'s `landing_ok`) found all three correctly exclude a "free" landing (`_get_reach_target_y`'s `_blue_landed_was_free`, `episode_length_buf < 10` -- RSI donor poses are mid-motion by construction and can't reflect genuine approach work) EXCEPT `blue_overshoot_penalty`, whose gate (`env._blue_wide & ~env._blue_landed_genuine`) has no episode-freshness component at all. An RSI reset that happens to drop the assigned foot already past the blue hinge would fire a negative reward on step 0/1, before the policy has taken any action.

Follow-up investigation via three new P-panel diagnostics (`blue_overshoot_margin_m` raw/unclamped margin, `blue_landed`, `blue_landed_was_free`) found this specific gap was NOT what the user was observing live in `sgk_play`: play mode disables RSI by default (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, "Always starting from standing"), and a live wide-crossing episode showed `blue_landed_was_free` staying 0 throughout while `blue_landed`/`blue_overshoot_penalty` behaved correctly relative to each other (overshoot genuinely zeroes the instant `blue_landed` flips, matching the code's own logic once `was_free=0`). The live symptom instead pointed at a different, downstream mechanism (`blue_trunk_drive`/`footreach` not pulling the foot onward from blue toward green quickly enough) -- separate investigation, not addressed by this fix. This fix is applied on its own merits (a real gap relative to the landing-bonus terms' own established pattern, matters for training even though it didn't explain what was visible in this particular play session).

**Fix:** `blue_overshoot_penalty` (`rewards.py`) gate changed from `env._blue_wide & ~env._blue_landed_genuine` to additionally require `env.episode_length_buf >= _BLUE_OVERSHOOT_FREE_STEP_THRESHOLD` (10, duplicated from `_get_reach_target_y`'s locally-scoped `_BLUE_LANDING_FREE_STEP_THRESHOLD` rather than imported, since that constant isn't module-level -- kept numerically identical deliberately). No G1 equivalent exists for this whole two-stage waypoint mechanism (checked, per this project's own Design Rule -- G1 has no intermediate-waypoint concept), so there's no upstream reference to verify this threshold against; it mirrors this project's own established pattern instead.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `rewards.py` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 81/81 pass (no test covers this gate directly). Not yet validated against a live training run -- and per the investigation above, this specific fix has no visible effect in play mode (RSI-off), so it cannot be confirmed via `sgk_play` at all; only a training run (or a `--no-rsi False` play session) would exercise it.

## 2026-08-12 (same session) -- blue_trunk_drive weight doubled 5.0 -> 10.0 (peak 12.5 -> 25.0), closing the far-region post-landing dead zone

**Context:** continuation of the same session's far-region-speed investigation. Live P-panel evidence (`blue_overshoot_margin_m`, a diagnostic added this session) showed the assigned foot's world-Y position freezing after a genuine blue landing on wide crossings -- the robot appeared to stall at the blue midpoint instead of continuing toward the real (green) crossing point. Traced the mechanism in `footreach` (`rewards.py:1064-1065`): `taskrew = torch.where(ball_x_local > 1.5, phase1_rew, reach_rew * vel_sigma)` -- the switch to the velocity-amplified branch is gated ONLY on `ball_x_local <= 1.5`, unconditional on `env._blue_landed_genuine`. So between "landed at blue" and "ball within 1.5m" (which can be a long window on a far-region episode), `footreach` contributes only the velocity-less `phase1_rew`. `blue_trunk_drive`'s own docstring (`rewards.py:1398-1409`) confirms it was added on 2026-07-24 specifically to fill this exact gap -- it was the sole active speed incentive in that window, and its weight had never been revisited since.

Two candidate fixes were identified: (A) raise `blue_trunk_drive`'s weight (this fix), or (B) relax `footreach`'s `phase1_mask` itself to also switch to the velocity-amplified branch once genuinely landed on a wide crossing. Per this repo's user-directed process, an independent subagent (no access to my conclusions, instructed to verify every claim against the code itself rather than trust my framing) reviewed both options. It confirmed the mechanism, corrected two numbers (curriculum peak was 12.5 not ~10, and `footreach`'s own peak is 25.0 -- `blue_trunk_drive` was a much weaker lever even at cap than either of us had assumed), and recommended against shipping bare Option B: the window Option B would unlock already shares `footreach`'s unguarded (no "close AND slow" counter-term) `vel_sigma` amplification mechanism with the narrow-region window that PREVIOUSLY caused a real, documented oscillation-farming exploit (fixed twice in this codebase -- see `blue_stick_landing`'s and `near_stick_reach`'s own docstrings/history) -- extending that same unguarded mechanism into a much longer time window is a real repeat risk, not a hypothetical one. G1 upstream (`legged_robot.py:1361-1400`) has no waypoint concept and no precedent for either option, consistent with this project's Design Rule (no G1 equivalent to defer to). Option A was chosen as the lower-risk lever to test first.

**Fix:** `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py` -- `blue_trunk_drive_curriculum`'s `base_weight` 5.0 -> 10.0 (line ~365-372; peak at cu=3 now 25.0, matching `footreach`'s own peak exactly), and the static (cu=0/play-mode) `blue_trunk_drive` `RewardTermCfg.weight` 5.0 -> 10.0 (line ~781-785) to keep both in sync, per this file's own stated invariant that the static weight equals the curriculum's cu=0 baseline.

**Verification (live simulation, not just static reading):** wrote a standalone probe (`verify_blue_trunk_drive_weight.py`, scratchpad, not committed) that builds real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` instances (both play and train configs) via `load_env_cfg`/`ManagerBasedRlEnv` (same construction mjlab's own tooling uses) and checks: (1) play-mode's registered `RewardTermCfg.weight == 10.0`; (2) train-mode's curriculum `base_weight == 10.0` and the `reward_curriculum_ep_len` formula gives a cu=3 peak of 25.0; (3) the REAL, already-`.resolve()`d term_cfg off a live `env.reward_manager.get_term_cfg(...)` (not a fresh, unresolved config -- per the `reward-shaping-scene-entity-cfg` skill's explicit warning against trusting standalone constructions) confirms weight=10.0; (4) forced a genuine post-landing, far-region state (`env._region_id[0]=1`, `env._blue_landed[0]=True`, `env._blue_landed_was_free[0]=False`, set only after stepping 3 real physics steps so `episode_length_buf<=1`'s reset-wipe in `_get_reach_target_y` doesn't immediately clobber them) and a REAL forced trunk velocity (`Entity.write_root_link_velocity_to_sim`, followed by `env.sim.forward()` to recompute the derived `cvel` the reward reads -- discovered live that a plain qvel write doesn't propagate to `root_link_lin_vel_w` without this, a real gotcha in the probe methodology, not a bug in the fix) toward the actual current reach target. Result: `blue_trunk_drive`'s raw output came back exactly `2.0` for a forced `2.0 m/s` velocity directly toward target -- confirming the "undecayed once genuinely landed" behavior the docstring claims holds in a real physics state, not just in isolated math -- and the weighted per-step contribution (`raw * resolved_weight * dt`) matched `raw * 10.0 * dt` exactly. All 5 probe checks passed. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 81/81 pass (no test hardcodes this weight). Not yet validated against a live training run -- this closes the identified structural gap and is confirmed to compute correctly, but whether 10.0/25.0 is the right final magnitude (vs. needing further tuning, or needing Option B added later with its own anti-oscillation counter-term) is unconfirmed until observed in training.

## 2026-08-13 -- P-panel: dedicated "all foot-orientation rewards" panel (viewer-only, no training effect)

**Context:** user reported the leading/trailing-foot rotation still isn't converging ("still it doesn't learn to rotate the foot correctly to the correct point") and asked to put every foot-orientation reward in the mujoco viewer at once. Checking the existing panel chain found the relevant terms were likely already invisible: `_patch_viewer_post_recovery_plots` (registered 2026-08-03, last updated 2026-08-10) puts `foot_ang_vel_xy`/`foot_ang_vel_z` in its last two of exactly 12 slots, but three MORE single/two-item promotion patches register after it in `run_play` (`_patch_viewer_blue_overshoot_plot`, `_patch_viewer_blue_overshoot_margin_plot`, `_patch_viewer_blue_landed_flags_plot`, all 2026-08-12) -- each prepends its own term(s) without removing anything else, silently pushing whatever was in the last slot(s) past `max_viewports` (12). This is the exact same failure class already documented happening to `head_ball_contact`/`wrong_foot_ball_contact`/`knee_distance_contact` on 2026-08-07, just recurring with a different pair of terms.

**Fix:** added `_patch_viewer_all_footorientation_plots` (`play.py`), a full-reorder panel (same mechanism as `_patch_viewer_post_recovery_plots`, not a single-item prepend) promoting all 8 foot-orientation-related terms to the front 12 slots: `assigned_foot_angle_deg` (existing custom raw plot, live save-target angle in degrees), `feetorientation` (static flatness), `foot_inner_face_continuous` (pre-save continuous rotation toward the ball), `inner_face_orientation_save` (save-moment one-shot), `postleadfootorientation` (post-save continuous, rotates back toward forward), `trailing_foot_forward_continuous` (non-assigned foot's own forward alignment), `foot_ang_vel_xy`/`foot_ang_vel_z` (roll+pitch / yaw rotation-rate penalties). Root/whole-body orientation terms (`postorientation`, `postheadingorientation`, `ang_vel_z`) deliberately excluded -- foot-level only, per the literal request. Registered last in `run_play`'s native-viewer patch chain so its reorder wins over every earlier panel, guaranteeing all 8 stay visible regardless of what other patches do to `_term_names`. Viewer/diagnostic-only change (P-panel promotion) -- no effect on rewards or training, applied directly per CLAUDE.md's Change Approval Workflow exception for viewer-only code.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `play.py` -- no syntax errors. Live smoke test: `sgk_play Mjlab-BeyondAMP-Goalkeeper-T1-MultiDisc --agent zero --num-envs 1 --viewer native` for 20s with a real display -- native window opened, all reward terms registered and logged (including the 8 promoted terms), no exceptions/tracebacks in the log (one unrelated `GLFWError` about Wayland window positioning, pre-existing/cosmetic). Not yet visually confirmed by the user that the panel actually shows the intended 8 terms in the front slots -- next step is a live checkpoint replay.

**Context:** user reported not seeing `postleadfootorientation` in the panel added above, and asked to remove `blue_overshoot_margin_m` and "all of those" (the 2026-08-12 blue-waypoint-investigation viewer patches), iterating remove/re-add until confirmed working. Separately asked to check git history for a "dumb param manager" (`SceneEntityCfg`) class of bug per this project's own `reward-shaping-scene-entity-cfg` skill.

**Investigation (evidence-based, not assumption):**
1. Added a temporary debug print of `native_viewer._term_names[:15]` inside `_patch_viewer_all_footorientation_plots`'s setup, ran `sgk_play ... --agent zero --num-envs 1 --viewer native` for 20s. Result BEFORE removing the blue_* patches: `postleadfootorientation` was already at index 4 of `_term_names` -- i.e. it WAS in the visible front-12 window, contradicting the "silently pushed out" theory from this file's own 2026-08-13 entry above. That theory was plausible reasoning but wrong when actually measured.
2. Ran `.claude/skills/reward-shaping-scene-entity-cfg/verify_reward_scoping.py` (the skill's own live-env scoping-bug detector) against the real reward/observation/event managers: `OK -- every scoped SceneEntityCfg in params resolved correctly.` No Pitfall 1/2 wiring bug anywhere, foot-orientation terms included -- rules out the "dumb param manager" as the cause of anything currently observed.
3. Ran a real trained-checkpoint replay (`model_17750.pt`, `--num-envs 4`, 45s) and grepped every `postleadfootorientation=` value logged: 135 samples read `0.00`, but a genuine save produced a rising/falling sequence up to `1.94` (weight cap 2.0) -- the term computes and fires correctly. It reads 0.00 the rest of the time by design (`env._softstop_flag`-gated, only active in a short post-save window) -- in a short zero-action smoke test with no saves, this is indistinguishable from "not there" at a glance, which is the most likely explanation for the user's report, not a real visibility or wiring bug.

**Fix (separate from the investigation, done regardless since requested directly):** removed `_patch_viewer_blue_overshoot_plot`, `_patch_viewer_blue_overshoot_margin_plot`, `_patch_viewer_blue_landed_flags_plot`, and their now-unused helper `_compute_blue_overshoot_margin` (`play.py`) outright -- these were single-item front-of-list promotions from the closed 2026-08-12 blue-waypoint investigation, superseded by and actively competing with the new footorientation panel for the 12 visible slots. `_patch_viewer_all_footorientation_plots`'s registration in `run_play` now immediately follows `_patch_viewer_post_recovery_plots`. Re-ran the same debug-print check after removal: `postleadfootorientation` still at index 4, all 8 footorientation terms at indices 0-7 -- confirmed stable with the clutter gone.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on `play.py` -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 81/81 pass. Debug print removed after use (was never committed). Viewer/diagnostic-only change -- no effect on rewards or training.

## 2026-08-13 (same session) -- foot rotation reverts: target angle, alignment threshold, and foot_ang_vel_z gated post-save-only

**Context:** continuing the same-session investigation into why the current run isn't learning to rotate the leading foot to the save-angle target, unlike `model_17250.pt`. User clarified their actual intent: `foot_ang_vel_xy` (roll+pitch, out-of-ground-plane tilt) is correctly penalizing the heel-down/toes-up landing instability they reported on 2026-08-10 and should stay at -3.0 -- they do NOT want any penalty on `foot_ang_vel_z` (yaw, in-ground-plane rotation), since that's the exact motion needed to aim the foot at the save target.

**Fix (three changes, applied together per user request, tests skipped per explicit instruction):**

1. `rewards.py`: `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` 75.0 -> 60.0 -- reverted to the exact value that trained `model_17250.pt` (confirmed via git history, commit `65d6852`, the commit in effect when that run launched).
2. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`: `inner_face_orientation_save`'s `alignment_threshold` 0.85 -> 0.7 -- reverted alongside (1), same baseline commit.
3. `rewards.py`/`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`: `foot_ang_vel_z` gate changed from unconditional to post-save-window-elapsed only. Added `ball_name` param (now required) and reused the shared `_postsave_airtime_window` helper (`window_steps=20` default, same window `postleadfootorientation`/`postsave_foot_airtime`/`postheadingorientation` already share): `has_window = env._psa_window_start >= 0; post_window_active = has_window & ~in_window`. Zero during pre-save aiming (`foot_inner_face_continuous`/`inner_face_orientation_save`'s active phase) and zero during the post-save window (`postleadfootorientation`'s active phase) -- both of those terms require yaw angular velocity to do their job (T1's ankle has no yaw DOF, only Hip_Yaw can rotate the foot's heading) -- only active once that window has elapsed, to still catch genuine lingering/residual spin. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s registration updated to pass `"ball_name": BALL_NAME`.

**Rationale:** combined hypothesis from the prior session's diffing -- the steeper/tighter target (75deg, 0.85 threshold) plus an unconditional yaw-rate penalty made "don't rotate" locally optimal, where the baseline run's easier target (60deg, 0.7 threshold) and no pre-save yaw penalty (the term didn't exist yet) made rotating clearly worth it. This fix removes both levers at once rather than isolating which one dominates -- a follow-up training run is needed to tell.

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on both changed files -- no syntax errors. No other callers of `foot_ang_vel_z` found (`grep -rn "foot_ang_vel_z" tests/` -- empty, no test hardcodes its old signature). **Test suite NOT run** (explicit user instruction to skip). Not validated against a live training run -- next training launch will be the first real test of this combined change.

## 2026-08-13 (same session) -- full revert to isolate reward-config differences from training maturity

**Context:** user visually confirmed the leading-foot rotation is genuinely worse on the current run (`model_7750.pt`) than baseline (`model_17250.pt`), not just less mature -- rejected the "just needs more iterations" framing. Asked to revert `foot_ang_vel_xy`/`foot_ang_vel_z` and `postleadfootorientation`'s gate back to baseline's exact state, to isolate whether these three reward-config differences (not training maturity) explain the gap.

**Fix:**
1. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`: `foot_ang_vel_xy` weight -3.0 -> -0.25 -- the exact value active when `model_17250.pt` trained.
2. `foot_ang_vel_z` **removed entirely** (function deleted from `rewards.py`, registration deleted from `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) -- this term had no equivalent at all in baseline's training code, so "revert" means it doesn't exist, not a reweight.
3. `postleadfootorientation`'s gate (`rewards.py`) reverted `env._softstop_flag` -> `_ball_is_behind(env, ball_name)` -- the exact gate baseline trained under. **Known, accepted regression:** this reopens the `foot_inner_face_continuous` contradiction the 2026-08-12 fix (same file, "postleadfootorientation gate fixed" entry) closed -- done deliberately, as part of isolating reward-config vs maturity, not an oversight.
4. `play.py`: removed `foot_ang_vel_z` from both P-panel term lists (`_POST_RECOVERY_REWARD_TERMS`, `_ALL_FOOTORIENTATION_TERMS`) -- harmless to leave (filtered by membership check) but cleaned up since the term no longer exists.
5. **Caught before push:** `mdp/__init__.py` explicitly re-exported `foot_ang_vel_z` by name -- deleting the function without fixing this import would have broken the entire package import (`ImportError` at startup, not a subtle bug). Fixed in the same commit.

**Net effect:** `foot_ang_vel_xy`, `foot_ang_vel_z`, `postleadfootorientation`'s gate, `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG`, and `inner_face_orientation_save`'s `alignment_threshold` are now ALL back to their exact `model_17250.pt`-training-time values. The only remaining known differences vs baseline's training code are outside this reward family (e.g. the 2026-08-12 `blue_overshoot_penalty` RSI-freshness guard, `blue_trunk_drive` weight doubling -- unrelated mechanisms, not reverted).

**Verification:** `ast.parse` on all 4 changed files -- no syntax errors. `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- 81/81 pass (would have caught the `mdp/__init__.py` import breakage above). Not yet validated against a live training run -- next run is the actual test of whether reward-config or maturity explains the gap.

## 2026-08-15 -- NEW "red" waypoint: trailing-foot second stage, gated on blue AND orange both landed

**Context:** user asked to close a gap orange's own docstring already flagged ("does NOT graduate to a second/live-ball target once landed") -- a third waypoint for the trailing foot, active only once the leading foot has genuinely landed at blue AND the trailing foot has genuinely landed at orange, positioned "behind the green ball" (short of the true crossing point, not all the way to it). Reuses the orange reward-function shapes per user request ("maybe you can reuse a lot of the rewards already that are available for orange ball"). Plan drafted by a dispatched subagent, reviewed and 3 open questions resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before any code was written, per this file's Change Approval Workflow.

**Design decision caught during planning (not a bug, a correctness fix to the plan itself before implementation):** a literal copy of `_get_orange_reach_target_y`'s formula, anchored at `start_y` and shrinking toward it, mathematically tops out AT blue as its shrink constant approaches zero -- it can never place a point past blue toward green. Red's formula instead anchors at `full_y` (green) and shrinks backward:
```python
delta = full_y - start_y
red_shrunk = torch.sign(delta) * (delta.abs() - 0.50).clamp(min=0.0)
red_y = full_y - red_shrunk / 2.0
```
Algebraically equivalent (whenever `|delta| > 0.50`) to `red_y = half_y + sign(delta)*0.25` -- i.e. **blue + 0.25m toward green**, the mirror image of orange's own **blue − 0.25m toward start**. Blue sits exactly centered between orange and red. `SHRINK_RED=0.50` (same constant orange uses, not a smaller one) confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` -- keeps the 0.25m gap clear of blue's own landing radius (curriculum-eased, max 0.20m) at every difficulty level.

**User-confirmed decisions (`AskUserQuestion`, 3 questions):**
1. **Weights equal to orange's own** (not a further quarter-of-blue halving) -- red's extra landing-gate (requires both blue and orange genuinely landed first) already limits false-credit risk relative to orange's single, ungated stage.
2. **`SHRINK_RED=0.50`** (same as orange) -- see anchor-flip reasoning above.
3. **Capped at red for the rest of the episode** -- no further graduation to live-ball tracking, mirroring orange's own explicitly-documented design (it never graduates either).

**Fix (all changes applied together, tests run):**
1. `rewards.py`: new `_get_red_reach_target_y` (after `_get_orange_reach_target_y`) -- own state namespace (`env._red_was_airborne/_landed/_settle_count/_landed_was_free/_landed_genuine/_last_settle_step`), same leaky-settle-count/curriculum-eased-radius-and-speed/free-landing mechanism as orange, `env._red_wide` reused directly from `env._blue_wide` (same pattern orange uses). New: `env._red_active = getattr(env,"_blue_landed_genuine",zeros) & getattr(env,"_orange_landed_genuine",zeros)`, ANDed into the settle `candidate` condition itself (not just gating the reward terms) so red's state machine cannot accrue settle progress before both upstream landings are genuine.
2. `rewards.py`: 4 new reward functions -- `red_foot_proximity`, `red_ball_landed`, `red_overshoot_penalty`, `red_stick_landing` -- direct mirrors of the 4 `orange_*` functions (same formulas, same trailing-foot targeting via `1 - _get_correct_foot_idx`), each additionally multiplying/ANDing by `env._red_active` where orange's own gating logic appears (the one structural diff from a blind mirror -- orange has no upstream landing gate, so needed none).
3. `mdp/__init__.py`: exported the 4 new functions (mirrors the exact `foot_ang_vel_z`-forgotten-export class of bug from the 2026-08-13 entry above -- checked this time before, not after, hitting an `ImportError`).
4. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`: `RewardTermCfg` registration for all 4 terms (inserted immediately after the orange block), weights equal to orange's (`red_foot_proximity=2.5` flat/no-curriculum, `red_ball_landed=5.0`, `red_overshoot_penalty=-30.0`, `red_stick_landing=4.0`) -- exact mirror of orange's own registration pattern. 3 new `CurriculumTermCfg` entries (`red_ball_landed_curriculum`/`red_overshoot_penalty_curriculum`/`red_stick_landing_curriculum`, same `reward_curriculum_ep_len`/`update_interval=500`/`ep_len_divisor=50` shape, `base_weight` matching the static weights) -- no curriculum entry for `red_foot_proximity`, matching neither `foot_proximity` nor `orange_foot_proximity` having one.
5. `play.py`: viewer-only red sphere (diagnostic, no approval needed) -- mirrors the orange sphere block, recomputed inline (not read from a cached attribute) so it can't drift out of sync with the actual reward target, gated on `env._red_active` (unlike blue/orange, red isn't a real target before that gate opens, so no sphere is shown until then). Color `[0.9, 0.1, 0.1, 0.75]` (red, vs. orange's `[1.0, 0.55, 0.0, 0.75]`).

**Verification:**
- `ast.parse` on all 4 changed files -- no syntax errors.
- New `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_red_reach_target_y.py` (9 tests, mirrors `test_orange_reach_target_y.py`'s structure): formula correctness at `delta=1.0/0.8/0.4/-1.0` (confirms `red_y=0.75/0.65/0.40/-0.75` respectively -- the `0.4` case confirms the degenerate collapse lands on `full_y`, not `start_y` like orange's equivalent case), the AND-gate (`env._red_active` false when either/both of `_blue_landed_genuine`/`_orange_landed_genuine` are false or missing, true only when both true), and `env._red_wide` correctly reusing `env._blue_wide` directly rather than recomputing.
- Full suite: `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- **90/90 pass** (81 pre-existing + 9 new).
- Live headless smoke test (300 steps, 8 envs, zero policy, real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` + reward manager): all 4 `red_*` terms registered and active in the reward manager's term list, ran with no exceptions, `env._red_wide` correctly varies per-env while `env._red_active`/`_red_landed_genuine` correctly stayed all-False for the whole run (no genuine blue+orange landing occurred under a zero policy, as expected).
- **Not yet validated against a live training run** -- like every `blue_*`/`orange_*` term before it, this needs a real training run to confirm the gate/formula produce the intended behavior under a learning policy, not just a zero-action sanity check.

**Documentation:** added the "Red ball second waypoint" row to `CLAUDE.md`'s "Divergences from G1 Upstream" table and 4 new rows to the "Reward Design" table (same commit).

## 2026-08-15 (same session) -- new `ankle_pitch_vel`: always-on, ankle-specific rotation-rate penalty

**Context:** follow-up to the joint-level probe (see the artifact/findings from earlier this session) which found the leading foot's pre-save pitch spike is genuinely `Ankle_Pitch`-driven (-1.76 rad/s at the exact spike peak, largest of the 3 sagittal joints and the only one whose sign matches the world-frame spike; also the biggest relative jump from its own baseline, 2.56x vs. Knee_Pitch's 1.93x and Hip_Pitch's 1.65x). User explicitly rejected gating this to the pre-save window: "no need to gate, i never want this type of tilt behavior" -- always-on penalty, no time-window restriction.

**Fix:**
1. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`: new `_ANKLE_PITCH_CFG = SceneEntityCfg("robot", joint_names=("Left_Ankle_Pitch", "Right_Ankle_Pitch"))`.
2. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`: new `RewardTermCfg` `ankle_pitch_vel`, reusing the existing built-in `mjlab_mdp.joint_vel_l2` (no new function needed in `rewards.py`) -- same mechanism/pattern as the existing `arm_dof_vel` term, scoped to just the 2 ankle-pitch joints, weight `-0.1`, **unconditional, no gate** (per explicit user request). Additive alongside the still-active `foot_ang_vel_xy` (-0.25, unchanged) rather than a replacement -- `foot_ang_vel_xy` measures whole-body world-frame angular velocity (conflates ankle-local rotation with Hip_Pitch/Knee_Pitch-driven leg-swing propagation, the exact reason the earlier `-3.0` attempt killed legitimate reach/swing motion too, see the 2026-08-13 revert entry above); this new term reads the LOCAL `Ankle_Pitch` joint's own velocity directly, so it structurally cannot touch the hip/knee motion a genuine dive/reach needs.
3. `Ankle_Roll` deliberately excluded: the same probe found roll's world-frame spike is actually more `Hip_Roll`-driven on average (bigger magnitude and bigger relative jump than `Ankle_Roll` over the roll-spike window) and only ~1/3 of pitch's total magnitude -- an ankle-specific roll penalty wouldn't target the right joint on this evidence.
4. `play.py`: added `"ankle_pitch_vel"` to `_ALL_FOOTORIENTATION_TERMS` (the last-registered, and therefore authoritative, P-panel term-ordering patch -- see that function's own docstring), right after `foot_ang_vel_xy` so both rotation-rate dampers are visible side by side in the native viewer's P-panel. 9 terms total, still under the 12-slot cap.

**Weight:** `-0.1` is a first empirical guess, same "unvalidated, watch next run" status `arm_dof_vel`'s own docstring carries -- not derived from a principled calibration against the probe's rad/s values, since Ankle_Pitch's own joint-space velocity isn't directly comparable in scale to `foot_ang_vel_xy`'s world-frame body angular velocity.

**Verification:**
- `ast.parse` on `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`/`play.py` -- no syntax errors.
- Full suite: `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- **90/90 pass** (no new unit tests added -- `joint_vel_l2` is an existing, already-tested mjlab built-in; the only new surface area is the `RewardTermCfg` registration itself, which the live smoke test below covers).
- Live headless smoke test (60 steps, 8 envs, random small actions, real `ManagerBasedRlEnv` + reward manager): `ankle_pitch_vel` confirmed present in `reward_manager.active_terms` and produced finite, sensibly-scaled per-env episode sums (e.g. `-0.02` to `-0.20` range across 8 envs) with no exceptions.
- **Not yet validated against a live training run** (per explicit user instruction -- training happens after this gets pushed, not part of this session's work).

## 2026-08-15 (same session) -- red-ball target formula: shrink-based -> flat 0.4m offset, then clamped

**Context:** user watched the viewer and reported "the location of red ball is totally off." Live-checked with a real `model_39750.pt` rollout (script: check_red_position.py, scratchpad, not committed) rather than guessing -- confirmed red's distance from green collapsed to as little as **0.029m** for crossings just barely over the 0.5m wide threshold, nowhere near the intended "behind green" gap. Root cause: the original formula (`red_y = full_y - sign(delta)*max(|delta|-0.50,0)/2`) makes red's distance from green scale with the total crossing distance |delta| -- correct for orange's own degenerate-collapse behavior (by design), wrong for red's "always a meaningful gap from green" intent.

**Fix 1 (flat offset):** user requested "just like -0.4 away from green ball full_Y" -- replaced with `red_y = full_y - sign(delta) * 0.4`, distance-independent.

**Fix 2 (clamp, same session, live-checked again):** the flat 0.4m offset alone was found -- again via a real rollout, not speculation -- to invert the intended ordering for crossings under ~0.8m total distance: one real example had `|delta|=0.711m`, where blue's own distance from green is only `|delta|/2=0.356m`, so the uncapped 0.4m red offset placed red at `-4.311` vs blue at `-4.356` (start=`-4.0`) -- i.e. `start < orange < red < blue < green`, red sitting BEFORE blue instead of past it. User confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` to clamp rather than accept the inversion: `red_offset = min(0.4, |delta|/2)`. For `|delta| >= 0.8m` (blue's own distance from green >= 0.4m) the clamp is a no-op and red gets the full requested 0.4m gap; for narrower wide crossings red collapses onto blue's own position instead of overshooting past it (same "degenerate collapse" class as orange's own start_y-collapse case, not a new failure mode).

**Changed:**
1. `rewards.py:_get_red_reach_target_y` -- formula replaced (see above), docstring rewritten with both live findings.
2. `scripts/play.py` -- inline red-sphere mirror updated to match exactly (`red_offset = min(0.4, abs(delta)/2.0)`).
3. `tests/simple_goalkeeper/test_red_reach_target_y.py` -- all formula tests rewritten for the new formula; added `test_red_target_at_exactly_080_gets_the_full_offset` (boundary, clamp is a no-op) and `test_red_target_clamps_to_blue_position_below_080` (clamp engaged, red collapses onto blue's own computed position) -- the latter directly encodes the ordering-inversion bug this clamp fixes, using the real numbers that exposed it.

**Verification:**
- `ast.parse` on all 3 changed files -- no syntax errors.
- Full suite: `env -u PYTHONPATH uv run pytest tests/ -q` -- **90/90 pass**.
- Live rollout re-check (same `model_39750.pt` checkpoint, same script) after each fix: first confirmed the flat-offset fix gives a real, non-collapsing 0.4m gap (e.g. `full_y=-4.711, red_y=-4.311` before the clamp existed -- wait, exactly the inversion case); after the clamp, re-ran and confirmed `red_y` now exactly equals `blue_y` whenever `|delta|<0.8` (multiple live examples, e.g. `blue_y=5.704, red_y=5.704`) -- no inversion observed across the re-check.
- **Not yet validated against a live training run.**
