## 2026-08-29 (later same day) — removed footreach's/blue_trunk_drive's wide-crossing blue decel-zones (same redundancy as near_decel, applied to their own ancestor); contact_yield_velocity weight 25->50; investigated "robot learns to kick the ball"

**Context:** a background self-fork audit (dispatched to check the rest of `rewards.py` for the same redundancy class as `near_decel`) reported back: `footreach`'s wide-crossing blue decel-zone and `blue_trunk_drive`'s own decel-zone are the exact wide-crossing siblings of `near_decel` — both force a speed-bonus falloff near blue, and `near_stick_reach`'s own docstring already quotes `blue_stick_landing`'s docstring saying the LATTER is "the real reason the [oscillation] problem doesn't surface [on wide crossings], not the decay." Same evidence class, never previously applied to its own ancestor. User asked why removing `near_decel` (narrow crossings) didn't already fix this: because the two are separate code blocks in the same function, gated on opposite conditions (`~env._blue_wide` vs `env._blue_wide`) — narrow and wide crossings are disjoint, so fixing one has zero effect on the other.

**Fix (all 3 user-approved, none proven yet):**
1. `footreach` (`rewards.py`): removed the wide-crossing blue decel-zone block (`blue_approach`/`_BLUE_DECEL_ZONE`/`_BLUE_DECEL_FLOOR`, was ~lines 1374-1386) — `vel_sigma` now goes straight from the base `1.0 + 3.0*vel_toward.clamp(0,3)` formula into phase1/phase2 combine, for BOTH narrow and wide crossings now. `blue_stick_landing` is the sole remaining anti-oscillation/plant mechanism for wide crossings.
2. `blue_trunk_drive` (`rewards.py`): removed its own matching decel-zone (`blue_approach` block, was ~lines 1815-1823) — `drive` now goes straight from the base clamp into the `active` gate, undecayed the whole approach.
3. `contact_yield_velocity` (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`): weight 25.0 -> 50.0.

**Investigated same session: "the robot learns to kick the ball" (user report, unprompted, watching a trained checkpoint).** Root-caused via a new diagnostic-only probe (`scratchpad/check_kick2.py`, not committed — ad hoc, not part of the repo's probe suite) tracing ball speed for 20 ticks (0.4s) after `stopball`'s own contact-detection instant on `model_39750`:

| tick after contact | mean ball speed | % still > 1.0 m/s |
|---|---|---|
| t+0 (contact) | 1.84 m/s | 60% |
| t+10 (0.2s, cleanstop's settle window) | 1.77 m/s | 54% |
| t+20 (0.4s) | 1.73 m/s | 52% |

**Root cause:** `cleanstop` only pays out once ball speed drops under 1.0 m/s and stays there for 10 consecutive (leaky) ticks — it fires whenever that eventually happens, which per this data is mostly NOT near the contact instant; the ball must be settling far later, from ordinary rolling friction over the following seconds, decoupled entirely from how hard the initial contact was. A violent kick and a gentle catch score identically on `cleanstop` as long as the ball eventually rolls to a stop, which it will regardless. Meanwhile everything upstream of contact explicitly carries FULL, undecayed speed into the real ball by design (`footreach`/`blue_trunk_drive`/`trailing_foot_reach`, all documented "carrying speed into the actual save is correct," 2026-07-24), and `stopball`/`softstop` themselves require `delta_vx > 0.6 m/s` to register a save AT ALL — a genuinely gentle touch may not even count as a "stop." `contact_yield_velocity` is the only term that looks at the contact instant itself, and per the same probe it's currently losing (see the 2026-08-29 earlier-same-day entry above) — hence weight raised to 50 (change #3 above). Not yet resolved; user is considering reshaping `cleanstop` itself to close this gap (see below) — no code change made for that yet, awaiting decision.

**Evidence:** 90/90 tests pass after changes 1-3. Kicking root-cause is checkpoint-replay-measured (1181 tracked contacts, `model_39750`), not speculative. None of the 3 changes above are validated against a live training run yet — watch for the wide-crossing oscillation exploit's return (same signature as the 2026-07-25 report) now that `blue_stick_landing` is the sole guard on both narrow AND wide crossings; if it reappears, strengthen `blue_stick_landing`/`near_stick_reach` rather than reinstating either decel-zone.

---

## 2026-08-29 (later still) — cleanstop reworked: fixed-delay averaged window instead of open-ended settle-loop, can now score negative

**User request, after reviewing a ball-vx-around-softstop plot** (built live via a throwaway probe, `scratchpad/check_dropoff.py` + `plot_dropoff.py`, not committed): average ball speed over ticks 8-12 after `softstop` fires (~0.15-0.25s @ dt=0.02s), use total speed (matching `cleanstop`'s existing metric, not just vx), `best_speed` stays 0.2, and — explicitly — merge this into `cleanstop` itself rather than adding a new sibling term ("why don't we integrate this in cleanstop? i think that is the better solution").

**What changed (`rewards.py:cleanstop`):** removed the old open-ended settle-loop (`settle_steps`, leaky-decremented consecutive-ticks-under-threshold counter) entirely. Replaced with: on `softstop`'s rising edge, arm a fixed window; average `ball_speed` over ticks 8-12 after that edge; fire exactly once, unconditionally (no longer gated on the ball ever actually dropping under `speed_threshold`). Payout kernel rescaled so it crosses exactly 0 (not the old ~0.05 floor) at `speed_threshold=1.0` and goes negative beyond it, clamped to `[-1, 1]`: `scale = clamp((exp(-decay_rate*(avg_speed-best_speed)) - zero_cross)/(1-zero_cross), -1, 1)` where `zero_cross = exp(-decay_rate*(speed_threshold-best_speed))`.

**Why this closes the kicking gap:** the old design could only ever pay `[0, 1]` and only fired once the ball happened to settle under 1.0 m/s for 10 straight ticks — for a hard kick that never reliably happens soon (or ever) after contact, so it just silently paid 0, indistinguishable from "no save." The new version always fires a fixed 0.16-0.24s after every genuine correct-foot save and can go negative, so a violent deflection now gets real negative gradient instead of vanishing.

**Evidence:** 90/90 tests pass (no dedicated unit test for `cleanstop`, consistent with its prior no-test history — validated via live smoke test only, same convention as `contact_yield_velocity`/`trailing_foot_reach`). Live smoke test (`model_39750`, 128 envs, 1500 steps): 354 firings, weighted values ranging -0.91 to +8.75, **25% scored negative** — confirms the mechanism actually produces negative payouts on a checkpoint known to kick, not just in theory. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-08-29 (same day, correction) — cleanstop's negative-payout shape was still broken; replaced with a single tanh

**What was wrong:** the shape shipped in the entry directly above (`scale = clamp((exp(-decay_rate*(avg_speed-best_speed)) - zero_cross)/(1-zero_cross), -1, 1)`) was caught, by plotting it and having the user look at the plot, to never actually reach anywhere near -1. A plain `exp(...)` is always positive and only decays toward 0 as speed grows -- after the linear rescale, the asymptotic floor as speed->infinity works out to `-zero_cross/(1-zero_cross) ~= -0.052`, so the `clamp(min=-1)` in the code was dead code, never engaging. A "genuinely violent kick" and a "middling save" both scored roughly the same tiny negative number.

**Fix:** replaced with a single continuous `scale = tanh(steepness * (speed_threshold - avg_speed))`, `steepness=2.0`. Symmetric around `speed_threshold` by construction (exactly 0.0 there), bounded to `(-1, 1)` with no explicit clamp needed, reaches genuinely close to -1 by ~1.8-2.0 m/s. `best_speed`/`decay_rate` params removed (no longer used by this simpler formula); `steepness` replaces them. User explicitly asked for "just one function" after an intermediate two-piece (separate positive/negative branches) version was tried and worked but was rejected for that reason.

**Evidence:** 90/90 tests pass. Live smoke test (`model_39750`, 128 envs, 1500 steps): 361 firings, weighted values -17.36 to +15.56 (full weight range, weight=17.36), **27% scored negative**. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-08-30 — first `6144_speedkickinggaps_2026-08-29` checkpoints reviewed: cleanstop/contact_yield_velocity/trailing_foot_lift all reported bad; clearance gradient rework, contact_yield_velocity X/Y split + timing fix, two stale-reference bugs found and fixed

**User report** (`model_2000.pt`/`model_14250.pt` from the run trained on top of the previous entry's commit): `cleanstop` looks really bad, `contact_yield_velocity` looks bad, and `trailing_foot_lift` looks bad -- trailing foot clipping the floor a lot. Asked for a full review of everything changed since the last commit plus research into why the policy isn't learning `cleanstop` matters.

**Investigation (checkpoint-replay probe comparing `model_2000` vs `model_14250`):**
- `softstop` fire rate 59%->96% (saves got much more reliable) but `cleanstop` avg scale went **+0.74 -> -0.07** in the same run, and foot vx at contact regressed **-0.01 -> +0.19** (more forward, not less) -- the policy got better at RELIABLY contacting the ball but not at contacting it GENTLY. Same root mechanism as the earlier "robot learns to kick" report, not fixed by the previous commit's changes.
- `contact_yield_velocity`'s own total went UP (2.09->11.27) despite vx getting worse, while contact-instant lateral-velocity std ballooned (0.28->0.95) -- consistent with the combined dot-product being satisfiable via Y motion masking a bad X.
- Measured the trailing foot's REAL height directly (not the reward number): flat at 0.030m, mean AND median, identical whether `trailing_foot_lift`'s gate is active or not. Per this project's own documented convention (foot collision capsule sits 0.03m below the tracked body Z, RSI divergence-table entry), 0.030m means the sole is exactly at the floor -- it never lifts, full stop, unrelated to today's target_height change (that change made the same non-lift score much lower, unmasking rather than causing it).

**User follow-up mid-session, separate bug report:** "why is there a delay between the softstop reward and the contact yield velocity... the foot goes backwards getting positive reward when the contact already happened and some time has past so we get fake reward." Confirmed via a probe comparing the TRUE first foot-ball touch (`ball_contact` sensor) against `_sb_deflection_now`'s firing instant (`model_14250`, 598 events): 77% fire with zero lag (no issue), but for the 11% with 5+ ticks (0.1s+) of lag, foot vx at true touch was +0.19 (forward, bad) while vx at the delayed `deflection_now` instant this term actually sampled had already reversed to -0.07 -- a genuine "fake reward" bug, confirmed, affecting roughly 1 in 9 save events.

**Fixes (4 code changes, all live-smoke-tested against `model_14250`):**

1. **`foot_clearance`/`trailing_foot_lift` gradient** (`rewards.py`, new shared `_clearance_reward` helper): replaced the symmetric Gaussian bump (`exp(-sigma*(h-target)^2)`) with `tanh(rise_steepness*h/target) * exp(-fall_sigma*max(h-target,0)^2)`. Root cause of the old kernel's weak pull off the ground: a Gaussian's gradient is maximized only near its own inflection point (~0.041m from target at sigma=300) and shrinks in BOTH directions from there -- at h=0 (target=0.10), the gradient (2.99) is ~50x smaller than at the inflection point (148.6), giving almost no incentive to leave the ground in the first place. The new rise half is steepest exactly at h=0 (tanh's own max slope) with an unchanged Gaussian falloff past target (overshoot still discouraged the same as before). Live smoke test: at the SAME ~0.03m height the checkpoint is currently stuck at, reward is now ~0.72-0.76 vs the old kernel's ~0.05-0.23 for identical behavior.

2. **`contact_yield_velocity` split into X/Y** (`rewards.py`: `contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y`, new shared `_contact_yield_state` helper): the original single dot-product let a large Y-axis swing at contact compensate for a bad (forward) X velocity before either was clamped. Now two independently-clamped terms, weight split 30/20 (was one term at 50), matching the original target vector's own X:Y magnitude ratio (sin(55)=0.82:cos(55)=0.57). Live smoke test on `model_14250` confirms the exact gaming pattern: X mean -24.8 (92% negative, badly wrong), Y mean +16.5 (95% positive) -- Y was propping up the old combined score almost single-handedly.

3. **`contact_yield_velocity`'s timing fixed in the same rework**: `_contact_yield_state` now triggers off the `ball_contact` sensor's own first-true instant for the correct foot (same sensor `feet_slippage`/`penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact` already use), not `env._sb_deflection_now`. Keeps the `in_front`/`landing_ok` sanity gates `deflection_now` had; drops the `delta_vx` threshold requirement entirely (that threshold, needing several ticks to build up, was the actual source of the lag -- not a meaningful filter for this term's purpose). Memoized per-tick via the same `env.episode_length_buf`-comparison idiom `_get_reach_target_y` already uses, since both X and Y terms call the shared helper every step and must see one consistent snapshot with the one-shot latch mutated exactly once.

4. **Two stale-reference bugs found and fixed while wiring up the rename**, neither caused by today's edits directly but both would have broken or silently misled:
   - `mdp/__init__.py` still imported the old `contact_yield_velocity` name -- would have crashed the entire task registration on import. Fixed to import `contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y`.
   - `play.py`'s `_patch_viewer_foot_restitution_plot` still promoted/ranged the single old `"contact_yield_velocity"` term name (and its fixed range, `[-5.5,5.5]`, was ALREADY stale before today -- comment claimed weight=5.0 but the real weight had moved to 25 then 50 without this plot ever being updated). Now promotes both new terms with correct per-term ranges (`[-30,30]`/`[-20,20]`).

**Also fixed (user request, "use different colors for target and current angle"):** `play.py`'s `assigned_foot_angle_deg` P-panel plot only ever showed the target as a static number in the title, not a line. Added a second line (mujoco's `MjvFigure` supports up to 100 lines per figure via `linedata`/`linepnt`/`linergb`; this plot only ever used index 0) holding a flat reference at the target angle -- mujoco's default palette already colors line 1 differently (green vs line 0's red) with no manual rgba needed. While wiring this up, found `play.py`'s own `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` (a manually-duplicated literal, "verify against rewards.py if that constant ever changes") had drifted: still 70.0 (last touched 2026-08-22) while `rewards.py`'s real value moved to 55.0 on 2026-08-23 (commit `9f6d20a`) -- the plot title had been silently showing the wrong target for a week. Replaced the duplicate with a direct import from `rewards.py`; can't drift again.

**Evidence:** 90/90 tests pass (after fixing the `mdp/__init__.py` import break the rename caused). Live smoke test (`model_14250`, 128 envs, 600 steps) confirms all four changes behave as designed -- see per-term numbers above. None of today's changes validated against a live training run yet.

## 2026-08-30 (later same day) — contact_yield_velocity_y direction flipped: now rewards yielding TOWARD the robot's midline, not away from it

**User request:** "i want the y contact yield velocity in the y direction to be pointed towards the center so you need to rotate the correct vector as for the velocity 90 degrees."

**Root cause:** the just-split `contact_yield_velocity_y` reused the ORIGINAL combined term's Y-contribution unchanged (`+sign*_FOOT_TARGET_COS`). Worked through geometrically, that direction was never independently chosen for Y -- it fell out of how `target_dir_w` was built for the X axis (rotate the toe-axis orientation target `(cos, sign*sin)` by `-sign*90deg` to get the X-axis block-face normal). Applied to Y, that rotation happens to reward the foot retreating LATERALLY OUTWARD (away from the robot's own midline) at contact, not inward -- correct for X, never re-derived for Y once it became its own term.

**Fix:** per the user's own framing, rotate the SAME toe-axis target vector by the OTHER mirrored 90 degrees (`+sign*90deg` instead of `-sign*90deg`). Worked out algebraically this is exactly a 180-degree swing from the original -- i.e. the previous formula negated. `rewards.py:contact_yield_velocity_y`: `yield_y = assigned_vel_w[:,1] * expected_sign * _FOOT_TARGET_COS` -> `yield_y = -assigned_vel_w[:,1] * expected_sign * _FOOT_TARGET_COS`. Now positive when the assigned foot's Y-velocity points back toward the robot's own midline (left foot at +Y moving -Y, right foot at -Y moving +Y), negative when it flings further outward.

**Known follow-up, not done:** `play.py`'s `_patch_viewer_contact_yield_vis` (the green/orange direction-line viewer overlay) still draws the OLD combined `target_dir_w`/`yield_dir`, which no longer matches either split term's true reward direction on its own (X kept the old geometry, Y is now independently flipped). Not updated -- wasn't asked for this round, flagging so it doesn't read as accurate next play session.

**Evidence:** 90/90 tests pass. Live smoke test (`model_14250`, 128 envs, 600 steps): mean flipped from the previous entry's +16.5 to **-16.1** (94% negative) on the exact same checkpoint -- expected and correct, not a regression: this checkpoint was trained under the OLD (outward-rewarding) direction, so it now reads badly under the new (inward) scoring, exactly mirroring the previous measurement. Not yet validated against a live training run.

**Follow-up (same day, user request): weight rebalanced so X clearly dominates.** "i dont want that the most important velocity is the [roughly-equal split]... i want you to decrease the y velocity drastically and put it towards the x." `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`: `contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y` weights 30/20 -> **45/5** (was a 1.5x ratio, now 9x; total unchanged at 50). Y kept nonzero so the corrected toward-center direction (previous entry) still has some gradient, just much less influence than X. `play.py`'s fixed P-panel ranges updated to match (`[-45,45]`/`[-5,5]`). 90/90 tests pass.

**Follow-up (same day, user report): the viewer's green direction line hadn't updated.** User caught this by eye: "the green vector didn't change its angle? it should be pointed -x and -y direction when the ball is on the side for the +y side." Confirmed -- the "known follow-up, not done" item flagged directly above was exactly this: `play.py:_patch_viewer_contact_yield_vis` still drew the OLD combined `-target_dir_w`, unaffected by the Y sign flip. Rebuilt the green line directly from each split term's own formula: `yield_dir = (-_FOOT_TARGET_SIN, -expected_sign*_FOOT_TARGET_COS, 0)` -- X unchanged (matches `contact_yield_velocity_x`), Y now matches `contact_yield_velocity_y`'s flip. For the assigned foot on the +Y (left) side this now correctly points into the (-X,-Y) quadrant. 90/90 tests pass, syntax-checked; this is a pure viewer/geometry change with no reward-manager dependency, not separately live-smoke-tested.

## 2026-08-30 (later same day) — contact_yield_velocity_x/_y and trailing_foot_lift added to the existing correct-foot-save curriculum

**User question + request:** "is contact yield velocity in the curriculum? because i think we should add those also with trailing foot clearance because those are important." Confirmed: neither `contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y` nor `trailing_foot_lift` were curriculum-scaled before -- all three sat at flat static weights the entire run.

**Fix:** folded all three into the EXISTING `correct_foot_save_curriculum` loop (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, the same one already scaling `single_foot_save`/`cleanstop`/`inner_face_orientation_save`/`foot_inner_face_continuous`), rather than adding new standalone `CurriculumTermCfg` entries -- user explicitly asked to reuse what already exists ("dont create new ones") after an initial version of this fix put `trailing_foot_lift` in a separate `reward_curriculum_ep_len` block. Base weights match each term's current static weight; all step-double at `cu>=3` (ep_len ~144 steps, "only makes sense once the robot already saves reliably" -- same reasoning the existing 4 terms already use):
- `contact_yield_velocity_x`: 45.0 -> 90.0
- `contact_yield_velocity_y`: 5.0 -> 10.0
- `trailing_foot_lift`: 2.0 -> 4.0

**Evidence:** 90/90 tests pass. Live smoke test (train-mode env construction + 50 zero-action steps, 32 envs): all three terms register correctly via `reward_manager.get_term_cfg`, weights read their base values at cu=0 (start of training) as expected, no errors. Not yet validated against a live training run.

## 2026-08-30 (later same day) — curriculum section reorganized into 3 tiers; fixed unreachable `activate_at_cu=3`

**User request:** "can we please organise this, because now it is a mess... make logical groups, so rewards that are essential for the final product, and other rewards rethink if they need curriculum at all and put them in a lower ranking group... also do this for 2 and 3, because we currently have a bug at the third one the correct_foot_save_curriculum since the training never hit cu>=3, it always stays max cu=2." Planned via `EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode` (plan saved at `~/.claude/plans/magical-fluttering-blanket.md`), one revision round (user moved `trailing_foot_lift` from the draft's Tier 3 into Tier 2 -- "just as important as the stuff from tier 2").

**Confirmed bug:** `cu = int(smoothed_ep_len / 50)`. `episode_length_s=3.0` @ `dt=0.02s` = 150 steps max, so `cu=3` requires the training batch's *smoothed mean* episode length to hit that absolute ceiling -- effectively zero early terminations (`bad_orientation`/`base_height`/`sharpforce`/`ball_exit`) averaged across the whole batch. Any nonzero rate on those channels (there is always one) caps the mean below 150, so `cu` never reaches 3 in practice -- confirmed by the user's own observation. All 7 `correct_foot_save_curriculum` entries had been running at flat `base_weight` the entire time, never their documented doubled value.

**Fix:**
1. `correct_foot_save_curriculum`'s `activate_at_cu` 3 -> 2 for all 7 entries (surgical -- only this group's own threshold, doesn't touch the shared `env._curriculumupdate` computation the 14 `reward_curriculum_ep_len` entries also depend on).
2. Reorganized `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`'s curriculum section (previously one long list in whatever order each reward was introduced) into 3 labeled tiers, moved physically, all existing per-entry comments preserved verbatim:
   - **Tier 1 (core save mechanics)**: `softstop`, `footreach`, `stopball`, `success` (ramp) + `single_foot_save`, `cleanstop`, `inner_face_orientation_save`, `contact_yield_velocity_x` (step).
   - **Tier 2 (waypoint scaffolding + trailing-foot mechanics)**: `near_stick_reach`, `blue_ball_landed`, `blue_overshoot_penalty`, `blue_stick_landing`, `blue_trunk_drive`, `orange_ball_landed`, `orange_overshoot_penalty`, `orange_stick_landing`, `red_ball_landed`, `red_overshoot_penalty`, `red_stick_landing` (ramp) + `trailing_foot_lift` (step).
   - **Tier 3 (secondary, reconsider need)**: `foot_inner_face_continuous`, `contact_yield_velocity_y` (step) -- kept curriculum-scaled for now, not reverted to flat.
   - `ball_difficulty` kept at the top (spawn difficulty, not a reward weight). Added a comment flagging `far_travel_curriculum` (`mdp/events.py`) as a 4th curriculum mechanism that exists entirely outside `cfg.curriculum[...]` -- found missing during this same reorg, not migrated (different registration path, out of scope for this pass), just documented so it isn't missed again.
3. Considered but deferred: a `grouped_reward_curriculum` class that could drive several rewards from one `CurriculumTermCfg` (dict of `{reward_name: base_weight}`) -- mechanically the existing classes can't share one object across rewards with different base weights, so true consolidation would need new code. Kept this pass to pure reorg + the one bug fix so the two kinds of change don't tangle.

**Evidence:** 90/90 tests pass. Live smoke test (train-mode env, 32 envs, 50 zero-action steps): all repositioned/modified terms register correctly via `reward_manager.get_term_cfg`, base weights read correctly at cu=0, entry count unchanged (23 before and after — nothing lost or duplicated in the move). Confirming the `activate_at_cu` fix actually unblocks the doubling needs a real training run (or a scripted curriculum fast-forward) showing `cu` reach 2 and the 7 terms' weights actually double -- not verifiable from a cold reset alone.

---

## 2026-08-30 (later still) — full weight-distribution audit; foot_clearance added to curriculum (Tier 2, matching trailing_foot_lift)

**User request:** "could u do a full overview of the weights distribution, because i feel like it is unfair organised with softstop being extremely high and trailing foot lift extremely low." A first attempt to delegate this to a background research fork returned empty (0 tool calls, no real findings) -- redone directly instead.

**Findings:** built the full peak-weight table (base weight × curriculum multiplier: ramp ×2.5 at cu=3, step ×2 at cu>=2 per today's earlier fix) across all ~62 registered reward terms. Range: `softstop` 91.2 (highest) down to `postlinvel`/`postlegdofpos` 1.0 (lowest, among positive terms). Important caveat on the "softstop vs trailing_foot_lift" comparison itself: `softstop` is one-shot (fires once/episode), `trailing_foot_lift` is continuous (fires every tick its gate is active, ~10-15% of a 150-step episode) -- so trailing_foot_lift's real per-episode budget (up to ~15 ticks × 4.0 = ~60) is much closer to softstop's 91 than the raw weight numbers (91 vs 4, pre-fix) suggest. Comparing one-shot peak weight against continuous peak weight directly is not apples-to-apples.

**One concrete inconsistency found and fixed:** `foot_clearance` and `trailing_foot_lift` share the exact same kernel (`_clearance_reward`, unified earlier today) and `target_height` (0.10) -- scoped to max(both feet) vs. the trailing foot only, otherwise identical math. `trailing_foot_lift` was added to the curriculum earlier today; `foot_clearance` was left flat (2.0). Added `foot_clearance` to the same Tier 2 step-loop, same base weight (2.0 -> 4.0 at cu>=2) -- two siblings using identical math no longer diverge in importance.

**No other reshuffling done.** Checked `postleadfootplantspeed` specifically for overlap with the new `contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y` (both are "foot velocity at an instant" by name) -- confirmed genuinely different events: `postleadfootplantspeed` is the foot's GROUND-landing speed in the post-save window, `contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y` is the foot's velocity at the BALL-contact instant. Not redundant. No other duplicate-purpose terms found this pass (the `near_decel`/blue-decel-zone class of redundancy was already fully resolved earlier today).

**My own assessment (asked for directly, "think to yourself we dont need weight reshuffle?"):** no, I don't think a broader reshuffle is warranted right now. The `foot_clearance` fix above closes the one concrete inconsistency the audit turned up. The rest of the spread reflects real, defensible priority differences once one-shot vs. continuous firing is accounted for -- core save events (`softstop`, `contact_yield_velocity_x`, `single_foot_save`) legitimately dominate, continuous shaping terms are smaller per-tick by design (they accumulate), and the smallest flat terms (`postlinvel`, `postlegdofpos`, `angular_momentum_penalty`) are narrow post-save nudges, not oversights. This is a judgment call from static analysis, not a live-training result -- the real test is whether a training run shows any of these terms failing to move the policy at all, which would be the actual evidence for revisiting this.

**Evidence:** 90/90 tests pass. Live smoke test (train-mode env, 32 envs, 50 steps): `foot_clearance`/`trailing_foot_lift`/`contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y` all register correctly, base weights read correctly at cu=0. Not yet validated against a live training run.

## 2026-08-30 (later still) — contact_yield_velocity_x/_y trigger reverted to `_sb_deflection_now`: the ball_contact-sensor retiming broke firing entirely

**User report:** watching `model_8250` (from `6144_yieldflip_curriculum_2026-08-30`, the first live run since the earlier same-day retiming) in `play.py`, `contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y` never fire -- confirmed live, zero events.

**Root cause of the zero-fire regression:** not fully isolated. The retiming (see the entry above this one) switched the trigger from `env._sb_deflection_now` to the `ball_contact` sensor's own first-true instant, splitting the sensor's geom columns via `found[:, :half]`/`found[:, half:]` -- that split was never verified against `sensor.primary_names` (the exact check `debugging-mujoco-contact-sensors`, `.claude/skills/`, requires before trusting a combined sensor's column order). Other candidate: `env._blue_wide`/`env._blue_landed_genuine` (read by the new `landing_ok` gate) not being fresh yet at this term's registration position. Not isolated further -- reverted instead per explicit user request ("please keep using the stopball flag").

**Fix:** `_contact_yield_state` (`rewards.py`) reverted to `env._sb_deflection_now` as the trigger -- same simple one-shot latch pattern the term used before the retiming, `ball_contact`-sensor logic removed entirely. The ~11% contact-instant timing lag this retiming was meant to fix (previous entry) is real but a strictly smaller problem than a term that fires 0% of the time.

**Evidence:** 90/90 tests pass. Live smoke test (`model_8250`, 128 envs, 800 steps): `contact_yield_velocity_x` n=248 fires (mean -16.2), `contact_yield_velocity_y` n=248 fires (mean -0.54) -- confirmed firing again on the exact checkpoint that showed zero fires. If the `ball_contact`-based timing fix is revisited later, use the contact-sensor debugging skill's probe pattern (print `sensor.primary_names`, verify column order live) instead of reasoning about geom order from source alone -- that skill exists specifically because this codebase has hit this exact mistake class before (`penalize_wrong_foot_ball_contact`, 3+ rounds).

## 2026-08-30 (later still) — contact_yield_velocity_x/_y now average 4 backward ticks; Y's max_credit_speed lowered to 0.3

**User request:** "i want 4 ticks before hand only and then compute it" (backward-only averaging window, discussed and asked for after confirming a forward-looking window was feasible but would reintroduce the just-reverted recovery-motion risk) "also set the target y velocity for contact yield to something small like 0.3 max."

**Fix 1 -- 4-tick backward average:** `_contact_yield_state` (`rewards.py`) now maintains a small ring buffer (`env._cyv_vel_history`, shape `(N,4,3)`) of the assigned foot's velocity, updated every tick. When `_sb_deflection_now` fires on tick t, the reward is scored on the MEAN of ticks t-4..t-1 (read from the buffer BEFORE the current tick's velocity is folded in) -- never tick t itself or anything after it, so no delayed payout and no reintroduction of the recovery-motion contamination risk the earlier ball_contact-based retiming had (that one looked forward; this one only ever looks backward). Smooths out single-tick noise from the discrete collision impulse.

**Fix 2 -- Y's `max_credit_speed` 0.5 -> 0.3:** raw lateral speed needed to reach full credit shrinks from ~0.87 m/s to ~0.52 m/s (the formula scales by `cos(55deg)~=0.574`). Updated both the registration (`goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`) and the function's own default (`rewards.py`) so they stay in sync.

**Evidence:** 90/90 tests pass. Live smoke test (`model_8250`, 128 envs, 800 steps): `contact_yield_velocity_x` n=243 fires (mean -25.8), `contact_yield_velocity_y` n=243 fires (mean -0.58) -- still firing correctly after both changes. Not yet validated against a live training run.

## 2026-08-30 (later still) — investigated far-region blue approach speed; added a landing_radius ground-circle viewer overlay

**User report:** "still not satisfied with the speed the booster goes towards [blue] ball... specifically talking about the far range." Investigated live on `model_8250` before proposing any fix (systematic-debugging: gather evidence first).

**Root cause found:** measured actual foot/trunk velocity toward blue during far-region approach (28,288 samples, wide+unlanded window): median foot |vy|=**0.002 m/s**, median trunk |vy|=**0.009 m/s** -- essentially stationary for most of the window, and 0% of samples ever reached the existing 3.0 m/s `footreach`/`blue_trunk_drive` vel_sigma clamp ceiling. So the positive speed incentive isn't the bottleneck -- the policy isn't using the incentive it already has. Cross-checked `blue_overshoot_penalty` (a CONTINUOUS per-step penalty, by its own docstring design, curriculum peak -150) and found it firing 20,238 times across far-region ticks in the same run, against a strict landing tolerance of only 0.08m. Conclusion: the policy has learned extreme caution (near-zero approach speed) to avoid triggering/prolonging this large, easily-tripped, continuously-paid penalty -- a classic risk-aversion pattern, not an under-incentivized-speed problem. Raising the positive speed weight further was NOT recommended for this reason (see chat) -- proposed instead: lower `blue_overshoot_penalty`'s magnitude, and/or widen `landing_radius`. Neither implemented yet, awaiting user's choice.

**Added (user request, viewer-only):** a ground-circle overlay in `play.py`'s `_patch_viewer_intercept_vis`, drawn at the blue waypoint showing `landing_radius` (hardcoded to the strict 0.08m/ball_difficulty=1 value, per explicit user request -- not read live from the curriculum-eased value) as a ring of connected line segments (`_add_ground_circle`, new helper -- mujoco has no native ring/annulus decor geom, so it's built from `_add_line` segments the same way every other marker in this file already is). Lets a human visually compare where the foot actually lands against the true tolerance zone.

**Evidence:** 90/90 tests pass, `python3 -m py_compile` clean. Pure viewer/geometry addition, no reward-manager dependency -- not separately live-smoke-tested beyond the syntax/import check.

**Correction (same session, self-caught):** the circle above was drawn at radius 0.08 -- stale. The strict `landing_radius` default has actually been **0.15** since a 2026-07-24 fix (`rewards.py:_get_reach_target_y`'s own comment: "was 0.08... too strict, widened to 0.15") -- I used stale memory instead of checking the current code, and the diagnosis two entries above ("8cm tolerance") inherited the same error (the underlying finding -- near-zero approach speed, `blue_overshoot_penalty` firing constantly -- still stands independent of the exact radius number). User asked to "increase it to 0.15" based on my wrong framing; since 0.15 is already current, clarified with the user and, pending their answer on whether to raise it further, fixed the visualization's radius to the correct current value (0.15) so at least the circle matches reality. `landing_radius` itself not changed.

## 2026-08-30 (later still) — landing_radius widened for blue/orange/red (strict settled at 0.18, easy at 0.30); overshoot penalties switched to a shared live radius (found: they never tracked the gate's own value AT ALL, stuck at 0.08 since 2026-07-23)

**Sequence of user requests, all same session:**
1. "increase the radius to 0.2 and easy to 0.3, also do this for orange and red" -- `_get_reach_target_y`/`_get_orange_reach_target_y`/`_get_red_reach_target_y`'s `landing_radius` default 0.15->0.20, easy-end hardcode 0.20->0.30, all three waypoints (`rewards.py`).
2. "update it so it uses a common variable" -- while widening, found `blue_overshoot_penalty`/`orange_overshoot_penalty`/`red_overshoot_penalty` each had their OWN separate hardcoded `landing_radius=0.08` parameter, never wired to the gate's value at all. Confirmed: this wasn't just stale after the 2026-07-24 widening (0.08->0.15) -- these three penalties had used a static 0.08m since their ORIGINAL 2026-07-23 implementation, un-eased by curriculum and untouched by every later widening. Removed the separate parameter from all three; they now read `env._blue_landing_radius_current`/`_orange_landing_radius_current`/`_red_landing_radius_current` directly (the exact live value the corresponding `_get_*_reach_target_y` function -- already called at the top of each penalty function -- just computed and cached). Can't drift apart again.
3. "lets have the strict at the real 0.15 then... matter of fact have it 0.18" -- after confirming the overshoot-penalty bug, reconsidered the strict end down from 0.20 to **0.18** (settled value). Easy end (0.30) unchanged. Updated all three waypoints' defaults plus the ground-circle viewer overlay (0.15) and `_get_reach_target_y`'s own easing-formula comment to match.

**Final state:** strict `landing_radius` = 0.18 (was 0.15 for ~5 weeks, 0.08 before that), easy = 0.30 (was 0.20). All three overshoot penalties now track this live, no separate stale copy possible.

**Evidence:** 90/90 tests pass at every step. Live smoke test (`model_8250`): `env._blue_landing_radius_current` correctly reads the updated value, `blue_overshoot_penalty` still fires (expected -- this checkpoint was trained under the old mismatched radii, so nothing changes retroactively for it). `python3 -m py_compile` clean on `play.py`. None of these changes validated against a live training run yet -- the actual test is whether a new run shows less far-region caution now that the overshoot penalty tracks a genuinely reachable radius instead of a phantom 0.08m one.

## 2026-09-04: Re-added inner_face_orientation_save/foot_inner_face_continuous, retargeted 55->0 deg off forward

**Context:** these two rewards were disabled 2026-09-02 (`632cbbc`) because their 55 deg off-forward (sideways block posture) target was the suspected cause of the leading foot visibly rotating sideways at save, even on a freshly-trained checkpoint (`6144_watchdogresume`, post-fix lineage). `success` and `postleadfootorientation` were checked and ruled out (`success` is pure distance-to-target, no orientation term; `postleadfootorientation` targets forward, not sideways).

**Fix (user request, "put back the orientation rewards, but point them 90 degrees from the y axis so just straight forward"):**
1. `rewards.py`: `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` 55.0 -> **0.0** (0 deg off forward = 90 deg off Y = straight forward). Verified the math in both reward functions degrades harmlessly at target=0 (no divide-by-zero; the `expected_sign` left/right mirroring becomes a no-op since forward is now the same target for both feet, which is correct -- the sideways mirroring only ever existed to support a nonzero target).
2. Re-enabled `inner_face_orientation_save`/`foot_inner_face_continuous` (uncommented in `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, and their curriculum entries) at their original base weights (17.36 / 3.47) -- same mechanism, opposite intent: now rewards the leading foot for staying pointed forward instead of turning sideways.
3. **Option B (user-chosen, via `AskUserQuestion`):** `contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y` (added 2026-08-23) also read the same target-angle constant for their own yield-direction math -- retargeting `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` to 0 would have silently changed their target too (from a diagonal yield direction to pure lateral). Split off a new `_YIELD_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG = 55.0` (the old value, unchanged) with its own `_YIELD_TARGET_COS`/`_YIELD_TARGET_SIN`; `contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y` now read the new constants exclusively. Decouples the two concepts (block-face orientation vs. contact-yield direction) that happened to share one constant only by historical accident.

**Evidence:** `ast.parse` clean on both `rewards.py` and `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`. Grepped every remaining `_FOOT_TARGET_COS`/`_FOOT_TARGET_SIN` reference in `rewards.py` -- only docstrings/comments remain, no live code path still reads the old constants from `contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y`. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-09-04: cleanstop decoupled from softstop's trigger + made asymmetric

**Context:** user reported cleanstop "not firing sometimes... it is just flat" and worried the reward system was broken -- a genuinely hard/violent touch on the ball could get ZERO feedback (not negative) if it didn't happen to also satisfy softstop's gate. Root-caused: `cleanstop` only arms its measurement window on the rising edge of `env._softstop_flag`, and `softstop`'s own `fired` condition is a strict superset of stopball's `deflection_now` (adds a redundant `ball_x_vel > 0.05` check that must ALSO hold on the exact same tick as stopball's `delta_vx > 1.0` check) -- any 1-tick misalignment between the two thresholds can leave `_softstop_flag` permanently unset for the rest of an episode, silently starving `cleanstop` even when a genuine hard correct-foot deflection happened.

**Fix 1 (retrigger):** `cleanstop` now arms off `env._sb_flag` (stopball's own one-shot latch) instead of `env._softstop_flag`. `_sb_flag`'s underlying `deflection_now` condition already requires `correct_foot_contact`/`landing_ok`, so the separate `correct_foot` gate `cleanstop` used to read from `env._softstop_correct_foot` is now provably redundant -- removed. Renamed `_cleanstop_prev_softstop` -> `_cleanstop_prev_trigger` to match. This fires whenever `stopball` itself fires -- strictly more often than the old softstop-gated version, since softstop's extra threshold is gone.

**Known remaining limitation (not fixed here, flagged for future work):** `correct_foot_contact` (inside `_sb_deflection_now`) is still the GROUND-contact sensor (`feet_contact`), not a ball-contact sensor -- an assigned foot that deflects the ball while fully airborne (never touching the ground that same tick) still won't set `_sb_flag`, so `cleanstop` still can't score that case. A ball-contact-sensor-based version of this detection has been tried and reverted twice already in this codebase (stopball/softstop's 2026-07-15 fix, contact_yield_velocity's 2026-08-30 fix) -- both times MuJoCo's contact window for the small rolling ball proved too narrow to reliably catch. Revisit using `.claude/skills/debugging-mujoco-contact-sensors`'s probe pattern before attempting this again, don't re-guess from source alone.

**Fix 2 (avoidance-risk mitigation, user request):** payout is now asymmetric -- new `negative_scale=0.4` parameter scales the negative branch only (`scale = raw_tanh if raw_tanh>=0 else raw_tanh*0.4`), capping the worst case at -0.4 instead of -1.0 while leaving the positive side at +1.0 unchanged. Rationale: engaging the ball also earns from `stopball`/`softstop`/`single_foot_save` (none of which care about stop quality) -- keeping cleanstop's downside shallow relative to those guarantees "attempt and mess up" still beats "never engage" in expectation, without a from-scratch numeric audit of the full reward budget. Chosen as a smooth scale (not a hard clamp) to avoid a flat, zero-gradient dead zone at the floor. Deliberately did NOT touch `softstop`'s own weight in the same pass -- shrinking the guaranteed on-contact bonus at the same time as widening cleanstop's negative reach would have been the actual dangerous combination.

**Evidence:** `ast.parse` clean on both files. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-09-04 (second pass): cleanstop given its own trigger, decoupled from `_sb_flag`/`landing_ok`

**Context:** live-checkpoint probe (`.claude/skills/debugging-mujoco-contact-sensors` methodology -- real trained checkpoint `model_20750.pt`, 6144_watchdogresume lineage, primary_names printed before assuming geom order, cross-checked reward gate state against live sensor data across ~4000 real steps) run to investigate the previous entry's "known remaining limitation." Found the airborne-kick hypothesis was WRONG (0/400 genuine assigned-foot ball touches happened while ungrounded) -- but found the REAL gap: of 246 genuine correct-foot touches with zero stopball credit, 52% were blocked purely by `landing_ok` (the wide-crossing "genuinely landed at blue first" gate), many at delta_vx of 4-10 m/s -- clearly hard, legitimate contact, not the beeline-past-blue exploit `landing_ok` exists to prevent.

**Fix (user request):** `cleanstop` now computes its own independent trigger inline, decoupled from `_sb_flag` (and therefore from `landing_ok`) entirely: correct-foot `ball_contact` sensor (genuine foot-vs-ball touch, not the `feet_contact` ground sensor stopball/softstop use) AND `delta_vx > 1.0` (reusing stopball's own `_sb_init_vx` baseline) AND `in_front`. `stopball`/`softstop` themselves are UNCHANGED -- still require `landing_ok`, still use ground contact; this only widens what CAN be quality-scored after a touch, not whether the positive on-contact bonus itself is earned. `rewards.py:cleanstop`.

**Evidence:** `ast.parse` clean. Live smoke test (`model_20750.pt`, 2500 steps, 64 envs): no exceptions; `cleanstop` fired 224 times vs. `stopball`'s 332 in the same rollout (ratio 0.67, up from the old softstop+landing_ok-gated version which could only ever fire at or below softstop's own, stricter rate). Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-09-04: softstop weight halved (36.46 -> 18.23, peak 91.15 -> 45.58)

**Context (user request):** `softstop`'s own one-shot bonus was found to be effectively double-counted -- `success`'s 1x/2x/3x tiering ladder already doubles ITS OWN ongoing per-step reward for the rest of the episode the instant `_softstop_flag` fires (not just once). Rough math: `success`'s curriculum peak is 12.5; the softstop-triggered delta (1.0x extra) accumulates `12.5 * dt * (qualifying steps)` -- over ~30 post-save steps with the foot parked at the target (which the post-save recovery rewards actively encourage), that raw contribution (~7.5) exceeds `softstop`'s own one-shot raw contribution (`91.15 * dt ~= 1.82`). So `softstop`'s own weight was carrying more incentive than its raw number suggested.

**Fix:** halved in both places it's declared -- `softstop_curriculum`'s `base_weight` and `"softstop"` `RewardTermCfg`'s static pre-curriculum `weight` (36.46 -> 18.23 each, matching the existing convention that these two must stay in sync). Curriculum peak 91.15 -> 45.58.

**Why this is low-risk:** `_softstop_flag` itself (read by `_ball_is_behind`, `success`'s tiering, `single_foot_save`, and now nothing in `cleanstop` per the entry above) is set by whether `softstop()` FIRES, not by its reward weight -- lowering the weight only shrinks softstop's own payout magnitude, it changes no downstream gating logic at all.

**Evidence:** `ast.parse` clean. Not yet validated against a live training run.

---

## 2026-09-04: play.py's contact-yield viewer arrow left reading the wrong (repurposed) constant

**Context (user report):** "the green target for the contact yield velocity is not the same anymore... the green arrow shows directly in the y direction." Root cause: `_patch_viewer_contact_yield_vis` (`play.py`) live-imports `_FOOT_TARGET_COS`/`_FOOT_TARGET_SIN` from `rewards.py` to draw the `contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y` target arrow. Those constants were retargeted 55->0 deg the same session (see the "Re-added inner_face_orientation_save/foot_inner_face_continuous" entry above) for a DIFFERENT reward -- `rewards.py` itself was fixed to split off `_YIELD_TARGET_COS`/`_YIELD_TARGET_SIN` (still 55 deg) for `contact_yield_velocity_x`/`_y`'s own use, but this one viewer-only import site was missed. With target=0 deg, `_FOOT_TARGET_SIN=0`/`_FOOT_TARGET_COS=1`, so the arrow's formula `[-_FOOT_TARGET_SIN, -sign*_FOOT_TARGET_COS, 0]` collapsed to pure `(0, +/-1, 0)` -- exactly the reported "directly in the y direction."

**Fix:** `_patch_viewer_contact_yield_vis` now imports and uses `_YIELD_TARGET_COS`/`_YIELD_TARGET_SIN` instead. `scripts/play.py`. Viewer-only, no training effect -- applied directly per this project's own viewer/diagnostic-code exception to the change-approval rule. Confirmed no other `_FOOT_TARGET_COS`/`_FOOT_TARGET_SIN` live-code references remain in `play.py` (only historical docstring text). The separate `assigned_foot_angle_deg` P-panel plot correctly still reads `_FOOT_TARGET_ANGLE_DEG` (0 deg) -- that one IS about `inner_face_orientation_save`/`foot_inner_face_continuous`, not contact-yield, so it was already correct and untouched.

**Evidence:** `ast.parse` clean on `play.py`.

---

## 2026-09-04: stopball/softstop's landing_ok no longer requires a "genuine" (non-free) blue landing

**Context (user report):** "if the booster immediately lands on the blue ball marker, softstop doesn't fire." Root cause: `stopball`/`softstop`'s `landing_ok` gate read `env._blue_landed_genuine` (== `blue_landed & ~blue_landed_was_free`). `_blue_landed_was_free` flags a landing as suspicious whenever it happens within the first 10 episode steps (`_get_reach_target_y`, `_BLUE_LANDING_FREE_STEP_THRESHOLD=10`) -- RSI reset placing the foot already near blue by construction, or a trivially-close crossing. This classification is a permanent per-episode latch: once flagged free, `blue_landed_genuine` stays False for the REST of the episode, so `stopball`/`softstop` could never fire again even after a completely genuine, hard, correct-foot deflection happened many ticks later.

**Fix:** `stopball`/`softstop`'s own `landing_ok` now reads raw `env._blue_landed` instead of `env._blue_landed_genuine` -- any landing counts, regardless of how early/suspicious it was. Deliberately scoped narrow: every OTHER consumer of `_blue_landed_genuine` (`phase1_active`, `blue_overshoot_penalty`, `blue_stick_landing`, `footreach`'s targeting switch, orange/red waypoint activation, etc.) is UNCHANGED, since those exist to stop a free/RSI-seeded landing from silently skipping the whole two-stage waypoint mechanism for the rest of the episode (the actual bug `_blue_landed_genuine` was introduced to fix, 2026-07-24) -- that concern is real and orthogonal to whether a LATER, independent deflection event should be scored. `success` (a third, separate `landing_ok` site) is left unchanged -- not reported as broken, out of scope. `rewards.py:stopball,softstop`.

**Evidence:** `ast.parse` clean. Live smoke test (`model_20750.pt`, 2500 steps, 64 envs): no exceptions; 51 genuine `stopball` fires occurred in episodes that had an earlier free/early blue landing -- under the old code these would have been permanently blocked for the whole episode. `stopball` fire count 380, `softstop` 322, both up from the equivalent-length pre-fix rollout. Not yet validated against a live training run.

## 2026-09-04: t_flight_range shrunk 0.7-1.1 -> 0.4-1.0 (user request, matches G1 exactly)

Fixes the catchstep/visibility mismatch from the other side (flight time now fits inside catchstep's 50-tick/1.0s ceiling instead of raising catchstep) and matches G1's own reaction-time range exactly. Confirmed via `legged_robot.py`: G1 never curriculum-ramps `catchstep`/`vanish_step`/`t_flight` -- all fixed from iteration 0. `goalkeeper_env_cfg.py`, both train and play blocks. `ast.parse` clean. Not yet validated against a live training run.

## 2026-09-04: `_vanish_step` bug -- never re-rolled per episode, now re-rolled + difficulty-skewed

**Bug found while researching the visibility-curriculum question:** `_compute_ball_visibility` (`observations.py`) only ever randomized `_vanish_step` ONCE per env, guarded by `if not hasattr(env, "_vanish_step")` -- never re-rolled on episode reset. G1 re-rolls it every reset (`self.vanish_step[ball_ids] = randint(...)`, `legged_robot.py:725`). Consequence: each of the ~4096-6144 parallel env slots got ONE fixed vanish_step value for the ENTIRE training run -- roughly 1-in-30 slots permanently hid the ball after a single flying tick, every episode, forever, while others never hid it at all. Not random per-episode partial observability as G1 intends -- a frozen per-slot property.

**Fix (user request):** `_vanish_step` now re-rolls on every episode reset (`just_reset` mask, same idiom used throughout this codebase). Also skewed toward higher (more-visible) values early in training, tied to the existing `env._ball_difficulty` curriculum (0->1, already used elsewhere e.g. `events.py`'s y_end_range coupling, same `getattr(env, "_ball_difficulty", 1.0)` play-mode-defaults-to-1.0 convention): random range floor = `round(20*(1-difficulty))`, so difficulty=0 samples `[20,30)` (mostly visible), difficulty=1 samples `[0,30)` (exact G1 randomness). `mdp/observations.py:_compute_ball_visibility`.

**Evidence:** `ast.parse` clean. Live smoke test (`model_20750.pt`, 1500 steps, 32 envs): env 0's `_vanish_step` varied across its own resets (`[25, 0, 26, 12]`) -- confirmed re-rolling, previously would have been frozen at one value for the whole run. Play mode confirmed `_ball_difficulty` absent (defaults to 1.0, matching this codebase's existing convention), `vanish_step` range across all envs `[4,29]` observed, consistent with the expected `[0,30)` full-difficulty range. Not yet validated against a live training run.
